# Lakko — AI-agent navigation

This file is loaded into the context of every session automatically. Keep it short and precise; details live in `docs/adr/` and in the plan at `~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md`.

## What Lakko is

A Telegram-class secure messenger with **E2EE by default for everything** (1×1, groups, channels, audio / video calls, broadcasts, audio rooms). Zero-trust server. All clients share a single Rust core + native UI.

**Scale is a service requirement:** the number of users is potentially on par with Telegram or WhatsApp. Every design is sized for that, in traffic per device, in server storage and in work per request, before it is built; a mechanism whose cost grows with contacts times contacts, or that only works at small scale, is not acceptable as the final design.

## Architectural decisions (single source of truth — `docs/adr/`)

| ADR | Decision |
|---|---|
| [0001](docs/adr/0001-mls-only.md) | MLS (RFC 9420) for everything — 1×1, groups, channels, call media keys |
| [0002](docs/adr/0002-rust-core.md) | Shared Rust core + native UI on every platform |
| [0003](docs/adr/0003-livekit-fork.md) | LiveKit fork as the SFU; SFrame (RFC 9605) for media — **superseded by 0011** (SFU choice); SFrame + MLS-exporter parts still in force |
| [0004](docs/adr/0004-anti-censorship.md) | REALITY + MASQUE + Shadowsocks-2022 + WebTransport |
| [0005](docs/adr/0005-contact-invitations.md) | Capability-based invites + 6 scenarios + pseudonym lifecycle |
| [0006](docs/adr/0006-licensing.md) | AGPLv3 monorepo + CLA |
| [0007](docs/adr/0007-multi-device-identity.md) | Two-tier identity (account-level deterministic + per-device random) for multi-device. **Phase 2a (the DeviceGroup) is built; Phase 2b, per-device leaves in conversation groups, is built by [ADR-0050](docs/adr/0050-per-device-conversation-leaves.md) on web (live-tested 2026-09-15) and on iOS + Android (unit-tested 2026-09-16, not yet run live) — G-40 in `docs/security/known-gaps.md`** |
| [0008](docs/adr/0008-livekit-fork-strategy.md) | LiveKit fork — operational strategy — **superseded by 0011**; `CallJwtClaims` shape survives in `crates/lakko-call-jwt` |
| [0009](docs/adr/0009-link-based-call-invitations.md) | Link-based call invitations protocol (ADR-0005 scenario E expansion + welcome-ferry + multi-party KP-forward) |
| [0010](docs/adr/0010-channels-two-tier-broadcast.md) | Channels — two-tier PublisherGroup + sender-key broadcast for >50k subscribers. **The ADR file still reads "design only — implementation lands in Phase 4"; that is stale** — the design shipped: `crates/lakko-channels` (`post` / `seed` / `subscribe` / `epoch` / `rotation_policy`), `lakko_mls::PublisherGroup`, `services/channels` with migrations `0001`–`0004`, the full FFI surface (G4-3c), four fuzz targets, and the co-publisher path of [ADR-0028](docs/adr/0028-group-join-ticket.md) OQ3 on all three clients. Extended in operation by [ADR-0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md) (staged fanout) + [ADR-0031](docs/adr/0031-multi-epoch-envelope-retention.md) (multi-epoch retention) |
| [0011](docs/adr/0011-rust-sfu.md) | Rust SFU on `webrtc-rs` / `str0m` — supersedes 0003 (SFU choice) + 0008. Stack winner decided in S-3-B-spike |
| [0012](docs/adr/0012-ech-hpke-adapter.md) | ECH wire-up via RustCrypto/hpke adapter; ring crypto provider retained. HTTP + WebTransport wired; REALITY architecturally not applicable |
| [0013](docs/adr/0013-sframe-cross-platform-aad.md) | Cross-platform SFrame AEAD AAD = the RFC 9605 `SframeHeader` bytes (KID+CTR), no host RTP `additional_data` — so web (RTCRtpScriptTransform) and native (FrameEncryptorInterface) interop. **The native-shim reconcile LANDED** (the row used to say the shim "currently binds RTP `additional_data`"): `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/sframe-bridge.cc` comments the parameter out at both the encrypt and decrypt entry points (`rtc::ArrayView<const uint8_t> /*additional_data*/`) and states that the host value is intentionally not bound, and `crates/lakko-sframe-native` says the same — so no host-specific `additional_data` enters the AEAD on any stack. The ADR itself carries a further "Update (2026-07)": `SframeHeader::encode` had been a Lakko-custom layout and was rewritten to RFC 9605 §4.3 against the Appendix C.1 vectors, so G-26 is closed in the codec too and only the live deployed-SFU RTP round-trip remains |
| [0014](docs/adr/0014-webauthn-action-binding.md) | WebAuthn action binding (G-13) via a minimal vendored `webauthn-rs-core` fork (`crates/vendor/`, MPL-2.0, `[patch.crates-io]`) adding one challenge-injection method; verification stays upstream-verbatim. Server-side landed in `services/auth`: `challenge = BLAKE3_keyed("lakko/webauthn-action/v1", action_hash ‖ nonce)` + bearer-auth `POST /auth/action/{start,finish}` (migration 0008 `webauthn_states.action_hash`, kind=3, single-use + account-bound + defence-in-depth re-check) + property tests via `webauthn-authenticator-rs` SoftPasskey proving an action-A assertion is rejected on action B + fork-delta CI guard (`services/auth/tests/fork_delta_guard.rs` pins the vendored `core.rs` hash so an accidental edit to the verification path fails CI). Client-side wiring landed on web + iOS + Android: `assertAction` ceremony + `action_hash = SHA-256("lakko/action/<op>/v1" ‖ id)` gating delete-passkey (strict) + remove-device (conditional on the account having a passkey, fail-closed on cancel), reusing each platform's passkey-assertion machinery. Server-side enforcement landed for delete-passkey: `/auth/action/finish` writes a single-use grant (`webauthn_action_grants`, migration 0009, TTL 120 s) and `DELETE /webauthn/credentials/:id` recomputes the action_hash + atomically consumes a matching grant (race-free `DELETE … rows_affected==1`), refusing 403 if absent — so a stolen token alone can't delete a passkey; non-existent-credential stays an idempotent 204. **A second action is server-gated the same way since:** `DELETE /account` (GDPR erasure) consumes a grant bound to `SHA-256("lakko/action/delete-account/v1" ‖ …)` when the account has a passkey (`ACTION_DOMAIN_DELETE_ACCOUNT` in `services/auth/src/webauthn_action.rs`), so the mechanism is a reusable per-action gate rather than a one-off for passkey deletion. remove-device stays client-gated (its MLS Remove commit lands on the zero-trust DS, which can't interpret an action_hash) — analysed + deferred in [ADR-0015](docs/adr/0015-device-removal-authorization.md). **Amended by [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (built 2026-10-03):** `POST /account/keys/reset/finish` deletes every passkey, vault, WebAuthn state and action grant of the account **without** an action grant, even when the account has a passkey (the lost-everything path, where the passkey may be gone too); the per-action grant stays on `DELETE /webauthn/credentials/{id}` and `DELETE /account`. The residual (a stolen session strips the passkeys) was G-46; since 2026-10-04 the reset needs a password-fresh reset grant, so a session alone no longer reaches it; `DELETE /account` on a passkey-less account, which a bare session could run until 2026-10-06, now needs the same fresh-auth grant |
| [0015](docs/adr/0015-device-removal-authorization.md) | Device-removal authorization (G-13). Threat analysis: a DeviceGroup `Remove` commit is signed by the per-device MLS key (seed encrypted under a non-extractable device key, invariant #7), so a remote token-only attacker **cannot** forge a removal — peers reject an invalid signature — which already closes the token-replay threat. The DS is opaque + the auth service is never in the removal path, so there is no server request to gate; the residual is on-device malware that bypasses the UI. **Decision: implement** peer-verified attestation — `services/auth` signs a compact Ed25519 attestation over `(account_id, action_hash, expiry)` on `/auth/action/finish`, carried alongside the `Remove` commit + verified in the `lakko-mls` apply path. Sprints 1–2 landed: the `lakko-attest` crate (format + sign + `verify_bound`); a dedicated attestation Ed25519 key in `services/auth` (migration 0010, generated on first boot, separate from the call-JWT key), `/auth/action/finish` signs + returns `attestation_b64` (1 h TTL), verifying key published at `GET /.well-known/device-attest-jwks.json`. Sprint 4 landed: the OPAQUE-only policy flag is wired into `lakko-mls` — device KeyPackages + DeviceGroup founders advertise the removal-policy `Extension::Unknown` capability, DeviceGroups carry `required_capabilities` for it from creation, `DeviceGroup::set_require_attested_removals` flips it on via a GroupContextExtensions commit + `require_attested_removals` reads it (property test proves propagation to a joined device). Sprint 3 landed: the apply-path verification — `Group::process_incoming_gated` surfaces a Remove commit's removed `(leaf, signature pubkey)` pairs before merge, and `DeviceGroup::process_incoming_verifying_removals` **refuses** (won't merge) a removal under the policy unless a removed leaf is covered by a valid `lakko-attest` attestation bound to the account + `SHA-256("lakko/action/remove-device/v1" ‖ pubkey)` + freshness (6 property tests: valid/missing/wrong-leaf/wrong-key/policy-off/non-remove). Sprint 5 FFI surface + web wiring landed: `setRequireAttestedRemovals` / `requireAttestedRemovals` / `processIncomingVerifyingRemovals` / `removeDeviceActionHash` exposed on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (web); web threads a DeviceGroup journal envelope (`version‖attestation_len‖attestation‖mls_message`) — `Devices.tsx` captures the attestation, every `publishGroup` site wraps, the `DeviceGroupSyncContext` receive loop unwraps + applies via `processIncomingVerifyingRemovals` against the fetched device-attest JWKS key (zero-key fail-closed fallback). iOS + Android wiring landed too — Swift/Kotlin `DeviceGroupJournalEnvelope` + `DeviceAttestKey` + the same capture/wrap/receive-loop shape (`xcodebuild` green after an `xcframework` rebuild; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` green). the policy is now auto-enabled — all three clients call `setRequireAttestedRemovals` (idempotent GroupContextExtensions commit) right after a passkey is first registered (`enableAttestedRemovalsIfNeeded`), so G-13 is implemented end-to-end (server attestation + peer-verified apply path + 3-platform transport + auto-enable). key pinning landed too — each `DeviceAttestKey` client accepts a build-time pinned verifying key (`VITE_LAKKO_DEVICE_ATTEST_KEY` / Info.plist `LakkoDeviceAttestKey` / `BuildConfig.LAKKO_DEVICE_ATTEST_KEY`); when set it never consults the JWKS endpoint (defends a compromised serving tier, not a compromised signing key). G-13 fully implemented; the only optional residual is distributing the pinned key via the signed bootstrap config (needs Phase-5 client bootstrap consumption, out of scope). **Amended 2026-10-19 by [ADR-0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md) (C-85):** under the policy the apply-path gate also accepts a removal covered by a self-removal proof signed by the removed leaf's own key, naming the epoch the commit leaves and the replacing KeyPackage the same commit adds (the rejoin of a device that lost its DeviceGroup state); every removal of a device by someone else still needs an attestation |
| [0016](docs/adr/0016-identity-supersession-transport.md) | Identity-supersession proof transport (G-4, invariant #6). The proof primitive + verifier + FFI already landed (sprints 1–3); this ADR picks the **carrier**. **Decision: Carrier B — the discovery identity bundle (pull).** The proof rides in the user's `identity_bundles` row (already re-published on recover), and a peer re-observes the account key only when it **fetches** the bundle, so verify-and-re-pin happens in the same response — no new transport, routing, or persistent secrets. Carrier A (new DS-mailbox envelope, push) rejected: the mailbox `IdentityPseudonym` is account-key-derived so the old inbox dies on rotation, and the rotation-surviving per-relationship pseudonym is unpersisted (push deferred as future work behind the KT-log chain verifier). Carrier C (piggyback an MLS Commit) rejected: leaks rotation state to the group. **Load-bearing property:** the server is transport-only — the proof is an old-key-signed public artifact stored opaque (nullable `VARBINARY(150)`, never parsed, invariant #2); a malicious server can only **strip** it (→ client sees a changed key with no proof → **fails closed**, OOB), an availability impact never an integrity break, since forging acceptance needs the old key's signature. Full equivocation resistance is out of scope here and lands with `verify_supersession_chain` + the KT log. Five correctness rules: atomic emit-or-abort (never publish new keys without a mintable proof = self-downgrade) · fail-closed verify (changed pin + absent/invalid proof → refuse, no blind TOFU, invariant #4) · pin keyed on `account_id` not the pubkey · monotonic rotation marker (no backward re-pin) · emit window <30 d (the 30-d `MAX_VALIDITY_SECS` is the ceiling). Sprint 0 landed: the discovery server threads an opaque optional `supersession_proof` through upload (preserved across non-rotation re-uploads via `COALESCE`, replaced on a new rotation — a post-`lakko-security` fix so a routine web login can't strip a still-valid proof) + fetch (`services/discovery/src/identity_bundle.rs`, migration `0010`), and the three hand-mirrored wire types (`apps/web/src/discovery/wire.ts`, Android `Wire.kt`, iOS `DiscoveryWire.swift`) carry the optional field. Sprints 1-5 landed end-to-end on web + iOS + Android (build + unit-test verified): the persistent per-contact pin store keyed on `account_id` with a monotonic rotation marker (web `contacts/local-store.ts` v2; iOS `PeerPinStore.swift` (UserDefaults); Android `PeerPinStore.kt` (EncryptedSharedPreferences)); atomic emit-on-recover (capture the OLD exportKey before overwrite → derive old+new ed25519 seeds → `signSupersessionProof` over a 14-day window → upload `{new keys + proof}`, or upload **nothing** if the proof can't be minted — mobile had to **add** the missing `uploadIdentityBundle` recover call); and the fail-closed verify gate `verifyBundleAccountKey` at every `identityFetch` site (TOFU only first-ever; changed pin requires a proof → `verifySupersession` → defence-in-depth authorised==fetched → monotonic marker from the proof's `expires_at`; any failure throws a typed `SupersessionRefused`, never blind-TOFU). Sprint 4 audit: recover-from-words is the only AccountIdentity rotation path (no change-password flow exists), so the emit is correctly placed. **Both of those sentences are historical, not current, and the reasoning is kept because it is what [ADR-0017](docs/adr/0017-phrase-seeded-identity.md) then made moot:** phrase-seeding means a recover no longer rotates the account key, so the **emit half was DELETED** — `AuthContext.tsx` carries the removal note in place of `captureOldExportKey` / `mintSupersessionProof` / `SUPERSESSION_EMIT_WINDOW_SECS`, and `signSupersessionProof` has **no production caller on any client** (tests only). The **verify** half is untouched and still fail-closed, which is the point: the acceptance gate must keep working for a peer that observes a rotation minted before the switch, or by the one remaining rotation path — ADR-0017's `mintFreshIdentity`, the sanctioned publish-**without**-proof, which is why "recover-from-words is the only rotation path" no longer holds either and why that path re-verifies out of band instead. Remaining: a `lakko-security` pass on the verify path (explicit-request gate) + a live cross-platform 2-device e2e (no deployed environment) |
| [0017](docs/adr/0017-phrase-seeded-identity.md) | Phrase-seeded AccountIdentity — decouple identity from the password (Part 1 of the account-recovery overhaul). **Built + reviewed** on web + iOS + Android. Derive `AccountIdentity = Identity::from_seed(bip39_phrase_seed)` instead of `from_seed(exportKey)`, so a routine recover / password change preserves identity (contacts never re-verify) and G-4/ADR-0016 supersession goes **dormant**. `from_seed` is seed-agnostic → **no MLS state-machine change**, no server change (the seed stays client-side, invariant #2); ~30 client `fromSeed` sites switched + a per-platform `account-identity-seed-store` (64-byte phrase seed wrapped under the hardware-backed device key: web IDB AES-GCM / iOS Keychain / Android EncryptedSharedPreferences, wiped on `forgetDevice`). Fresh-device acquisition: no cached seed → park `needs-recovery-phrase`; `provideRecoveryPhrase` HARD-BLOCKS against the published bundle (`verifySeedAgainstPublishedIdentity` — mismatch / 404 / network → refuse, never blind-TOFU; `lakko-security` Part-1 #1). G-4 emit-skip: `recover` compares old vs new account pubkey and skips the proof when equal (always, phrase-seeded), fail-loud on the impossible mismatch. **Lost-everything fallback (Phase 3):** `mintFreshIdentity` — the single sanctioned **publish-without-proof** — reachable only post-ACCESS behind an explicit warned button, mints a fresh random phrase + identity, publishes the new bundle (propagating a publish failure **before** any local mutation so a network blip never strands the account), shows the phrase (acknowledge-gated), lands the session; contacts re-verify once OOB. `lakko-security` Phase-3A: **APPROVE-WITH-NOTES** — adjudged **consistent with invariant #6** (no proof is mintable once the old seed is gone; the acceptance-side fail-closed gate is intact) and the ACCESS-hijack residual **strictly no-worse** than the existing ACCESS threat (OOB safety-number re-verify is the backstop); torn-state + cleanup findings fixed. **Two of the three deferred defence-in-depth items have since LANDED** (the ADR file itself still lists the third as deferred — it is behind the code): the **per-account publish cool-down** is `services/discovery` migration `0011_identity_bundle_fresh_identity_cooldown.sql` (`previous_signature_pubkey` + `last_unproven_key_change_at`, so the server can tell a HEAL — re-publishing the immediately-previous key — from CHURN) gated by `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_FRESH_IDENTITY_COOLDOWN_SECS` (default 3600); and the **KT-log "fresh start" marker** is the `is_reset` flag threaded discovery → transparency append → the `/transparency/latest` response, which is what lets a client distinguish a proven rotation from an unproven fresh mint. Still deferred: the mint notification / audit entry. **Amended 2026-10-02 by [ADR-0072](docs/adr/0072-recovery-model-simplification.md):** the phrase is 12 words (the derivation from the 64-byte seed is unchanged), every device that holds the seed also holds the phrase under its device key, and `mintFreshIdentity` is followed by a recovery-credential replace (`POST /recovery/credential/{start,finish}`): before it, the old phrase kept running the recovery login and password reset after a fresh identity and the new one ran neither. Built in `services/auth` and on web; the words of the new phrase are withheld until the replace succeeds. **Amended again by [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (built 2026-10-03 on the server and all three clients):** `mintFreshIdentity` is now the client half of `POST /account/keys/reset/{start,finish}` (the credential replace moved inside that transaction; the standalone `/recovery/credential/*` routes are gone); the sanctioned publish-without-proof carries an auth-signed **key-reset attestation** (binding the OPAQUE-authenticated account to the client-asserted key at generation `g`, as ADR-0036's genesis attestation binds it to the first), discovery refuses an unattested, unproven key change outright (`409 identity-change-needs-attestation`) and the **heal** that let an old device re-publish its old key is retired; the client order is server first, then the attested publish, retried from an owed record; a reset signs out and removes every other device and deletes the passkeys |
| [0018](docs/adr/0018-recovery-factor-cascade.md) | Recovery factor cascade — ACCESS-only factors (Part 2 of the account-recovery overhaul). **Built + reviewed** (server + web + iOS + Android). **Load-bearing split: ACCESS vs IDENTITY** — the zero-trust server can grant a session token but **never** holds E2EE key material (invariant #2), so factors recover ACCESS only; IDENTITY comes only from the recovery phrase (ADR-0017) or another device. Four factors, all issuing the same `(account_id, session_token, session_expires_at_ms)` triple, PoW-gated + enumeration-safe: **(a) another device** — `device_grants` (migration 0011, hash-at-rest, atomic single-use redeem) + `POST /device/authorize/{start,redeem}`; IDENTITY via **Path B (MLS exporter)** — `DeviceGroup::export_secret` gives both devices the same key, the existing device seals `(grant ‖ phrase seed)` with `seal_with_key` into a magic-tagged rendezvous row, the fresh device opens + redeems + caches (server stays opaque; `lakko-security` **APPROVE**). **(b) passkey** — already issued a token; only the post-ACCESS ADR-0017 acquisition changed. **(c) TOTP** — in-house RFC 4226/6238 (no new dep), `totp_secrets` (migration 0012, AEAD-encrypted at rest under `LAKKO_TOTP_ENC_KEY`), ±1-step window + strictly-newer replay + atomic CAS + 5-in-15-min lockout + enumeration-safe dummy-verify + 503-when-unconfigured. **(d) phone/email OTP** — invariant #1: `recovery_contacts` + `otp_challenges` (migration 0013) store **only** a pepper-keyed BLAKE3 hash (purpose-byte-separated contact vs code), plaintext transient + never logged; pluggable `OtpSender` (`LogOnly` default + real `SmtpOtpSender` (lettre) + Twilio-shaped `SmsOtpSender`, per-kind from env); recover/start enumeration-safe (`{sent:true}` regardless, send only on confirmed match), recover/finish single-use + attempt-capped + pruner-swept. `lakko-security` (c)+(d): **APPROVE-WITH-NOTES** — ACCESS-only / never-key-material + plaintext-never-stored-or-logged both hold; the one finding (inaccurate pruner comment) fixed. The server-held TOTP secret + contact hash are **ACCESS verifiers in the OPAQUE-envelope trust class**, not invariant-#2 violations. Every factor lands the client in the same post-ACCESS ADR-0017 identity-acquisition state machine. Deferred: a live cross-platform 2-device e2e (no deployed env). **Factor (d) was REMOVED on 2026-10-02 ([ADR-0072](docs/adr/0072-recovery-model-simplification.md)):** `recovery_contact.rs`, `otp_sender.rs`, the `/recovery-contact/*` routes and the client screens are deleted and migration `0026` drops both tables, because sending a code makes the server see the address next to the account; everything this row says about (d) is history. **The remaining server factors are (a) another device, (b) passkey, (c) TOTP.** Three words of the phrase are not a server factor: a device that holds the phrase checks them itself. **Path B changed format the same day:** it seals `grant(32) ‖ phrase_entropy(16)` (48 bytes) under the exporter label `lakko/device-recovery/v2` with the row magic `lakko/recovery-payload/v2`; the v1 payload carrying the 64-byte seed is refused, no dual accept (built on web; iOS and Android in progress) |
| [0019](docs/adr/0019-client-authorised-log-leaf.md) | Client-authorised log leaf (G-30 #2 — leaked-old-key equivocation). **Built + reviewed.** Each KT-log entry carries an opaque client `leaf_auth`: a 64-byte **genesis self-sig** (the served key signs its own first log entry — Ed25519 over `"lakko/transparency/leaf-genesis/v1" ‖ username_hash ‖ pubkey_len ‖ pubkey`, in `crates/lakko-transparency/src/leaf_auth.rs`) on a first observation, or a 145-byte **supersession proof** (ADR-0016) on a rotation. The `transparency` append **verifies + refuses** an unauthorised new binding under `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_LEAF_AUTH`; `discovery` threads `leaf_auth` through **upload** and forwards it to the transparency append — the `/transparency/latest` fetch is a `services/transparency` route the clients call directly, not a discovery one, so the two ends of the carry live in different services; the 3 clients verify a genesis self-sig against the served key on a first observation (`verify_leaf_genesis`, FFI/wasm) and the supersession chain on a rotation (`verify_supersession_chain`). Neither establishes trust at TOFU — both are consistency gates on the log entry's self-authorisation. C-56 |
| [0020](docs/adr/0020-witness-cosigning.md) | Witness co-signing for KT-log **split-view resistance** (G-30 #1 — operator equivocation). **Built + `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES** (Sprints 1-6). An independent **witness** co-signs each operator root ONLY after verifying a **Merkle consistency proof** (RFC 6962 §2.1.2 — `merkle.rs` `consistency_proof` / `verify_consistency`) that the new root append-only-extends its last-co-signed root; two divergent forks cannot both extend one root, so a malicious operator cannot fork alone. `witness.rs`: a 105-byte cosig blob `version ‖ tree_root(32) ‖ log_size(8 BE) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/transparency/witness/v1` (distinct from the operator root sig), FFI **verify-only** (`verify_witness_cosig`; signing stays server-side). New `services/witness` (`lakko-svc-witness`): a dedicated Ed25519 key, `POST /witness/cosign` (operator-sig + no-rollback + consistency-from-high-water, refuses a fork → 409) + `GET /witness/key`. `services/transparency` **co-signs-on-read** (signs the head root fresh, reuses the cached cosig keyed on `(tree_root, log_size)`, re-obtains only on growth; witness unreachable → serve **None**, never a stale cosig). The 3 clients pin the witness key build-time (`VITE_LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_KEY` / Info.plist `LakkoTransparencyWitnessKey` / `BuildConfig.LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_KEY`) and require a valid cosig over the served root immediately after the no-rollback gate (`witnessGateRefuses` in `verifyIdentityInLog` + `verifyRotationChain`) — **fail-closed** (missing/invalid → `mismatch`); no pinned key → operator-only transition posture. Strengthens invariant #4 (root gate now needs operator AND witness) + #6 (rotation anchored to a non-forkable root). Residual: operator+witness collusion — the single-witness case; **the K-of-N witness quorum that raises this bar is now implemented in [ADR-0039](docs/adr/0039-kt-log-witness-quorum.md)** (operator must corrupt K distinct pinned witnesses, real only under independent witness operation) + independence-as-deploy-property. The live recovery-e2e proves the transparency → witness cosign round-trip AND (Finding-2, step 7) the **live fork-refusal**: the driver reconstructs the operator SIGNING key from the container's fixed seed, mints a validly operator-signed but FORKED root (same `log_size` as the witness head, different `tree_root`), POSTs it to `POST /witness/cosign`, and the real `services/witness` refuses with **409** (`NotConsistent`); an invalid-operator-signature root is refused with **400**. Verified firsthand against the dockerised 6-container stack (`recovery_kt_pipeline_publish_log_fetch_verify_rotate` passes). ADR-0020's single-witness scope fully landed; the K-of-N generalisation is [ADR-0039](docs/adr/0039-kt-log-witness-quorum.md) |
| [0021](docs/adr/0021-sfu-peer-identity-exposure.md) | SFU peer-identity exposure for group-call roster names. **Accepted; implemented end-to-end on web + iOS + Android, follow-ups included** (this lead-in used to read "implementation in progress" — a leftover the rest of the row has contradicted since the Jun 2026 follow-ups landed). The SFU's `account_pseudonym = account_id[..8]+"…"` is collision-prone (UUIDv7 prefixes cluster by creation time), partially leaks the account_id, and has no local map to a contact name. **Decision:** the SFU emits a **per-call peer tag** `peer_tag = BLAKE3_derive_key("lakko/sfu/peer-tag/v1", len‖call_id ‖ len‖account_id)` truncated to 8 bytes (16 hex) over the **JWT-verified** `account_id` (`sub`) + the `call_id`; the client re-derives the same over its contacts' persisted account ids and matches (unique hit → displayName, else the tag / "Participant N"). Properties: **spoof-proof** (the tag is bound to the auth-signed `sub`, so a peer can only present their own account's tag), **non-leaking** (one-way; account_id never on the wire), **per-call unlinkable** (call_id-keyed → fresh each call, strengthens invariant #5), **collision-resistant** (full hash). Server stays opaque (invariant #2). One canonical derivation `lakko_sfu_proto::peer_tag` (golden-pinned `2151ad585010e90b`), FFI-shared so SFU + all clients agree. Alternatives rejected: identity-pubkey exposure (SFU lacks the pubkey; auth doesn't own the binding; self-asserted = spoofable), stable hash (linkable across calls), prefix-match (ambiguous). **Landed:** the `lakko-sfu-proto` derivation + golden + `PeerSnapshot.peer_tag` + `ServerFrame::PeerJoined.peer_tag` (`#[serde(default)]`, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`) + `services/sfu` derives at both wire sites over the JWT-verified `account_id` + FFI exposure (`sfu_peer_tag` / `sfuPeerTag` + the projection fields) + the **web vertical end-to-end** (`AcceptedContact.accountIdHex` captured at accept; `Chat.tsx` precomputes a `peer_tag → displayName` map + collision-falls-back-to-pseudonym resolver — names render in web group calls). **`lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES** (one-way / per-call-unlinkable / spoof-proof via the JWT sub / no fail-open / 64-bit budget adequate; both low findings fixed). **Native (iOS+Android) — landed:** `peer_tag` threaded through the call signaling + the resolver active on both — this closed the prerequisite "No persistence yet" gap (for the accepted list) via a per-platform `AcceptedContactsStore` (iOS `UserDefaults`+`Codable`; Android `SharedPreferences`+`@Serializable` base64 DTO) rehydrated on `ContactsRepository` + saved on accept, and `ChatViewModel.startGroupCall` precomputes the `peer_tag → label` map + `resolveDisplayName` for `SfuCallUi`. **Caveat:** the native label is the peer's short pubkey fingerprint (the same the contacts UI shows) — iOS/Android contacts have no user-chosen display name (unlike web), so the "human name" payoff awaits editable native contact names (follow-up). ADR-0021 implemented end-to-end on web + iOS + Android. **Follow-ups all landed (Jun 2026):** (1) **editable native contact display names** — iOS/Android `AcceptedContact.displayName` + a Contacts-screen rename affordance + a `displayLabel` (name-or-fingerprint) the roster resolver keys on, so a renamed native contact shows its real name (closing the fingerprint-only caveat above); (2) **web 1×1 universal peer resolution** — `peerContactByMemberPubkeys` reads the live MLS `Group.members()[].signaturePublicKey` to match the non-self member to a contact, so EVERY 1×1 chat resolves the name regardless of creation path (`groupIdB64` fast-path for accept-created chats); (3) **backup round-trip of the per-contact account_id** — `lakko-backup` v3 (presence-byte `account_id`, v1/v2-compat), threaded through the wasm + UniFFI `BackupContactRecord.account_id`, and the **native contact-in-backup round-trip** (both `AuthRepository`s compose accepted contacts into the backup + reconstruct/merge-by-pubkey on restore via the pure `reconstructRestoredContacts`, iOS + Android unit-tested 5/5). All gates green (`tsc`/`vitest`/`vite build` + `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`testDebugUnitTest` + `xcodebuild`/`xcodebuild test`). Invariants #2/#5 preserved (#5 strengthened). **Superseded by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2 (built 2026-10-16, host-tested only):** the SFU knows no account (the admission token carries a room and a random peer id, no `sub`), so it derives and sends no `peer_tag`; `lakko_sfu_proto::peer_tag`, `sfuPeerTag` and the `peer_tag` / `account_pseudonym` roster fields are deleted, and so are the three clients' peer-tag resolvers. Each participant now announces its own SFU peer id, signed with its MLS leaf key and sealed under a key derived from the call seed (`lakko_calls::relay`), and every receiver verifies it against the call's roster; a tile is named only from a verified announcement. The per-call unlinkability and spoof-resistance of the tag are kept by the announcement; the server no longer sees the binding at all |
| [0022](docs/adr/0022-group-admin-roles.md) | **Group-admin roles** — owner / admin / member, peer-verified (Phase-3 blocker C). **Built end-to-end on web + iOS + Android; `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES on BOTH the design and the landed Sprint-1 code.** MLS is egalitarian (any member can commit Add/Remove), so roles are layered on top: a role table (`crates/lakko-mls/src/group_roles.rs` `RoleTable` — one owner + an admin set, **keyed on Ed25519 signature pubkey**, never leaf index) stored in a custom `GroupContext` extension `0xFF02` (the ADR-0015 `0xFF01` removal-policy precedent), bounded + fail-closed codec (version gate + admin_count cap + exact-length). Enforcement is a **peer-verified refuse-to-apply gate** (`Group::process_incoming_verifying_roles`): it reads the committer from `ProcessedMessage::sender()` (no OpenMLS fork) + the role table from the **pre-merge** `GroupContext` (Finding 2 — a self-promoting commit is judged against the table it inherits, not the one it writes) and **refuses to merge** (fail-closed, invariant #4) an Add/Remove/role-change whose committer's role does not authorise it — add/remove need owner-or-admin, the owner is unremovable, a role-table change is owner-only. **Owner-bootstrap option A:** `Group::create` seeds the owner table at creation (closing the ungated-first-set window); a legacy group with no table stays open (no regression). Implementation finding: OpenMLS `CannotRemoveSelf` makes a member self-leave impossible to commit, so the gate's self-Remove branch was dropped as unreachable (v1 has no member self-leave — an admin removes them; ADR-0045 later added the `Leave` arm and a member-facing Leave-group flow on all three clients, so this is history). 8 property tests prove the enforcement table (`tests/group_admin_roles.rs`; the original 7 plus `set_group_roles_refuses_locally_what_every_peer_would_refuse` from the ADR-0045 security pass) + a `role_table_decode` fuzz target. **FFI:** `Role` enum + `RoleTable` + `ownRole`/`groupRoles`/`setGroupRoles`/`processIncomingVerifyingRoles` on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (web uses flat-concatenated admin keys). **Clients:** all 3 chat receive loops apply commits via `processIncomingVerifyingRoles` (enforcement live; the SFU call group reuses the chat exporter, not a separate `Group`, so the call-path caveat does not apply); the members UI gates add/remove on `ownRole` (hidden for a plain member); an owner-only "Make admin"/"Remove admin" toggle drives `setGroupRoles`. Invariants #2 (role table opaque to the server) / #3 (MLS state-machine change — ADR + property tests + fuzz + lakko-security) / #4 (fail-closed) / #5 (roles in-group, not server) all hold. **Extended by [ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md)**, which moved the role check from the committer onto each proposal's own author (the by-value arm unchanged), **built the deferred ownership transfer** (`Group::transfer_ownership` / `propose_ownership_transfer` over `RoleTable::with_owner_transferred` + `OutgoingOwner`), added the `Proposal::SelfRemove` branch this ADR deferred as unreachable, and minted **C-63** (never leave the group with an owner who is not in it). **The transfer now HAS a client affordance** — this row previously read "no client affordance … the owner dead-end is closed in `lakko-mls` and still open in the product", and `0274fa7` closed the product half on all three (web `contacts/client.ts`, iOS `ChatRepository.proposeOwnershipTransferLocked`, Android `ChatRepository`), each driving **`proposeOwnershipTransfer` only**: `isOwner` and "uses the proposal path" are the same read (the role table's presence), so the direct arm is structurally unreachable from a client. `owner_survives` closes a hole `set_group_roles` has carried since ADR-0022 shipped: an owner could always install an owner key no leaf holds, permanently un-administrable. Open follow-ups: admin-manages-admins, admin-quorum recovery for a **lost** owner (transfer needs the owner's key, so it does not answer that half), the Phase-2b per-device-leaf device→account resolution |
| [0023](docs/adr/0023-message-edit-delete-reactions.md) | **Message edit / delete / reactions** (Phase-3 block B). **Built end-to-end on web + iOS + Android; `lakko-security` APPROVE** (forgery vector closed — op authority comes from the MLS-authenticated sender leaf, not the attacker-controlled envelope body; parser bounded/fuzzed/no-panic; server opaque; gate fail-closed on all 3 clients; two residuals — client-enforcement + reaction-leaf-renumber — accepted in the ADR). The chat wire carrier is the JSON `Envelope` (`lakko-attachments`), NOT the dead proto `ApplicationPayload` (defined in `schemas/mls-app/v1.proto` but never wired — left non-authoritative). Edit/delete/reaction ride as four optional `#[serde(default)]` v1.x envelope fields (`edit_of` / `delete_of` / `react_to` + `emoji`) referencing the target by its **DS `messageId`** (per-group monotonic, observed identically by every client); `parse_body` classifies into `MessageBody::{Chat,Edit,Delete,Reaction}` (bounded, fail-closed — `MAX_EMOJI_BYTES=64`, >1 op field or a reaction without a valid emoji degrades to `Chat`). Ordering is the DS journal total order → LWW = highest-`messageId` op wins (no HLC on the single-journal path; multi-device `lakko-sync` `EditChain` merge deferred to Phase 2). **Security core:** a peer-verified **author-only integrity gate** — `lakko-mls` now surfaces the Application sender leaf (`Incoming::Application { bytes, sender_leaf }`, captured via `ProcessedMessage::sender()` like ADR-0022; threaded onto the FFI `ProcessedMessage.senderLeaf`), and every client applies an edit/delete **only if** the op's author matches the target message's original author, else drops it (fail-closed, invariant #4) — so a member cannot rewrite/erase another's message. Reactions are open to any member but attributed to that same author for dedup/aggregation/per-user toggle. **What "author" means changed under the ADR-0045 security review (C-68), and the original rule was an impersonation, not a residual:** the gate compared a raw **leaf index**, and OpenMLS hands a vacated leaf to the next member added — so a remove-then-add, which ADR-0045 composes into ONE commit landing in a single poll batch, let the newcomer inherit the departed member's identity and rewrite, delete and un-react their history. All three clients now resolve the sender leaf to the member's **Ed25519 signature key at receipt** — the one moment the roster IS the epoch the message was sealed at — and carry the key onward (`apps/web/src/chat/author-identity.ts` + the Swift/Kotlin twins). The core still surfaces `Incoming::Application { bytes, sender_leaf }`; the resolution is the client's. **FFI:** `composeEditEnvelope` / `composeDeleteEnvelope` / `composeReactionEnvelope` + `parseBodyEnvelope` (→ `ParsedBody { kind, text, attachments, target, emoji }`) on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (web; `target` is a `bigint`). **Clients:** per-message action menu (web hover/right-click, iOS/Android long-press) — Edit + Delete on own messages, React (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢 🙏) on any; renders an "(edited)" marker, a "message deleted" tombstone, and reaction chips with counts; the sender applies its own op locally (it never receives its own Application message back). Open: multi-device convergence (Phase 2). **The other two entries on this list are closed:** reaction attribution across a leaf renumber went from accepted-residual to fixed-defect via C-68 above, and typing-indicators + read-receipts shipped as [ADR-0024](docs/adr/0024-typing-read-receipts.md) |
| [0024](docs/adr/0024-typing-read-receipts.md) | **Typing indicators + read receipts** (Phase-3 block B). **Built end-to-end on web + iOS + Android.** Extends the ADR-0023 JSON `Envelope` with two more optional v1.2 fields — `typing: Option<bool>` + `read_up_to: Option<i64>` — reusing `parse_body` (now → `MessageBody::{…,Typing,ReadReceipt}`, still fail-closed: >1 op field degrades to `Chat`) + the ADR-0023 sender attribution + the FFI pattern. **Transport decision:** the DS journal is the only client-to-client transport (append-only FIFO, 30-day TTL); `lakko-presence` is unwired + orthogonal (online/away tokens); so both signals ride the journal, with client-side discipline keeping typing ephemeral. **Typing:** throttled send (≤1 `typing:true` per ~4 s while editing; one `typing:false` on blur/send, no `false` spam); receiver auto-expires the "X is typing…" state after ~6 s; **re-sync replay guard** — a typing signal whose DS-row `created_at` is older than the expiry window is ignored (so a full re-download never resurrects stale typing). **Read receipt:** a monotone per-`(group, sender)` `read_up_to` watermark — **keyed on the sender's signature key, not its leaf** (C-68: the watermark is monotone and never reset on a membership change, so a leaf-keyed registry handed a newcomer the departed member's `readUpTo` outright and `isReadByEveryOther` then reported messages as read by someone who never saw them) — broadcast so the author renders Sent → Read on its own messages (Read = every other member's watermark ≥ the messageId; 1×1 = the other person read it); monotone → idempotent on re-sync. **FFI:** `composeTypingEnvelope` / `composeReadReceiptEnvelope` + `parseBodyEnvelope` extended (`ParsedBody` gains `is_typing`; read-receipt's `up_to` rides `target`) on UniFFI + wasm. Invariants #2 (server relays opaque envelopes) / #5 (in-group only, no server artefact) hold. Accepted trade-offs: typing rows persist until the 30-day prune (anti-pattern, mitigated by throttle + tiny payload + stale-ignore); read receipts broadcast read-state (a per-user opt-out is deferred). Open: a real ephemeral transport (`lakko-presence`), the read-receipt privacy opt-out, multi-device read-watermark sync (Phase 2) |
| [0025](docs/adr/0025-bootstrap-transport-dialer.md) | **Bootstrap transport dialer** (Phase 5 — anti-censorship operational wiring). **Proposed; the Rust dialer landed.** Before this, `lakko-transport` was fully tested but **dead code** — REALITY/MASQUE/Shadowsocks-2022/WebTransport/QUIC `Transport` impls, the `HappyEyeballs`/`race_select` fail-closed selector, and the signed `BootstrapConfig` + 4-stage `verify` + `DohFetcher` all existed but were a dependency of no client/service (the clients dial over platform HTTP: web `fetch`, iOS `URLSession`, Android Ktor). | [0026](docs/adr/0026-anti-censorship-wiring.md) | **Anti-censorship wiring — end-to-end architecture** (Phase 5). **Accepted; Phases 1-4 + WS-over-tunnel landed end-to-end on iOS + Android — every native HTTP + WebSocket surface routes through the tunnel when armed; only the Phase-5 deploy + live censored-net test remain.** Defines the whole system that puts the built-but-dead transport stack on the wire: client plaintext-HTTP rides an **encrypted obfs tunnel** to a new **server-side obfs-terminating proxy** that byte-relays to the internal HTTP services (the obfs layer gives transport confidentiality; the MLS payloads stay E2EE — #2/#4 untouched). Components: (1) `services/obfs-proxy` (runs the obfs listeners → byte-relay to a fixed internal target, a dumb confined relay — no HTTP parsing, no open relay); (2) `ByteStreamConnection` (framed `Connection` ↔ byte stream); (3) a Rust-owned `TunneledHttpClient` over `BootstrapDialer`; (4) UniFFI `tunneled_request` (no wasm); (5) native client routing when "armed" (fail-closed, no silent downgrade); (6) bootstrap-config fetch at boot. **Hard constraint:** web-in-browser **cannot** do raw-socket transports (sandbox) — anti-censorship is a **native** feature (iOS/Android/desktop-Tauri); browser web stays on direct HTTPS. **Phasing:** 1 server proxy · 2 client bridge+HTTP+loopback-e2e · 3 FFI · 4 native wiring · 5 deploy + **live censored-net test** (deferred — no environment). **Phases 1+2 shipped.** Phase 1: `services/obfs-proxy` (`lakko-svc-obfs-proxy`, lib+bin) — `relay.rs` full-duplex frame↔TCP relay (`select!` + mpsc over the single `&mut Connection`), `config.rs` env parsing (internal target + REALITY bind/cover-SNI/allow-list), `main.rs` REALITY accept loop + axum health. Phase 2: `crates/lakko-transport/src/tunnel_http.rs` `TunneledHttpClient` — minimal HTTP/1.1 over a `Box<dyn Connection>` from `BootstrapDialer` (`Connection: close` + read-until-close, `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`-bounded, `tunneled_request` / `tunneled_request_via_dialer`) — the inner HTTP is plaintext-inside-the-encrypted-tunnel. A streaming `ByteStreamConnection` (AsyncRead/Write, for WS-over-tunnel) is deferred to the WS phase. **`services/obfs-proxy/tests/loopback_e2e.rs` proves the full path headless:** a real `RealityTransport` client → real `RealityListener` → the proxy `relay` → a fake internal HTTP origin → response back through the tunnel, parsed by `tunneled_request` (8 obfs-proxy tests + 4 `tunnel_http` unit tests). Phase 3 (done, iOS xcframework verified firsthand): `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/transport.rs` `tunneled_http_request` (UniFFI, no wasm — web excluded) — parses+verifies the signed config against a pinned trust anchor → `BootstrapDialer::from_signed_config` → `block_on` the dial+request on a private current-thread runtime → `FfiTunneledResponse`; fail-closed (`FfiError::Transport`), 2 fail-closed unit tests. This pulls the (audited) `lakko-transport` stack into the mobile FFI so REALITY/MASQUE/Shadowsocks run on-device. **aws-lc fix (one line):** adding `lakko-transport` first pulled `aws-lc-sys` (the AWS-LC C crypto) transitively and `build-ios` failed at the link (exit 101 — `aws-lc-sys` objects built for a newer iOS than linked); the culprit was `web-transport-quinn`'s `default = ["aws-lc-rs"]`, so it is now pinned `default-features = false, features = ["ring"]` — `cargo tree -i aws-lc-sys` is empty for both the `aarch64-apple-ios` and `aarch64-linux-android` FFI targets, and `build-ios` rebuilds the xcframework green (both device + simulator slices). **Phase 1+ (SS-2022 + WebTransport proxy listeners) shipped** (see the `obfs-proxy` service row); **MASQUE listener built but NOT wired** — CONNECT-UDP's unreliable, unordered datagrams are unfit for relaying a reliable HTTP service (the `#[ignore]`'d e2e documents it). **Phase 4 (native wiring + arming) shipped on iOS + Android.** A new UniFFI `fetch_bootstrap_config(doh_domain, path, trust_anchor, now)` sources the config: DoH-resolve + IP-pinned HTTPS GET + parse + `BootstrapConfig::verify` against the build-time-pinned trust anchor → the **verified raw config bytes** (fail-closed — an unverified config never leaves the call). An `AntiCensorshipManager` (iOS `Transport/`, Android `transport/`) owns the user "Anti-censorship" preference (UserDefaults / SharedPreferences) and, when on, fetches+verifies + publishes the armed **ambient** `AntiCensorshipConfig.current`; the pin is read from `Info.plist LakkoBootstrap{TrustAnchor,DohDomain,ConfigPath}` / `BuildConfig.LAKKO_BOOTSTRAP_{TRUST_ANCHOR,DOH_DOMAIN,CONFIG_PATH}` (all default-empty → can't arm → no insecure default). A Settings toggle + a boot-arm (`LakkoApp.task` / `MainActivity.onCreate`) drive it; fail-closed (fetch/verify failure → disarmed + a loud `unavailable` state, never a silent armed-with-unverified-config). The routing seam is spread to the native REST clients — iOS via `AntiCensorshipTransport.perform` over `URLSession` (reads `current` per-call), Android via a custom `AntiCensorshipEngine` reading `current` **per-request** over Ktor/OkHttp — covering auth, discovery, DS, storage, channels, transparency, push, webauthn, call-JWT, TURN. So when armed the whole app's core API traffic rides the obfuscated tunnel; disarmed = the direct path byte-for-byte unchanged (#4 — no silent downgrade either way). **WS-over-tunnel landed end-to-end** (SFU signaling now routes through the tunnel when armed). Transport layer: `crates/lakko-transport/src/byte_stream.rs` `ByteStreamConnection` (ADR-0026 component 2 — presents the framed `Connection` as one `AsyncRead + AsyncWrite` byte stream via a pump task: split-duplex + mpsc + `select!` recv/send over the single `&mut conn`, the relay idiom; 4 unit tests) + `ws_tunnel.rs` `tunneled_websocket` (a real RFC 6455 client handshake + frame codec via `tokio-tungstenite` `handshake`-only — no inner TLS, the tunnel encrypts — over a `ByteStreamConnection`); `services/obfs-proxy/tests/loopback_e2e.rs` proves it headless (a WebSocket — handshake + `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` + text + binary frames — round-trips through a REALITY tunnel + the byte-relay to a WS echo origin). FFI: `lakko-ffi-uniffi::ws` `open_tunneled_websocket` → `TunneledWsConnection` (`send_text` / `send_binary` / `recv` / `shutdown`; a process-wide multi-thread runtime owns the long-lived bidirectional stream; sink/stream split behind mutexes; 2 tests incl. a real-object loopback round-trip). Native: the iOS + Android `SfuSignalingClient` gained an armed branch — `connectTunneled` opens the FFI WS, runs a **blocking** `recv()` loop on a dedicated thread (iOS GCD queue / Android `Dispatchers.IO`), and routes sends through `send_text` (Android via an ordered outbound channel); disarmed = the direct URLSession/Ktor WS path unchanged. The `DeviceAttestKey` JWKS fetch is routed too (iOS via `perform`, Android via the `AntiCensorshipEngine`), so **every** native HTTP + WS surface now rides the tunnel when armed. Only Phase 5 (deploy + live censored-net test, no environment) remains. Alternatives rejected: platform-socket injection (opaque TLS), on-device SOCKS, structured RPC-instead-of-HTTP, web-as-first-class (sandbox-impossible) |
| [0027](docs/adr/0027-external-join-approval.md) | **External-join proposal approval** (G-3). **Accepted; complete on all three clients — Phases 1-3, the Phase-4 admin UI, the joiner-side core/FFI, and (via [ADR-0028](docs/adr/0028-group-join-ticket.md)) the request-to-join UI and the join-ticket transport this row used to list as the only outstanding work.** Lets a group admin see + approve a non-member's MLS external join (RFC 9420 §12.1.8.3) — the prerequisite for the Phase-4 channel-subscriber join ([ADR-0010](docs/adr/0010-channels-two-tier-broadcast.md)). The data-less `Incoming::ExternalJoinProposal` unit variant became `ExternalJoinProposal { joiner_signature_key }` (extracted from the external `Add`'s KeyPackage; refuses a non-`Add` / non-`NewMemberProposal`-sender proposal, defence in depth), retained in an in-memory per-group queue (`MAX_PENDING_EXTERNAL_JOINS` cap, de-duped by signature key — anti-spam). `Group::approve_external_join(identity, proposal_ref)` is **role-gated** (ADR-0022 owner/admin, fail-closed on an enforced group; open group stays open). It originally reused the audited `add_member` path (committing the joiner's external Add ≡ adding their KeyPackage → Commit + Welcome; peers re-verify the resulting Add's committer role on receipt, so a forged local approve can't take effect on them either) — **ADR-0045 S7 (`576ea14`) replaced that with an `Add` proposal the approving admin authors itself**, so an approve advances no epoch and returns no Welcome; the role gate and the peer re-verification are unchanged, they now run over the proposal instead of the commit. The direct arm survives only as `pub(crate) approve_external_join_directly` for the table-less `PublisherGroup`. `reject_external_join` (local discard, no epoch advance) + `pending_external_joins()` accessor. 5 property tests (owner-approves-adds / reject-removes / dedup / unknown-ref-errors / non-admin-refused). Decision: reuse `add_member` over openmls's commit-all `commit_to_pending_proposals` (single-joiner targeting + audited-path reuse); pending retention was in-memory in v1 and is **persisted since 2026-09-26** as a row in the identity's storage provider (`lakko/pending-external-joins/v1\0 ‖ group_id`, rides `snapshot_storage` with no client store change; `reject_external_join` therefore takes the `Identity` on both FFI surfaces), so a `/g/` request survives the owner's restart — the defect seen live that day. Alternatives rejected: auto-approve any join (open-door + spam, #4), external-commit self-join (no admin gate), leave data-less (doesn't close G-3). **Phase 2 (FFI) landed**: `approveExternalJoin`/`rejectExternalJoin`/`pendingExternalJoins` + the joiner-signature-key field on `ProcessedMessage` on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (web; pending list = flat-concatenated 32-byte keys). **Phase 3 (Welcome transport) landed**: `crates/lakko-mls/src/welcome_rendezvous.rs` `WelcomeRendezvous { joiner_signature_key, welcome }` — a bounded, fail-closed envelope (`version ‖ sig_key_len ‖ sig_key ‖ welcome`) the admin posts to the group journal; the not-yet-member joiner (which knows the group_id from its proposal) polls the journal, matches the envelope to its own signature key (`welcomeRendezvousFor`), and opens the inner Welcome. The DS stores it opaque (#2); existing members skip it (it is not an MLS message → their receive loop drops it). 8 unit tests + the `welcome_rendezvous_decode` fuzz target. **Phase 4 client UI landed (web + iOS + Android)**: each client's chat poll loop classifies an `ExternalJoinProposal` into an `externalJoinRequest` event carrying the joiner key (not a timeline bubble — it drives a members-panel pending list via `group.pendingExternalJoins()`, the MLS-core retained set being the source of truth); an owner/admin-only members-panel section (same role gate as add/remove, hidden when empty) renders pending requests with Approve (drives the role-gated approve → broadcast the commit + `wrapWelcomeRendezvous` & post the Welcome rendezvous to the same journal, suppressing the sender echo) / Reject (local drop of the retained proposal). Web (`Chat.tsx` + `contacts/client.ts` + `chat/client.ts`) / iOS (`ChatClient`/`ChatRepository`/`ChatViewModel`/`ChatScreen`) / Android (the Kotlin twins) all verified (`tsc`+`vitest`+`vite build` · `xcodebuild` · `:app:compileDebugKotlin`). **Joiner-side core/FFI landed**: `Identity::create_external_join_proposal(group_id, epoch)` (`crates/lakko-mls/src/key_package.rs`) — a non-member crafts the external `Add` proposal (`JoinProposal::new`) for a group's id+epoch (learned out-of-band), embedding a fresh KeyPackage whose private keys persist in its own provider so the same identity later opens the Welcome; FFI-exposed (`createExternalJoinProposal` on UniFFI + wasm). A full in-process e2e property test (`joiner_api_full_loop_proposes_approves_joins_via_rendezvous`) proves the whole G-3 loop: non-member proposes → admin `process_incoming_verifying_roles` surfaces it → `approve_external_join` → the Welcome rides a `WelcomeRendezvous` → the joiner matches it by its own sig key + `join_from_welcome` → member at the post-approve epoch. **Both items this row used to list as Remaining shipped in ADR-0028**: the client-side request-to-join UI (`requestToJoinFromLink` + `pollForJoinWelcome` on web, iOS and Android) and the transport conveying `(group_id, epoch)` to the non-member — which turned out not to need the ADR-0010 channel ticket at all, because a `/g/` capability carries only the `group_id` and the joiner reads the epoch off the journal's public MLS framing. Invariants #2/#3/#4 + ADR-0022 role gate |
| [0028](docs/adr/0028-group-join-ticket.md) | **Group join-ticket (invite-link for joiner-initiated external join)** (G-3, the live consumer of ADR-0027). **Accepted; fully implemented — Phase 1 (codec + epoch reader + FFI + fuzz) + Phase 2 (owner create-link UI) + Phase 3 (joiner request-to-join flow) all landed on web + iOS + Android. G-3 is closed end-to-end across all three clients (modulo a live admin approving + a deployed-env e2e).** A capability-based group invite-link mirroring [ADR-0009](docs/adr/0009-link-based-call-invitations.md): an owner/admin mints a signed `GroupInviteCapability` (`version ‖ group_id_len ‖ group_id ‖ issuer_pubkey ‖ expiry_ms ‖ sig`, inline `/g/<base64url>` link) that conveys only the `group_id` to a non-member, who reads the group's **current epoch from the journal's public MLS framing** (`epoch_of_message`, no group secret — openmls `ProtocolMessage::epoch()` works pre-decrypt) and crafts an external-join proposal ([ADR-0027](docs/adr/0027-external-join-approval.md) `create_external_join_proposal`) for that epoch. **Amended 2026-09-26 (v2, flag day):** the capability carries a sealed preview — group name, inviter name, member count at mint time — under a link key that rides in the URL fragment, so the invite page can say who invites to what while the ledger and the access logs stay ciphertext-only (UX `docs/ux/group-invite-page.md`). **Amended 2026-09-27 (short links, flag day):** all three ledgers (`/i/`, `/c/`, `/g/`) address a row by a random 6-byte server-minted id (8 base64url chars, `BINARY(6)`, migration `0018`; insert retried on a key collision, never an upsert, no longer idempotent by content), the six open fetch/consume routes moved behind discovery's per-IP rate limit, and the `/g/` fragment is a 12-byte link secret (16 chars) HKDF-expanded to the preview key with the group id and issuer bound in, so a pointer link is about 45 characters. **Two load-bearing decisions:** (1) **no epoch in the ticket** — else it goes stale on the first commit; the joiner reads it from the journal head, **paging to the end** (the DS pages at 100 rows by default and a single `after=0` read on a 370-row journal produced a stale epoch that every member refused in silence, found live 2026-09-19; `journalHeadEpoch` / `JournalHeadEpoch` on the three clients, used by the group-link and the channel co-publisher joiners); (2) **the security boundary is the admin approval gate** (ADR-0027 role-gated, peer-verified), **not** a ticket secret — a leaked link lets a stranger *ask*, never *in*; anti-spam = PoW + the per-group pending cap + dedup. The link reframes the "channel join-ticket" of ADR-0027's row: ADR-0010 channel *subscribers* are not MLS members (sender-key broadcast), so this ticket is for adding a **member/publisher** to any MLS group. **Phase 1 landed**: `crates/lakko-contacts/src/group_invite.rs` (`GroupInviteCapability` + `compose`/`decode_and_verify`, bounded fail-closed; 17 unit tests, the original 14 plus the issuer-signed revocation added for OQ1) + `lakko_mls::epoch_of_message` + `epoch_after_message` (the framed epoch, plus one for a Commit: the number a joiner must target when the journal head is a Commit; one test covering an application row, a Commit and a Welcome) + FFI (`composeGroupInviteCapability` + `GroupInviteCapability` + `epochOfMessage` + `epochAfterMessage` on UniFFI + wasm) + the `group_invite_decode` fuzz target. **Phase 2 landed (owner create-link UI on web + iOS + Android)**: a members-panel owner/admin-only "Create invite link" button (same role gate as add/remove) calls `composeGroupInviteCapability(identity.ed25519Seed, group.groupId, 0)` and shows the copyable `https://lakko.me/g/<base64url>` link (web `contacts/client.ts createGroupInviteLink` + `Chat.tsx`; iOS `ChatRepository`/`ChatViewModel`/`ChatScreen`; Android the Kotlin twins). Verified web `tsc`/`vitest`/`vite build`, iOS `xcodebuild`, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`. **Phase-3 web landed (joiner flow)**: `contacts/client.ts` `requestToJoinFromLink` (decode+verify the capability fail-closed → read the current epoch from the journal head's public framing via `epochOfMessage` → `createExternalJoinProposal` → post to the journal) + `pollForJoinWelcome` (poll the journal → `welcomeRendezvousFor` → `joinGroupFromWelcome`); a public `/g/:slug` route + `JoinGroup.tsx` screen drives decode → "Request to join" → poll-loop → navigate to the joined chat (anonymous visitors prompted to log in, mirroring `/i/`). The live requester now exists on web (closes G-3 end-to-end on web, modulo a live admin approving). **Phase-3 native landed (iOS + Android joiner)**: `ChatRepository.requestToJoinFromLink` + `pollForJoinWelcome` (decode+verify the capability → read the epoch from the journal head → `createExternalJoinProposal` → post → poll → `parseWelcomeRendezvous` match → `joinGroupFromWelcome`); a `/g/` deep-link (extended `InviteIntake` + `MainActivity.handleGroupJoinIntent` / iOS `onOpenURL`, plus the Android manifest `pathPrefix="/g/"`) routes to a `JoinGroupLandingScreen` that drives request → poll → join. Verified iOS `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. **ADR-0028 is fully implemented on all three clients; G-3 is closed end-to-end** (the only residuals are a live admin-approval e2e on a deployed environment + the optional anti-abuse hardenings in the ADR's open questions). **Open questions resolved: OQ1 (anti-abuse blob ledger)** — `services/discovery/src/group_invite.rs` (upload/fetch/consume + migrations `0014`/`0015`, random server-minted `blob_id` (content-addressed until 2026-09-27) + per-joiner consume dedup + a soft `max_uses` ceiling that is NOT in the signed capability) + the 3-client pointer-link wiring (`uploadGroupInvite` on mint → short `/g/<blob_id>` link (an inline fallback until 2026-10-19, see the amendment at the end of this row); `fetchGroupInvite` + `consumeGroupInvite` on join); and **OQ2 (issuer-in-roster surfacing)** — the joiner posts a `group_join_attribution` envelope (see the `lakko-contacts` row) alongside its proposal, and every client's members panel surfaces "via &lt;issuer fingerprint&gt; · member / not a current member" per pending request (advisory; the role gate stays the boundary); and **OQ3 (channel PublisherGroup reuse)** — a `lakko_mls::PublisherGroup` is a thin wrapper around a vanilla `Group` with a deterministic `group_id`, so the ticket reuses verbatim to add a **co-publisher**: `PublisherGroup::group_id()`/`epoch()` accessors + a property test (`co_publisher_joins_via_external_join_ticket_and_shares_channel_seed` — both publishers derive the same `channel_seed` after a ticket-driven external join) + the owner-side external-join surface (`groupId`/`processIncomingVerifyingRoles`/`pendingExternalJoins`/`approveExternalJoin`/`rejectExternalJoin`) delegated onto the FFI `PublisherGroup` (UniFFI + wasm); the subscriber path (sender-key, ADR-0010) is untouched. **Owner-side web co-publisher UI landed** — `apps/web/src/channels/copublisher.ts` + a `CoPublishersPanel` in `Channels.tsx`: the channel owner opens the persistent `PublisherGroup`, mints a `/g/` co-publisher link over its `group_id`, runs a 5 s DS journal poll loop (`processIncomingVerifyingRoles` — the first DS-journal/membership transport the channel client ever had), and approves/rejects pending requests; **reseal-after-add** is load-bearing (approve commits the Add → MLS epoch advances → channel seed changes → the approve path reseals the new-epoch seed to every subscriber via the audited `sealSubscriberSeedEnvelope` + atomic `/rotate` fanout, WITHOUT a second `rotate()`), else existing subscribers break (`tsc` clean, web vitest 222 green). **Joiner-side web landed too** — `copublisher.ts` `requestToCoPublish` + `pollForCoPublishWelcome` (decode the `/g/` cap → `channelIdFromPublisherGroupId` recovers the channel id from the PublisherGroup `group_id` → external-join proposal + post → poll the journal → `joinFromWelcome` → `freshPublisherKeys` mints local publisher keys at the post-approve epoch) + a "Join as co-publisher" affordance on the New-channel screen; the `channel_id_from_group_id` inverse derivation landed in `lakko-mls` (+ unit test) + FFI (`channelIdFromPublisherGroupId`, UniFFI + wasm). **iOS + Android co-publisher landed too (owner + joiner)** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Channels/CoPublisher.swift` + a `CoPublishersPanelView` + `JoinCoPublisherScreen` in `ChannelsScreen.swift` (Swift), and `apps/android/.../channels/CoPublisher.kt` + a `CoPublishersPanel` + `JoinCoPublisherScreen` in the Compose `ChannelsScreen.kt` (Kotlin): the same owner shape (open the persistent `PublisherGroup`, mint a `/g/` link over its `group_id`, 5 s DS-journal poll via `processIncomingVerifyingRoles` — the channel client's first DS-journal/membership transport on native too — approve/reject with the load-bearing **reseal-after-add**, the `RotationCron` fanout WITHOUT a second `rotate()`) + the same joiner shape (`requestToCoPublish`: recover the channel id via `channelIdFromPublisherGroupId`, read the epoch from the journal head, external-join proposal + a self-signed OQ2 attribution; `pollForCoPublishWelcome`: match the owner's `WelcomeRendezvous` by leaf key, `joinFromWelcome`, then `freshPublisherKeys` at the post-approve epoch the subscribers were resealed to) as web. Verified iOS `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + Android `just regen-bindings` + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL (the native `.so` needs a cargo-ndk rebuild for the OQ3 FFI symbols at CI/deploy — local NDK unset). **OQ3 (channel co-publisher) is now implemented on all three clients; the only residual is a live 2-publisher reseal e2e (no deployed channels env).** Invariants #2 (server transport-only) / #4 (admin gate fail-closed) + ADR-0027 role gate **Amended by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6 (2026-10-16):** a PublisherGroup id is now the owner's derivation over its account seed and the channel id, so `channel_id_from_group_id` / `channelIdFromPublisherGroupId` are removed; a co-publisher `/g/` link carries the channel id (and optionally the channel name) in its sealed preview (preview kind 2, `composeCoPublisherInviteCapability`), and the joiner opens the group with `publisherGroupLoadJoined` under the id the link conveyed, recording the channel -> group pair (web channel log, iOS / Android co-published marks). The OQ3 text above that says the joiner "recovers the channel id from the group id" is history. The co-publisher approve and the rotation are recorded before their effects leave the device (G-50, C-82). **Amended 2026-10-19 (maintainer; web, iOS, Android, host-tested only): the inline form is removed for `/i/`, `/g/` and `/c/`.** Every link is the ledger pointer `/<k>/<8-char id>#<secret>`; a failed upload, a network failure included, mints no link (the person sees the error and tries again; the link's reach key is retired); a slug that is not an 8-character ledger id is malformed, so an inline link opens as dead (contact: Contacts screen; group, call: their invalid state). The co-publisher link, which was always the inline form, is uploaded to the group-invite ledger (no use limit, never expires, the minting device keeps its revocation seed) and fetched by the joiner. `lakko-sync` link records now require a 6-byte ledger id and a 32-byte revocation seed. ADR-0028 / ADR-0009 head notes |
| [0029](docs/adr/0029-mid-call-key-rotation.md) | **Mid-call call-key rotation (overlap keyring)** (G-18). **Accepted (maintainer chose Variant 2); implemented end-to-end on web + iOS + Android — headless-verified, only the deployed-SFU live-rekey e2e deferred (like G-26).** When a group's MLS epoch advances DURING a live SFU call the per-call SFrame seed changes; the call must rotate the key with a ~1 s overlap window (the receiver keeps the previous key so in-flight old-epoch frames still open, then drops it — C-8/C-9/C-10). A recon found the existing overlap mechanism in `GroupCallSession` was **dormant** (no client instantiated it — clients seal/open at the raw-seed level, off-thread, without the MLS group). **Decision (Variant 2 = core-via-FFI):** extract the overlap keyer into a seed-based `lakko_calls::CallKeyRing` (current + previous `CallKey`; `try_open_with_overlap` / `try_open_framed_with_overlap` try current then, during the window, previous, on an AEAD failure; `rekey_from_seed_at` / `prune_expired` / `has_live_previous`; time-source-free — the caller supplies `now_ms`), which `GroupCallSession` **delegates** to (its 5 property tests stay green) and which is FFI-exposed (uniffi + wasm) **and** surfaced in the lean `lakko-sframe-native` C ABI (`lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_*`, `Mutex`-guarded so the codec-thread open races the signaling-thread rekey safely). Clients rotate on a mid-call epoch advance: the chat receive loop re-derives `group.export_secret("lakko/call/v1", call_id, 32)` at the new epoch and calls the live SFU call's `rekey(newSeed)` (skips a no-op when unchanged), which fans out to every attached transform — **web** (`RTCRtpScriptTransform` worker holds a `CallKeyRing` per transform + a `rekey` postMessage + a self-prune timer; `SfuCallSession.rekey` + an `activeSeed` so late receivers arm at the current epoch; `Chat.tsx` `createEffect` on `membersVersion`), **iOS** (`SFrameAttach` rekey/prune → `LakkoFrameEncryptor::Rekey` seal-swap + `LakkoFrameDecryptor::Rekey`/`Prune` ring overlap → `WebRTCPeerConnection.rekeySFrame` → `SfuCallSession.rekey` → `ChatViewModel` poll+refreshMembers trigger), **Android** (the JNI twin). Encryptor = seal-key swap (no overlap on the send side — already-sealed frames are bytes); decryptor = ring overlap. Fail-closed throughout (a re-derivation failure keeps the current key, never plaintext — invariant #4). Headless-verified on every stack (core `CallKeyRing` 6 property tests + uniffi 3 + wasm 3 + the native C-ABI overlap round-trip + the C++ bridge `test_mid_call_rekey_overlap` + web `vitest` + `xcodebuild` + `:app:compileDebugKotlin`). Residual: a live two-peer mid-call-rekey proof over a **deployed** SFU (runtime gate, deferred like G-26). **iOS correction (2026-11-10, seen on an iPhone):** the iOS rekey walked `RTCPeerConnection.senders` / `.receivers`, which return new wrapper objects on every read without the associated SFrame transform, so it never rotated an iOS sender (a web joiner admitted to an iPhone-owned link call opened none of the iPhone's frames); since then (amendment of that date) the native bridge holds one refcounted `LakkoCallKeys` per call (a shared `CallKeyRing` and a generation) that every encryptor and decryptor is built on, so a rekey is one call and no platform enumerates transforms; iOS `SFrameCallKeys` / `CallKeysSeeds`, Android `LakkoCallKeys` / `CallKeysSlot`; an arm at a superseded seed never rolls the call back. Invariants #2 (server opaque) / #4 (no plaintext fallback) / C-8/C-9/C-10 (overlap retain / bound / most-recent-only) |
| [0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md) | **Batched channel-rotation fanout — staged prepare + atomic commit** (G-1, Phase 4). **Accepted (maintainer chose Variant 1); implemented end-to-end on the service + web/iOS/Android.** The single-shot `POST /v1/channels/:id/rotate` (whole subscriber envelope set in one request, one write-lock transaction — C-42) does not scale to the 50k–1M subscribers ADR-0010 targets. Splits rotation into a **chunked, resumable, idempotent STAGE** (`POST /rotate/stage { target_epoch, envelopes }`, ≤`MAX_STAGE_CHUNK_ENTRIES=5000`/chunk, 413 over-cap, upserted into a separate `channel_staged_envelopes` table keyed `(channel, target_epoch, subscriber)` that the read path NEVER serves — the epoch is not bumped) + a **single atomic COMMIT** (`POST /rotate/commit { target_epoch }`, one transaction: re-verify `target_epoch == current+1`, promote staged → live, bump `current_epoch`, clear staging). **C-42 preserved** — now "the commit is atomic" instead of "the one giant request is atomic"; a subscriber never observes a torn rotation. Load-bearing: staged ≠ served, monotone target epoch (rejects a stale/replayed commit), server opaque (staging is ciphertext-only, #2), idempotent+resumable (a re-sent chunk is an upsert). Service: `channel_staged_envelopes` (migration `0003`) + `stage_envelopes`/`commit_rotation` on both `ChannelStore` impls + `StorageError::StaleTargetEpoch`. Clients (web/iOS/Android): the rotation-cron fanout chunks the sealed envelopes into ≤5000-entry stage calls then one commit (`stageRotation`/`commitRotation` on each REST client). Deferred: staging TTL/GC for an abandoned prepare (OQ2); a streaming/push delivery layer. Invariants #2/#4 + C-42 |
| [0031](docs/adr/0031-multi-epoch-envelope-retention.md) | **Multi-epoch channel seed-envelope retention + subscriber catch-up** (G-1, Phase 4). **Accepted; implemented end-to-end on the service + web/iOS/Android.** Before this, both the server (`channel_envelopes` keyed `(channel, subscriber)`, latest-wins) and the clients (one `subscribedSeed` per channel) kept **N=1** — so a subscriber offline across a rotation (epoch N→N+1) got only the current seed and rendered every post made under epoch N **undecryptable** (each `ChannelPost` is AEAD-sealed under its own epoch's seed, the `epoch_id` in its clear header). Decision: retain the most-recent **N=4** seed envelopes per subscriber, keyed by epoch, on the server; the client keeps a per-channel `epoch → seed` map, fetches the retained set on catch-up, and decrypts each post with the seed matching its `epoch_id`. Service: migration `0004` re-keys `channel_envelopes` PK to `(channel, subscriber, epoch)` (backfill to `current_epoch`); every write path (`deliver_envelope` — now takes an optional epoch — / `rotate` / `commit_rotation`) files at a concrete epoch + prunes to the highest N; `fetch_envelope` (unchanged wire) returns the **highest** retained epoch, a new `fetch_envelopes` (`GET .../subscribers/:pk/envelopes`) returns the whole retained `[(epoch, envelope)]` set ascending; `LAKKO_CHANNELS_ENVELOPE_RETENTION_EPOCHS` (default 4, floor 1). Clients (web/iOS/Android): `subscribedSeed` (single) → per-channel `epoch → seed` map + `fetchEnvelopes` + decrypt-by-epoch (a post whose epoch seed is absent renders an "older key window — no longer available" placeholder + is **skipped**, never force-decrypted — invariant #4) + re-subscribe on offline-past-N (idempotent re-register → publisher reseals next fanout). **No FFI change** (`decodeChannelPost().epochId` + `verifyAndDecryptChannelPost` already existed). **C-42 preserved + strengthened** — `channel_envelopes` only ever holds epochs `<= current_epoch` (`deliver_envelope` **refuses** a future epoch outside the staged/commit path; `rotate`/`commit_rotation` file in the same transaction that bumps the epoch), so `fetch_envelope`'s highest-epoch return is always `<= current`. Invariants #2 (server opaque — epoch is bookkeeping alongside opaque ciphertext) / #4 (missing-seed post skipped, no non-E2EE fallback) + C-42 |
| [0032](docs/adr/0032-multi-joiner-call-links.md) | **Multi-joiner shareable call-invite links — owner-founds + deterministic committer election** (G-21/G-22, Phase 3). **Accepted (maintainer drove the design); implemented end-to-end on web + iOS + Android + guest-account join.** Closes the single-joiner V1 limit for `/c/<slug>` call links. The multi-party wire primitives were already built + tested (`call_welcome` welcome-ferry + `call_kp_forward`/`wrapCallKeyPackage` + `dispatchIncomingEnvelope` + `lakko-calls::link_invite`, C-32/C-33/C-35/C-36); a recon found the client wiring genuinely absent. **Decision (Variant A):** the **owner is the sole founder** (joiners never `Group::create` → no founding race → the G-21 two-groups bug closed by design; the embedded one-time owner-KP goes unused, so the one-time-KP-in-a-shared-link problem disappears — each joiner supplies its own fresh KP); an **admission toggle** (auto / approve, reusing ADR-0027); and a **deterministic committer election** — `committer = owner_account_id if present (SFU-connected) else the lowest-leaf present member`, recomputed on every roster change — so **failover + reclaim are automatic + split-brain-free** (owner drops → lowest-leaf successor is a full owner that admits new joiners immediately, no waiting; owner returns → reclaims). Live-call joins reuse the SFU `signal-relay` in-band; cold-start reuses the stranger-inbox welcome-ferry. G-22 dedup = committer-side `(call_id, joiner leaf)` add-once. **Accepted residual:** for a public shareable link "which committer admits me" is inherently weakly authenticated (strangers) — a fresh-joiner redirect during owner-downtime is a low-severity annoyance, NOT a real-call E2EE break (#2/#4 hold; the real participants' media is untouched); a strong-auth successor-delegation is deferred. Invariants #2 / #3 (no MLS state-machine change — orchestration over audited paths) / #4 / #5 preserved. **Implemented:** core `elect_committer` + `found_call`/`request_call_join`/`admit_joiner`/`join_call_from_welcome` + N-joiner/failover/reclaim E2E (Sprint 1) → FFI `electCommitter` (Sprint 2, uniffi + wasm) → web reference (owner-founds host/join over the SFU `signal-relay`, election-driven committer, per-admission SFrame rekey) + **guest-account join** (`signupGuest` — a username-less full OPAQUE account minted via the audited PoW-gated `signup` path, reachable pre-ACCESS from a public `/c/<slug>` link, onboarding = claim username later) + ~18 s dead-call timeout (empty owner-founds call is dead) → iOS + Android mirror (all builds green). **3 `lakko-security` hardenings** applied web-first then mirrored: post-join owner-membership check (fail-closed anti-redirect — the joined group must contain the capability's `ownerSignatureKey`), per-`from`-peer + per-KP admission dedup, double-tap latch. `lakko-security` **APPROVE-WITH-NOTES** (signupGuest = verbatim reuse of the audited PoW-gated signup; multi-joiner fail-closed + server-opaque + no MLS state-machine surface; residuals ADR-accepted). Residuals resolved: the **account-creation-spam throttle** landed ([ADR-0033](docs/adr/0033-call-admission-antispam-throttle.md) — a burst of new-guest joins on one `call_id` at `POST /call/jwt` → end-call + throttle, fail-open, owner-suspension deferred behind a hard-to-forge signal); **per-signature-key admission dedup** landed (`lakko_mls::key_package::signature_key` + FFI `keyPackageSignatureKey` — all 3 clients dedup on the stable leaf key so a peer re-minting fresh KeyPackages is added once, closing G-22 fully). The only remaining residual is a live deployed-SFU N-joiner/failover e2e (G-26-class). G-21/G-22 closed end-to-end. **Guest lifecycle amended 2026-10-02** (the ADR's "Guest lifecycle" bullet, UX `docs/ux/call-links.md` section 9): a guest goes straight into the call with no recovery-phrase screen; the phrase stays on the device under a key of its own, so no backup, no three-word enrolment and no push registration exist for a guest; a guest never lands in the app and is asked after the call `Create account` (the signup username claim, then the existing phrase-proved password reset, then the ordinary phrase screen) or `No, thanks` (the account is deleted at once on auth, in the directory and on the device); an account with no login handle and no passkey is swept by `services/auth` a day after it was made, and a directory row with no username by `services/discovery`. Web built and walked on a local stack 2026-10-02 (silent guest signup, decline, create incl. a taken username, the guard screen); the call itself was not part of that walk. iOS and Android built the same day, host-tested only; on iOS a guest session starts no session runtime (the link call under the guest root is unverified on a device). **Guest lifetime on the device (same day):** the marker carries when the account was made; after 23 hours the device deletes the account unasked at the next open, a guest whose session cannot be restored is wiped at launch, and `No, thanks` deletes the directory entry first and strictly when a username was already taken (`guestDeletePlan`); walked on web on the local stack. Unbuilt: a half-claimed guest that never reopens the app keeps its username (the servers are not told an account is a guest) |
