Residuals **1** (the co-membership cluster — still needs per-device-blinded ids), **3**, **4** and **8** are **unchanged**; **residual 5** keeps its crash + store-read-failure paths (only its F-E path closed). `derive_push_gid` and its golden are **untouched** — the epoch never enters the derivation (`derive_push_gid_ignores_the_mint_epoch`), so C-60's algebraic independence AND the routing id's **stability across ordinary commits** (what lets a subscriber survive commits without re-subscribing) both hold exactly as before; the ADR-0041 / C-59 background-decrypt reverse map is untouched. **Verified firsthand:** `cargo test -p lakko-contacts` 214 passed (41 `#[test]` in `push_routing.rs`), `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, clippy `-D warnings` on both FFI crates, bindings + wasm + `LakkoCore.xcframework` + Android `.so` rebuilt, web `tsc --noEmit` + vitest 548 (130 across the two push suites) + `vite build`, `just spec-check` OK; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL with `GroupPushRoutingTest` 98 + `PushRoutingSecretStoreTest` 10 green, `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED with `PushGroupRoutingTests` 67 green. **After the serializing lock + the two review follow-ups** (verified firsthand by the maintainer against the current tree): web `tsc --noEmit` clean + vitest **562**; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL with `GroupPushRoutingTest` **107** + `PushRoutingSecretStoreTest` **10**, zero failures; `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED with `PushGroupRoutingTests` **73**, zero failures; `audit-index-check` clean (8 I + 62 C + 15 T). `lakko-security` **run TWICE. First, on the landed arc: REQUEST CHANGES**, one blocking HIGH (the unserialized receive path, reproduced by execution — residual 10 above), plus two LOW (a stale `invariant-index.md` C-60 envelope description + C-61 "no test pins it" claim, both since corrected; and the flag-day tie class where two different pre-v2 secrets both read `mint_epoch = 0`, so a pair among them falls to the byte tiebreak rather than recency — bounded to the transition window, self-healing at the next rotation, which always stamps above 0) and one INFO (the ADR's "surface the mint epoch in diagnostics?" open question was already answered in code — all three log both epochs; defensible since an MLS epoch is public in the framing pre-decrypt). **Second, on the serializing-lock fix: APPROVE WITH NOTES**, no CRITICAL/HIGH, its two actionable findings (the per-realm web lock; the unbounded hold across network I/O) both since closed — **which is what the Accepted status rests on**. Invariants #2 (server opaque, push-gw byte-identical) / #3 (untouched — no `lakko-mls` change) / #4 (reject-future + reject-stale both **drop**, and both cross-version refusals fail to **defer**, never downgrade) / #5 (**unchanged posture** — restores the ADR-0043 forward-unlinkability in the cases F-A was silently revoking, does not touch the cluster) **ADR-0078 phase 3 (2026-10-16):** the routing secret also keys the group's wake key (`lakko/push/wake-sign/v1`) and unlock key (`lakko/push/wake-unlock/v1`); a rotation therefore also changes the key wakes must be signed with, and an evicted member can sign wakes only until it. |
| [0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) | **Proposal-based membership commits with a designated committer** (G-38 residual 1). **Status: PROPOSED — BUILT END TO END, S7 INCLUDED** (core + both FFI surfaces + all three clients; S7 landed `576ea14`, so there is **no direct-commit path left in a role-table group** and G-38 residual 1 is closed for external joins too). What is still open is the **external crypto review** — which since [ADR-0046](docs/adr/0046-external-crypto-review.md) is tracked **once**, in **G-39**, as a **release gate** (no build reaches users while it is open) rather than as a per-ADR blocker: carried inside each MLS-touching document it made every one of them permanently un-Acceptable however finished it was, while specifying nothing an auditor could act on. The per-change half of invariant #3 — an ADR plus property tests before the change lands — is unchanged, still enforced per change, and **has been satisfied on every sprint** (this ADR, and 268 tests in `lakko-mls`; an earlier revision of this clause read "neither has happened on any sprint", which was a fragment left behind from when it named the crypto review and the `lakko-security` pass together — the latter has since run). What remains beyond G-39 are the three client-side gaps listed at the end of this row. Closes the last route to a divergent MLS epoch, the one **no client-side lock can address by construction** because the second committer is a **different device**: A and B both build a membership commit against epoch N, the DS journal orders by arrival, the loser's commit is dropped by every peer as `WrongEpoch` while its own author has already eager-merged it — both sides report N+1 holding different state, silently and permanently, with the receive loop's catch-and-continue swallowing it. **Decision: Option B.** Members do not commit; a member emits an MLS **Proposal** and exactly one elected owner-or-admin sweeps the pending set into one Commit. A proposal is **not epoch-advancing**, so concurrent intents compose instead of forking the loser — *the retry moves from the expensive artifact to the cheap one*. The recon originally recommended Option C (client-side loser rollback + bounded retry) and the maintainer **overturned** it on scale: the hazard's frequency grows with (admins × churn) and Option C's convergence **degrades in the same variable**, plus its retry needs a per-intent "is my intent already satisfied?" predicate (two admins approving the *same* join have nothing to redo). **Option A (a DS-side epoch gate) is NOT built** and the case against it survives verbatim: the DS cannot verify a commit, so it must accept well-formed garbage and bump its counter on it, handing **anyone who has ever held a `/g/` invite link a permanent group-freeze primitive** (the DS has no auth and the pseudonym IS the `group_id`); it would silently skip DeviceGroup commits (wrapped in the ADR-0015 envelope, unparseable as MLS); it would **break** ADR-0040's restart sweep; and it changes the DS's transport-only posture. **Decision 1 — who commits:** `lakko_mls::group_roles::committer_rank(table, pubkey)` — rank 0 the owner, rank k+1 the k-th admin in the role table's own canonical order, keyed on **signature public key never leaf index**. A pure total function of the authenticated `0xFF02` GroupContext extension, so there is no election message, no handover protocol and no committer state — it cannot split-brain on *who* rank k is. It **narrows** who commits without widening who **may** (that stays exactly ADR-0022's set). Deliberately not `lakko_calls::link_invite::elect_committer`, which keys on leaf index and needs an SFU presence oracle a chat group has none of. **Decision 2 — liveness: a request has NO deadline.** The lowest-ranked *active* candidate sweeps; if none is running, the request simply waits. The retry cap, the terminal *stalled* state and the `owner_grace` constant were all deleted, because from inside a client *the budget ran out* and *the admins are asleep* are the same observation. The variant question (a candidate **heartbeat** vs a **staggered** takeover) is **RESOLVED — the maintainer chose the heartbeat, variant 1** (`3f09ca1`), on the ground that online/offline visibility is ordinary messenger functionality so the privacy cost does not outweigh making the model literal; variant 2 is kept as the documented fallback, not deleted. The heartbeat **is built in the core, on both FFI surfaces and on all three clients** and is described in the `lakko-contacts` module-map row — a field-less 27-byte beacon envelope + the pure `acting_committer_verdict` fold + the `should_emit_beacon` throttle + four constants, plus `Group::committerRank` for ranking a beacon's sender. ~~It is INERT~~ — **the election is LIVE**: every client emits a throttled beacon from a foregrounded chat screen on its own ticker (web `setInterval`, iOS `Timer` bound to screen visibility + `applicationState`, Android a `repeatOnLifecycle(RESUMED)` loop whose scope IS the foreground gate), stores observations keyed on the sender's **signature key** (never a leaf, never a rank), and folds them at **verdict** time with ranks re-derived through `Group::committerRank` — so an admin-only group no longer waits for an absent owner. Pinned as **C-64**. **The asymmetry this sentence used to record is closed:** the freshness window is `2 x interval + worst-case poll`, and the web client — which had **no periodic poll at all** until the chat route gained its automatic receive loop — now long-polls the open chat's journal (25 s `wait`, paused only while the tab is hidden and no call is up, the same foreground condition its beacon already required), so all three clients meet the constraint. The correctness caveat is unchanged by the choice and must not be read as softened by it: a heartbeat removes the *wait*, **not the race** (each candidate computes over its own poll-timed view, and a beacon is stale by up to one interval even when its sender is perfectly healthy), so the stand-down journal-head re-read and the committer rollback are required in **both** variants. **Decision 3 — the invariant-#3 piece:** before this, a received proposal was `ProcessedMessageContent::ProposalMessage(_) => Incoming::Proposal` — **discarded unexamined**, safe *only* because Lakko stored none. Now `crates/lakko-mls/src/group_proposals.rs` `authorise` is **the** rule, written once and consumed at three layers: the **receipt gate** (store only if the author's role authorises it, else drop), the **sweep filter** (`CommitBuilder::build`'s closure — never `commit_to_pending_proposals`, which hardcodes `\|_\| true`), and the **peer-verified commit gate** (judge **every** proposal a commit carries against its own author's role, because the committer is not trusted). By-reference is judged against the **proposer**, by-value against the **committer** — so every direct-commit path behaves byte-for-byte as it did under ADR-0022. **Decision 4:** external joins converge onto the same store + sweep — **S7, BUILT in `576ea14`**. This row long read "still NOT built — `Group::approve_external_join` calls `add_member` and eager-merges, so it is the one remaining direct-commit path in a role-table group", and the `a90fa90` `refuse_if_proposals_pending` hardening had made that user-visible by refusing an approve while any membership request was pending. Both are now history: approving publishes an `Add` **proposal the admin authors itself**, the guard no longer reaches it, and no direct-commit path is left in a role-table group. Decision 4 was wrong on one point, and the module map records it: the `pending_external_joins` quarantine is **not** retired, because it is what keeps a non-member out of the member proposal store whose quota a `/g/` link-holder could otherwise fill (C-66). The external-join arm stays **explicitly separate** in the filter, defaulting to **exclude**, or a stranger with a `/g/` link would be auto-admitted past ADR-0027's gate. **Decision 5:** naming the states only; the UX spec is [`docs/ux/proposal-based-membership.md`](docs/ux/proposal-based-membership.md). **No migration and NO dual path** — no deployment, no users, so it is a flag day; a "legacy direct-commit fallback" would be a second ungated route into membership, i.e. the hazard rebuilt under a prudent name. A group with **no role table** keeps direct commits, and `Group::create` has seeded one since ADR-0022. **BUILT (four core sprints, three commits): S1+S2 the gate + the committer (`9a60ab3`), S3 both FFI surfaces + the S0 UX spec (`c5c0788`), ownership transfer + `owner_survives` (`e780952`), the staged-sweep rollback + the mute fix (`fe3de6c`).** Each of the last three found something the ADR did not record, and all are now written into it: **(1)** the rollback point is the **absence of the eager merge**, not a "retained epoch-N snapshot" — `stage_pending_proposals` leaves the group at N holding a staged commit and `merge_staged_sweep` / `abandon_staged_sweep` adjudicate it; nothing is captured or persisted separately, and the discard is per-group `clear_pending_commit` rather than a whole-provider `restore_storage` that would roll **sibling groups** back past commits they already published. **This is ADR-0040's own deferred "Option B" (defer the eager merge in the core), scoped to the sweep path only** — the four direct-commit paths still merge eagerly, so ADR-0040's outbox wraps both shapes unchanged and the two answer different questions (*did my commit reach the wire* vs *did it win the wire order*). **(2)** a staged sweep **freezes that group's own membership operations** (OpenMLS `is_operational()` refuses `propose_*` / `add_members` / `remove_members` / `self_update` on `PendingCommit`) and the staged commit is **durable** (`write_group_state`), so a client that stages and never adjudicates wedges membership **across restarts** — a hard new client obligation; incoming messages are unaffected. **(3)** a pending proposal **muted the whole group's chat**: OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while the proposal store is non-empty, so from one admin's proposal until the sweep — which under decision 2 may be hours — *every* member failed to send. Fixed by `create_application_message` **parking** the pending set around the encrypt call (clone, clear, encrypt, restore; unchanged fast path when nothing is pending), with a failed unpark beating a successful send. **(4)** the obvious alternative fix was refuted in source: **by-reference resolution runs at commit receipt on EVERY member** (`ProposalQueue::from_committed_proposals`), not only at sweep time on the committer, so the store must be populated on every member for exactly the interval that fix would have kept it empty — which is what makes "store at receipt" **mandatory rather than tidy**. **(5)** the security content of the ownership sprint was **`owner_survives`, not the transfer wrapper**: a role table names 32-byte keys, so `set_group_roles` could always install an owner **no leaf holds** — permanently un-administrable, since a role change is owner-only and the owner's leaf is unremovable — and this refuses that commit on **every peer** independently of whether anyone uses the transfer API; it also catches the shape ADR-0045 itself created, a transfer riding alongside the new owner's removal in one composed commit. **Indexed as C-63** (whose sweep-side enforcement layer only acquired a production caller with the client sprints, while its authoring layer still has none). Transfer itself is deliberately **not a new commit type** — it is the owner-only role-table rewrite with the owner slot moved, judged against the **pre-merge** table — and it does **not** relieve ADR-0022's lost-owner case, because minting one needs the owner's key. **Two more sprints landed since:** **S3b the FFI back-fill** (`6c77cd6` — the staged pair + ownership transfer + `OutgoingOwner` on both surfaces, so a client can now stage-and-adjudicate rather than only eager-merge; this row previously said "neither surface exposes them", which is CLOSED), and the **decision-2 beacon sprint** (`94825ec` core + `94da492` both FFI surfaces + the slim extension surface, `5d6af94` the three background filters, `f53d11a` the three foreground filters). The beacon sprint added a filter obligation nobody anticipated: a beacon is an ordinary MLS application message, and `parse_body_envelope`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback degrades it to a **chat bubble reading `lakko/committer-beacon/v1`** plus two control bytes — so all three clients gained an arm ahead of the chat parser on **both** the foreground timeline path and the background notification path, and a recognised beacon surfaces as its **own event variant rather than being dropped**, because the sender leaf and the DS row age — the two things the field-less payload deliberately omits — exist only at that point in the chain and are exactly what the fold consumes — which is no longer a future tense: all three clients now drive it. **THE THREE CLIENT SPRINTS LANDED (2026-08-25):** S4a `1de5fe7` (the web reference — admins propose, the owner sweeps, the five obligations each placed where a test can run it), S4b `02f0037` (the beacon emitter + observation store + fold, which gives `composeCommitterBeacon` its first call site anywhere and ends the admin-only group's wait), `000d9f8` (an unplanned **send-path** serialization), and S5+S6 `1f230ab` (the iOS + Android mirrors). **Six things the clients found that the ADR did not anticipate**, all now in its `What the client sprints found` section: **(a)** the ADR's own verdict answer was wrong in the **dangerous** direction — epoch-alone plus "not one of our artifacts" reads another member's ordinary application message as a **loss** and abandons a sweep that **won**, which is the same brick in reverse; the clients instead apply the rows below their own commit and ask whether the epoch moved, which needs no discrimination and makes the proposed `contentTypeOfMessage` FFI addition unnecessary; **(b)** the stand-down therefore stands down on **any** unapplied row, since standing down costs one retry while staging on an unapplied commit costs a commit every peer drops; **(c)** a **send** can be sealed at one epoch and published across a commit that changes it — `create_message` gates on `is_active` not `is_operational`, so a staged sweep does not block it and the loss is a **certainty** for the whole verdict read, and with `max_past_epochs` unset (openmls default 0) such a message is **permanently undecryptable**, not late; closed by taking the per-group MLS lock across seal-and-publish on all three, which closes the **local** half only; **(d)** **C-61 moves with the removal** — under ADR-0045 a removal usually becomes a proposal, so rotating the push-routing secret at `removeMemberFromGroup` would seal it at an epoch the evicted member can still open (and tie the ADR-0044 stamp); it moves to the **sweep** via the membership-shrink diff, and the general rule is that any client discipline seamed onto a direct-commit call site must be re-seamed onto the sweep; **(e)** **concurrent role changes wedge membership** — RFC 9420 admits one `GroupContextExtensions` proposal per commit and OpenMLS enforces it as a HARD build failure (`TooManyGCEProposals`), so a pending set holding two cannot be swept at all and every committer holding both has its membership operations dead; the silent single-drop the sweep was thought to do is only the asymmetric case, where one committer happens to hold just one. **Closed in the core (2026-08-28), C-65**: `refuse_if_role_change_pending` at propose time, plus a one-role-change bound in `sweep_keeps` for the ADR-0040 crash window the propose-time guard structurally cannot see (a device that loses its own record before persisting proposes a second legitimately, and every PEER then holds two). Ownership transfer is a role-table rewrite, so it collides both ways and inherits the guard. What remains is readable copy on Android — web and iOS pre-check correctly narrowly (on a pending role change, not on any pending proposal); **(f)** the `refuse_if_proposals_pending` guard reached `approve_external_join`, which was the sharpest practical argument for S7 — and S7 answered it by removing the direct commit the guard was protecting, rather than by loosening the guard. **Ownership transfer shipped end to end (2026-08-28, `0274fa7`)** — UX spec §6.1 plus all three clients, so the owner dead-end is now closed in the product too. The design pass settled a question rather than answering it: the **direct arm is structurally unreachable**, since `isOwner` and `usesProposalPath` are the same read (the role table's presence), so clients drive `proposeOwnershipTransfer` only — iOS proved it by execution, the direct arm advancing the epoch AND stripping the transferor locally, so the next role change is refused as *proposer is not the owner*. **Self-eviction handling shipped too** (§6.2 + `Group::is_active()` + `isActive` on both FFI surfaces): before it, no client handled its own removal landing, so the chat stayed live with the composer enabled over a group that can never receive another message — and five background disciplines kept running against it, one of them (the ADR-0043 shrink rotation) doing real harm rather than spinning, because a `Remove` rewrites no `GroupContext` so an evicted admin's role table still named them admin and the client minted a secret, subscribed to a push id nobody will ever notify, and retried a throwing broadcast on every chat-open. Tearing down the device's own push row **closes the half of G-36 residual 2 ADR-0043 recorded as unclosable**. **Still not built** (S7 has come off this list): the UX spec's **superseded** re-request (a request that LANDED and was then dropped by a sweep — distinct from the queue-full case, which `21f6c8c` / `1839da7` built for both requests and approvals) and its committer-side failure surface (the last is unimplementable as specified — it is gated on an *activation delay* belonging to the staggered variant the maintainer did not choose); and the status flip. **The `lakko-security` pass RAN (2026-08-29) and returned REQUEST CHANGES** — three HIGH findings plus a confirmed impersonation, all seven closed in `c50bdd7` / `0274fa7` / `8763f3e` / `5b2b654` and recorded in the ADR's new `What the security review found` section, indexed as **C-66** (a group must never lose the ability to change its own membership), **C-67** (durable bookkeeping must never claim more progress than actually landed) and **C-68** (an author is a signature key resolved at receipt, never a leaf index). **The headline is that none of the seven needed an attacker and four needed nobody at all:** the ADR-0023 author gate compared a raw leaf index and OpenMLS reuses a vacated leaf, so a remove-then-add — one commit under this ADR — let a newcomer rewrite and delete a departed member's history, permanently on both natives; `Update` proposals bypassed `owner_survives` and could flood the pending set; a full pending set took away the owner's own `Remove` and `RoleChange` so a flooder locked the owner out of responding, with the only exit committing their payload (first mis-triaged as *weaker than what an admin already holds*, which is wrong because **capabilities compose** — removing members is loud and repairable, filling the queue removes the means of repair); an unbuildable sweep is a permanent, invisible, group-wide membership freeze reachable from ONE proposal, and one of its fifteen enumerated causes is **KeyPackage expiry**, i.e. elapsed time alone against a request decision 2 gives no deadline; a Welcome fan-out failing after a won merge was unrecoverable, because the merge clears every signal the recovery paths branch on (**the merge is not the end of the sweep's obligations** — an artifact still owed after the epoch-advancing step needs its own durable name); and a receiver could strand itself off the group forever, because the durable snapshot and the journal cursor live in stores no transaction can span while the cursor advanced first (**the cursor may lag the durable state, never lead it**). Because enumerating a library's build rules is a race Lakko does not own, the enumeration is backed by `narrow_sweep_by_exclusion`, which needs only to know *which* proposal is uncommittable and not *why* — it rescued a live unenumerated case immediately, an admin proposing its own departure wedging its own sweep. **The external crypto review has still NOT happened** and remains the largest open item; the surface it would cover grew again with these seven fixes. Invariants #1 / #2 (untouched — no server change, no wire-format change, no new artifact on the journal) / #3 (**a direct change, four ways**: the per-proposal gate, `owner_survives`, the new stage/merge/abandon commit lifecycle, and the send-path park; 268 tests across the `lakko-mls` lib and its seven integration binaries — the figure was 180 across seven when `719d0df` recorded it, before the ownership-transfer, membership-state and typed-refusal binaries) / #4 (fail-closed at every new decision point — an unauthorised or unclassifiable proposal is dropped, an unauthorised commit is refused not merged, a second stage is refused, `abandon` re-reads and refuses to report a rollback it did not perform, a failed unpark beats a successful send) / #5 (untouched) |
| [0046](docs/adr/0046-external-crypto-review.md) | **External cryptographic review — a programme gate, not a per-change blocker.** **Accepted (maintainer's call).** Security invariant #3 requires *"an ADR + property tests + (after Phase 2) an external crypto audit"* for a `lakko-mls` change, and the third clause had been carried **inside each ADR that touches it** — four of them — as a reason that ADR could not be Accepted. That reading had a consequence nobody chose: **no MLS-touching ADR can ever be Accepted, however finished**, and the debt was simultaneously blocking everything and specified nowhere. ADR-0045 is the case in point: built end to end across core, both FFI surfaces and three clients, through a `lakko-security` pass whose seven findings are closed, 268 core tests (242 on the day this ADR was written), seven indexed invariants — and permanently `Proposed` for a reason having nothing to do with it. **The invariant does not say what that reading assumed:** it scopes the audit with *"after Phase 2"*, a **programme phase**, not a per-change precondition; an ADR records a decision, a cryptographic review examines an accumulated interacting surface from outside, and binding the first to the second was a misreading rather than a policy. **Decision:** the review is tracked **once**, as **G-39**, which owns the scope and the trigger; invariant #3's text is **unchanged** and its per-change half stays enforced per change; an MLS-touching ADR may reach `Accepted` on its own merits and carries a pointer to G-39 instead of its own copy of the debt; and the gate is a **release** gate — no store submission, no GA tag, while G-39 is open, which is where *"after Phase 2"* bites and is what keeps this from being a way to avoid the audit. **Explicitly does NOT** relax #3, shrink the scope, lower the per-change bar, or make an unaudited change safe — it makes one **shippable to nobody**. **Risk stated:** one tracked item is easier to defer than four blockers each stopping its own document; the mitigation is that the gate is unambiguous and the debt is now specified, so deferring is a visible choice rather than a default. Alternatives rejected: keep the per-ADR blocker (it produced the current state — a blocker that blocks unconditionally and forever stops being read); drop the requirement (the internal `lakko-security` pass found three HIGH findings and a confirmed impersonation in an already-shipped surface, four needing nobody at all — an external reviewer does not share those blind spots); audit per-ADR for real (a review examines an **interacting** surface, and the interactions between ADR-0022's role table, ADR-0045's proposal gate and C-63's commit rule are exactly what a per-change review would each see only a slice of) |
| [0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md) | **Media messages, emoji picker and a server-side GIF proxy.** **Accepted** (GIPHY behind Lakko's server is the maintainer's choice). UX spec: [`docs/ux/media-messages.md`](docs/ux/media-messages.md). Voice messages, video notes and GIFs are **ordinary encrypted attachments** with optional, bounded metadata on `AttachmentRef` (`kind` / `duration_ms` / `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb`), no wire-version bump; a field that fails its bound or type is dropped and the envelope survives; **inline playback is gated on a MIME allowlist, never on `kind`**. One recording format per kind on every platform (voice AAC-LC in MP4, video note H.264 + AAC in MP4, square, <= 60 s); a browser that cannot record MP4 disables recording. Emoji data is bundled; reactions accept any single emoji grapheme within `MAX_EMOJI_BYTES`. GIF search and media go through a stateless `services/gif-proxy` (session-token auth, id-based media route — no client-supplied upstream URL, size and content-type caps, per-account rate limit, no persistence or query logging, `503` when unconfigured); the picked GIF is re-uploaded as an encrypted attachment with `kind: gif`, so **recipients never contact the proxy or GIPHY**. Cost stated in the UI: the Lakko server learns a signed-in account's GIF search terms. Invariants #2 / #5 unchanged for message content. **History bundles carry the metadata too (§5):** `lakko_mls::history_placeholder` is version 5 since 2026-11-19 (version 4 added the ADR-0092 content block, version 5 the ADR-0047 section 6 `inline_thumb` of the first photo or video, at most 4096 bytes; version 2: per-attachment `kind` / `duration_ms` / raw `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb`; version 3 adds the ADR-0087 channel invitation card and the ADR-0052 reply and forward snapshots; older versions refused), structure-only in `lakko-mls`, with the semantic bounds applied in both FFI crates through `lakko_attachments::RawMediaMetadata::bounded` — a codec change, not a state-machine change, with a round-trip property test. **Core landed:** the `lakko-attachments` fields, bounds, lenient decode, `inline_media_type`, the extended fuzz oracle, and the UniFFI / wasm / extension FFI source (new UniFFI record fields default to `None`, so existing Swift/Kotlin initializer calls stay source-compatible after regeneration) | **Micro thumbnail in the message (section 6, maintainer 2026-11-19):** the first photo or video of a message carries `inline_thumb`, a JPEG of at most 4096 bytes (about 96 px, chosen) inside the end-to-end encrypted envelope, kept only on that ref, so notifications on every platform and the chat list row show the picture with no blob fetch (the background paths hold no token for a read pass); on UniFFI, wasm and the NSE surface as `inlineThumb`; the vault record is version 3 with it
| [0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) | **Profile names, carried end-to-end encrypted and bound to the sender's key.** **Accepted** (the maintainer chose own profile name + username). UX spec: [`docs/ux/profile-names.md`](docs/ux/profile-names.md). **Rust core built** (clients not yet wired; bindings not regenerated): a bounded, fail-closed `lakko-contacts::profile` envelope `domain "lakko/profile/v1\0" ‖ version(1) ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ name_len(1) ‖ name ‖ username_len(1) ‖ username` (display name trimmed, 1-64 scalars, **<= 255 bytes** — the one-byte `name_len` overrides the ADR's 256 — no line break, no `Cc`, no `Cf` from an explicit table, so ZWJ/ZWNJ emoji and Persian spellings are refused too; username canonical or empty), rejected as a whole on any violation, `compose` refusing exactly what `decode` refuses; `profile_is_newer` (strictly greater, per signature key); `IncomingEnvelope::Profile` so it never renders as a bubble; fuzz `profile_decode`. **Carriers:** the contact request's sealed plaintext is now `profile_len(u16 BE) ‖ profile ‖ intro` (no dual format) — a well-framed but invalid profile opens the request with `profile = None` rather than dropping it, a broken framing fails the open; and an MLS application message per group (the join / change / growth republish is client work). **Trigger 4, added 2026-09-19:** the three triggers are push-only and a newcomer depends on other devices noticing the growth, which a device that was away, or a closed web tab, never does (live: three of five members read `Member` on the device that joined last) — so a newcomer also asks, with a field-less `lakko/profile-request/v1` envelope (`lakko-contacts::profile_request`, 26 bytes, the committer-beacon shape) that every reader answers through the throttled growth path; sent on join and, at most daily per group, on opening a group with an unnamed member (`should_request_profiles`, one rule on both FFI surfaces; the extension crate decodes it only to suppress it). **Binding** is the receiver's: stored under the sender's signature key resolved at receipt (C-68), username shown only after discovery resolves it to that key. **Username character set (§4):** `[a-z0-9_]`, 3-32 after normalisation, defined once in `lakko-contacts::username` and enforced at the OPRF blinder (`lakko-discovery-client::validate_username`), so register and lookup refuse by construction; `@channel` handles keep their own policy. FFI source: `validateUsername` (typed `Username*` variants appended to `FfiError` / a `usernameError` JS property), `composeProfileEnvelope` / `decodeProfileEnvelope` / `profileIsNewer` / `normalizeDisplayName`, the `Profile` dispatch kind, `composeContactRequest` + `openEnvelope` reshaped; the NSE extension gets decode (no fields) + min/max length only. Invariants #1/#2 (the server sees ciphertext only) / #5 (no server-side profile store) hold; #3 untouched (no `lakko-mls` change) |
| [0049](docs/adr/0049-identity-recovery-word-vault.md) | **Identity recovery: three of the twenty-four words, a confirmation prompt on another device, and a passkey** (account-recovery overhaul, Part 3). **Accepted; the word vault, the another-device confirmation prompt and the passkey vault are built on web + `services/auth` (2026-09-15); new-device chat membership is NOT built and is blocked on per-device conversation leaves (ADR-0007 Phase 2b), see the ADR's corrected section 5** (2026-09-15; the maintainer chose the direction after the live test on `test.lakko.me`: three numbered words on every recovery path, another device without a camera, passkey as a third route, twelve or twenty-four words rejected as UX). UX spec: [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md). **The word vault:** three words cannot derive the seed (33 bits of a 264-bit string), so the client seals the 32-byte BIP39 **entropy** under a key Argon2id-stretched from the words at three fixed positions chosen at random at registration, and `services/auth` stores `verifier || sealed_entropy` (wrapped again under a server-static `LAKKO_VAULT_ENC_KEY`, the TOTP pattern) behind a guess budget: the TOTP 5-in-15-minutes lockout plus a **10-strike destruction cap**. `POST /vault/enroll` (bearer) / `POST /vault/recover/{start,finish}` (anonymous + PoW, enumeration-safe: an unknown account gets pepper-derived positions and a dummy compare). A correct open reconstructs the full phrase with `Mnemonic::from_entropy` and hands it to the **existing** `provideRecoveryPhrase` (fail-closed bundle check) and, for ACCESS, the existing recovery OPAQUE login, so nothing downstream is new. Modelled on Signal's Secure Value Recovery (Argon2-stretched PIN, enclave-held secret with a guess counter); three BIP39 words are about 2^20 times a four-digit PIN. **Another device:** the fresh device posts its pairing payload (the bytes the QR carries) to `POST /device/request`; signed-in devices show "Is this you? Code 47 19 03" and **Confirm** runs the unchanged ADR-0018 flow (grant + Path B seed seal + history bundle). The six-digit comparison code is BLAKE3 over the payload and is what stops a malicious server substituting its own device. QR stays as an option. **Passkey (phase 2):** WebAuthn PRF output wraps the entropy into a server-held opaque `passkey_vaults` blob so a synced passkey carries identity; no PRF support exists yet in the vendored `webauthn-rs-core` or the web client, so until then a passkey login is followed by the vault or the prompt. **Finding 12 is a requirement here:** after any another-device flow the old device adds the new device to every chat group (ADR-0045 proposals for role-table groups); after a vault or passkey restore with no other device alive, chat membership is not recoverable (MLS forward secrecy) and the UI says so instead of showing an empty list. **Invariant #2 is weakened for the recovery path, stated explicitly:** the operator, ignoring their own counter and holding the environment key, faces a 2^33 memory-hard search per account (arithmetic on an assumed per-evaluation cost, to be measured; the ADR proposes the exception text for the invariant). Argon2 parameters are a measured cost budget carried in the row, not a fixed set. Migrations `0017_word_vault.sql` (`word_vaults`, `vault_opens`) and `0018_device_requests_passkey_vaults.sql` (`device_requests`, `passkey_vaults`); the comparison code is SHA-256 over the payload, computed on both devices, never by the server; the web passkey ceremonies request the PRF extension and strip its output before the blob reaches the server; a fresh-device passkey login is now possible on web (it was refused outright before) and lands either from the passkey vault or in the restore prompt. Enclave / SVR3-style hardening recorded as the follow-up. Flag for `lakko-security` before merge. **Amended 2026-10-02 by [ADR-0072](docs/adr/0072-recovery-model-simplification.md) — most of this row is history:** the word vault is gone entirely (no `word_vaults` / `vault_opens`, no `/vault/enroll` or `/vault/recover/*`, migration `0029`); three words are checked locally by a device that already holds the phrase and bring no keys to a new device; the invariant-#2 weakening stated above is **withdrawn**, since nothing derived from the phrase's words is stored server-side; the phrase is 12 words. What stays: the another-device confirmation (section 2) and the passkey vault, now opt-in per passkey over the 16-byte entropy, with `PUT /vault/passkey`, `POST /vault/passkey/get`, `DELETE /vault/passkey`, `GET /vault/passkeys` |
| [0050](docs/adr/0050-per-device-conversation-leaves.md) | **Per-device conversation leaves, a second device is a full client** (ADR-0007 Phase 2b; closes G-40). **Accepted 2026-09-15; S1 core, S2 FFI, S3 discovery and S4 web built the same day and live-tested on `test.lakko.me` (a device added through the ADR-0049 confirmation was admitted to the account's existing chat by the confirming device and received later messages); S5 natives built 2026-09-16 on iOS + Android (xcodebuild test and gradle unit tests green, not yet run on a device; QR-pairing path only, no native ADR-0049 prompt); S6 security+docs open.** Two facts the build added to the ADR: a founder adds every device in ONE commit (`Group::add_members`; one commit per leaf stranded every leaf but the last at a Welcome epoch nobody could reach), and the published KeyPackage is a last-resort package (one fetch admits a device to N chats; the forward-secrecy cost and the one-time-batch follow-up are recorded in 2.6). On web the ADR-0045 sweep also runs at the tail of every background poll, so a proposal lands without its chat being opened. Built later the same day: the scheduled re-admission arm (any member re-checks the device lists of the accounts in a chat against its roster at the tail of its background polls; a leaf of the own account whose directory row is gone is retired, which is how `forgetDevice` — it deletes its row first — retires itself), the pairing state bundle (contacts, renames, pins, known group ids), and the history bundle on the confirmation route, which had been skipped outright. Unbuilt: incremental contact sync between devices, channel subscriptions in the bundle. Every conversation leaf becomes the per-device `DeviceIdentity`, bound to the account key by a signed binding carried as the leaf's `BasicCredential` identity bytes (an empty credential = a Phase 1 account-key leaf; `Identity::generate(name)` no longer carries the name), verified by every peer at every admission gate because OpenMLS carries it and checks nothing; the account key stays the published, pinned, KT-logged identity and the safety number. The core's single leaf-to-identity resolver (`pubkey_at_leaf`) becomes `account_key_at_leaf`, and roles, authorship, read state, external-join dedup, committer rank and the call dedup key on the resolved account key; the `0xFF02` role-table wire is unchanged because Phase 1 keys already are account keys. Two self-service arms in `authorise`: a member may add or remove a leaf of its own account, and may add a bound device of an account already in the group (automatic re-admission after a lost-everything restore); `MAX_LEAVES_PER_ACCOUNT = 8`; a self-service-only pending set may be swept by the proposing account's lowest-leaf device. Discovery stores one KeyPackage per (account, device) and serves the list; DS, auth, push-gw and the KT log do not change. Pairing and the ADR-0049 confirmation fan the new device into every known group through one hook in `runScanFlow`; the pairing bundle carries contacts and channel subscriptions; incremental sync uses the reserved DeviceGroup proto fields. UX spec `docs/ux/second-device.md` |
| [0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md) | **Group and channel names carried end-to-end encrypted to every member and subscriber.** **Accepted 2026-09-17** (the maintainer chose the envelope-plus-record design over a `GroupContext` extension); UX spec [`docs/ux/group-and-channel-names.md`](docs/ux/group-and-channel-names.md). Until then a group name lived on the creator's device only (web lost it on reload, iOS had no field) and a channel name on the publisher's, so subscribers saw the bare handle. **Group name:** a bounded, domain-separated envelope `lakko/group-name/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms ‖ name_len ‖ name` on an MLS application message (`lakko-contacts::group_name`, the profile template; empty name = clear; whole-envelope rejection; `IncomingEnvelope::GroupName`, never a bubble; fuzz `group_name_decode`), **accepted only from the owner or an admin resolved at receipt through the live role table** (plain member dropped, no-table group open, unreadable dropped), ordered by a strictly-greater stamp per group that a growth re-send re-emits verbatim (the ADR-0044 lesson), sent after the creating commit, on rename and on growth (throttled like the profile republish); background filters (web SW, Android background decrypt, iOS NSE via the decode-only extension surface) suppress it. **Channel name + description:** a bounded record `lakko/channel-meta/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms ‖ name ‖ description` carried as the **plaintext of an ordinary channel post** (`lakko-channels::meta`; authenticity = the post signature, confidentiality = the epoch seed; `classify_post_plaintext` -> `Text` / `Meta` / `Malformed` runs before the readers' text path, on both FFI surfaces so no client hand-rolls the sniff; fuzz `channel_meta_decode`), re-issued by the publisher at creation, on edit and after every rotation so a late subscriber always finds one it can read. Alternatives rejected: server-side name fields (#2), a `GroupContext` extension (a commit per rename under ADR-0045, and `lakko-mls`), the seed envelope. Core + both FFI surfaces built 2026-09-17; the three clients are the same day's fan-out, see PRODUCT-READINESS |
| [0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) | **Reply, forward and pin** — the three new-message / keep-message actions of the Telegram-class message menu. **Accepted 2026-09-26** (the maintainer chose: forward re-encrypts and carries an origin reference only for a local jump; several pins, shared outranks personal; the quote is a snapshot). UX spec [`docs/ux/message-actions.md`](docs/ux/message-actions.md). **Decision 1 — Reply is a reference carried BESIDE the text, never one of the mutually exclusive ops** (`edit_of` / `delete_of` / `react_to` are things done TO a message; a reply is a message that also points somewhere, so putting it in that set would make replying and typing exclusive): `reply_to` + a bounded snapshot `reply_author` / `reply_excerpt` taken when the reply is composed, so every receiver — a later joiner, a fresh device — draws the same quote, and a later edit or deletion of the original changes nothing; one level deep. **Built end-to-end 2026-09-26** (core + both FFI surfaces + web / iOS / Android: Reply row first in the card, composer strip, quote block, tap-to-jump, quote persisted; seen live in the web harness only, not yet walked on two devices). **Decision 2 — Forward** re-encrypts the attachments (a forwarded reference would let the storage tier correlate two conversations through one blob) and carries the original author as a display name only, plus a conversation-id + messageId origin reference inside the sealed payload for a local jump; the server learns nothing. **Decision 3 — Pin** is two features: a per-device personal pin and a shared pin distributed as a group envelope (`lakko-contacts`, its own domain + codec + fuzz target), owner/admin — or either participant in a 1×1 — may unpin, shared outranks personal, several pins with a bar showing one at a time. **Decision 2 built end to end 2026-09-26** (walked web -> iOS, iOS -> iOS, Android -> Android). **Decision 3 built end to end 2026-09-26** — `lakko-contacts::pin` (31-byte fixed envelope, one message per envelope, `pin_is_newer` per message, fuzz `pin_decode`), both FFI surfaces + the NSE decode arm, the three clients per UX spec 4.1 (scope sheet, personal set + shared map with stored unpins, the receive gate under the per-group lock, growth re-send, background suppression, the pinned bar); walked live: personal pin on all three, the shared pin in a one-to-one iOS <-> Android both directions, and in a group the owner's `Pin for everyone` + `Unpin` from web reaching a web member whose own sheet offered `Pin for me` alone; and an iOS owner's `Pin for everyone` reaching an Android member and a web member; NOT walked: the list control, the growth re-send. Channel pins unbuilt. No `lakko-mls` change (invariant #3 untouched); #2 / #5 hold — every new field rides inside the MLS-sealed payload |
| [0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md) | **A journal on the Delivery Service is opened by a key, not by its name** (closes G-42). **Accepted 2026-09-27; S1-S4 built the same day and deployed to the test stack: the `lakko-ds-proof` crate, the DS side (`services/ds/src/access.rs`, migration `0002`, seven DB-gated tests), the FFI signer on UniFFI / wasm / the NSE surface, and all three clients (web S3, iOS + Android S4) signing, claiming, registering each epoch before the commit that reaches it, healing on apply, knocking to join and delivering sweep Welcomes to mailboxes. Walked live: a message crossed web, iOS and Android in all six directions in a claimed group, and the same group by name alone was refused (unsigned read / write / forged proof 401, competing registration 409). S3/S4 amended the server before the clients: open question 1 resolved as a two-epoch WRITE tier with a read-only tail for the journal retention window (else a device offline across two commits was stranded for good), an idempotent claim, and a knock that takes several payloads and an empty list as an epoch probe; `lakko-mls` gained two read-only accessors, `Group::staged_export_secret` (the N+1 key of a staged sweep, registered before publishing) and `Group::process_knock` over `is_external_join_proposal_for` (a knock row is refused unless it is a stranger's external-join proposal for this group, closing a fork a member could cause by posting a real commit to the knocks), both property-tested and on both FFI surfaces. Not walked yet: the `/g/` knock join, the sweep's mailbox Welcome, the three background readers; `lakko-security` S5 open.** `services/ds` serves and accepts any journal to whoever names it; the name travels (a forward carries the origin `group_id` - ADR-0052's "opaque id they cannot open" was false of the journal; a mailbox is `BLAKE3(identity_pk)` and the key is public; the `/g/` joiner reads a journal it is not in), so a leaked name reads metadata, writes garbage and knocks. **Decision:** a claimed journal answers only a request signed with a key it was claimed under - a per-epoch Ed25519 key every member derives from `Group::export_secret("lakko/ds/access/v1", ...)` (rotates with the epoch, eviction revokes at the commit; the server holds public keys per epoch, no `lakko-mls` change), the identity key for a mailbox (reads gated, writes stay the open cold inbox); a failed proof is one uniform `401` (amended before S1: a silent drop would hide a wrong-clock member's lost publishes, and a 256-bit name cannot be probed); the non-member's one act moves to a PoW-gated **knock** whose response carries the epoch (the ADR-0028 whole-journal read goes away) and the link joiner's Welcome moves to its mailbox (C-69 amended); rendezvous mailboxes stay capability-by-name as the stated residual. One shared crate `lakko-ds-proof` (canonical bytes + verifier, the `lakko-auth-token` shape). Rejected: hiding the id from co-members only (a symptom), the auth session token on the DS (names the account: invariant #5), a symmetric MAC key (the server could mint requests), the ADR-0042 static-secret pattern (the exporter gives rotation for free), a DS epoch gate (still rejected per ADR-0045). Flag day; a `lakko-security` pass before merge. **Who registers an epoch key is now [ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)'s** (the security pass's HIGH finding): a journal is claimed under a root key and only the root and its registrars register epochs; readers, writers and knocks are unchanged |
| [0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) | **Anti-abuse is decided by the server, not paid for by the phone.** **Accepted 2026-09-27; S1 (wake through the DS, server + three clients) and S2 (`lakko-anon-token` + `lakko-anon-token-server` + `POST /auth/tokens`) built the same day; S3 (discovery, DS knock and push-gw take a token instead of proof of work), S4 (auth: server counters, one heavy proof at signup start only) and S5 (sign-in and recovery by name at auth with a peppered login handle, no discovery lookup before a session; handle bound at signup auth-first, after sign-in and on every session landing) built 2026-09-28; all deployed to the test stack and walked live in part (the DS wake, short links, token spends, a handle bind); open: another-device recovery on a device with no cached account id, the signup effort measurement, the security pass**. Per-request proof of work (34 server call sites across auth, discovery, push-gw and the DS) is removed: a phone without a JIT paid seconds per request while an attacker's hardware pays little. (1) A group wake becomes a consequence of the signed ADR-0053 publish: an `x-lakko-wake: <push_gid>;<kind>` header, forwarded by the DS to push-gw over the internal network only after the proof gate passed; `/push/notify-group` leaves the public vhost, the caller-less `/push/notify` is deleted, the DS caps wakes per group; the DS holds the group / `push_gid` pair in memory only. (2) Anonymous tokens (the privately verifiable Privacy Pass shape, a VOPRF on the existing `voprf` crate) issued by auth to a session under a **per-period** quota refilled in the background, never requiring a re-login; spent once per consuming service on lookup, OPRF, invite consume, channel-name register, the DS knock and push-gw register / subscribe; chat messages never spend one. (3) Bearer routes and pre-session guessing routes lose the proof for per-account counters and a per-IP backoff, with extra limits on OTP sends and device-request prompts. (4) Account creation keeps one deliberately heavy proof, the single rationing point the token quota rests on. Plan S1-S5 in the ADR **Amended by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 3 (2026-10-16):** the `x-lakko-wake` header is a 312-character sealed wake (push-gw's X25519 key, signed by the group's wake key, nonce + timestamp inside); the DS checks only its shape, forwards it unread and caps per journal; push-gw verifies, dedups the nonce, caps per `push_gid`. `/push/register` and `/push/unregister` are removed; auth's default `push-register` quota is 400 a day (every group subscription is its own sealed row). |
| [0055](docs/adr/0055-websocket-delivery.md) | **WebSocket where a push beats a poll, HTTPS everywhere else.** **Proposed 2026-09-28.** A foreground client holds one WebSocket to the DS: the DS pushes `{journal, head}` notices for the journals it reads (rows are still fetched over the signed HTTPS GET, so a lost notice costs latency, never a message) and relays MLS-sealed ephemeral signals (typing leaves the journal; read receipts stay). Subscriptions carry the ADR-0053 proof and are re-verified on epoch rotation; long poll stays as the transport fallback; background delivery stays on APNs / FCM / Web Push; auth, discovery, tokens, storage, push-gw and publishing stay HTTPS; gRPC rejected (gRPC-Web proxy in the browser, HTTP/2 inside the ADR-0026 tunnels). Capacity of the test host as configured, measured 2026-09-28: about 700 concurrent sockets, bound by nginx `worker_connections 768` x 2 workers at two connections per proxied socket, then the DS soft open-file limit 1024; with those raised, about 17 000 (host `fs.file-max` 51200 at three descriptors per socket; memory is about 137 KiB per idle socket, measured on Linux with axum 0.8.9, about 20 000 in the 2.8 GiB available). Plan S1-S4 in the ADR |
| [0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md) | **A fresh device names its account by login handle, not by account id.** **Accepted 2026-09-28.** Since ADR-0054 S3 a device with no cached account id could not start another-device recovery (the id came from a username lookup, which now needs a session). `PairingQr` v2 carries the S5 `login_handle` (field 5; `account_id` field 2 reserved) and `pairing::decode(bytes, expected_login_handle)` compares it with the confirming device's own handle; `POST /device/request` takes the handle (unknown handles land on a stand-in account nobody polls); `POST /device/authorize/redeem` takes the grant alone and returns the account id. A `lakko-mls` codec change under invariant #3: property tests + the `pairing_qr` fuzz target. Rejected: a pre-session id resolve (a UUIDv7 stand-in leaks existence through its timestamp). **S1 built 2026-09-28** (proto, core, fuzz, both FFI surfaces, bindings, auth + migration `0022`, host-tested against the local MariaDB); S2 (the three clients) open |
| [0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md) | **A journal has an owner; only those who change a group register its epochs.** **Accepted 2026-09-28** (option (a) of the security pass; closes its HIGH finding on ADR-0053). Every group journal gets a root key derived per group from the owner's account seed (DeviceGroup: the account; PublisherGroup: the channel owner; ordinary group: the ADR-0022 owner). Epoch keys are registered by registrars the root appoints in three ranks - root, admin, member (ADR-0050 self-service commits) - with seeds derived by the root and sent in-group; a higher rank replaces what a lower one registered and revokes it in the same transaction, so an evictee's fake next epoch and its registrar key go with the removal. A same-root claim is a root reset, which re-claims a re-founded DeviceGroup / PublisherGroup. A push routing id is bound to one journal (keyed hash, unique) by the root or an admin; a wake naming another journal's id is dropped. Residuals: an admin removed by another admin races until the owner acts; squatting a deterministic id before the owner's first claim. **S1 + S2 built 2026-09-28** (DS host-tested against the local MariaDB; web, iOS and Android host-tested; deployed to the test stack, not walked live): `lakko-ds-proof::statement` (derivations, six signed statements), `services/ds/src/ownership.rs` + migration `0006`, the `lakko-contacts::registrar_seed` envelope, both FFI surfaces, the NSE decode arm. Built differently from the text, recorded in the ADR: the registrar seed is **sealed to the member's account X25519 key** (in the clear, every member would read every admin's seed); a same-root claim is idempotent and replacing the chain is an explicit **reset**; the replace rule covers a **suffix** of lower-rank registrations, so an evictee that raced two epochs ahead is still replaced in one step; an ownership transfer hands the old owner's root seed to the new owner (envelope rank `root`, `composeRootHandoverEnvelope`), who signs `root` naming its own root and re-appoints everyone; a lazy claim that meets `409` registers nothing. **Second security pass 2026-09-28 (REQUEST CHANGES, four HIGH), fixed the same day and next:** registration carries a mode - *Append* only at `latest + 1`, a member at most one epoch past the newest admin / root registration, *Replace* only over strictly lower ranks with no root override (an owner or admin repairs a fake next epoch from above); a root-only `registrars-set` replaces the whole registrar set from the roster; claims fail closed at creation on all three clients; the root handover survives retries (new owner keeps its registrar seed until confirmed, the old owner probes with a `root` statement naming itself, the receiver keeps the opened seed wrapped); a push binding precedes the secret's broadcast. Unbuilt: a registrar for co-publishers (a PublisherGroup carries no application message). **Amended by [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (built 2026-10-03):** "a claim signed by any other root is refused" gains one exception, the **attested root reset**: `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/reset` carrying `key_reset_attest_b64` (a `lakko-attest` `KeyResetAttestation` verified under `LAKKO_DS_ATTEST_KEY`, naming exactly this journal, at a generation strictly above `ds_group_owner.key_generation`, migration `0009`) is heard from ANY signer, who becomes the root; the chain, the registrar set and the push binding are dropped, the generation recorded; the nonce is spent only after the attestation and generation checks pass. "Same root re-claims" no longer describes the key-reset case, where the root changes with the seed; only an account's DeviceGroup journal ever holds a generation above 0 (group journals are never re-rooted: ADR-0073 phase 2 recreates a group the old identity owned instead, deliberately, since an attestation re-rooting every journal the account owns would be a server-held list of them) **Amended by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6 (2026-10-16):** the attested root reset is gone (the key-reset attestation no longer names a journal; `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/reset` takes the statement alone, migration `0012` drops `ds_group_owner.key_generation`, the DS no longer reads `LAKKO_DS_ATTEST_KEY`); a DeviceGroup journal id is `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/device-group/id/v2", account_seed)`, so a key reset founds a NEW journal and claims it plainly, and a rotation (lineage seed unchanged) moves the root by a plain transfer. **Push binding retired by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 3 (2026-10-16):** `ds_push_bindings`, `LAKKO_DS_BINDING_PEPPER`, the `PUT .../push-binding` route and statement kind byte 6 (now refused) are removed (DS migration `0011`), and no client binds; a journal can no longer wake another group because a wake must be signed by that group's wake key, which only members hold. |
| [0058](docs/adr/0058-chat-folders.md) | **Chat folders, carried between the account's own devices.** **Accepted 2026-09-28 (the maintainer chose a per-element merge, a final deletion, and to cover the existing synced maps too); S1 core + both FFI and the three clients built 2026-09-28, merged into main 2026-09-29; not walked live.** UX spec [`docs/ux/chat-folders.md`](docs/ux/chat-folders.md). A folder is a saved view of the merged list (All chats stays unchanged): three kind toggles, three exclude toggles (muted / read / archived), chats added and excluded by hand, its own pins; a folder bar of tabs on all three clients and all widths. State is a set of independently HLC-stamped registers - folder meta, one `NONE` / `INCLUDED` / `EXCLUDED` register per (folder, conversation), the pin list, a removal tombstone, the folder order - each conversation named by a `(kind, id)` ref so channels fit; LWW per register with a bytewise tiebreak on equal stamps; a folder deletion is final (later edits to it are refused); tombstones and `NONE` kept. Two new DeviceGroup kinds: `FolderChanges` 18 (a live edit) and `StateSnapshot` 19 (14-17 stay with ADR-0050). A late device catches up by a snapshot of the whole folded state - folders AND the four existing maps (pins / mutes / archive, read cursors, drafts, conversation order), which no new device receives today - re-sent with original stamps by the lowest-leaf prior member on DeviceGroup growth, and from `BackupPayload` v5 when no other device is alive. Bounded fail-closed decode; client-side creation limits (20 folders, 200 + 200 chats, 10 pins) are not decode bounds. Fold in `lakko-sync` on both FFI surfaces (replacing the three hand-written folds); `folder_matches` + `order_conversations_in_folder` in `lakko-ui-rules`. No server change; invariants #2 / #5 hold |
| [0059](docs/adr/0059-channel-discover.md) | **Discover: an opt-in channel directory, topic buckets, search, and channels shared in groups.** **Proposed 2026-09-28.** A channel's owner may publish a listing (handle, name, description, topic, language; `lakko-channels::listing`, fuzzed) signed by a per-channel owner key `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/channels/owner/v1", account_seed ‖ channel_id)` registered at `POST /v1/channels`; the client verifies the handle through the OPRF lookup before subscribing. `services/channels` ranks listings per (topic, language) bucket from token-gated subscriber counts only (new token context `ChannelSubscribe = 6`; counts published in bands, the subscriber list becomes owner-signed) and serves fixed-size buckets with no session or token, cacheable; clients pad picked buckets with random ones to 8 per refresh; search spends `DirectorySearch = 7`. Channel metadata v2 (ADR-0051) gains topic, language and `listed`, so members who opt in share only listed channels inside a group via a new `lakko-contacts::shared_channels` envelope the server never sees. Operator removal through an internal listener. Records **G-43**: `services/channels` authorised no publisher operation; **closed in code 2026-10-01** (section 9): posts, rotate, stage, commit and envelope delivery require an owner or appointed co-publisher proof over the exact body, signed by all three clients, not walked live |
| [0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) | **Who can find me, and why knowing someone is not permission to reach them.** **Accepted 2026-09-28**; UX spec [`docs/ux/who-can-find-me.md`](docs/ux/who-can-find-me.md). A person chooses who finds them by `@username` or by name: nobody / contacts of contacts / members of all or chosen groups and channels / everyone (default: contacts of contacts + all groups and channels). An identity mailbox on the DS stops being open: a write needs a proof by a reach key the owner registered (classes contact / link / reply / group / member / channel / fof, one shared key per class so the server learns no contact count; since ADR-0078 section 9 `group` / `member` / `channel` are one key per account, not per group or channel), or a single-use delegation from one; a proofless write to a closed mailbox is answered like an unclaimed one. Reach keys are handed out, never looked up: the group and channel grant rides in profile envelope v3 (search in those scopes is local), channels gain E2EE member cards, contacts of contacts get OPRF-keyed introduction records relayed by the owner's contacts, and only Everyone publishes to discovery (username `public` flag, `display_names` as OPRF hashes of word prefixes from 3 letters, decided 2026-09-28 over whole-name matching; `lakko-discovery-client` owns normalisation and the terms). A discovery-signed username certificate replaces the ADR-0048 lookup verification. Residuals: the server sees whether a person is in Everyone and the size of their reach-key set; a contacts-of-contacts searcher learns which contact held the record; prefix search lets a token holder walk prefixes and list the people in Everyone and their own contacts of contacts; a `member` key lets a hidden-from co-member store bytes the owner's client drops.  Amended after S2 (2026-09-28): a channel subscriber's key is Ed25519 with the seed-envelope X25519 derived from it (`lakko-crypto` conversion); a reach proof names its signing key in a sixth proof-header field; rendezvous traffic moves to `/ds/rendezvous/{hex}/messages` and a proofless `GET /ds/mailbox/` is 401 whether claimed or not. S1 core, S2 services and S3 FFI built 2026-09-28; S3b (every write path carries its key: a reply grant in contact requests, `/c/` cold starts and `/g/` join attributions, a link grant in `/i/` and `/c/` capabilities, a contact grant in 1:1 profiles, committer delegations) built 2026-09-29; one inbound mailbox per person, so Welcomes go to the account mailbox and device mailboxes stay closed (ADR-0050 section 2.7 amended); S4/S5 clients built 2026-09-29 on web, iOS and Android (host-tested, not walked live; Android gained people search, contacts-of-contacts introductions and channel member cards on 2026-09-29, and where web and iOS disagreed the maintainer chose web and iOS was aligned). Core blocker sprint 2026-09-29 (core, FFI and services; the three clients wired the same day: the setting and reach-key versions travel over the DeviceGroup carrier and merge through `merge_reach_state`, Everyone publishes its public record, search opens public records, channel unsubscribe is signed): the DeviceGroup `ServiceEnvelope` carrier for the setting and reach-key versions (field 20), an Everyone public record returned by both lookups, member card binding v2 and a signed channel unsubscribe route, the channel Members info section. Discovery stores one-way lookup keys, never OPRF outputs: `finalize` returns `output` + `lookupKey` on both FFI surfaces, only the key reaches the server and the username certificate binds it, so a copy of the database opens no public record; the operator, holding the OPRF key, can still read the names of people in Everyone (residual). Renumbered from 0058 on 2026-09-29: four branches had claimed 0058. Plan S1-S6 in the ADR |
| [0061](docs/adr/0061-interface-localization.md) | **Interface localization — one catalog, built-in languages, signed updates that do not reveal the language.** **Accepted 2026-09-28**; UX spec [`docs/ux/localization.md`](docs/ux/localization.md). No client was localized (web: English literals in JSX; iOS: no `.xcstrings`; Android: `strings.xml` holds `app_name` only). First set `en ar es fa he hi pt-BR ru tr uk zh-Hans`; system language by default, English fallback; the choice is per device and never sent to a server; switching is live on all three (web signal + `lang`/`dir`, iOS environment locale + layout direction with its own lookup instead of `Bundle`, Android `setApplicationLocales`). One source catalog `l10n/catalog/*.json` (ICU MessageFormat subset: arguments + plural; `security_critical` flag; `machine`/`reviewed` status — a security-critical key ships translated only when reviewed) compiled by `tools/l10n`; a new crate `lakko-l10n` (FFI) owns the resolution rule, the formatter with CLDR plurals, and the verify + acceptance of a signed `TranslationBundle` (dedicated offline key, domain `lakko/translation-bundle/v1`, static `/.well-known/lakko-translations`, native only) that carries **every** language so the fetch reveals none, is monotone, and may never change a key the built-in catalog marks security-critical. Core prose never reaches the UI: errors render from their code. S1-S6 built 2026-09-28 to 2026-10-01 (renumbered from 0058 when main was merged); machine translations, security-critical strings await native review; not walked live |
| [0062](docs/adr/0062-conversation-kind.md) | **A conversation's kind is set in the group at creation, and a one-to-one keeps its two people.** **Accepted 2026-09-30; S1 core, S2 FFI and S3 clients (web, iOS, Android) built 2026-10-01, not walked live on any platform.** Until then each client guessed one-to-one vs group from the people count and a local flag, so a two-person group read as a one-to-one on everyone but its creator, the three clients disagreed on edge counts, and nothing stopped a device adding a third person to a one-to-one. The creator now writes the kind into the GroupContext as `0xFF03` (`lakko-mls::group_kind`, `version 0x01 ‖ kind 0x01 one-to-one / 0x02 group`, exact-length / version / kind fail-closed, also named in the seeded `RequiredCapabilities` and advertised by every leaf); `Group::create(identity, kind)` takes it as a required argument and `Group::group_kind()` reads it; `join_from_welcome` refuses a role-table Welcome without a readable kind (since the ADR-0081 second amendment of 2026-10-19, also any Welcome with no role table, C-86). No commit may change it (`group_kind_unchanged` at the peer commit gate, `proposal_keeps_group_kind` at receipt and in the sweep; the four role rewrites copy the extension list through one helper). In a one-to-one `one_to_one_holds` (peer commit gate, sweep, direct add/remove) refuses a commit that removes a person, exceeds two people, or rewrites any GroupContext extension; `one_to_one_allows` is the early per-proposal answer; external join is refused (`retained: false`); a person's own devices still join and retire (ADR-0050). Refusal reasons `OneToOneMembershipFixed`, `OneToOneHasNoRoles`, `GroupKindChanged` appended; `one_to_one` integration tests + fuzz `group_kind_decode`; **C-70**. FFI `GroupKind` / `groupKind()` on UniFFI, wasm and (read-only) the NSE surface. Clients read the kind for the lists, info, titles, the shared-pin authority, typing text and call routing (**a group of any size now calls over the SFU**, so a two-person group changed path), drop a group-name envelope in a one-to-one, and deleted the local kind stores, the count derivation and the chat -> group flip. Leaving a one-to-one stays a local delete. Flag day on KeyPackages; invariants #3 / #4, #2 / #5 untouched Renumbered from 0060 on 2026-10-01: main had taken 0060 for scoped discovery |
| [0063](docs/adr/0063-on-device-feed.md) | **The feed is ranked on the device, by one shared rule engine.** **Accepted 2026-09-28; S1 (the `lakko-feed` crate) and S2 (UniFFI + wasm `feed.rs`) built the same day; S3 (web) and S4 (iOS + Android) built 2026-09-29, not walked live; the native clients compiled and their feed tests passed only in scratch copies, since the worktree does not compile on iOS or Android until the `39dd75bd` sealed-invite-link bindings are ported there; S3e measured on a laptop (Node, release wasm, synthetic): at 100k candidates build 73.5 ms + rank 72.7-184.7 ms, linear, no index needed yet**; UX spec [`docs/ux/feed.md`](docs/ux/feed.md) (Feed becomes the second of five destinations). A new crate `lakko-feed` (not `lakko-ui-rules`, since it parses rules strangers share): a bounded canonical `FeedRule` codec (`lakko/feed-rule/v1\0`, <= 4096 bytes, `export` strips conversation ids; file `.lakkofeed` and a `/feed/import#` fragment link; fuzz `feed_rule_decode`), the Latest / Popular / For me presets as constants, `rank(rule, conversations, candidates, now)` returning indices with replies folded under their parent (total, deterministic, independent of input order, integer arithmetic only so no platform's libm can reorder near-equal items; scoring shapes chosen, not measured), and `contiguous_seen`, the one definition of how far a conversation is read. Clients assemble candidates from their existing encrypted message logs, add a local channel-post log (no client stored posts before) and poll channels in the foreground; mentions are derived on the device from `@username`; an item on screen for 2 s in the feed counts as seen, and the ADR-0024 read receipt and the DeviceGroup `ReadCursor` carry the contiguous seen prefix instead of the highest received id. One-to-one chats are never candidates. No server, wire or `lakko-mls` change. Recommendations are ADR-0059. Renumbered from 0058 on 2026-10-01: main had taken 0058 for chat folders |
| [0064](docs/adr/0064-conversation-backup-and-device-re-entry.md) | **Conversations come back on another device: saved history, and a device that re-enters by itself.** **Accepted 2026-10-02; nothing built yet.** UX spec [`docs/ux/conversation-backup.md`](docs/ux/conversation-backup.md). (1) A per-conversation and per-channel **vault** on `services/storage` that no request ties to an account: id, AEAD key and Ed25519 write key derived from the account identity seed and the conversation ref; a small versioned head plus numbered chunks, sealed and padded; routes signed by the write key, never a session token; a claim spends a new `VaultClaim` anonymous token; on the server every object is written once under a versioned name and describes itself, and an index (MariaDB in deployment, a file beside the objects for one instance, one trait as in `services/channels`) coordinates versions and can be rebuilt from the objects, so the service can run as several instances; an `index` vault orders loading (most recent first). (2) One shared record codec in a new crate `lakko-vault`; devices merge by message id under a version rule; saving is always on, no setting. Cost stated: the recovery phrase opens the saved history. (3) The direct hand-over at pairing stays for the newest messages of the most recent conversations and moves to the same full record (it drops authors, reactions and replies today); the vault, written in batches and never per message, supplies the rest. (4) **Knock and entry at once:** a device with no group for a conversation posts an external-join proposal to the knock (a member that sees a bound device of an account already in the group admits it at once, ADR-0050 2.5a) and, at the same time, enters by an external commit built from the GroupInfo saved in the head. In a role-table group that commit is accepted only when it carries the mandatory ExternalInit alone and adds a leaf bound to an account already in the group, under the leaf cap. The DS Append of epoch N+1 decides which lands first. Devices save the GroupInfo of the next epoch before publishing a commit, and the head keeps the two newest, so a conversation only the person is in always re-enters. (5) **A phrase sign-in adds a device**: the DeviceGroup is entered the same way and founded again only when no `devices` vault exists (today a phrase sign-in re-founds it and cuts the other devices off). Every other table-less group refuses every external commit (closes an unchecked merge that exists today). (6) One new visible state, *waiting*, inside the conversation; the "Synchronizing..." label goes on every route. (7) Channels: the subscriber key, seeds and posts are in the channel vault; a publisher enters the PublisherGroup the same way and then reseals. Amends ADR-0027 alternative 2 (for a device of a present member only) and ADR-0057's DeviceGroup re-founding. Invariant #3 change: property tests in the MLS sprint, G-39 surface grows |
| [0065](docs/adr/0065-attachment-lifetime-and-access.md) | **Attachment files have a lifetime, an owner who can delete them, and a gate.** **Accepted 2026-10-02; the saved register and the delete-key derivation are built in the core; the server sprint (blob index with a MariaDB and a file implementation, the 365-day sweep, `keep`, owner `DELETE` under the per-blob delete key, upload and read passes bought with `blob-upload` / `blob-read` tokens, an nginx rate limit) built 2026-10-26 in `services/storage`, host-tested only; the clients (passes, the gone and limit states, delete on delete-for-everyone and of unsent uploads and old profile photos, the keep tick, Save and the Saved screen) built 2026-10-26/27 on web, iOS and Android, host-tested only; the blob delete key comes from the lineage seed; a saved message is named by its segment's group id and DS id.** UX spec [`docs/ux/saved-messages-and-file-lifetime.md`](docs/ux/saved-messages-and-file-lifetime.md). Found: `/blob` takes no credential (anyone uploads 50 MiB objects without limit and anyone handed an id and a key downloads), nginx has no rate limit there despite the comments, nothing is ever deleted (a message deleted for everyone leaves its ciphertext), and a missing file shows as a retryable load failure. Decision: (1) one index row per blob in the ADR-0064 storage index (size, node, created day, last-access day, delete key; no account); (2) a blob with no access for 365 days is deleted by a daily sweep, last access written at most once a day; (3) `POST /blob/keep` refreshes files of pinned messages and of **saved** messages, sent by devices about monthly; (4) **Save** is a new account-level bookmark: a register in the DeviceGroup synced state carrying the message ref, a display snapshot and the blob ids, with a Saved screen; (5) two anonymous token contexts, `BlobUpload` and `BlobRead`, each buying an hour-long stateless pass with a budget (files and bytes to upload; downloads and keeps), because a token per file would cost thousands of tokens a day per person, plus an nginx per-address backstop; (6) an upload carries a delete public key derived from the account seed and the blob id, so only the uploading account deletes: on delete-for-everyone and for uploads never sent. The vault of ADR-0064 stores references, so restored history shows expired files as gone. Open: an armed iOS device uploads outside the anti-censorship tunnel today (found by the recon) |
| [0066](docs/adr/0066-sfu-forwards-rtp-packets.md) | **The SFU forwards RTP packets; it does not assemble frames.** **Accepted and built 2026-10-02 (`src/stream_map.rs`, `media.rs` on `Event::RtpPacket` + `StreamTx::write_rtp`, `PacketDescriptor` in `frame_descriptor.rs`); host-tested, deployed to the test stack, not yet measured on a call and never run against a browser or native client.** Measured on a live call on the test stack: in `str0m` frame mode the SFU held everything behind a missing packet until N complete frames had queued (defaults 15 audio, 30 video); audio is never resent, so each lost audio packet became a 300 ms stop and a burst, and with the video hold at 10 the SFU forwarded no video for 2.3-2.8 s per loss burst while the publisher's packets kept arriving. Decision: `str0m` RTP mode; every packet is forwarded as it arrives under the publisher's sequence number plus a per-stream offset, so loss reaches the subscriber as a gap its own retransmission and jitter buffer handle; the generic frame descriptor is carried verbatim; simulcast switches at a keyframe with re-based offsets; payloads are never read (invariant #2, C-39 hold). Amends the media half of ADR-0011. The media loop now feeds every peer a timeout each pass (a written packet is released on a tick), nothing is written to a peer before its transport connects, and the `lakko_sfu_media_frames_*` counters count packets. The interim fix it replaced (audio hold 1 frame, video hold 3, a keyframe request on a gap) is gone |
| [0067](docs/adr/0067-loss-resilient-call-media.md) | **Call media survives a burst of packet loss.** **Proposed 2026-10-03.** Measured after ADR-0066: the SFU adds no wait; the home network drops 20-41% of packets for about a second every 30 s, and each hole costs a keyframe request and a publisher bitrate collapse. Steps, each measured live before the next: (1) every client logs the receivers' own counters per interval and as a total (freezes, freeze duration, dropped frames, jitter-buffer delay, concealed audio;
