web `calls/media-counters.ts` in `SfuCallSession`, deployed to the test stack); (2) camera video in three temporal layers (`L1T3`), a client-only change since the SFU relays the generic frame descriptor that carries the dependencies (web `calls/camera-sender.ts`, deployed and reported as `L1T3` by both senders on a live call; Android `calls/CameraLayers.kt`, built and unit-tested; the iOS binding exposes no `scalabilityMode`; mid-call camera-on keeps `addTrack` and asks via `setParameters`); (3) redundant audio (RED): libwebrtc builds the RED container before the frame encryptor / encoded transform, so SFrame seals primary and redundancy together and the SFU forwards the payload verbatim; the vendored `str0m` gains audio `red` (third fork change, `CodecConfig::enable_red`, listed before Opus so Chrome sends it), the SFU sends a RED packet only to a subscriber that negotiated red (`red_negotiated`), built 2026-10-04, host-tested, deployed to the test stack, not yet seen on a live call; (4) video FEC (RED + ULPFEC) behind `LAKKO_SFU_VIDEO_FEC` (default off): forwarding the publisher's FEC cannot work (ULPFEC protects the header extension block, which the SFU rewrites per leg), so the SFU terminates it: 4a, built 2026-10-04 and host-tested, the SFU rebuilds a publisher's lost video packet from the publisher's own FEC (`services/sfu/src/fec_receive.rs`, sixth fork change `DirectApi::keep_received_rtp` / `take_received_rtp` / `handle_recovered_rtp`), refuses a rebuilt packet whose timestamp does not fit its neighbours, and no longer forwards publisher FEC; 4b, built the same day and host-tested, the SFU sends its own FEC packet to each red + ulpfec subscriber in the slot of each publisher FEC packet, computed over the packets as that subscriber received them (`services/sfu/src/fec_send.rs`, seventh fork change `StreamTx::keep_sent_rtp` / `DirectApi::take_sent_rtp`), with no renumbering and the publisher's protection rate; neither part has been seen against a real browser; the fifth fork change clamps the loss fraction in receiver reports (a repaired loss was reported as about 97% loss and collapsed the sender's bitrate); web also asks the camera for 1280x720 (it opened at 640x480) and logs a sender line (encoder target, bitrate estimate, the loss the SFU reports, codecs). Measured with layers: frozen time per minute 7.1 -> 4.8 s and 7.4 -> 5.7 s (with the since-removed SFU requests in the same build); (5) a viewer receives both simulcast layers: web publishes `q` (quarter size, 150 kbit/s) and `f`, the SFU gives each subscriber the main track plus a permanent light companion under the MSID stream id + `~light` (`TrackRole::LightCompanion` in `services/sfu/src/media.rs`), and the web tile shows the light one while the main one stands (`apps/web/src/calls/layer-display.ts`, a `shown` line in the call log); on the synthetic one-second dip the median longest gap with no decodable frame is 522 ms on the main track and 324 ms on either, shorter in 15 of 20 seeds; switching one track to the light layer (`uplink_fallback.rs`) was measured not to help and is off unless `LAKKO_SFU_UPLINK_FALLBACK=1`; built 2026-10-04, deployed to the test stack on web, not seen on a live call; the rule is `lakko_ui_rules::layer_display` on both FFI surfaces; iOS and Android publish both layers and show the companion by the same rule, host-tested only, never run on a device |
| [0068](docs/adr/0068-server-side-bitrate-control.md) | **The SFU decides a publisher's bitrate.** **Accepted 2026-10-04; built the same day behind `LAKKO_SFU_BITRATE_CONTROL` (default `client`; the test stack runs `server`), host-tested, not yet measured on a call and never run against a browser.** Measured: a home network lost its uplink for about a second every 30 s, and the sending browser turned each second into about 25 s of low bitrate (its send-side controller cut the estimate tenfold and climbed 8% a second). In `server` mode the SFU negotiates no transport-wide congestion control, keeps `abs-send-time` and `goog-remb`, estimates each publisher's path itself (`services/sfu/src/uplink_estimate.rs`: delay trend over send-time groups; over-use cuts to 0.85 of the arriving rate; a burst of loss with no delay growth changes nothing; loss lasting 3 s steps the estimate down; no floor) and sends it as REMB. One rule for every browser and both native clients. Read from the libwebrtc M125 source: REMB lifts the sender only in its 2 s start phase, and the sender's own receiver-report rule still cuts by `1 - loss/2` per lossy report and climbs 8% a second, so a burst is expected to cost 4 to 8 s, not 25. No `str0m` change. Fallback recorded: Lakko's own media stack in the browser **Amended 2026-10-11 (the publish budget).** Measured on live calls by packet capture at the SFU: with the SFU's estimate held at 1000 kbit/s Chrome's main sender sent 720 kbit/s and Firefox's a flat 1537 kbit/s, while Firefox's light sender, which carries a ceiling the client set, stayed at 125 kbit/s; on a Wi-Fi publisher, lowering what it sent from about 3.2 to about 1.1 Mbit/s took the 99th percentile of its own audio's lateness at the SFU from 264 ms to 93 ms; one publisher's audio and video lateness correlate at 0.91 to 1.00 (one queue), so a publisher's standing queue is the conversation's delay. REMB is a hint a browser weighs, a sender's maximum bitrate is a setting, so the estimate also goes out as `ServerFrame::PublishBudget { main_kbps, middle_kbps }` (kind `publish-budget`, each 1..=20000): a ceiling for the publisher's main and middle camera senders, computed by the SFU alone (`services/sfu/src/publish_budget.rs`, pure: headroom for audio and for the light sender, 15% for retransmissions and FEC, floors 200 / 100 kbit/s, plan maxima 2500 / 500; a lower ceiling at once, a higher one no sooner than 2 s and only for a 10% change; every constant chosen, not measured), sent only under `LAKKO_SFU_BITRATE_CONTROL=server` and on unless `LAKKO_SFU_PUBLISH_BUDGET=0`; REMB goes on beside it. A client sets each figure as the first encoding's maximum bitrate of that sender and holds no allocation rule of its own: web `apps/web/src/calls/publish-budget.ts` (2026-10-11), iOS `PublishBudget.swift` and Android `PublishBudget.kt` (2026-10-12; the ADR's amendment text still says the native clients ignore the frame). Host tests only: the rule, the frame on both FFI surfaces, the frame arriving over signaling in the SFU's synthetic-client test, each client against fakes. Not seen on a call and not run against a browser; the repository records no deployment of it. That Firefox obeys the ceiling on its main sender is inferred from its light sender; Safari is not known |
| [0069](docs/adr/0069-sframe-random-counter-start.md) | **An SFrame sender starts its frame counter at a random 62-bit value.** **Accepted 2026-10-04 (the maintainer chose it over a generation in the KID); built the same day in `lakko-calls`, the C ABI, both FFI crates, the C++ bridge, the web worker and the rebuilt iOS / Android libraries; host-tested, deployed to the test stack on web, not seen on a live call.** A `lakko-security` pass found that rejoining a chat-group call in the same MLS epoch (the seed is the chat group's exporter for the call id; a join advances no epoch), a reload or a re-published camera derived the same key with a counter at zero: reused `(key, nonce)` pairs the SFU holds (G-44; the earlier "V1 false alarm" note covered link calls only). The counter is now `u64`, the nonce is the salt XOR the counter in the low 8 bytes (identical to the old nonce below `2^32`, so the golden vector still opens), a fresh key starts at a CSPRNG value in `[0, 2^62)`, receivers accept any 64-bit counter; two keys under one `(seed, KID)` collide with probability at most `(L1 + L2) / 2^62`. Flag day for call media; no stack seals the golden bytes any more, each proves its seal by opening what it sealed. Amends C-5 |
| [0070](docs/adr/0070-call-media-through-a-loss-burst.md) | **A call picture does not stop in a burst of loss, in any browser.** **Accepted 2026-10-04 (the maintainer asked for a design that holds on ordinary bad networks, in every browser and at ten participants; chose the adaptive playout delay); steps 1, 3 and 4 built 2026-10-05 on the SFU and web, host-tested, deployed to the test stack, never seen on a live call or against a browser.** Rule: the server and Lakko's own client code decide, the browser executes; nothing rests on a browser's built-in heuristic, and a mechanism is tested in more than one engine before it is called working. Replaces ADR-0067 step 5 as first built: browser simulcast made a live call worse (below about 900 kbit/s a browser sends its light layer alone, and a subscriber's REMB was taken for its downlink budget, which parked its main track on the light layer; the second is fixed, only transport-wide feedback is a budget). (1) The camera is two ordinary senders: the main one as before simulcast and a light one (a clone at a quarter size, 150 kbit/s, SFrame stream index 3); `ClientFrame::SdpOffer.light_mids` names the light m-lines and the SFU forwards that track as the `~light` companion, with no layer selection. (2) The tile shows whichever track is alive (`lakko_ui_rules::layer_display`; where a browser has no per-frame callback the web client feeds it from receiver statistics). (3) The light track is protected harder: missing packets asked for every 20 ms (vendored change 9, a per-stream NACK interval), every packet sent to a subscriber twice 30 ms apart (change 10, `StreamTx::resend`; `LAKKO_SFU_LIGHT_DUPLICATE=0` off). (4) The SFU stamps a minimum playout delay per (subscriber, publisher) in the playout-delay header extension: 0 until a burst on the publisher's uplink or the subscriber's downlink, then 300 ms, stepping down after 180 s without one (`services/sfu/src/playout_delay.rs`; `LAKKO_SFU_PLAYOUT_DELAY=0` off). Measured on the synthetic dip (30% of datagrams arriving for one second, a viewer 300 ms behind): the light track shows no stop in 12 of 20 uplink dips (worst 225 ms) against 0 of 20 unprotected, and in 0 of 20 downlink dips (median 275 ms); the model's loss pattern is an assumption, to be replaced by a capture from a live call. **Step 5 built 2026-10-05 on the SFU and web, host-tested, deployed to the test stack, never run against a browser:** a third ordinary sender, the middle one (half size, 500 kbit/s, SFrame stream index 4, active only from three people: `middle_sender_wanted`), named in `SdpOffer.middle_mids` and forwarded as `<peer>~mid`; a subscriber sends `ClientFrame::VideoWants` (per publisher `main` / `middle` / `light`, the whole set each time) and the SFU forwards the main or the middle track only when wanted and the light one always, resuming a paused track at a keyframe with continuous sequence numbers; a tile picks by `lakko_ui_rules::tile_video_quality(people, tile_width_px, pinned_or_stage)` (one other person or a pinned / stage tile at least 960 px wide: main; under 240 px: light; else middle; chosen, not measured). **Step 6 built 2026-10-06 on the SFU, off unless `LAKKO_SFU_DOWNLINK_CONTROL=1`:** the SFU estimates each subscriber's downlink itself from transport-wide feedback on the lines it sends on (the uplink estimator generalised as `PathEstimator`; vendored changes 12 and 13), probes with bounded padding before giving a larger picture (`downlink_probe.rs`), gives up the larger tracks first (`downlink_allocation.rs`) and says so in `ServerFrame::VideoGiven`; on a synthetic 7.5 Mbit/s link it settles on one main and two middles in about 4 to 5 s and is back on three mains 5 to 7 s after the limit is lifted, and a publisher whose frames arrive 200 ms late in a clump costs the subscriber no picture (the SFU does not cut on a queue its own sending built); the five acceptance tests are `#[ignore]`d (about a minute each; on 2026-10-06 each passed 3 of 3 alone in an independent check, after two earlier builds that did not), a publisher stall much longer than 200 ms still overflows a narrow link's queue, the web client reads `video-given` and a tile shows the lower of what it wants and what the SFU gives (`apps/web/src/calls/video-given.ts`, host-tested). **Since then (2026-10-07 to 10-11, SFU and web; dated notes in the ADR):** a retransmission carries no RTP padding (vendored change 14: Chrome restored a padded second copy one byte short, so step 3 did nothing for a browser in fifteen cases of sixteen until then); every key-frame request for a publisher track goes through one gate (one per 500 ms; a subscriber's own request relayed at once, then after 1, 2, 4 and every 8 s; the SFU's own requests slow to one per 10 s after 5 unanswered); at the end of an unrepaired burst on a publisher's stream the SFU asks for a key frame itself and stops asking for the packets of the burst (`burst_keyframe.rs`, vendored change 15 `StreamRx::give_up_missing`; `LAKKO_SFU_BURST_KEYFRAME=0` off); a fourth round of downlink rules after the first live call with the switch on, where a viewer on a shared mobile link was moved between middle and light 13 times in six and a half minutes (the limit follows a set the path is carrying, a set that fits is kept, nothing is given while a queue stands, a set the path took back waits 10, 20, 40, then 60 s; a sixth `#[ignore]`d acceptance test on a mobile-like link model; the switch is still off by default); `ServerFrame::VideoNeeded { main, middle }` tells a publisher which of its two heavy senders some subscriber has named in `video-wants` (on at once, off after 10 s unwanted; `video_needed.rs`; `LAKKO_SFU_VIDEO_NEEDED=0` off, on by default; a client that sends no `video-wants` is not supported with it on) and the web client drives `encodings[0].active` of its main and middle senders from it (`apps/web/src/calls/sender-activity.ts`); the web seals through `RTCRtpScriptTransform` or, where that is absent, `createEncodedStreams` (`sframe-transport.ts`), and a browser with neither is refused before a guest account is created (`link-join-gate.ts`); the stage view with an explicit pin button (`docs/ux/group-call-grid.md` 6.2). **Seen on live calls on the test stack, web only (the commit record of 2026-10-09 and 10-11; Edge took part in the 2026-10-09 call):** video between Chrome, Firefox and Safari, intact retransmissions, the main sender going off in a grid and coming back for a pinned person in five browsers, no downlink oscillation, the burst-end key frame. Not seen: the stage following the speaker, the pin button. **iOS and Android ported 2026-10-11:** three ordinary senders in place of the simulcast code (`CameraSender.swift`, `CameraSenders.kt`), `light_mids` / `middle_mids` in the offer, `video-wants` by tile width through the shared rule, `video-given`, `video-needed`, the pin button, the speaking-floor rule; unit tests exist for each, and nothing native was run on a device, an emulator or a call. Unbuilt: the `jitterBufferTarget` fallback for a receiver that ignores the extension, a fallback to main when a wanted middle track carries nothing **2026-10-11 and 10-12 (three dated notes in the ADR).** **(a) The burst-end key frame classifies what is missing.** After a live call on 2026-10-11 where a Firefox viewer stood still 3.3 to 4.0 s about every 30 s and in six episodes the SFU asked for nothing (the rule of 2026-10-09 judged a stream that carries FEC by number alone, and a stream near half FEC explained missing media away as lost FEC), each packet still missing when a burst is judged is known media (an FEC packet that arrived lists it as protected, or the frame descriptors on the two sides of its gap say so), known not media, or unknown; one packet of known media asks for the key frame whatever the FEC share, packets known not to be media never ask, and the count rule stays for the unknown ones. The SFU reads two more fields of the generic frame descriptor, both in the clear (the end-of-frame flag and the frame id; no payload, C-39); frame ids are used on a stream only after eight frames in a row followed with nothing missing, because simulcast layers of one libwebrtc sender share a counter (read in the M125 source). Evidence in the repository: unit tests and the extended `video_fec` fuzz target; its commit records it as not deployed and not seen on a call. The maintainer reports it has since been deployed to the test stack and that on one live two-person call two of eight bursts were classified `media-missing` and six fell to the count rule; no file in the repository records that. **(b) The SFU negotiates and relays no RFC 6464 audio-level header extension** (2026-10-12; a header extension is outside the SFrame seal and this one told the server who speaks and how loudly; recorded under C-39). **(c) Speaking indication:** the threshold follows each person's own noise floor (`NoiseGate`: speech is three times the floor, bounded) in place of one fixed number, written three times (web `speaking.ts`, iOS `Speaking.swift`, Android `Speaking.kt`), not as a shared Rust rule; the web ring is decided from the stream the browser plays (an analyser) and then from the receiver's audio-energy statistics, never from the sender-written level, which is printed in diagnostics only (`audio-level.ts`). **(d) Sound and picture of one person are kept together by Lakko's own rule.** Read in libwebrtc M125: the engine synchronises audio with one video stream per sync group (the stream id), so only the main track is synchronised and nothing tied middle or light to the sound; measured on a live call in Chrome, a tile on the light track ran 100 to 300 ms ahead of the sound. `lakko_ui_rules::av_align` (one `AvAlign` per remote person, both FFI surfaces) takes each receiver's measured delay once per reading and returns the playout-delay target per receiver: the sound is the reference, an unsynchronised track follows it (raised at most 240 ms a second, lowered at most 80, a trim of at most 90 ms, a 30 ms dead band), main is observed and never written, and a shown track that stays late makes the sound wait by at most 300 ms; every constant chosen. Web writes `RTCRtpReceiver.jitterBufferTarget`, feature-detected, on by default with a `localStorage` switch (`av-align.ts`). **(e) iOS and Android** have no such setter in their bindings, so the native shim calls the engine's `RtpReceiverInterface::SetJitterBufferMinimumDelay` by vtable slot (`apps/shared/sframe-bridge/receiver-delay.h`; slot offsets and the argument's calling convention read from the machine code of each shipped slice, which differ: an 8-byte slot at `+0x68` on iOS and Android x86_64, a relative 32-bit slot at `+0x34` on Android arm64). Guards: offered only when the loaded WebRTC image is one the table was read from (Mach-O `LC_UUID` / ELF build id), a bracket probe before the first set of a process (`media_type()` before the slot and `GetFrameDecryptor()` after it must answer correctly, else no set for the process), and drift tests on the pinned versions; any other image is observe-only. Not an E2EE gate: it carries no media and no key. Built and host-tested (a fake receiver laid out by the host compiler; that cannot show how the shipped binaries are laid out). **Nothing of (c), (d) or (e) has been seen on a call, and nothing native has run on a device.** The ADR's own notes lag the tree in two places: the alignment note ends with no client wired to the rule, and all three are; the setter note lists the Android x86_64 relative-vtable build flag as found and not fixed, and `tools/build-sframe-jni.sh` passes it for arm64 only since the same commit. Web diagnostics gained `jb_target_ms` / `jb_net_ms` on the receiver line, a `sync` line per person, the `voices` line and `ceiling=` on the send line |
| [0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md) | **The codec's frame header stays in the clear; the SFrame header moves to the end of the frame.** **Proposed 2026-10-07 (the maintainer decided to do it; the status line is not yet flipped); phase 1 (core, wasm, C ABI entry points, web) built by 2026-10-09, phase 2 (the shared native bridge, iOS, Android) built 2026-10-11.** Found on live calls: Firefox opened every sealed frame and decoded none, because a receiver without the generic frame descriptor finds a key frame by reading the codec's own header at the start of the payload, where the SFrame header sat. Amends ADR-0013's layout and AAD; keys, nonces and counters stay those of ADR-0035 / ADR-0069. A sealed media frame is `clear_prefix (0..=16 B) ‖ ciphertext_and_tag ‖ sframe_header ‖ trailer` (trailer = `clear_prefix_len ‖ sframe_header_len ‖ version 0x01`, read first by the receiver); the AAD is `sframe_header ‖ clear_prefix ‖ trailer`, so every byte that is not ciphertext is authenticated. The sender picks the prefix: 10 bytes of a VP8 key frame, 3 of any other VP8 frame, none for Opus or for any other or undetermined codec (unknown is never guessed to be VP8); the VP8 frame tag decides what a key frame is. The receiver states no codec and refuses anything inconsistent; a header-first frame and a frame in this layout never open as each other. One implementation, `lakko_calls::media_frame` (`ParticipantKey::seal_media_frame` / `open_media_frame`, `CallKeyRing::try_open_media_frame_with_overlap`), on wasm and on the C ABI (`lakko_sframe_seal_media_frame`, `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_open_media_frame_with_overlap`, `lakko_sframe_media_clear_prefix_len`); not on UniFFI, since native per-frame media goes through the C ABI. The web learns the codec per frame from the frame's metadata, else from the sender's payload-type table. **Phase 2:** `apps/shared/sframe-bridge` seals and opens only this layout, `GetMaxCiphertextByteSize` allows the frame plus 36, and the encryptor is told its codec through a thread-safe `SetCodec` (audio is always Opus; video keeps nothing clear until told), which iOS (`LakkoSFrameAttach setCodecName`) and Android (`LakkoFrameEncryptor.setCodecName`) call from `syncSenderCodecs` after each negotiation and each attach. Cost stated in the ADR: an SFU can read the VP8 frame tag of every frame and the dimensions of key frames (`services/sfu` reads neither: it takes key frames from the frame descriptor); frames no longer match an external RFC 9605 stack; the G-39 surface grows by the trailer parser on unauthenticated input (fuzz `media_frame_open`, compile-checked, not run). Seen on live calls, web only: video between Chrome, Firefox and Safari. Not run: anything native on a device, an emulator or a call, and no web-to-native call. Not done: the header-first `seal_framed` / `open_framed` are still in the core, both FFI crates and the C ABI (the golden tests and the SFU's synthetic-client test call them); only VP8 has a rule. **Amended 2026-10-26 (maintainer):** the high three bits of the trailer's first byte say what the frame holds (`MediaContent`: the codec's frame, or an RFC 2198 RED container of Opus), so a receiver without `audio/red` (Firefox, which heard nobody in Chrome or Safari) decodes the primary frame (`red_primary`) of a RED publisher's audio, which the SFU now sends it under its Opus payload type; `MediaCodec::RedOpus = 3`, C ABI `lakko_sframe_seal_media_frame_for_codec`, Android tells the microphone's encryptor its codec; walked on the local stack web only |
| [0072](docs/adr/0072-recovery-model-simplification.md) | **Recovery model simplification.** **Accepted 2026-10-02 (maintainer decisions), amended the same day; built in the core, both FFI surfaces, `services/auth` and the web client (unit tests and type checks only, nothing walked live); iOS and Android were brought to the same model the same day: both compile and pass their unit tests (iOS `LakkoTests` 2564 on a simulator with the core rebuilt; Android 2947 on the host JVM, which does not load the native core, and the Android `.so` was not rebuilt), no flow was walked on a device, and no passkey ceremony or PRF evaluation was ever executed (PRF needs iOS 18; on Android below API 34 a PRF request is dropped at passkey registration; whether a provider returns PRF output, and whether the three clients derive the same output for one synced passkey, is unverified).** UX spec [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md). Removed: Shamir social-recovery shares (`lakko-shamir`, both FFI modules, the share screens) and the email / phone one-time-code factor (ADR-0018 (d); an email-only lookup was considered and rejected: the server must see the address next to the account to send a code, and a mailed code can only return ACCESS). The recovery phrase is **12 words** (flag day), shown in a 3 x 4 grid at phone width and 4 x 3 on wide screens, numbered row-major. A new device restores keys from another device (listed first), a passkey that carries keys, or the whole phrase; last resort a fresh identity. **Three words are checked LOCALLY** on a device that holds the phrase, like a wallet (`lakko_mnemonic::word_check`: three random positions per prompt in drawn order, `check_words`; a client-side budget of 5 misses then a 15-minute pause, which is a gate in front of a credential the device already holds, not a server control); on a match the device runs the unchanged `/recovery/login/*` with its seed and offers "Choose a new password" through `/password/reset/*`. The server stores nothing about the three words, so ADR-0049's invariant-#2 exception is withdrawn outright. **A server-held three-word verifier returning a session was this ADR's first decision 5; it was built and removed the same day** (migration `0027` is its trace, `0029` drops it): it left a searchable 33-bit value on the server, needed enrollment and a tombstone, and gave a session on a device without keys, from which "start over with a new identity" was reachable by anyone knowing the username and three words. **Every device with keys holds the phrase**, and the another-device transfer v2 carries the 16-byte entropy (48-byte plaintext, v1 refused). **A passkey is a second way to recover keys**, opt-in per passkey behind an informed-trust screen (the PRF output seals the entropy; never sent to the server; no PRF -> sign-in only, said so). **Amended 2026-11-11 (maintainer): no opt-in any more** — every passkey added on a device holding the phrase stores the blob in the add flow (no choice screen, no "Stop restoring keys"; a passkey holding none offers "Restore keys with this passkey" on its row), and `services/auth` registers every passkey as discoverable (`require_discoverable`, `residentKey: required`), since webauthn-rs asked for a non-discoverable one a new device could not sign in with. **After a fresh identity the recovery credential is replaced** — **since [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (2026-10-03) only inside `POST /account/keys/reset/finish`: the standalone `POST /recovery/credential/{start,finish}` routes are DELETED** (`Scope::RecoveryReplace` 10 per hour is still charged on both reset calls); the old residual "existing sessions are not revoked" is answered for the reset case (every other device's session is superseded by the key generation), and "recovery credential owed" became "publish owed". Two of the three items this row listed as unbuilt are closed by ADR-0073 in code (not walked): a device holding a stale phrase after the account started over elsewhere is signed out and wiped (the landing rule + `401 key-generation-superseded`), and the new words are withheld while the publish is owed. Still unverified: PRF availability on real devices. Amends ADR-0017, ADR-0018, ADR-0049; amended by ADR-0073 |
| [0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) | **Reset account keys: a server-attested key generation that signs out and removes every other device.** **Proposed 2026-10-03; phase 1 BUILT the same day on `services/auth` + `services/discovery` + `services/ds`, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android; unit-tested (auth DB tests 120 + 2, discovery 107, DS 85, web vitest 3914, iOS `LakkoTests` 2613 with the core rebuilt, Android 2997 on the host JVM with the `.so` not rebuilt), NOT walked live on any device or against a server, no passkey ceremony executed. Phase 2 ("a contact reset their account keys") BUILT 2026-10-04 on web, iOS and Android (web vitest 3996, iOS `LakkoTests` 2637, Android 3042 on the host JVM; nothing walked live) WITHOUT the proposed `lakko-mls` owner-slot exception — the maintainer chose Recreate group only, so `lakko-mls` is untouched and G-39's surface does not grow.** UX spec [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md) section 15 (the person who resets), section 16 (everyone else; **rewritten 2026-10-04 as silent continuity: the maintainer rejected every visible surface of the built phase 2, which is to be replaced; its core and server half (R-S1: discovery serves the reset attestation on the identity fetch, `continuity_reach_seed`, the conversation lineage register in `lakko-sync` carried as a DeviceGroup service envelope, fuzz `lakko-sync/synced_state_decode`) was built 2026-10-11, and the three clients were moved to it on 2026-10-11/12 (R.13b: the visible flow and its 45 catalog keys removed; reset verdict and re-pin, conversation lineage, claim-then-build successors, held send, the continuity key, a re-send marker in the chat envelope; web 4643, iOS 3315, Android 3501, unit tests only, NOT walked; an alignment pass on 2026-10-12 gave each rule one behaviour on the three clients, put a network guard under each unit suite and explained the flaky tests: web 4741, iOS 3367, Android 3553, still not walked; open: a person who rotated before this device first saw them cannot be added until their devices re-bind). While building, a flaw in R.2 was closed: a group member's claimed account id establishes nothing, a reset is judged only from a binding this device verified against the directory**) and section 17 (proving it is you, built 2026-10-06). What the maintainer decided: a reset signs every other device out and removes it from the account, deletes the passkeys (password and TOTP survive, open question 1), and the signed-out device wipes everything local. How: `accounts.identity_version` is the per-account **key generation** (bumped by `POST /account/keys/reset/{start,finish}`, one transaction: bump, replace the recovery credential, delete passkeys + vaults + WebAuthn states + action grants + device grants + device requests, mint a 177-byte `KeyResetAttestation` (`lakko-attest`, the fifth kind: `account_ref` = BLAKE3-derive-key over the account id so the DS never learns the id, the DeviceGroup journal id, the generation, the new pubkey, `signed_at`), issue the caller a fresh pair); every session token is a **v2 access token carrying the generation** (default 900 s) plus an **`r1` refresh credential** (30 days, sliding) only `POST /auth/session/refresh` accepts; auth's `require_session` and the refresh route read the row and refuse a lower generation with `401 key-generation-superseded`; discovery, storage and gif-proxy verify statelessly (latency = the access TTL) except discovery's two identity writes, gated by the row's own `key_generation`; discovery accepts a key CHANGE only with a supersession proof or a strictly-newer attestation (`409 identity-change-needs-attestation` otherwise: **the ADR-0017 heal that let an old device publish the old key back is retired**) and deletes every KeyPackage row of the account with it; the DS `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/reset` hears a Reset from ANY signer carrying a valid attestation for exactly that journal at a strictly-greater generation (re-roots the DeviceGroup, drops the chain, registrars and push binding); `/recovery/credential/*` deleted (folded in); `Scope::KeyReset` 3 per day. Clients: one `SessionTokenProvider` (refresh at <= 120 s left, one in flight; a transport-seam interceptor on iOS / Android, a fetch wrapper on web), the one `401` rule (one refresh, one retry; `key-generation-superseded` -> the signed-out screen + the full forget-device wipe, publishing nothing; `refresh-invalid` -> sign-in, no wipe), the landing rule on every session landing (published key differs at a higher generation -> wipe; same generation -> block, wipe on tap; never upload), the reset in the new order (server first, then the attested discovery upload and the attested DS re-founding, retried from an owed record with the SAME attestation, words withheld meanwhile, a 30-day client bound on an owed attestation). **Deviations recorded in the ADR's build section:** no auth `503 key-reset-disabled` (the key is always loaded); a token above the row refused; the natives re-publish the KeyPackage at the next landing / via the chat repository rather than as a numbered step; no DeviceGroup push binding is bound on any client after the re-founding; iOS probes the refresh at most once a minute on a DS `401`; without a pinned attest key web skips the local check on a JWKS fetch failure while iOS / Android fail closed; a discovery gap-lock deadlock on concurrent first publishes found and fixed. **Phase 2 as built:** the trigger is the fail-closed gate's `proof-absent` refusal plus the KT log's `is_reset` (nothing else sets state; the client never advances the pin itself); the contact is flagged (`keyState`, `previousKey`, `pendingKey`, found by account id across the key change) and re-verified out of band over the new key; every owner-or-admin observer proposes the old leaves out through the ADR-0045 path (re-proposed on chat open while the `applied` test says no), the ADR-0050 re-admission ADD branch is suppressed for a flagged contact, a one-to-one with the old identity closes (nothing sent, late rows dropped), an owned group gets the notice + Recreate group, which posts the one new wire artefact, `lakko-contacts::group_closed` (admitted only from an owner or admin at receipt, suppressed by the three background filters and the NSE), into the old group; the resetting device shows its orphaned channels. A second pass the same day aligned the clients (web vitest 4008, iOS 2640, Android 3051): the closer's account key stored with the closed state, the recreate sheet's partial-failure state on web, a closed one-to-one inert on all three, `Open contact` beside the refusal line. A third pass (web 4023, iOS 2649, Android 3066) put the keys-reset gate on every path that seals a contact request to a fetched or link-carried identity (invite accept and people search ran NO pin check on any client before it), made a recreate retry re-post the close notice instead of founding a second group, and ends a live one-to-one call when its chat closes (iOS, Android). Still open (the ADR's "Phase 2 as built"): accepting an INCOMING request from a flagged contact's new keys is ungated on web and Android; a `/c/` join to a flagged owner on web; the re-pin marker is `max(held, now)` not the generation. **Residuals:** old-identity leaves stay in a group the old identity owned (recreated, never evicted), in a group with no role table, and where no admin is the person's contact (**G-45**, narrowed); a stolen session could reset, strip the passkeys and sign out every device (**G-46**; closed for the session-only attacker on 2026-10-04 by the password-fresh grant, generalised on 2026-10-06 into one fresh-auth rule (password or passkey within ten minutes) covering the reset, account deletion, adding a device, adding a passkey and, client-side, changing the password, with a notice on the account's other devices when a device joins (UX section 17; server DB-tested, three clients unit-tested, nothing walked live); what remains is session + password, a grant lifted from memory within its ten minutes, and `DELETE /discovery/keypackage/device/{id}` not under the generation floor); password + TOTP survive; the access-TTL window at storage / discovery's ungated writes; owned channels orphaned (subscribers get no signal). Mints **C-71** (a key change at the directory is attested or proven). Amends ADR-0014 / 0017 / 0050 / 0053 / 0057 / 0072; `lakko-security` not yet run **Amended by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6 (2026-10-16):** the `KeyResetAttestation` is version 2, **145 bytes**, with no `device_group_journal_id` (auth would have to learn the seed-derived DeviceGroup id); the DS attested reset described above is removed, the reset clears the own predecessors and founds the DeviceGroup at the id the new seed derives with a plain claim. |
| [0074](docs/adr/0074-account-takeover-resistance.md) | **Account takeover resistance: recall after a reset, device removal that revokes, key rotation that keeps the other devices.** **Accepted 2026-10-07; T1-T4 BUILT 2026-10-07/08 (server + core + web / iOS / Android), unit-tested only (`services/auth` DB tests 150 + 2, `services/discovery` 111, web vitest 4234, iOS `LakkoTests` 2880 with 0 failures, Android 3265 on the host JVM without the native core); NOTHING walked on a device or against a server. T5 (rotation, core + server; auth 162 + 2, discovery 115, DS 87) and T6 (rotation on the three clients; web 4315, iOS 2976, Android 3370, unit tests only) BUILT 2026-10-08, Android aligned with iOS on 2026-10-11 (owned group journals, channel owners, reach keys and the backup are real owed steps; before that its rotation reported success without doing them). The mailbox rule was decided and built on the three clients the same day (ADR section 3.5a: senders resolve a proven predecessor to the current key before addressing, grants are kept per person, the rotating device re-sends its profile as an owed step, the old mailbox is read for 30 days and then abandoned; a second pass the same day added rule 5, what was granted before a rotation is spent (own `/i/` links revoked, unanswered contact requests re-sent from the new key), and rule 6, a refused write re-checks the address once, and aligned the clients; a third pass closed the remaining differences and the device paired after a rotation: the pairing state bundle is format 2 (own predecessor seeds with adoption times, contacts' proven predecessors), a sender never admits an own device bound to another own key, and the receiver re-mints while it holds no conversation; web 4444, iOS 3049, Android 3450, unit tests only). Open (the ADR's "3.5a as built"): `/c/` links minted before this build stay in the ledger, the address re-check needs the person's account id, a device the bundle never reaches stays out of the account's older chats, and two paths write a proven key change outside the ADR-0016 gate (iOS for an unpinned requester, the state bundle receiver). T8 (moving conversation leaves) is unbuilt.** UX spec [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md) section 18. Three scenarios with the phrase lost: one phone and a new one (add the device, no reset); one phone, stolen (reset; the hard case is a thief who knows only the password); several devices, one stolen (remove it). **Shared:** auth records `accounts.identity_pubkey` (set once at the genesis mint, which thereafter attests only that key; moved by reset / recall); a 138-byte client-signed **account statement** (`lakko-attest::account_statement`, kinds recall-status / recall / device-revoke / session-upgrade / recall-start, FFI sign on both surfaces); credentials are `v3` access / `r2` refresh carrying `device_id` (the conversation leaf signature key) and `issued_at` (flag day, `v2` / `r1` refused), with a per-(account, device) `not_before` floor (`device_session_floors`, migration `0030`). **Removal (T1/T2):** `POST /auth/session/logout`, `POST /auth/session/bind-device`, `POST /account/devices/revoke` (fresh-auth grant AND a statement signed by the account key, so a password alone cannot remote-wipe the owner's devices); auth answers a revoked device `401 device-revoked`, on which the device wipes itself with no button; discovery requires the token's device to equal the published device, tombstones deleted ids for 30 days, requires the statement to delete another device's row and puts that delete under the generation floor (closing G-46's second part); clients admit an own-account device into conversations only if it is in the DeviceGroup roster (before this a removed device could re-publish its KeyPackage and be re-added, and could delete the other devices' rows); the removal is one owed operation (revoke, directory delete, DeviceGroup remove, chats) with a `Finish removing` control when a passkey gesture is owed. **Recall (T3/T4):** before the first change of the password or the identity key under a given key, auth captures a **recall anchor** (that key + the password envelope in force; ordered, an older standing outranks a newer one; 30 days; migration `0031`); a superseded device that held the keys runs a **staged wipe** (everything except a recall record: account id, old seed, username) and shows "Was it you?"; anonymous `POST /account/recall/{status,start,finish}` take a statement signed by the anchor key (checked before any counter, one uniform `recall-refused`) and an OPAQUE login against the ANCHORED envelope (5 starts per 15 minutes, anchor destroyed after 20), then run the reset transaction with a new password, TOTP and passkeys cleared; `DELETE /account` is refused while a window is open (`409 recall-window-open`). The session middleware reads the generation BEFORE the device floor, so an attacker who reset cannot make the owner's devices destroy their recall record. **Stated costs:** between a hostile reset and the owner opening the app the attacker is the owner toward contacts; no device, or the window missed, means no recall; phone plus password is total loss. **Residuals:** storage and gif-proxy stay stateless (a revoked device keeps them for the access TTL); a session that never bound a device is not revocable per device; an account that never minted genesis has no recorded key (never anchored, and whoever holds a session first records theirs); a lost `recall/finish` answer is retried with the identical request only while the client still holds it in memory; a device paired after a rotation that the state bundle never reaches binds under the new key and stays out of the account's older chats; the attested DeviceGroup reset needs `root_endorsement_b64` (T5), sent by all three clients since T6. `lakko-mls` untouched. Amends ADR-0073; `lakko-security` not yet run **Amended by [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6 (2026-10-16):** the root endorsement (`root_endorsement_b64`, `signDsRootEndorsement`) and the attested root transfer are removed on all three clients and in the core; the DeviceGroup id is derived from the lineage seed (the oldest own predecessor), so a rotation keeps the journal and moves its root with a plain transfer. **Amended by section 3.7 and ADR-0075 section 3 (2026-11-01, found walking the base scenarios on iOS and Android):** a phrase saved by rotation left a restored device on a key no conversation knew, and a contact first met after a rotation pinned a key that said nothing about the leaf it talked to; the sender read Sent and the restored person received nothing. The backup (version 7) now carries the own predecessor seeds so a restored device binds under the conversation key, and a 201-byte two-way conversation link (`lakko-transparency::conversation_link`, signed by the conversation key AND the current key, opaque in `identity_bundles.conversation_link`) lets a contact record the conversation key as a proven predecessor; a supersession proof alone is signed by the old key only, so accepting it at first observation would let a directory attach a key of its choosing to a person. |
| [0075](docs/adr/0075-recovery-email.md) | **A recovery email, an account without a saved phrase, and taking an account back with the phrase.** **Accepted 2026-10-12 (the maintainer decided D1-D8, including an exception to invariant #2); core, `services/auth` (migration `0033`) and web / iOS / Android BUILT the same day, unit-tested only (`lakko-mnemonic` 43, auth 190 + 2, web 4921, iOS 3430, Android 3619 without the native core); NOTHING walked on a device or against a server, no letter sent through a real relay, letters English only.** UX spec [`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md`](docs/ux/identity-recovery.md) section 19. The phrase is no longer shown at sign-up (still generated; ADR-0017 unchanged). `services/auth` stores `email_key = keyed-BLAKE3(pepper, login_handle ‖ normalised address)` per account, never the address. **No phrase saved (mail-protected):** the server also holds an email vault (`lakko_mnemonic::vault::{seal,open}_entropy_under_email`, `emailVaultSeal` / `emailVaultOpen` on both FFI surfaces), the 16-byte phrase entropy sealed under `Argon2id(login_handle ‖ address, server-held salt)`, released only after a mailed code (and the TOTP code when enabled); the client opens it and runs the existing phrase restore, so the code brings back password, keys, contacts and chats. **The stated exception to invariant #2:** the operator sees the address when sending mail and can open the vault; so can whoever controls the mailbox and knows the username. **Phrase saved:** saving a phrase is the ADR-0074 key rotation to a new phrase followed by deleting the vault (an earlier copy would otherwise stay valid); a mailed code then replaces the password envelope only (recall anchor captured first, no fresh-auth grant), and ADR-0074 recall gains a phrase arm: the anchor keeps the recovery envelope and the phrase holder takes the account back from any device for 30 days. Adding an email may be skipped with a warning. The account's devices learn of these events through a server notices table. Rejected: a self-mailed recovery file, mail as a notification channel, a reset-only code, an OPRF vault key, keeping the keys when a phrase is saved. As built: sign-up offers email, a passkey or a phrase on one step; new words are shown only to a phrase-protected account; a rotation or reset drops the email vault, so a mail-protected account must type its address again; the account's mode is `accounts.phrase_saved`; accepted cost: the old seed alone recalls a phrase-protected account **Amended 2026-11-01 (maintainer), section 3:** saving a phrase changes keys only when the current phrase was ever sealed in an email vault (`accounts.phrase_in_vault`, set on the vault write, cleared only by a rotation, reset or recall). Otherwise the client shows the words it already holds and calls `POST /account/phrase/saved` (fresh-auth grant, `409 phrase-save-needs-rotation`); `GET /account/phrase` answers `{saved, rotation_needed}` on a server with no mail too |
| [0076](docs/adr/0076-username-change.md) | **Choosing a username later, and changing it.** **Accepted 2026-10-13; core, `services/discovery` (migration `0028`), `services/auth` and web / iOS / Android BUILT the same day, unit-tested only (`lakko-sync` 96, auth 196 + 2, discovery 121, web 4999, iOS 3468, Android 3662 without the native core); NOTHING walked.** A guest (ADR-0032 call-link account: no username, a random password) claims a name and sets its first password on one screen, with today's sign-up routes. A rename: `POST /discovery/username/replace` retires the old name (never answered by lookup or search, unclaimable by others for 30 days, then pruned) and is refused within 30 days of the last one; `register` refuses a second current name; `PUT /auth/login-handle` needs the fresh-auth grant for a CHANGE and can re-key the ADR-0075 recovery email from `previous_handle` to the new handle without a letter; `POST /auth/login-handle/check` lets a session ask `same` / `other` / `none`, and every client binds only on `none`, so a stale device no longer reverts a rename. The username travels between the account's devices as a `lakko-sync` register (tag 12, envelope `lakko/sync/username/v1`); a device seeds an empty register only after auth confirmed its name's handle. A rename removes the account from Everyone on the server until the client re-publishes. The username certificate format is unchanged: a copy of the old name's certificate verifies forever (residual). Open: a rename interrupted between the two servers is rolled back only at the next run holding a fresh-auth grant. **Merged with main 2026-10-14:** the guest claim is one flow with main's ADR-0032 guest lifecycle (question after the call, `No, thanks` deletes, 23 hours), the step is this ADR's (name and first password, then the ADR-0075 protect step, no phrase shown), the other claim entry points are removed; `DELETE /account` needs no fresh-auth grant for an account with no login handle and no passkey, and `DELETE /auth/login-handle` needs the grant once the account is older than the unclaimed lifetime |
| [0077](docs/adr/0077-account-binding-and-store-gate.md) | **Work started for one account never acts for another; a wiped account's stores take no late write; a refused persist publishes nothing.** **Accepted 2026-10-14; decisions 2 and 3 by the maintainer 2026-10-13; built on web, iOS and Android (`180602c1` to `3af913ad`), host-tested, not walked live.** An epoch per binding owner advanced at every landing and sign-out; work captures it before its first wait and stops (as a cancellation) when it moved (web `binding-epoch.ts` `SessionBinding`, iOS `BindingEpoch.swift`, Android `BindingEpoch.kt` with `takeOver`). A per-account store gate (iOS and Android read/write locks, web a synchronous check before each IndexedDB `readwrite` transaction plus a `Storage.setItem` wrapper and `account-memory.ts`): Forget device seals first and waits for writes in flight; the MLS snapshot, commit outbox and DeviceGroup snapshot throw when sealed. | [0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) | **Lakko's servers cannot link accounts.** **Proposed 2026-10-14; seven of eight decision points decided by the maintainer 2026-10-15** (one issuer key family and a quota for call admission, a few seconds of Welcome delay plus dummy Welcomes, one reach key per class rotated on leave, log enforcement by a source lint and a runtime capture test, ADR-0055 kept with its per-connection journal set as a class (c) residual, a per-period compact filter of KT reset entries instead of the per-account identity sweep, sections 1-11 as a release gate). Open: the backup must stay available after the phrase is lost (maintainer requirement; six candidate factors rejected). C-75. The reply-key pool moved to phase 5. **Phase 2 (calls admitted anonymously) built 2026-10-16** in the core, `services/auth` (migrations `0034` spent set, `0035` drops the ADR-0033 tables), `services/sfu`, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, host-tested only, not deployed, no call placed: `POST /call/jwt` spends one `call-join` token (wire byte 12) and mints a token naming `room = BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/sfu/room/v1", call_id)` and a random `peer_id`, no `sub`, no `call_id` (closed claim set); a TURN credential with a random username tag rides the same answer and the bearer TURN route is gone; auth caps admissions per room in memory; the SFU keys on the peer id and refuses a second live socket for it; `GET /v1/rooms/{room}` answers with no token and `GET /v1/calls/{id}` is gone; a peer is named by a signed announcement sealed under the call seed's relay key (`lakko_calls::relay`, fuzz `relay_open`), which also carries the camera media state. C-76. **Phase 3 (push) built 2026-10-16** in `lakko-contacts::push_wake` (sealed wake, sealed subscription, golden-pinned derivations, fuzz `push_wake_open`), `services/push-gw` (migration `0006`, first-boot X25519 key in its DB, `GET /push/wake-key`, `POST /push/{subscribe,unsubscribe}`, nonce dedup, per-group cap), `services/ds` (migration `0011`, push binding removed), auth (`push-register` quota 400), both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android incl. their background paths; host-tested only, not deployed. Residuals: class (b) co-membership at a live push-gw; DS + push-gw timing link; an evicted member signs wakes until the next rotation; an operator who is a member reads that group's subscription tokens. C-77. **Phase 6 built 2026-10-16** (ADR section 6 "Phase 6 as built"; C-79): DeviceGroup id `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/device-group/id/v2", lineage account seed)`, PublisherGroup id `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/pubgrp/id/v3", owner account seed ‖ channel id)`, `channel_id_from_group_id` removed, the co-publisher link carries the channel id in its sealed preview, the key-reset attestation lost its journal id (145 bytes) and with it the DS attested reset and the ADR-0074 root endorsement; proptests show neither id is computable from the account id or channel id; host-tested only. Built with it: G-50 closed in code on web / iOS / Android (C-82), and the iOS channel PublisherGroup made durable (`ChannelPublisherIdentity`), which it never was. **Phase 8 (tokens, reach keys, ledgers, logs) built 2026-10-16**, host-tested only, not deployed: token pools refill on a randomised schedule (first top-up within 20 s of a session, then every 30-90 min per context, to about a day of use) and a take never issues; `group` / `member` / `channel` reach keys are one per class per account (`lakko_contacts::reach` refuses a scope on them), rotated on a shrink, on leaving a group or channel and on a setting change, the profile republished in every group; `services/discovery` migration `0031` recreates the three invite ledgers with a per-link `revocation_pk` and no owner, issuer, label or bootstrap column, `POST /discovery/invite/by-owner` and `signOwnerListQuery` are gone (`linkRevocationPublicKey` new on both FFI surfaces), each client lists and revokes its links from its own records and revokes them before deleting the account; new crate `lakko-log-hygiene` (route-template request spans in every service, a source lint over every service, discovery and DS capture tests, all in CI). C-78. Phases 4, 5 and 7 (knocks, Welcome timing and the reply-key pool, the discovery sweep) unbuilt |
| [0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md) | **An invite link makes two people contacts.** **Accepted 2026-10-16; built the same day in `lakko-contacts`, `lakko-sync`, both FFI surfaces, `services/discovery` (migration `0032`, the `/i/` column widened to 693) and web / iOS / Android; host-tested only, not walked.** UX spec [`docs/ux/contact-invite-link.md`](docs/ux/contact-invite-link.md). The `/i/` capability carries a signed inviter preview (display name, username; version 3, `MAX_SEALED_LEN` 693); a contact request carries a link answer `link_id || link_secret` inside its sealed plaintext (version 5); the account's links are a synced register (`lakko-sync::synced::link_record`, DeviceGroup service envelope `lakko/sync/link-record/v1\0`, persisted state version 3, revocation final by ordering revoked first). The inviter's device accepts a request through a live link of its own with no card, by the one verdict `SyncedState::link_answer_verdict` (constant-time secret compare); a dead link gives an ordinary card with a note. The invited person has the inviter as a contact at once (Contacts, All chats, a text-only pending chat whose messages are sent in order when the inviter's Welcome lands; calls disabled). Links are listed and revoked from any device; the minting device deletes the ledger row on seeing a revocation. C-83. **Reworked 2026-10-17 (the maintainer rejected five things; core, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, host-tested only, not walked):** no timer: a request carrying a link answer stays undecided in the device's stored inbox until the synced state is held, then is decided; the dead-link note is stored with the request; only one of the inviter's devices answers, by claim-then-build on the account's DeviceGroup journal (`lakko-contacts::link_claim`, envelope `lakko/link-claim/v1\0`, 69 bytes, its own dispatch kind, `link_claim_outcome`: the earliest claim for `link_id ‖ requester_account_key` from a device in the current DeviceGroup roster wins and the device whose leaf posted it builds; the client feeds each claim's DS message id and its author leaf key, resolved from the MLS sender at receipt (C-68); a device recognises its own claim rows by the sealed bytes it recorded before the publish, so a crash between the publish and the recording of its id loses nothing; a removed device's claim is passed over, and a loser keeps the request unshown until the register says it was answered, re-checking against the roster; a won claim is durable and retried); the link record carries the ledger row's revocation seed (link-record envelope version byte `0x02`, persisted state version 4), so any device that revokes deletes the row at once; one held-send queue for messages to a pending contact on all three (Android's and web's queues inside the pending record removed); the signed-out invite page on iOS and Android. The build is ordered so a removed winner is never followed by a second one-to-one and a requester never goes without the Welcome: the winner founds in ONE commit with every other own device, records the founding, and publishes a link-founded note (`lakko-contacts::link_founded`: request key, group id, founding Welcome bounded at 32 768 bytes, a maximal one-to-one Welcome measured at 7 846) into its own DeviceGroup BEFORE any Welcome leaves, then writes the own account mailbox first and the requester's after (also the order of the ordinary `startGroupWith`); every device keeps the earliest note per request from a roster-resolved sender, and a device that holds one never founds for that request, it writes the note's Welcome to the requester and records the use; a retry resumes the recorded founding (one order per client, `runLinkBuild` / `runBuild`, unit-tested). The mailbox catch-up and the store of joined Welcomes of the previous round are removed. A DeviceGroup message that lands after a concurrent DeviceGroup commit is unreadable to the other devices (no past epochs kept, `lakko-mls` untouched), so a claim or note row **counts only if the epoch it was sealed at equals the journal's epoch at its row** (the readability rule, `lakko-contacts::journal_epoch`, fed every row by each client and kept per DeviceGroup next to the claim record): a straddled claim never wins, for its author too (claimed again), and no Welcome is written until the founder's note counts (posted again). Residuals: a forked DeviceGroup (two of the account's devices committing at one epoch, both taking effect) is not covered, nor an own claim or note published before an own commit already merged when a device read its very first batch; no client offers B a control to delete a pending contact. **Amended 2026-10-19 (maintainer; core, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, host-tested only, not walked):** the moment B pressed Add decides: the verdict judges expiry at the request's DS server stamp (`created_at`, kept with the undecided request across a restart; unparseable = dead) and revocation and uses now (`LinkRecordEntry::counts_for_request`, `link_answer_verdict(..., pressed_at_ms)`), and a device holding no record of the link answers the new `Unknown` and waits; a revocation wins until A's app answered (a won claim with no recorded founding and no held note refuses on a dead link); a request on a link that does not count shows no card (the dead-link card, its note and the "no longer works" page are removed) and A sends B a signed **link refusal** (`lakko-contacts::link_refusal`, 135 bytes: `lakko/link-refusal/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ link_id ‖ requester_account_key ‖ Ed25519 sig by A's account key`, mailbox kind `LinkRefusal` 5, through the request's reply grant), which B verifies against the pending contact's stated key and stored link id before removing the pending contact and its held queue silently (no delete button); a signed-out visitor sees no inviter card, only sign-in, and the link is checked again after landing; a dead link opens Contacts; Add writes no pending contact before the ledger consume answered live (`invite-add.ts` / `InviteAddOrder`). Since the same day every link has a ledger row (the inline form is removed, ADR-0028 head note of 2026-10-19), so Add always consumes; no server change **Amended 2026-10-24 (maintainer; web live-tested on the local stack):** Open chat on the "already in your contacts" page sends the link answer; a request from a person the inviter's device holds a live one-to-one with takes the `restore` step (re-add with that one-to-one, record a live link's use, found nothing); a removed person's card offers Add to contacts (`lakko_ui_rules::info` `is_contact` + `InfoSection::AddContact`) |
| [0080](docs/adr/0080-device-cards.md) | **Device cards: each device tells the account's other devices what it is.** **Accepted 2026-10-16; core, both FFI surfaces, the NSE surface, `services/discovery` and web / iOS / Android built the same day, host-tested only, not walked live, not deployed.** UX spec [`docs/ux/second-device.md`](docs/ux/second-device.md) section 3. `lakko-contacts::device_card`: a bounded fail-closed envelope `lakko/device-card/v1\0 ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms ‖ last_active_ms ‖ platform ‖ model ‖ app_version ‖ os ‖ city ‖ country` (closed platform set; model / os / city <= 64 bytes, app version <= 32, country empty or two ASCII capitals; no control, line-break or format character; compose refuses what decode refuses; `DeviceCard::normalized` cleans OS-supplied text), `IncomingEnvelope::DeviceCard`, fuzz `device_card_decode`. Sent only as a DeviceGroup `ServiceEnvelope`; a receiver keys it on the sender's leaf signature key resolved at receipt (C-68), keeps a strictly newer `updated_at_ms`, drops it when the leaf leaves. A device sends after the person's action when its last card is 30 minutes old, on a field change, and re-sends its stored card within 30 s of DeviceGroup growth; never on a timer or in the background. City from anonymous `GET /discovery/locate` (DB-IP City Lite via `maxminddb`, `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_CITY_DB`; per-IP bucket; stores and logs nothing), not asked under anti-censorship or with Settings > Privacy "Show my city to my other devices" off (per device, not synced). Devices screen: this device on top marked online, others by last activity, Remove with the new copy, Remove all other devices over the ADR-0074 removal with one fresh-auth proof. No `lakko-mls` change. C-84 |
| [0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md) | **A DeviceGroup commit cannot strand the account.** **Accepted 2026-10-18; built the same day in `services/ds` (migration `0013`), `lakko-ds-proof`, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android; host-tested only (DS DB-gated tests, `lakko-mls` tests, web vitest, Android unit tests; iOS compiled with its tests, not run), not walked live, not deployed. C-80; residual G-52. A `lakko-mls` change under invariant #3 (staged direct commits, below), so the G-39 surface grows.** A reconnaissance found no DeviceGroup fork (the DS accepts one Append per epoch and every client drops its commit on `409`), but a registration whose commit never reaches the journal (a crash before the persist, a failed publish with no DeviceGroup outbox, persist errors swallowed on web and Android) leaves a root-registered key nobody can replace, and the "fork verdict" repair then re-founds and resets on every other device, splitting the account. Decision: `POST /ds/group/{hex}/commit` registers the next epoch and appends its commit row in one transaction (row hash kept with the registration, an identical re-send answers the original id, the DS still never parses the row), used for every DeviceGroup commit and, by the maintainer's choice, for every ordinary-group commit too (ADR-0045 sweep and direct commits through the ADR-0040 outbox); a DeviceGroup commit outbox on all three clients (the N+1 snapshot beside the current one until the DS answers); fail-closed DeviceGroup snapshot writes; a device that lost or fell behind its DeviceGroup rejoins by a fresh KeyPackage posted to the DeviceGroup knock and admitted by a sibling after the binding, directory and tombstone checks, re-founding only when the account has no other device; the re-found-and-reset repair is removed. No `lakko-mls` change. **Open question 1 decided 2026-10-18 (maintainer): the DS refuses a plain `/access` Append for every journal** (`400 append-needs-commit`); claim, Replace, registrar set and root transfer stay on their routes, and `register_epoch` is removed from both FFI surfaces. **As built (ADR section "As built"):** the DS recognises an identical re-send before the write tier under any retained epoch key and decides a `409` before the write tier; a DeviceGroup snapshot write may name the generation it read and is refused when another handle wrote since (beyond the ADR: the long-lived sync handle must not put N back over a commit's N+1); ordinary-group direct commits are STAGED (maintainer, same day: sending first reopened the G-35 brick): `lakko-mls` `stage_add_members` / `stage_remove_member` / `stage_set_group_roles` hold the commit pending at N on the sweep's lifecycle, the ADR-0040 entry and the snapshot carrying it are written fail-closed, the commit route is called, and only an answer merges (a `409` or a definitive refusal abandons per group and drops the entry; an unanswered commit is re-sent by the owed verdict, at every poll on web and iOS, at chat open and after membership actions on Android), as the staged sweep does; PublisherGroup commits use the route and the record keeps the epoch-N access seed (`PublisherGroup.dsAccessSeed`, `DsProofSigner.forGroup`), so a replay always learns its answer; a lost journal step sends no Welcome; a rotation whose journal step got no answer keeps its entry for that step while the reseal runs; "lost" = no, undecryptable or unrestorable snapshot while the directory lists another own device, "behind" = a drained read with the DS `latest_epoch` above the handle's and no own commit owed (no grace); the rejoin knock is the ADR-0056 pairing payload, a sibling admits by the listed-and-bound directory check, a durable admission record and lowest-other-leaf (fallback 10 min; re-knock 30 min; all chosen). Residual (G-52): under the attested-removal policy a present previous leaf waits for a person. **Amendment 2026-10-19 (maintainer decision of 2026-10-18), closes G-52, C-85; built in `lakko-mls`, the pairing proto, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, host-tested only, not walked, not deployed; a `lakko-mls` change under invariant #3, so the G-39 surface grows:** the ADR-0015 gate also accepts a removal covered by a **self-removal proof** (`lakko-mls` `self_removal.rs`, 137 bytes: an Ed25519 signature by the removed leaf's own key over the DeviceGroup id, the epoch the commit leaves, the leaf key and the `KeyPackageRef` of the replacing KeyPackage), honoured only when the commit leaves that epoch and adds that KeyPackage; it rides in the envelope's attestation slot (121 vs 137 bytes, distinct domains, each entry tried as either), so no envelope or `processIncomingVerifyingRemovals` change. The epoch is beyond the brief: the replacement keeps the leaf key, so a proof without it let a member later remove the rejoined device by re-adding the stale KeyPackage (OpenMLS builds that commit). The rejoining device probes the knock for the latest epoch (an empty knock, one `ds-knock` token), signs (`signDeviceGroupSelfRemoval`) and knocks with `PairingQr` field 16 (`buildRejoinKnock` / `encodeRejoinKnock`); the sibling checks it (`deviceGroupSelfRemovalCovers`, again at the commit's epoch inside the outbox build), detects an answered knock by the KeyPackage's leaf (`containsLeafOfKeyPackage`) and commits ONE remove-and-add (`DeviceGroup::replace_device_at`, kind `rejoin-replace`) with the proof in the envelope, or a plain add when no previous leaf is there. A stale proof waits for the next re-knock. A device that no longer holds its old leaf key (a reinstall) is only added; its old leaf waits for the passkey flow, by design **Second amendment 2026-10-19 (maintainer, option (b)), C-86; built in `lakko-mls`, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, host-tested only, not walked, not deployed; a `lakko-mls` change under invariant #3:** a check before deleting the ordinary-group direct-commit paths found a table-less ordinary group was obtainable (`join_from_welcome` accepted a Welcome with no role table, and no client checked after joining). Now `join_from_welcome` refuses a Welcome without the `0xFF02` role table (typed `NoRoleTable`) or without a readable `0xFF03` kind, before anything is written; PublisherGroup (co-publisher join, now also checks the group id) and DeviceGroup (incl. the by-name pairing join) have their own entry points, moved to on all three clients; the mailbox intake had only skipped the co-publisher Welcome because it rides a `welcome-rendezvous` row, and the core now refuses it whatever row it came in. Then the direct add / remove / role-change paths, their staged send-and-verdict plumbing, the ADR-0040 F1 in-memory reset they needed (Android `runCrashSafeCommit` with it) and `publishRemoveThenRotate` (with its C-61 seam tests) were deleted on web, iOS and Android; each membership operation refuses a table-less group with the typed refusal (one test per client). Kept: the ADR-0045 proposal/sweep path, PublisherGroup commits, the DeviceGroup outbox, shared runners still used; the core's staged twins stay, uncalled **Third amendment 2026-11-11 (maintainer), a restored device does not wait for a device that is not there:** found on the test stack, a passkey restore whose only other device was gone knocked forever (the gone device stays in the directory), so the sync runtime never started and the username, pins and folders restored from the backup never showed. Now every running device long-polls its DeviceGroup knocks in the foreground and posts a presence note (`lakko_contacts::rejoin_present`, 25 bytes, `composeRejoinPresent` / `isRejoinPresent` on UniFFI and wasm) to the knock's rendezvous at once, then admits (no designated admitter, no 10-minute fallback; the DS commit route picks one commit); the restored device waits `max(4 x rtt, 1 s)` (chosen) for a note or a Welcome and otherwise founds the DeviceGroup; a device that comes back after that rejoins by knocking |
| [0082](docs/adr/0082-on-device-translation-and-transcription.md) | **Translation and transcription happen on the device that shows the message, or not at all.** **Accepted 2026-10-25 (the maintainer chose on-device only, no cloud path, and for v1: no sync between the account's devices, no sender-attached transcript, packs from the platform); built 2026-10-25 on branch `translation-adr-0082` in `lakko-ui-rules::translation`, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android, unit-tested only, no engine run anywhere, nothing walked. Android translates through the system `TranslationManager` (API 31+) where the system has an on-device translation service and through ML Kit Translate only where it has none (amended 2026-10-27; ML Kit reports the chosen languages to Google, accepted for the fallback, never while anti-censorship is on, a rule lifted 2026-11-19); Android has no language detection. Web residual: a browser with `processLocally` that ignores the track would listen to the microphone (ADR "As built").** UX spec [`docs/ux/translation-and-transcription.md`](docs/ux/translation-and-transcription.md). The device that shows a message translates its text or transcribes its voice / video audio after decryption, through a platform engine required to process locally (iOS `TranslationSession`, `SpeechAnalyzer` / `SFSpeechRecognizer` with `requiresOnDeviceRecognition`; Android `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer` after `isOnDeviceRecognitionAvailable`, never `createSpeechRecognizer`, ML Kit Translation; web `Translator` / `LanguageDetector` (desktop Chrome) and `SpeechRecognition` only with `processLocally = true`); no engine for a language = no control, no remote fallback, a test per client for each local-only assertion. No wire change: the recipient transcribes. Packs come from the platform vendor (the language is revealed to it, stated in the dialog), none while anti-censorship is armed (superseded 2026-11-19, maintainer: packs and the ML Kit fallback work with anti-censorship on, a blocked download shows the ordinary failure; the line and its catalog key are removed on every client). Results stored per device behind the ADR-0077 store gate, keyed on (conversation, message id, `last_op_id`, target language), not in backup / vault / DeviceGroup sync in v1; never in the NSE / background paths; no language or text in logs. Shared offer / bar / language-match rules planned in `lakko-ui-rules::translation`. Own bundled engines (Lakko-served packs, the ADR-0061 approach) kept as the later path, chiefly for the web. **Amended 2026-11-18 (maintainer):** each language uses the platform's on-device engine where it has one, else an on-device engine Lakko ships (unbuilt); the web client tries engines in that order per language (`apps/web/src/translation/engine-order.ts`, `device-engines.ts`), and the macOS desktop app (26.4+) uses Apple's `TranslationSession` / `SpeechAnalyzer` / `NLLanguageRecognizer` through the host helper `lakko-engines` (ADR-0085 section 16); a translation pack there is downloaded by macOS in System Settings, which the pack dialog opens; with anti-censorship on no pack was offered until 2026-11-19, when that rule was removed (the host's `packs-blocked` route is gone); Windows and Linux have none yet; host-tested only, not walked on screen |
| [0083](docs/adr/0083-screen-share.md) | **Screen sharing: one share per call, ordered the same on every device.** **Accepted 2026-10-25; built the same day in the core, both FFI surfaces, the SFU, web (sends and receives) and iOS / Android (receive only); host-tested only, never run in a browser, on a device or in a live call.** UX spec [`docs/ux/screen-share.md`](docs/ux/screen-share.md): sent from desktop browsers (and the future desktop client), never from iOS, Android or a phone browser; received and shown on every client; the shared tab or system sound when the browser offers it; one-to-one and group calls; a pin wins over a new share. A share state `lakko/call/screen-share/v1\0` (call id, peer id, `seq`, sharing / stopped, sound flag) signed by the sharer's MLS leaf key, carried sealed on the SFU relay in a group call and as `CallSignalingPayload.screen_share = 17` in a one-to-one call. One share at a time by a fold every device computes alike (C-87): a start takes `seq = max seen + 1`, a stop names its share's `seq`, the current share is the greatest live `(seq, signer_key)`, a superseded sharer stops its capture; written once in `lakko-calls::screen_share` on UniFFI and wasm. Group media: SFrame stream index 2 for the picture (reserved since ADR-0035) and new index 5 for its sound, `SdpOffer.screen_mids`, SFU role `Screen` forwarded always under `<peer>~screen`, allocated before cameras, `PublishBudget.screen_kbps`. One-to-one: further tracks in a `~screen` stream on the existing connection, sealed as the camera is there. iOS and Android receive only (their decryptors already read the KID); `call_stage` gains a sharing input; `Permissions-Policy` becomes `display-capture=(self)`. `lakko-security` pass due before merge |
| [0084](docs/adr/0084-profile-photo.md) | **The profile photo: an encrypted storage blob referenced from the profile.** **Accepted 2026-10-26 (maintainer: blob plus reference). Core (`lakko-contacts::profile` v4, flag day: v3 refused), both FFI surfaces, the NSE length constants and web / iOS / Android built 2026-10-26, host-tested only (unit suites and builds), never run in a browser, on a device or against a server; Android adds Media3 1.4.1 for the video re-encode.** UX spec [`docs/ux/profile-screen.md`](docs/ux/profile-screen.md) (Settings > Profile rebuilt around avatar, name and username edited in place, Save enabled only on a change, an in-app "Save changes?" dialog on leaving). The still (square JPEG <= 640 px, <= 256 KiB) and an optional profile video (square H.264 MP4, no audio, <= 480 px, <= 10 s, <= 4 MiB) are each `encrypt_attachment`-ed and `PUT /blob`; the profile carries `avatar_len(0|84|170) ‖ still_ref ‖ [video_ref ‖ duration_ms]`, each ref `blob_id ‖ key ‖ nonce ‖ size ‖ width ‖ height`, square and bounded, whole-envelope refusal; ordered by the profile stamp. Receivers download lazily, verify, cache per blob id; background paths never fetch. UniFFI `FfiProfile.avatar: FfiAvatar?` (default nil), wasm `AvatarRef` / `ProfileAvatar` + a last optional arg of `composeProfileEnvelope`. Residuals: storage can cluster a person's contacts by the addresses fetching one blob; ADR-0065 keep/delete not wired; no server change |
| [0085](docs/adr/0085-desktop-client.md) | **S4 built 2026-10-30 (ADR section 14): anti-censorship in the host (`tunnel.rs`, Settings > Security), the setting on means nothing goes direct, a build with no pin says so; the SFU signaling socket through the host over the app scheme (`platform/host-socket.ts`); host tests through a real Shadowsocks listener and the `obfs-proxy` relay; `obfs-proxy` runs on the test stack since that day (`just deploy-test-obfs`: REALITY 8444, Shadowsocks 8388, `/bootstrap.json`), the first server it ever ran on, and from a Mac over the public internet each transport reached auth and the SFU; no call has been placed through it. Through the tunnel every client is one address to per-address limits (not built). Four defects fixed in `lakko-transport` on the way, reaching iOS and Android: REALITY and Shadowsocks never read the signed `remote`, a dropped `recv` killed a Shadowsocks connection, a body above 16 KiB could not cross Shadowsocks, chunked responses were not decoded. `tools/bootstrap-config` is new (nothing could sign a config before).** **The rest of S3 and the Windows and Linux host were built 2026-10-30 (ADR section 13): withdrawing a delivered notification, Accept / Decline on a call notification, the microphone and camera refusal dialog, tray, autostart and the `keyring` key store; compiled for Linux and windows-gnu in a container, never run on either OS, nothing seen on screen on macOS; the Windows TPM path is not built.** **The desktop client is a Tauri host that keeps the keys and talks to the network; the web client's code runs inside it.** **Proposed 2026-10-16; only the S1 spike is built (macOS, `apps/desktop/src-tauri`, a workspace of its own): the app scheme serves the desktop-mode `apps/web` build and forwards `/api/*`; measured in WKWebView: secure context, a hidden window keeps timers (throttled) and requests running for the 120 s measured, `RTCRtpScriptTransform` attaches; no call placed, Windows and Linux not measured. S2 on macOS: every web device-key caller moved to `DeviceCipher` (`auth/device-cipher.ts`); the host derives the key with the web client's HKDF (pinned against WebCrypto), keeps it in the login keychain in debug builds and needs the signed build for the Secure Enclave path (an unsigned binary gets `-34018`); sign-in and a restart walked on this Mac against the test stack. S3 on macOS (ADR section 12, 2026-10-29): window, close-is-not-quit, Dock badge, start at login, menus, shortcuts, and system notifications through the host (`notify.rs`, `mac-notification-sys`; a message when a conversation's unread count rises, a ringing call behind the window, the device-added warning; a click opens the conversation); `just dev-desktop` runs a registered `Lakko.app` so macOS attributes them to Lakko; notifications not yet seen on screen, no withdraw on macOS.** UX spec [`docs/ux/desktop.md`](docs/ux/desktop.md).
