The maintainer chose Tauri 2 for macOS, Windows and Linux with the host doing the system work (option A of the ADR); proxying every wasm call over IPC and a Rust application layer were set aside, because the web client's logic is TypeScript calling wasm (97 files, about 285 exports, no facade) and call media must seal inside the webview. The host keeps the per-account device key in the OS key store (Keychain / TPM via CNG / Secret Service; the seam is `apps/web/src/auth/key-store.ts`), serves the bundled `apps/web` build from its own URI scheme and forwards every relative `/api/*` request (direct, or through `lakko-transport`'s tunnel when armed, never falling back), opens the SFU signaling WebSocket, and owns window, tray, badge, notifications, links, autostart and updates. The host never decrypts a message and holds no MLS state; ADR-0040 / ADR-0077 code runs where it runs today. Unverified and measured first (S1): JavaScript in a hidden window on each OS's webview, and media sealing APIs in WKWebView and WebKitGTK. Decided by the maintainer the same day (ADR section 9): the key never enters the webview (the host does the AES-GCM; the web client moves every device-key caller to an async `DeviceCipher`); hardware-backed where the machine has it (Secure Enclave on macOS, TPM on Windows), otherwise the OS software store as a recorded exception to invariant #7 (every Linux in v1), no store at all is refused; links by the `lakko://` scheme plus an in-app "Open a link", the link secret never on a command line; updates verified by `lakko-tuf` from the first version with our own per-OS install; an Apple Developer ID exists, no Windows Authenticode certificate. The proposed text of the invariant #7 exception is in the ADR and not yet in the invariant list. Amends ADR-0002 **S5 in part 2026-10-31 (macOS, not yet walked by hand):** file drop, image paste, the save dialog, `lakko://` links; on macOS in Chrome and Firefox the invite pages try the app by themselves once per link (Safari reports a missing app as an error, so it keeps the button). **Amended 2026-11-01 (maintainer):** a link is clicked, never copied. The "Open a link" item and its paste dialog are gone; links in messages are live on every client (`docs/ux/message-links.md`, `lakko_ui_rules::message_links`), and the host gained `POST open-url` for the system's default browser. The host takes the whole `lakko://` link on every OS, so on Windows and Linux the secret rides a command line (accepted, section 5), and takes this deployment's `https` link too, for the OS interception S5's signed, packaged build turns on. The TUF repository, install and packaging are not started **Collapsed call in its own window (2026-11-10, `docs/ux/calls.md` 4.5.4a):** in the desktop app a collapsed call is a frameless, always-on-top, transparent second window (`call_window.rs`, page `call-window.html`, 12-point corners drawn by the page) instead of the in-app floating window; the main page keeps the call's media, draws the shown picture into a canvas and relays JPEG frames (at most 10 a second, chosen) and the window's state through host memory (`/__host/call-window/*`, each route bound to one webview; the second window may reach nothing else); the main window's background throttling is disabled (macOS 14 and later only, for the whole run): measured 2026-11-10 on macOS 27.2 with the window minimised and no call, it changes no timer rate (a page timer fires once a second either way) but without it the page stops altogether about four minutes after it is hidden; the frame clock therefore runs in a worker (`desktop-call-window-ticker.ts`, about eight ticks a second measured hidden; a hidden `<video>` keeps decoding and drawing it reads about 6 new frames a second), the relay holds a Web Lock (Tauri's suggestion for Windows and Linux, no effect measured on macOS) and logs its frames per second every 10 s; built and unit-tested, launched without a call, never seen in a call, Windows and Linux not measured. **Translation and transcription on macOS (2026-11-18, section 16):** the host runs `lakko-engines` (`apps/desktop/macos-engines/main.swift`, Swift, built and bundled by `just dev-desktop`) as a child process over JSON lines (`src-tauri/src/engines.rs`, routes `/__host/engines/*`, page global `__LAKKO_SYSTEM_ENGINES__`), no `unsafe` in the host; text and audio stay in memory; the bundle gains `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription`; host-tested against the real helper, not walked on screen; **2026-11-19 (maintainer, ADR-0082 amendment):** the `packs-blocked` route and its refusal of a speech-pack install are removed, packs are offered with anti-censorship on |
| [0086](docs/adr/0086-circle-directories.md) | **Circle directories: finding people through contacts, groups and channels at any scale.** **Proposed 2026-10-30; the maintainer chose the mechanism; `lakko-circle`, both FFI surfaces and the DS routes are built, host-tested only, no client uses them.** To supersede the ADR-0060 section 4 card and bundle relay (390 MB to 8 GB a week per person). A person's contacts are found through one small encrypted directory that person keeps on the DS: a public header (epoch, one-way key tree over the contacts, chain of older epoch keys), a filter sealed under the epoch key, one numbered sealed row per contact. Every contact holds the epoch key, which opens every row, so a row carries a card the person it describes sealed under their search terms (`lakko_circle::card`, decided 2026-10-30 after rows sealed under the epoch key alone let any contact read a whole circle): a random card key XORed into 65 slots of 32 bytes, one per term key `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/circle/term-key/v1", oprf_output)`, every sealed card 2,893 bytes and a row 2,992, about 938 TB with 2 copies at a billion accounts of 150 contacts (arithmetic). Other phones hold a filter per contact, test it locally on a search, read one row on a hit (the read carries an ADR-0053 proof by the current epoch key) and open the card with the OPRF output of the term. A removal writes about log2 N wraps and registers a new read key: the removed person's reads are refused at once, no row moves, others pull 570 bytes at their next read at 150 contacts (954 at 10,000, measured). One reach key per contact replaces the shared `fof` key, so turning a contact off is one delete; the weekly rotation goes. Groups: a contact request goes inside the group, no `group` or `member` key to rotate. Channels: one card under a stable member key, no per-epoch re-upload, search covers 64 channels. A device keeps the filters of its 150 most used contacts current, the rest load in batches. Channels are off by default, groups on. Two release gates: reads go through an independent relay at another operator, and a short-lived anonymous credential replaces a held reach key, so a removed person keeps nothing that still works (its own ADR, not yet written). What remains of enumeration: a member with tokens can walk prefixes from 3 letters, bound by the `discovery-lookup` quota; a card once read stays read; the operator, who holds the OPRF key, is not bound once it has a member's epoch key. A row takes a sealed card only (`cardNotSealed`), so a client cannot put a plain card where every contact can read it |
| [0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) | **Every group and channel has an @name and is public or private; a channel is a conversation.** **Proposed 2026-10-31; the product decisions are the maintainer's, nothing is built.** UX spec [`docs/ux/group-and-channel-privacy.md`](docs/ux/group-and-channel-privacy.md). One namespace for people, groups and channels (`name_claims`, one OPRF input, `[a-z0-9_]{3,32}` on every name, `channel_names` and `blind_channel` go). A group or channel holds its name with a per-conversation handle key derived from the ADR-0057 journal root, naming no account; writes are signed statements, a 180-day lease replaces the account link for cleanup, ownership transfer rekeys through the root handover. A public one is published like a person in Everyone (the ADR-0060 sealed record and prefix terms, found by `@name` and by name); a private row reserves the name, holds no id and answers no lookup, and a refused claim reveals only that a name is held. A knock becomes a sealed KeyPackage with no epoch (one versioned knock key per group, ADR-0078 section 4 moved up), so a request survives commits; the DS caps a journal at 4,096 rows, the admin's cursor stops at the 64-entry retained cap. A public channel has one seed envelope per epoch sealed under a read key in its record, so a request is the subscribe, answered at once; a human approval at a million subscribers is 116 days, and the automated one 386 GB a day. A private channel admits a key only with a per-link invite key at the service and a sealed ticket the publisher checks, and moves to the ADR-0086 key tree before release. A channel shares a group's view and info blocks and is not a group (corrected by the maintainer 2026-11-01): the owner and co-publishers (on screen, publishers) post and moderate, everyone else comments and reacts, there are no calls, broadcasts are undesigned. The Manage page goes, the rotation and admin loops become app-wide, a post's plaintext is the chat envelope with ordinary attachments under a 45-day idle life, `listed` folds into public. Comments sit under a post in place; a subscriber's write names no key across posts: a per-post key from the account seed, an anonymous token per write, reaction labels the service cannot read or compare between posts, own reactions in a `lakko-sync` register; the service still sees counts per label (section 13a). The end of a membership, when no leaf of the account remains, wipes the conversation on every device through an owed record and a `lakko-sync` register, with a new `Group::delete`. A group may start with its creator alone. `lakko-mls` touched by `retain_join_request` and `Group::delete` (invariant #3, G-39 grows). **Amended 2026-11-08 (maintainer):** a new publisher reads earlier posts, the owner's device sealing it the newest 64 channel epoch seeds it holds (`lakko-contacts::channel_epoch_seeds`, core + FFI built, no client yet); a publisher request with no answer follows a group request (waiting line, then "No answer yet." with `Ask again` after 7 days, the same `/g/` link re-sent, never told of a refusal; UX 15.2.2); only the owner's device rotates the channel key, so a channel whose owner does not open Lakko does not rotate until the owner returns; a publisher that removes a subscriber asks the owner's device to rotate (`lakko-contacts::channel_rotate_request`), so that subscriber stops reading new posts once the owner's device next opens Lakko. **Amended 2026-11-12 (maintainer):** a device asks again after a day, not 7 days (groups and publisher requests); the page a group invite link opens has no `Request again` and opens the chat by itself when the join lands |
| [0088](docs/adr/0088-client-noise-suppression-rnnoise.md) | **Client-side noise suppression with RNNoise.** **Proposed 2026-11-07; the maintainer decided the feature and its switch (on by default) the same day; built on web and desktop, and on Android the same day; host-tested only, never run with a microphone, on a device or on a call; iOS not built.** UX spec [`docs/ux/calls.md`](docs/ux/calls.md) section 4.1.3 (`Sound` > `Noise suppression` in the call Settings sheet, per device, not synced, never sent, applies at once; if the suppressor cannot start the call goes on with the platform processing, nothing shown, one log line). Each client runs RNNoise on its own microphone after the platform echo canceller and before the encoder, so before SFrame; no wire, server or E2EE change (invariants #2 / #4 unaffected). One implementation: `nnnoiseless` 0.5.2 (the BSD-3-Clause Rust port of xiph/rnnoise, its original model, pinned exactly, default features off) in `crates/lakko-noise`, chosen over xiph's C library (emscripten and per-platform C builds) and over the npm packages `@jitsi/rnnoise-wasm` / `@shiguredo/rnnoise-wasm` (prebuilt binaries, web only). Web and desktop: an `AudioWorklet` running the import-free `lakko_noise.wasm` (`crates/lakko-noise-wasm`) in a 48 kHz `AudioContext`; the call publishes the raw track first and `replaceTrack`s the processed one onto the microphone sender (the SFrame transform stays on the sender); off, or any failure (no worklet, wasm, 48 kHz context, a suspended context), puts the raw track back. The mid-call microphone switch now asks for echo cancellation, noise suppression and gain control like the first capture. Android (ADR section 4a): libwebrtc's capture post-processing stage (`ExternalAudioProcessingFactory.setCapturePostProcessing` in the pinned AAR; read in the webrtc-sdk m125 source: after AEC / NS / AGC, full band, channel 0, FloatS16, 10 ms, under the SDK adapter's mutex), cleaned in place through `lakko_noise_*` in `crates/lakko-sframe-native` and JNI `noise_jni.cc` in `libsframe_jni.so` (hand-written JNI for the buffer address; UniFFI would copy every 10 ms); a fresh suppressor per call and per switch-on, freed only once the audio thread is out; a stage at any rate but 48 kHz mono is passed through, not resampled (amended 2026-11-10: a mono stage at 24, 16, 12 or 8 kHz is raised to 48 kHz, cleaned and lowered back, `process_s16_in_place_at` / `lakko_noise_process_s16_in_place_at_rate`; other rates still pass through); failure leaves the platform processing and logs one line (tag `CaptureNoise`); the switch heads the call Settings sheet (`SharedPreferences` `lakko_call_audio`); `CaptureNoiseProcessorTest` against a fake native suppressor. iOS: the maintainer chose (2026-11-09) to move from stasel/WebRTC 125.0.0 to webrtc-sdk 125.6422.07 (the Android vendor and version), whose `RTCDefaultAudioProcessingModule` takes a capture post-processing delegate, built the same day: the SFrame attach and the playout-delay slots re-read from the new binary's machine code and found identical, the new images pinned; host-tested, and on 2026-11-10 run in three self-only calls on an iPhone 16 Pro (iOS 27.2), every capture buffer through RNNoise (16 kHz raised to 48 kHz at first, then 48 kHz) once the call audio session was fixed (webrtc-sdk copies the shared session into its WebRTC configuration, so Lakko now states it in full; until then device calls had no audio at all). Quality, CPU, latency and WebKit support are open, to be measured. `lakko-noise-wasm` is a third crate outside `forbid(unsafe_code)` (its `#[no_mangle]` exports) |
| [0089](docs/adr/0089-camera-background.md) | **Camera background in calls: one segmentation model run by Lakko's own code.** **Accepted 2026-11-08; built the same day in `lakko-segment`, `lakko-segment-wasm`, the C ABI, both FFI surfaces and web / iOS / Android (one Rust rule also decides where a frame is placed in the model input, `model_input_rect`, after the clients disagreed); seen in Playwright Chromium and Firefox on a 1:1 call and on the Android emulator alone, iOS unit tests and the sheet in the simulator only; no device, no shipped browser, no person-cut quality or phone cost measured.** UX spec [`docs/ux/call-backgrounds.md`](docs/ux/call-backgrounds.md) (Settings sheet `Background` section, a preview that works with the camera off, failure turns the camera off). Model: MediaPipe selfie segmenter weights, landscape 144x256 (Apache-2.0 per Google's model card); the MediaPipe Tasks libraries are excluded (they send metrics to Google). Runtime `tract-onnx` in a new crate `lakko-segment` (inference, mask smoothing, and the frame gate: while a background is selected a frame it was not applied to is dropped, never sent), `lakko-segment-wasm` for web and desktop, `lakko_segment_*` C ABI in `lakko-sframe-native` for iOS and Android; blur and blend on each platform's GPU, applied before the camera copies (native `CapturerFan`, web one processed track the session clones) and before SFrame. Spike 2026-11-07 (scratch, inference only, M3 Pro, one thread): landscape native median about 3.6 ms, wasm `+simd128` under Node about 4.9 ms; `tract-tflite` cannot load Google's `.tflite` (custom op), so the ONNX file is a third-party conversion until a pinned conversion replaces it; wasm about 4.7 MB gzipped as built. Not measured on a phone or in a browser |
| [0090](docs/adr/0090-publisher-group-device-leaves.md) | **An owner or a publisher posts from any of their devices.** **Accepted 2026-11-13 (maintainer: a phone that restored the account alone takes publishing back by itself; only an owner device admits a co-publisher's new device; any owner device rotates, the DS takes one commit per epoch); S1-S4 built 2026-11-14 (core, `lakko-contacts` envelopes, `lakko-sync` registers, both FFI surfaces, web, iOS, Android; unit suites green: web vitest 8301, iOS `LakkoTests` 5303, Android 5695, DS 98); walked on web only: on `test.lakko.me` an owner's second web device was admitted by the first by itself (one staged commit through the DS commit route, reseal, Welcome), got a composer and posted, and the first received the post. Not walked: a native second device, decision A's re-founding, a co-publisher's device request, a rotation by a second owner device. `lakko-security` ran 2026-11-14 and asked for three changes, made on all three clients the same day (suites: web 8312, iOS 5309, Android 5702): an admission mark is taken only from the owner's account and counts only up to the subscriber rows this device has read; refused reads or a DS epoch never delete a PublisherGroup, they stop posting until a signed read at the held epoch succeeds again, and the group is replaced only before joining an owner-signed Welcome for the same id, restored if that join fails; Android's decision A reseals a private channel without an admission seed only to the account's own reader key.** Found on the walk and fixed on all three clients: the staged-rotation stage entry sent `subscriber_pubkey_hex` and standard base64 where the channels service requires `subscriber_id` and base64url without padding, so no rotation with subscribers had succeeded on any client since the staged rotation was built. Flag day: a PublisherGroup in the old account provider is not carried, so an existing owner's earlier channels are read-only on its own device, and such a channel shows neither a composer nor the waiting line (open, G-54). G-54 closed in code by it. Every PublisherGroup leaf becomes an ADR-0050 bound device identity (the group id stays the owner-seed derivation of ADR-0078 phase 6; `create` / `load` take the device identity and the owner's account seed; `open_or_create` deleted; `is_owned_by(owner_account_seed)`; at most 8 leaves per account; a peer gate refuses a bad binding, the ninth leaf and a commit whose committer is not the owner's account). An owner device adds the account's DeviceGroup devices that have no leaf and retires leaves whose device left; a co-publisher's publishing device asks the owner with a new `lakko-contacts::publisher_device_request` (admit with KeyPackage and committer delegations, or retire). Every PublisherGroup commit (rotate, remove a publisher, approve, admit, retire) is staged, sent through the ADR-0081 commit route and merged only on the DS's answer; the subscriber reseal follows the merge only. Any owner device rotates (random 0-60 s wait and a journal re-read first; an unfinished reseal is taken over after 5 minutes; both chosen); an admission re-keys subscribers like a rotation. The admission mark travels in a new `channel_admission_mark` envelope merged by maximum; the read key, admission seeds and newest 64 epoch seeds are re-sent to an added device's account. Signing seeds and publish counters stay per device (G-51). A device that founded the account's DeviceGroup alone re-founds each owned channel's PublisherGroup at the same id with a same-root journal reset (co-publishers invited again; a private channel's earlier subscribers are resealed from the owner's admission seeds, which ride the `lakko-sync` `channel_admission_seed` register keyed by channel, admission version and seed id, so two seeds of one version coexist; open question 4 closed). A co-publisher's device accepts a PublisherGroup Welcome only from a device of the owner account key recorded in its channel-subscription register (persisted synced state version 15); an old device that returns becomes a reader and is admitted. No server change. Invariant #3 change (G-39 grows), C-90 / C-91. Amends ADR-0087 sections 12 and 15 (dated notes) and ADR-0064 section 7 |
| [0091](docs/adr/0091-conversation-rejoin.md) | **A device that fell behind a conversation notices it and comes back.** **Proposed 2026-11-18 (direction and five decisions are the maintainer's: prevent first, then recover; composer hidden while out; no time limit; an own device in the chat, else an owner or admin, re-admits and sends the missed messages); phase 1 and phase 1b built the same day on `services/ds` and web (incl. desktop), iOS and Android, host-tested only; phase 2 S0 (spike), S1 (core) and S2 (UniFFI, wasm, NSE surface) built 2026-11-19, host-tested only, no client drives it yet (S3-S5 unbuilt).** UX spec [`docs/ux/conversation-rejoin.md`](docs/ux/conversation-rejoin.md). Found live: the desktop app failed one commit of a group on 2026-11-11, skipped it as every client skips an unappliable row, and could never read the group again while believing it was active. Phase 1: a failing row framed at the device's epoch that is a commit (`epochAfterMessage` = `epochOfMessage` + 1) while the same read's DS `latest_epoch` is above the device's epoch is held (cursor before it, retried after 2, 5, 15, 30, 60, 120, 300 s; after 8 attempts skipped and a behind mark kept), every other failing row is skipped as before; journal repair sends no Replace while a commit is held or the device is behind; every failed apply is logged on the three clients as one line with no identifiers, a held commit this device refuses as a divergence. No server or `lakko-mls` change in phase 1. **Phase 1b (open questions 6 and 7, maintainer approved):** a signed DS group read carries `commits: [{epoch, id}]`, every registration above the read's proof epoch that has a commit row (ADR-0081; at most 64, absent where `latest_epoch` is; `services/ds/src/journal.rs` `registered_commits_above`), and a failing row is held exactly when it is the registered commit leaving the device's epoch, so a commit-shaped row posted through the plain publish route is skipped at once; each client records with its conversation cursor the epoch of the snapshot written with it (web `chat/cursor-epoch.ts` in `localStorage`, iOS `PersistedGroup.cursorEpoch` in the message-log slot, Android `GroupSettingsStore`), and a group loaded below that epoch reads again from the commit leaving it (cursor never backward, at most 20 pages); the three DeviceGroup loops pin the persisted cursor before the registered commit leaving the handle's epoch (web `journal-batch.ts` rule 5, iOS and Android `FailedRowVerdict`); the ADR-0050 re-admission and DeviceGroup row lines no longer print a group id, row id or error text (web `chat/log-tag.ts`, iOS `ErrorLogTag.swift`, Android `ErrorLogTag.kt`). **Phase 2 S0-S2 (2026-11-19, ADR section "Phase 2 S0-S2, as built"; an invariant #3 change, G-39 grows):** S0 on OpenMLS 0.8.1: a KeyPackage extension `0xFF04` reaches every peer with the `Add` once the package's own leaf advertises it; a colliding `Add` is stored but no commit builds without its `Remove`; the pair builds in one sweep. `lakko-mls` `reentry.rs`: the marker (`version ‖ group_id ‖ epoch_left`, 41 bytes), `Identity::create_reentry_key_package` (same signature key and binding, last-resort, never published) and `forget_key_package`, and `reentry::judge`, used by the proposer's check, the receipt gate, the sweep (`sweep_keeps`, `selection_is_admissible`) and the peer commit gate: an `Add` colliding with a current leaf must carry the marker for this group and the current epoch and that leaf's account (`RefusalReason::ReentryPairInvalid`), a `Remove` refused only as `OwnerUnremovable` / `RemoveNotOwnerOrAdmin` / `OneToOneMembershipFixed` stands on a re-entry `Add` by the same sender, the sweep keeps the pair together or drops both; `group/reentry_join.rs`: `Group::reentry_request_target`, `propose_reentry_replacement` (Add then Remove), `join_reentry(welcome, identity, recorded)` (replaces the stale active group only for a recorded package, a newer epoch, same group and kind, a sender in the stored roster; refusals `ReentryWelcomeRefused`, nothing changes); `conversation_behind.rs`: `conversation_behind_verdict`, `reentry_admission_verdict`, `reentry_request_due`, `reentry_route` and the chosen numbers. `lakko-contacts`: `conversation_reentry` (82-4177 bytes, adds `gap_from_ms`, dispatch kind `ConversationReentry`, mailbox kind `0x06`, a knock row sealed under its own AAD domain so the DS sees a join-shaped row), `knock_key` (60 bytes, dispatch kind `KnockKey`, `knock_key_is_newer`). FFI: UniFFI `conversation_reentry.rs` + `Identity.createReentryKeyPackage` / `forgetKeyPackage`, `Group.reentryRequestTarget` / `proposeReentryReplacement` / `joinReentry`, appended `FfiError::MembershipReentryPairInvalid` / `MembershipReentryWelcomeRefused`, `FfiMailboxKind.conversationReentry`, dispatch kinds appended; wasm the same names (dispatch kinds 35, 36, `MailboxKind.ConversationReentry = 6`); NSE `isConversationReentryEnvelope` / `isKnockKeyEnvelope` and their lengths. Fuzz `lakko-mls/reentry_marker_decode`, `lakko-contacts/conversation_reentry_decode`, `knock_key_decode` (built, not run). Index entries C-92, C-93, G-58 |
| [0092](docs/adr/0092-attachment-kinds.md) | **Attachment kinds: one menu, nine kinds, and polls whose anonymity is proven.** **Accepted 2026-11-15 (maintainer decisions listed in the ADR); nothing built.** UX spec [`docs/ux/attachments.md`](docs/ux/attachments.md). The paperclip opens one `LakkoMenu` on web, desktop, iOS and Android (today: a bare file chooser on web, a two-row sheet on iOS, a stock Material `DropdownMenu` on Android): Photo or Video, Camera, File, Audio, Poll, Checklist, Location, Contact, Article; Photo or Video, File and Audio use the system pickers (no photo-library permission), the rest are Lakko screens; channel subscribers get no menu (comments take no attachments). New strict chat-envelope content kinds (`poll`, `checklist`, `location`, `contact_card`, `article`; poll and checklist bodies signed by the author's account key) and ops (`poll_vote_of`, `poll_close_of`, `check_of`, `check_add_of`), LWW in DS order, authority at receipt (C-68); `MediaKind::Audio` with title / artist; location as 1e-7 fixed point, no tiles, nothing sent before a tap, no live location; a contact card carries name, username and account key and no reach grant; an article is a closed binary block format (`lakko/article/v1\0`, bounded, canonical, fuzzed), rendered natively, never HTML; no video re-encoding in v1. **Anonymous group polls:** a ballot is a sealed non-MLS journal row proven by the shared epoch access key (ADR-0053), carrying a log-size linkable one-out-of-many proof (Groth-Kohlweiss / Triptych family; LSAG rejected for scale) and a tag over group and poll only, over a ring of every account that was a member while the poll was open (version 0 signed by the author, extensions derived from commits); posted after 0 to 10 s; new crate `lakko-ballot`, no `lakko-mls` change. A rotation never adds a second key of one person (leaves stay on the conversation key; ADR-0074 Stage B must post the two-way link before the Add); a client posts no ballot before its session landing. A newcomer gets every open poll and checklist from the admitting owner or admin (`open-items` hand-over) and can vote. **Channel polls:** one account, one vote by a keyed-verification anonymous credential issued by auth once per account and 30-day period against a stored commitment to a lineage-derived secret, presented to `services/channels` (which holds a derived issuer key) with a per-poll tag; new crate `lakko-anon-cred`, auth and channels routes and migrations; the service learns votes, voter counts and per-label counts, not accounts, options or close time; Sybil bounded by signup cost; a key reset gives one more vote. Both constructions join G-39. Open: own credential crate vs libsignal `zkgroup`; the ring-proof witness (for the review) **Merged with main 2026-11-18, numbers moved:** this ADR was numbered 0090 on its branch (main took 0090 for PublisherGroup device leaves); the own poll-choice register is `lakko-sync` tag 22 and the persisted synced state version 16 (main took 20, 21 and 13 to 15); the wasm dispatch kinds of `OpenItems` / `PollBallots` are 33 / 34 (main took 31 / 32); the history placeholder is version 4, carrying the audio tags per attachment, then main's version-3 card, reply and forward, then the content block; the four `FfiError` variants follow main's `RecoveryPayload` and `Donation`; web and iOS channel-post logs are versions 4 and 3, Android's 4 (content kept beside main's media references) |

The full strategic plan and trade-offs live in `~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md`.
The tactical decomposition of all 6 phases with sprint breakdowns lives in [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](docs/ROADMAP.md).
**What an ordinary user can actually rely on today lives in [`docs/PRODUCT-READINESS.md`](docs/PRODUCT-READINESS.md) (2026-09-17): it is the product-level truth and overrides any per-ADR "built end-to-end" claim in the table above; update it with every change that moves one of its rows, and never mark a row OK on the strength of an ADR.**
The Phase 3 closeout punch list — gap inventory + sprint waves with exit criteria — lives in [`docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md`](docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md).

## Module map

### `crates/` — Rust core

| Crate | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `lakko-core` | **An empty placeholder, not an umbrella.** `src/lib.rs` is twelve lines with no `pub use` and no `mod`, its `Cargo.toml` has no `[dependencies]` section at all, and nothing in `crates/`, `services/`, `apps/` or `tools/` names `lakko_core`. Reach for the individual crate; this row exists so nobody hunts for a re-export surface that was never built |
| `lakko-crypto` | HKDF-SHA-256, AEAD (`Algorithm::{ChaCha20Poly1305, Aes256Gcm}`), Argon2id, BLAKE3, X25519 agreement, Ed25519 signing — modules `aead`, `agreement`, `error`, `hash`, `kdf`, `random`, `secret`, `signing`. **HPKE and OPRF are NOT here** despite the crate's own Cargo description saying so (they never were — no commit ever added either): the RFC 9180 `hpke` crate lives only in `lakko-transport`'s ECH adapter, the OPRF (`voprf`) in `lakko-discovery-client` / `lakko-auth-client` / `services/discovery`, and the "HPKE seed envelope" of `lakko-channels` is a DHKEM-shaped construction composed **from** this crate's X25519 + HKDF + AEAD rather than an HPKE implementation |
| `lakko-mls` | OpenMLS wrapper, group state, exporters for calls. **Group-admin roles (ADR-0022)** — `group_roles.rs` (`RoleTable`: owner + admin set keyed on signature pubkey; bounded fail-closed `0xFF02` codec) + `Group::process_incoming_verifying_roles` (the peer-verified refuse-to-apply gate: committer via `ProcessedMessage::sender()`, pre-merge role read, fail-closed on an unauthorized add/remove/role-change) + `set_group_roles` (owner GCE commit) + `Group::create` seeds owner-at-creation; `lakko_leaf_capabilities` advertises both `0xFF01` (DeviceGroup) + `0xFF02` (roles). 8 role property tests + the `role_table_decode` fuzz target. **External-join approval (ADR-0027, G-3)** — `Incoming::ExternalJoinProposal { joiner_signature_key }` surfaces a non-member's external Add (extracted from its KeyPackage, refusing a non-Add / non-`NewMemberProposal` sender) into an in-memory per-group queue (cap + signature-key dedup); `approve_external_join` (role-gated owner/admin, reuses the audited `add_member` path → Commit + Welcome) / `reject_external_join` / `pending_external_joins`. **S7 (`576ea14`) converged it onto the sweep**: `Group::approve_external_join` now publishes an `Add` **proposal the approving admin authors itself**, carrying the retained KeyPackage — so it advances no epoch, returns no Welcome, leaves the ADR-0040 outbox entirely, and two admins approving two joiners against one epoch compose instead of forking. `refuse_if_proposals_pending` no longer reaches it (there is no direct commit left to guard), closing the user-visible dead end. The direct arm survives **only** as `pub(crate) approve_external_join_directly`, one caller, refusing any group that carries a role table — because `PublisherGroup` seeds none and the proposal path refuses a table-less group by construction, so the ADR-0028 OQ3 co-publisher approve keeps its synchronous epoch advance and its load-bearing reseal. The `pending_external_joins` quarantine is **NOT** retired, contrary to decision 4: it is what keeps a non-member out of the member proposal store, whose quota a `/g/` link-holder could otherwise fill to freeze the group (**C-66**). `pending_external_joins()` filters members, so an approved joiner retires itself from every admin's list. The Welcome moved to the sweep, which posts a `WelcomeRendezvous` for **every** added key because it cannot tell which came from a link — **C-69**. Joiner side: `Identity::create_external_join_proposal(group_id, epoch)` (`key_package.rs`) crafts the external `Add` proposal as a non-member (embeds a fresh KeyPackage whose private keys persist in its own provider). 12 external-join property tests (the original 6, plus 6 S7 added for the proposal path — the planted-stranger exclusion and its control, a stranger's own `Add` never entering the proposal store, the table-less group keeping the direct arm, the direct arm refusing a group that has a committer, and an approve no longer blocked by a pending proposal) incl. a full in-process e2e (`joiner_api_full_loop_*`: non-member proposes → admin approves → Welcome via `WelcomeRendezvous` → joiner `join_from_welcome` → member). **Proposal-based membership ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md), G-38 residual 1 — built end to end; **the residual is CLOSED for external joins too since S7**, so this parenthetical no longer reads "OPEN for external joins, since `approve_external_join` still eager-merges")** — `group_proposals.rs` is the new home of the **one** per-proposal rule: `ProposalKind` (`Add` / `Remove` / `Leave` / `Update` / `RoleChange` / `Unsupported`, the last always refused) + `classify` + **`authorise`**, written down once and consumed at **three** call sites so they cannot drift — the **receipt gate** inside `process_incoming_verifying_roles` (store an incoming proposal only if the *author's* role permits it, else drop it and surface `ProposalRecord { stored: false }`, which is the wire-visible form of a refusal since a proposal advances no state), the **sweep filter** (`CommitBuilder::build`'s closure; never `commit_to_pending_proposals`, which hardcodes `\|_\| true`), and the **peer-verified commit gate** (`authorise` over **every** proposal a received Commit carries, judged against the **pre-merge** table — the committer is not trusted). By-**reference** is judged against the proposer, by-**value** against the committer, so every direct-commit path is byte-for-byte its ADR-0022 self. Two corrections the build made to the ADR are recorded in the module docs: for a by-reference proposal the **receipt gate IS a boundary** (a peer that dropped it has nothing for OpenMLS's reference resolution to find → `InvalidCommit(MissingProposal)`, enforced by RFC 9420 itself), and the sweep filter's stated demotion race **is not reachable** (OpenMLS empties the store on every merged commit and the table can only change *by* a commit) so it is defence in depth over the store's other writers. Committer election: **`group_roles::committer_rank`** — rank 0 the owner, k+1 the k-th admin in the table's canonical order, keyed on **signature public key never leaf index**; a pure total function of the authenticated `0xFF02` extension, so no election message and no committer state. Proposer side: `propose_add_member` / `propose_add_member_from_wire` / `propose_remove_member` / `propose_group_roles` (each locally pre-authorised as a courtesy — the peers' gates are the enforcement), `pending_proposals`, `own_committer_rank`. Committer side: `commit_pending_proposals` (eager) and the **staged pair `stage_pending_proposals` / `has_staged_sweep` / `merge_staged_sweep` / `abandon_staged_sweep`** — the ADR-0045 rollback point, which is the **absence of the eager merge** rather than any retained snapshot: staging leaves the group at epoch N holding an un-merged commit so the caller can publish, read the DS journal verdict and only then merge or abandon. This is **ADR-0040's deferred "Option B" scoped to the sweep path only** (the remaining direct-commit paths still merge eagerly, so the outbox wraps both unchanged — after S7 that is `add_member` / `remove_member` / `set_group_roles` plus `self_update`, and the crate-private `approve_external_join_directly` reachable only through `PublisherGroup`), and the discard is per-group `clear_pending_commit`, not a whole-provider `restore_storage` that would roll sibling groups back. **A staged sweep freezes that group's own membership ops** (OpenMLS `is_operational()`) and is **durable**, so a caller must reach a verdict; incoming messages are unaffected. All three clients discharge that obligation the same way — every sweep adjudicates what it staged, an owed verdict is resolved at restart through ADR-0040's `sweepPendingCommits` seam, and the owed-verdict check runs **ahead of** the acting-committer rank check, since an admin can be demoted while holding one and gating the resolution behind the rank check would freeze that device permanently. Note the staged sweep also does **not** block a chat send (`create_message` gates on `is_active`, not `is_operational`), which is what makes an epoch straddle a certainty for the duration of a verdict read and why the clients serialize seal-and-publish under the per-group lock (**C-64**). **`create_application_message` parks the pending proposal set around the encrypt call** — OpenMLS refuses `create_message` while the proposal store is non-empty, so a stored proposal would otherwise mute the whole group's chat until the sweep; the set never leaves the OpenMLS storage provider (so `snapshot_storage` / `load` keep working unchanged) and a failed unpark beats a successful send. **Security-review hardening (2026-08-29, C-66/C-67/C-68)** — `authorise` now refuses `ProposalKind::Update` outright (it bypassed `owner_survives`, and openmls does not bind an Update's leaf node to the sender's existing key, so a member could rotate its own key out from under the role table; each also carries a fresh encryption key hence a distinct `ProposalRef`, which the dedup cannot collapse, so 64 filled the pending set). `sweep_keeps` grew from two clauses to five, enumerating fifteen `CommitBuilder::build` hard-failure conditions across three families — the single `GroupContextExtensions` slot (C-65), key uniqueness across signature / encryption / init keys, and the added leaf's admissibility to *this* group (capabilities, required capabilities, leaf extensions, credential types, **KeyPackage lifetime**) — because an unbuildable sweep is a **permanent, group-wide, invisible** membership freeze: the store empties only on a merged commit, the direct paths are refused while proposals pend, nothing discards, and `create_application_message` parks the set so chat keeps working over it. One proposal suffices, and the lifetime condition fires from **elapsed time alone** against a request decision 2 gives no deadline. Since fifteen conditions is a mirror of rules this crate does not own, `stage_pending_proposals` backs them with `narrow_sweep_by_exclusion` — additive selection plus one re-offer pass, bounded by `2 x candidates + 1` builds, which needs to know only *which* proposal is uncommittable and never *why*, and which immediately rescued a live unenumerated case (an admin proposing its own departure wedges its own sweep with `CannotRemoveSelf`). Each trial re-asks `owner_survives`, because dropping an `Add` can strand a role change naming that person owner — openmls builds it, every peer refuses it, trading a local freeze for a fork. And `pending_capacity` / `is_owner_response` admit a small reserve past `MAX_PENDING_PROPOSALS` to the **owner's** `Remove` or `RoleChange` only, shared by `authorise_own_proposal` and `gate_incoming_proposal` so the two cannot disagree — closing a lockout where a flooder took away the owner's means of response and the only exit committed the flood. **Automatic re-request (2026-09-01)** — the client half of the maintainer's rule *retry when a queue was full, never when the request was refused for its own state*. **The trigger is an observation, not a timer**, and that is the design: a queue drains when a committer sweeps rather than when time passes, so a schedule would fire uselessly or spin forever. All three clients re-send when the pending set is **strictly smaller** than at the refusal — a state their poll already reports. One rule, derived from the core's *definition* rather than from either reason's name: the set shrinks only when a commit **merges**, and `RetryLater` means *resolves once a committer has swept*, so a nineteenth `RetryLater` reason works with no edit here. No attempt cap — attempts are paid for by a sweep that was going to happen anyway, so an unbounded number cannot spin, while a cap would abandon a merely **busy** group; stopping is by **state** (membership ended, already applied, already pending, no role table). The record is in memory because its content is a comparison baseline and a count from a past session compares nothing. Firing is a lock-guarded take-and-remove that the **manual path also claims**, so a user tapping again disarms rather than races. Subjects keyed on the 32-byte signature key, never a leaf. **A fix this found, since closed end to end:** `approve_external_join` re-wrapped `propose_add_member`'s error in prose and destroyed the `RefusalReason` it had just produced, so an approve refused by a full queue reached every client with no reason at all and could never participate. The core now propagates the typed error and wraps only what carries none, and the client arm followed — **six kinds, not five**. Approving needed a third stopping condition the others do not (it opens with `role_table_or_refuse`, so a table-less group answers permanently and a retry would wait forever), carries **one** field as its descriptor because the joiner's signature key is both identity and handle, and delegates the not-retained rule to `externalJoinAffordance` rather than restating it. **Two in-memory lifetimes meet there and the mismatch is handled rather than prevented:** the quarantine lives on the `Group` while the retry record lives beside the chat, and the ADR-0040 crash-abort reset and the web rehydrate both rebuild the `Group` mid-session — so a record can outlive its request with no restart, and the verdict reads the live retained set and drops rather than firing an approve the core would refuse. **Typed refusal reasons (2026-09-01)** — `refusal.rs`: `RefusalReason` (18 fieldless variants) + `RefusalDisposition { RetryLater, DoNotRetry, NotPermitted }` + `Error::MembershipRefused { reason, detail }`, whose `Display` is byte-identical to the sentence the refusal always carried, so logs are unchanged and only the *reading* is new. Built because three clients had drifted three ways on one intended sentence — web surfaced the spec's string, iOS threw its own near-variant prose, Android surfaced the core diagnostic raw — and mapping core prose to fixed copy by substring is the drift class the UX spec flags. **The axis is three-valued and two would have been wrong:** under a two-valued availability/authorisation split `SweepAlreadyStaged` classifies as retryable while this crate's own docs say to reach the verdict rather than retry, so the type would have contradicted the crate. The cut is mechanical — **`RetryLater` is the disposition that resolves on its own**, the identical call succeeding once any committer sweeps with the caller doing nothing — and exactly two reasons qualify. **FFI shape forced by `flat_error`**, which lowers a variant as its ordinal plus `to_string()` and drops associated data: one flat `FfiError` variant per reason, **appended at the end** because uniffi decodes by ordinal and inserting mid-enum would make older bindings decode unrelated errors as the wrong variant. wasm throws a real `Error` carrying `refusalReason` / `refusalDisposition` as own properties, `.message` unchanged. `Incoming::ExternalJoinProposal` also gained **`retained`** — the quarantine used to drop a request at `MAX_PENDING_EXTERNAL_JOINS` while still firing the event, so an admin saw a pending request no approve could ever act on; reported as **data, not an error**, since erroring there would let a stranger fail a member's receive loop. **Membership state** — `Group::is_active()` (+ `isActive` on both FFI surfaces): OpenMLS marks a group `Inactive` when a commit removes your own leaf, persisting it **before** the merge, and documents it as a state the group cannot exit from. Reads still answer (`members`, `epoch`, `group_roles`) but are frozen; `create_message`, `export_secret` and — decisively — `unprotect_message` all refuse, the last **before it looks at the message and not epoch-scoped**, so nothing new can ever be decrypted, including old-epoch rows still unfetched on the DS. `epoch()` reports N+1 over epoch-N secrets, so a caller must consult this rather than reason from the epoch. Pinned by `tests/group_membership_state.rs`, whose property is that the accessor and the roster inference agree on every eviction path — not that the accessor is false, which would test OpenMLS. Deliberately **absent from `lakko-ffi-extension`** (see the ADR-0041 row: that process cannot persist a verdict, C-59). `DeviceGroup::is_active()` (+ `isActive` on both FFI surfaces, 2026-09-30) is the same read for the account's DeviceGroup: a device that applied its own removal shows "This device was removed" instead of polling a journal it can no longer read. **Ownership transfer (ADR-0022's deferred follow-up, built here)** — `RoleTable::with_owner_transferred` + `OutgoingOwner::{Admin, Member}` + `Group::transfer_ownership` / `propose_ownership_transfer`: deliberately **not a new commit type**, just the owner-only role-table rewrite with the owner slot moved, judged against the pre-merge table. Its security content is **`group_proposals::owner_survives`** (**C-63**) — the one rule that judges a **commit** rather than a proposal: a commit must not leave the group with an owner who is not a member of it, nor erase the role table. It closes a hole that predates the feature (`set_group_roles` could always name an owner key no leaf holds — permanently un-administrable) and one ADR-0045 created (a transfer composed with the new owner's removal). 37 proposal + 12 ownership-transfer property tests in those two binaries (22 proposal at the ownership sprint; S7 and the security review grew it), plus per-layer isolation lib tests. `group_admin_roles.rs` is **no longer unchanged** — the security review added an eighth. The crate runs 297 tests (measured 2026-09-26) across the lib (186) and **nine** integration binaries: `group_proposals` (37), `membership_refusal_reason` (21), `group_ownership_transfer` (12), `device_leaves` (12), `group_admin_roles` (8), `group_id_width` (8), `end_to_end` (5), `group_membership_state` (5), `external_join_persistence` (3 — the ADR-0027 retained queue surviving a restart). **On both FFI surfaces since `6c77cd6`:** the staged pair (`stagePendingProposals` / `hasStagedSweep` / `mergeStagedSweep` / `abandonStagedSweep`) and ownership transfer (`transferOwnership` / `proposeOwnershipTransfer` / `OutgoingOwner`), so a client can stage-and-adjudicate rather than only eager-merge. **Peer ranking landed in `94da492`** (this row previously read "Not exposed: the free `committer_rank`" — that is CLOSED): `Group.committerRank(signatureKey)` on **both** surfaces answers the peer-facing half `ownCommitterRank` could not, returning `Some(0)` for the owner, `Some(k+1)` for the k-th admin, and `None` for a plain member / non-member / wrong-length key / no role table. It is a **`Group` method, not a free function taking a caller-supplied table**, and both surfaces carry the reason as a doc heading — *rank the key when you take the verdict, not when the beacon arrived*: a rank is a **position** in the role table's canonical order, so promoting, demoting or removing any admin renumbers every admin sorting after it, and a rank captured under one table compared against a rank taken under another is not merely stale but a position in a **different ordering**. Callers therefore persist the sender's 32-byte **signature key** (never a leaf index — OpenMLS reuses a vacated leaf for the next Add) and rank it at fold time. This **orders** the authorised committers; it does not widen them. `undefined`/`None` and `0` are never interchangeable — wasm's doc says `if (rank)` is wrong in both directions. Verified by `peer_committer_rank_agrees_with_own_rank_from_every_vantage_point` + `committer_rank_never_conflates_absent_with_rank_zero`. **DS access ([ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md)), two read-only additions:** `Group::staged_export_secret` exports from the epoch a staged sweep moves to (OpenMLS's own staged-commit exporter), so a committer can register the next epoch's Delivery Service key before publishing; and `Group::process_knock` / `is_external_join_proposal_for` refuse a knock row unless it is a `PublicMessage` proposal from a `NewMemberProposal` sender framed for this group, before OpenMLS processes it. Pinned by `a_staged_sweep_exports_the_secret_of_the_epoch_it_moves_to` and `a_knock_row_is_processed_only_when_it_is_an_external_join_proposal`; on both FFI surfaces as `dsStagedAccessSigner` and `processKnock` (`Group` and `PublisherGroup`). A third, from the live walk: `Group::pending_external_join_account_key` names the account behind a pending external-join request from the retained KeyPackage's verified device binding, so an admin's pending list can show a contact by name instead of "Member" (the request is keyed on the device leaf); pinned by `a_pending_join_request_names_the_joiners_account`, on both FFI surfaces as `pendingExternalJoinAccountKey`. **Pairing payload v2 ([ADR-0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md), 2026-09-28):** `pairing::encode(login_handle, key_package, rendezvous)` / `decode(bytes, expected_login_handle)` bind the payload to the ADR-0054 S5 32-byte login handle instead of the account id (`PairingQr` field 5, field 2 reserved, `PAIRING_VERSION` 2 and nothing else accepted), compared in constant time through `lakko_crypto::secret::SecretBytes`; 11 tests incl. three proptests (round trip, any wrong handle refused, arbitrary bytes never accepted unbound); on UniFFI as `buildPairingQr(loginHandle, keyPackage, rendezvousPseudonym)` / `parsePairingQr(wire, expectedLoginHandle)`, on wasm as `encodePairingQr` / `decodePairingQr` with the same argument order. **Since 2026-10-29 (ADR-0056 and ADR-0080 amendments)** `encode` takes a fourth argument, the joining device's ADR-0080 device card envelope (`PairingQr` field 17, 0 to `PAIRING_DEVICE_CARD_MAX_LEN` = 270 bytes, bounded and never decoded here; empty on a rejoin knock), `DecodedPairingQr.device_card` returns it, and both FFI surfaces decode it for display only (`buildPairingQr(..., deviceCardEnvelope)` / `encodePairingQr(..., deviceCardEnvelope?)`, `DecodedPairingQr.deviceCard`, absent when undecodable). **The DeviceGroup self-removal proof ([ADR-0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md) amendment, 2026-10-19, C-85):** `self_removal.rs` (`SelfRemovalProof`, 137 bytes `version ‖ epoch ‖ removed_pubkey ‖ key_package_ref ‖ sig`, exact-length fail-closed decode; `sign_self_removal`, `covers`, `covers_key_package`, `key_package_ref`); `Group::process_incoming_gated` hands its gate a `GatedCommit` (removed leaves plus the added `KeyPackageRef`s) and `DeviceGroup::process_incoming_verifying_removals` accepts a removal covered by an attestation or by a proof for this group, the commit's pre-merge epoch, the removed leaf and an added KeyPackage; `Group::replace_member` / `DeviceGroup::replace_device_at` remove a leaf and add a KeyPackage in one commit with a Welcome; `contains_leaf_of_key_package`; the pairing payload carries the proof as field 16 (`encode_rejoin_knock`, decoded as 0 or 137 bytes, `DecodedPairingQr.self_removal_proof`). Tests in `device_group.rs` (13 self-removal tests incl. a proptest) and `self_removal.rs`; fuzz `self_removal_proof_decode`. On UniFFI and wasm as `signDeviceGroupSelfRemoval`, `deviceGroupSelfRemovalCovers`, `deviceGroupSelfRemovalProofLen`, `DeviceGroup.replaceDeviceAt` / `containsLeafOfKeyPackage`, `buildRejoinKnock` / `encodeRejoinKnock`. **Conversation kind ([ADR-0062](docs/adr/0062-conversation-kind.md), C-70):** `group_kind.rs` — `GroupKind { OneToOne, Group }` in GroupContext extension `0xFF03` (`version 0x01 ‖ kind`, bounded fail-closed), seeded by `Group::create(identity, kind)` beside `0xFF02` and in `RequiredCapabilities`, advertised in the leaf capabilities, read by `Group::group_kind()` (`None` only for `PublisherGroup` / `DeviceGroup`); `join_from_welcome` refuses a role-table Welcome with no readable kind (and, since 2026-10-19, any Welcome with no role table, C-86); `role_rewrite_extensions` carries the list verbatim through `set_group_roles` / `propose_group_roles` / both transfer paths, and `group_kind_unchanged` (peer commit gate) + `proposal_keeps_group_kind` (receipt, sweep) refuse a change (`GroupKindChanged`). **The one-to-one rule** is a second commit-wide rule beside `owner_survives`: `one_to_one_holds` — pre-merge accounts a subset of post-commit accounts, at most two, no GroupContext rewrite at all — at the peer commit gate, in the sweep (`selection_is_admissible`, narrowing before building) and on `add_members` / `remove_member`; `one_to_one_allows` is the early per-proposal refusal at the proposer and the receipt gate; approve of an external join into a one-to-one is refused and the knock surfaced `retained: false`. `RefusalReason` gains `OneToOneMembershipFixed` / `OneToOneHasNoRoles` (`NotPermitted`) and `GroupKindChanged` (`DoNotRetry`); a kindless Welcome is a plain `Error::Group`. New `one_to_one` integration binary (10 tests) and fuzz `group_kind_decode` (built, not run). On UniFFI and wasm as `GroupKind`, the `Group(identity, kind)` constructor and `groupKind()` **Group ids ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6, 2026-10-16, C-79):** `DeviceGroup::group_id_for(&account_seed)` = `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/device-group/id/v2", account_seed)` (`create_founder` / `load` take the seed, `load_by_group_id` opens a Welcome-joined group by its own id, `join_from_welcome` binds no id); `publisher_group_id_for(owner_account_seed, channel_id)` = `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/pubgrp/id/v3", seed ‖ channel_id)`, `PublisherGroup::load_joined(identity, group_id, channel_id)` for a co-publisher, `channel_id_from_group_id` removed; proptests that neither id is computable from the account id or channel id, goldens. **Staged direct commits ([ADR-0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md), 2026-10-18, an invariant-#3 change):** `stage_add_members` (and `_from_wire`), `stage_remove_member`, `stage_set_group_roles` build the same commit as their eager twins (same local refusals) and leave the group at epoch N holding it as pending, so the sweep's lifecycle (`has_staged_sweep`, `staged_export_secret`, `merge_staged_sweep`, `abandon_staged_sweep`, a per-group `clear_pending_commit`) serves both; while any commit is staged every membership operation, eager or staged, is refused with `SweepAlreadyStaged`. The eager paths stay for commits no Delivery Service decides on (founding, `PublisherGroup`). New binary `tests/staged_direct_commit.rs` (12 tests, one a proptest): merged equals eager, abandon leaves N and siblings untouched, a staged commit survives a snapshot/restore, a second stage is refused. On UniFFI (`stageAddMemberFromWire` / `stageAddMembersFromWire` / `stageRemoveMember` / `stageSetGroupRoles`) and wasm (`stageAddMember` / `stageAddMembers` / `stageRemoveMember` / `stageSetGroupRoles`), no new `FfiError` variant. **Welcome admission and the end of the direct paths ([ADR-0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md) second amendment, 2026-10-19, C-86; host-tested only):** `Group::join_from_welcome` admits a Welcome as a conversation only with the `0xFF02` role table (else the typed `NoRoleTable`) and a readable `0xFF03` kind, checked on OpenMLS's staged welcome before `into_group`, so a refused join leaves no group state and the KeyPackage usable (the ADR-0050 binding check moved there too); the table-less groups join through their own entry points, which refuse a conversation Welcome: `PublisherGroup::join_from_welcome(identity, welcome, group_id, channel_id)` (also refuses another group id) and `DeviceGroup::join_from_welcome` / `join_welcome_before_account` (the ADR-0056 by-name join); on UniFFI `publisherGroupJoinFromWelcome` / `deviceGroupJoinWelcomeByName`, on wasm `PublisherGroup.joinFromWelcome` / `DeviceGroup.joinWelcomeByName`; no new `FfiError` variant, the NSE surface has no join. The clients' direct add / remove / role-change paths for a table-less group are deleted (each now refuses it with the same typed refusal), so the staged twins `stage_add_members` / `stage_remove_member` / `stage_set_group_roles` and their FFI methods stay in the core with their tests and no client calls them **Rejoin ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) amendment of 2026-10-31, C-88; host-tested, no client wired):** a Welcome replaces an ended conversation and nothing else, `join_admitted` refuses a stored active group with `Error::GroupStillActive { group_id }` after every admission check, a refused join changes nothing, `PublisherGroup` and `DeviceGroup` replace nothing, and `Group::delete` (`deleteLocally` on UniFFI and wasm) removes an ended group's rows, the eviction's epoch key pairs and queued-proposal rows included; `Group::delete_after_ended_elsewhere(identity, ended_epoch)` (`deleteAfterEndedElsewhere`, ADR-0087 section 9) does the same for a device that heard of the end from its own account's `conversation_ended` register and never applied the commit, and refuses a stored group active above `ended_epoch`, which is one this device came back to **Removing a publisher ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 15, phase B, 2026-11-06; an invariant #3 change, host-tested):** `PublisherGroup::remove_publisher(owner, account_key)` removes every leaf of an account in ONE commit, merged at once (crate-private `Group::remove_leaves_directly`, refused on a role-table group), owner only (`is_owned_by`: the account whose seed derives the group id) and never the owner's own account; `PublisherGroup::step_down` (refused for the owner) is an application message `lakko/publisher-step-down/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ channel_id` (46 bytes, `compose_publisher_step_down` / `is_publisher_step_down` / `is_publisher_step_down_envelope`) the owner's device applies as `remove_publisher` of the sender's account, since OpenMLS refuses a self-removal commit and a table-less group stores no proposal; peers enforce neither rule. `PublisherGroup::members()` / `is_active()`. Binary `tests/publisher_group_removal.rs` (5 tests, two proptests: a removed publisher cannot derive the next epoch's seed; after a step-down the owner rotates). On UniFFI and wasm as `PublisherGroup.members` / `isOwnedBy` / `isActive` / `removePublisher(identity, accountKey)` / `stepDown(identity)` and free `isPublisherStepDown(plaintext, channelId)` / `isPublisherStepDownEnvelope` / `publisherStepDownLen` **Welcome founder peek (2026-11-07, ADR-0060 section 1 amendment; host-tested):** `Group::peek_welcome_founder(welcome, identity)` -> `WelcomeFounder { group_id, founder_account_key }` stages the Welcome as `join_admitted` does (shared `stage_welcome`), resolves the sender leaf to its ADR-0050 account key (typed `DeviceBindingInvalid` refusal otherwise) and writes nothing, so a later join of the same bytes works (the KeyPackage is last-resort); runs no admission check. `tests/welcome_founder.rs` (two proptests and one test). On UniFFI `peekWelcomeFounder(welcome, identity) -> FfiWelcomeFounder { groupId, founderAccountKey }`, on wasm `peekWelcomeFounder(welcome, identity) -> WelcomeFounder` (getters `groupId`, `founderAccountKey`); not on the NSE surface **Device leaves in the PublisherGroup ([ADR-0090](docs/adr/0090-publisher-group-device-leaves.md), built 2026-11-14 with all three clients; an invariant #3 change, C-90 / C-91):** every PublisherGroup leaf is an ADR-0050 bound device identity; `create(device, owner_account_seed, channel_id)` / `load(device, owner_account_seed, channel_id)` / `load_joined(device, group_id, channel_id)` / `join_from_welcome(device, welcome, group_id, channel_id, owner_account_key)`, each refusing a device without a verifying binding; the owner's account key is recorded beside the group (`lakko/publisher-group-owner/v1\0 ‖ group_id`). Every commit is staged: `stage_commit(device, admit, retire, rotate)`, `stage_rotate`, `stage_admit_devices`, `stage_retire_devices`, `stage_remove_publisher`, `stage_approve_external_join`, then `merge_staged_commit` / `abandon_staged_commit` on the DS commit route's answer (`has_staged_commit`, `staged_channel_seed`, `delete`, `owner_account_key`, `is_owned_by(owner_account_seed)`, `is_owner_device(device)`). `check_publisher_commit`, run by the committer and by every peer in `process_incoming`, refuses a committer that is not a device of the owner's account (`NotOwner`), any proposal but Add and Remove (`UnsupportedProposal`), an added leaf without a verifying binding (`DeviceBindingInvalid`) and a ninth leaf of one account (`DeviceLimitReached`). **This supersedes, for the PublisherGroup, three statements earlier in this row:** `open_or_create`, the eager `rotate` / `remove_publisher` / `approve_external_join`, `Group::approve_external_join_directly` and `Group::remove_leaves_directly` are deleted (so no direct-commit arm survives for the PublisherGroup, and `remove_publisher` of ADR-0087 section 15 is `stage_remove_publisher`, merged only on the DS answer); `is_owned_by` takes the owner's account seed. Not built: the gate does not compare a committer's update-path leaf node with its old leaf. `tests/publisher_group_devices.rs` (11, four proptests); on UniFFI and wasm with the same names (UniFFI `publisherGroupLoad` / `publisherGroupLoadJoined` / `publisherGroupJoinFromWelcome`, `publisherOwnerAccountKey`, `publisherGroupIdForSeed`); the receive keeps the name `processIncomingVerifyingRoles` **Owner-device entry from a saved GroupInfo (ADR-0064 section 7 amendment, built 2026-11-14 in the core and both FFI surfaces, host-tested only, no client calls it yet):** `PublisherGroup::group_info` / `staged_group_info` (the GroupInfo of the epoch a staged commit moves to, kept beside it in a provider row) and `PublisherGroup::enter(device, group_info, owner_account_seed, channel_id)` (an external commit with the `ExternalInit` alone, built in a throwaway provider and HELD by a provider row `lakko/external-entry/v1\0` until `merge_staged_commit` / `abandon_staged_commit`, since OpenMLS 0.8.1 merges an external commit at build; leaf lifetimes of the GroupInfo tree not checked, residual 9), and the gate arm in `check_publisher_commit` (a non-member's external commit only with exactly one `ExternalInit`, its leaf bound to the owner's account, under 8 owner leaves; no commit may change the GroupContext extensions); `src/group/external_entry.rs`; on UniFFI `publisherGroupEnter` -> `FfiPublisherGroupEntry { group, commit }` and `PublisherGroup.groupInfo` / `stagedGroupInfo`, on wasm `PublisherGroup.enter` -> `PublisherGroupEntry` (`commit`, `takeGroup()`) and the same two methods; the head saves each epoch's DS access seed too (`lakko-vault` `ReentryEpoch.ds_access_seed`, wire `epoch ‖ seed(32) ‖ group_info`, redacted in `Debug`; FFI `VaultReentryEpoch.dsAccessSeed`), written from `PublisherGroup.dsAccessSeed` / `stagedDsAccessSeed`, and an entering device proves the epoch it leaves with `DsProofSigner.forGroup(epoch, savedSeed)` **G-55 closed in code (2026-11-14, host-tested only):** the entry reaches the journal wrapped (`entry_wrap.rs`, key `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/ds/entry-wrap/v1", ds_access_seed_N)`; `enter` takes the saved seed as a fifth argument, `process_incoming` opens a wrapped row before the gate and refuses an unwrapped external commit; `Group::ds_access_seed` / `staged_ds_access_seed`), and the DeviceGroup rejoin knock is sealed under the lineage seed and padded (`pairing::encode_rejoin_knock(seed, ...)` / `open_rejoin_knock`), so neither row shows the account key to the Delivery Service; FFI `publisherGroupEnter(..., dsAccessSeed)`, `buildRejoinKnock` / `encodeRejoinKnock(accountIdentity, ...)`, `openRejoinKnock`; fuzz `wrapped_entry_open`, `rejoin_knock_open` |
| `lakko-attachments` | **The chat wire envelope** — the JSON `Envelope` that every MLS Application payload actually carries, and the one place its grammar is defined. `compose` / `compose_edit` / `compose_delete` / `compose_reaction` / `compose_typing` / `compose_read_receipt` on the send side; `parse` + **`parse_body` → `MessageBody::{Chat,Edit,Delete,Reaction,Typing,ReadReceipt}`** on the receive side, bounded and fail-closed (>1 op field, or a reaction without a valid emoji, degrades to `Chat`). Also per-attachment encryption (`encrypt_attachment` / `decrypt_attachment` / `AttachmentRef`, blob id = SHA-256 of the ciphertext) against the content-addressed `services/storage`. **Media metadata ([ADR-0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md))** — `AttachmentRef` gains optional `kind` / `duration_ms` / `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb` (`ThumbRef`, a second encrypted blob), `WIRE_VERSION` still 1. Deserialization goes through a lenient wire shape: a field of the wrong JSON type or outside its bound is **dropped alone** and never fails the ref or the envelope (a failed envelope would render as raw JSON through the legacy fallback). Bounds: duration kept only for `voice` (1..=15 min) / `video_note` (1..=60 s), waveform 1..=128 decoded bytes, dimensions 1..=4096, thumb size 1..=256 KiB with a decodable ref; zero is dropped too. The same sanitizer runs in `compose`, so the wire never carries a value receivers discard. `MediaKind::{Voice,VideoNote,Gif,Other}` never fails on an unknown kind; **inline playback is `inline_media_type(mime)` alone** (MIME essence, case-insensitive: `image/{jpeg,png,gif,webp}`, `audio/mp4`, `video/mp4`; never `kind`, never SVG), also exported on UniFFI + wasm. `RawMediaMetadata::bounded` applies the same bounds to already-decoded values (the history-bundle route), pinned to the envelope route by a test. **Reply ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 1)** — three more optional lenient fields beside the text, `reply_to` (the DS `messageId` answered) + the snapshot `reply_author` (<= 255 bytes) / `reply_excerpt` (<= 512 bytes), **never in the op set**: `MessageBody::Chat` carries `reply: Option<Reply>`, an op envelope that also carries them is still that op, a snapshot without a target is dropped, an over-long snapshot is dropped alone on parse and truncated on a char boundary by `compose_reply`; the quote is what the replier saw and is never re-read from the original (a later edit or delete changes nothing), one level deep. On UniFFI `compose_reply_envelope` + `ParsedBody.reply_to/reply_author/reply_excerpt`, on wasm `EnvelopeBuilder.buildReply` / `composeReplyEnvelopeText` + the `replyTo` (`bigint`) / `replyAuthor` / `replyExcerpt` getters; the NSE extension matches the reply away (a notification previews no quote). **Forward ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 2)** — three more lenient fields beside the text: `forwarded_from` (the ORIGINAL author's display name as a snapshot, <= 255 bytes, a label and never a route to the person) and the origin pair `forward_origin_group` (standard base64 of the origin group id, <= 128 bytes) + `forward_origin_message` (its DS `messageId`), kept only as a whole; `MessageBody::Chat` carries `forward: Option<Forward { author, origin }>` (present when the name or a whole origin survives), `compose_forward` truncates the name and drops an over-long origin; attachments are RE-ENCRYPTED by the caller before compose (the refs passed are the new ones), so the two conversations never share a blob; the origin rides only inside the sealed payload. On UniFFI `compose_forward_envelope` + `ParsedBody.forwarded_from/forward_origin_group_b64/forward_origin_message_id`, on wasm `EnvelopeBuilder.buildForward` + the `forwardedFrom` / `forwardOriginGroupB64` / `forwardOriginMessageId` (`bigint`) getters. **A JSON envelope of another wire version is `MessageBody::Unsupported { version }`, never `Chat` text** ([ADR-0061](docs/adr/0061-interface-localization.md) section 6: the client renders its own catalog sentence; on UniFFI and the NSE surface it is `MessageBodyKind::Unsupported`, appended last, with the version in `target`, on wasm `kind == "unsupported"` + `unsupportedVersion`; the legacy `parse` / `parse_envelope` keep the English placeholder). 62 tests + the `message_body_parse` fuzz target. **`parse_body`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback is why service envelopes need a filter ahead of it** — see the `lakko-ffi-extension` row. **Since 2026-09-29 the fallback no longer reaches a Lakko service envelope:** a non-JSON plaintext opening with `lakko/` (`SERVICE_ENVELOPE_PREFIX`, `is_service_envelope`) is `MessageBody::ServiceEnvelope`, never rendered (`MessageBodyKind.serviceEnvelope` on UniFFI and the NSE surface, `"service-envelope"` on wasm; every client drops it in the timeline, previews and notifications) — found live when a profile v3 from another branch reached v2 clients and showed as a bubble of domain text and binary. The per-kind filter arms stay: they are what applies a recognised envelope |
| `lakko-proto` | Generated types from `schemas/` (7 `.proto` files via `build.rs`). **No service depends on it** — the dependents are all client-side (`lakko-mls`, `lakko-calls`, both FFI crates), so `schemas/` is the shared source of truth but this crate is not the shared artifact |
| `lakko-transport` | quinn (QUIC) + tungstenite (WS) + pluggable obfs trait + signed bootstrap rendezvous + DoH fetcher + chunked AEAD stream + SS-2022 TCP transport + ECH (parser + RustCrypto/hpke adapter wired into HTTP + WebTransport) (Phase 5: G5-5 + G5-5-doh + G5-aead-stream + G5-3-header + S-5-shadowsocks-tcp + G5-6 + G5-6-wireup all done; G5-3 complete; G5-6 wire-up landed via [ADR-0012](docs/adr/0012-ech-hpke-adapter.md)). `DohFetcher` resolves bootstrap domains over JSON-DoH (Cloudflare / Google / Quad9 / AdGuard), then IP-pinned HTTPS GET + 4-stage `BootstrapConfig::verify`. `DohFetcher::fetch_pinned_bytes(domain, path, max_bytes)` exposes the same IP-pin pipeline as a generic `Vec<u8>` getter with body-size cap — it is **designed for** downstream callers (TUF metadata fetch via `lakko_tuf::urls`, KeyPackage bundles, signed identity blobs) to reuse rather than re-implement IP pinning — stated in the present tense here for a long time, but the only production call site is `lakko-ffi-uniffi`'s bootstrap-config fetch; none of those three consumers exists yet; refuses payloads exceeding the cap with `DohError::BodyTooLarge`. `AeadStreamWriter` / `AeadStreamReader` ship chunked ChaCha20-Poly1305 framing (16 KiB max chunk). `ShadowsocksTransport::connect` + `accept_inbound` deliver a working SS-2022 client/server stack over TCP with full handshake (salt + sealed prelude) + per-direction subkey separation + AEAD body. `parse_ech_config_list` decodes draft-ietf-tls-esni-18 §4 wire format with strict version pinning (0xfe0d only), bounded allocations (16 KiB outer / 16 inner configs / 4 KiB per config), full field validation (cipher_suites non-empty + 4-byte aligned, public_name 1..=255 + UTF-8, max_name_length non-zero). ECH wire-up (G5-6-wireup, ADR-0012) — `crates/lakko-transport/src/ech/adapter.rs` exposes three `LakkoHpkeSuite*` structs implementing `rustls::crypto::hpke::Hpke` on top of `hpke 0.13` (RustCrypto), bundled as `ALL_SUPPORTED_SUITES` for `rustls::client::EchConfig::new`; `wireup.rs::build_ech_mode_from_bytes` runs a two-stage validation (our parser + rustls's own) and returns `EchMode::Enable`. `HttpTransportConfig::ech_config_list` + `WebTransportConfig::ech_config_list_b64` plumb the bytes into the rustls ClientConfig builder; WebTransport bypasses `web-transport-quinn::ClientBuilder` when armed and assembles `quinn::ClientConfig` manually with a Lakko-side `CertHashVerifier` (W3C `serverCertificateHashes` semantics) + refuses ECH-without-cert-pins (`EchRequiresCertHashes`). REALITY is intentionally not wired for ECH — both target the same outer-ClientHello SNI slot for SNI hiding via different mechanisms (REALITY already provides SNI privacy). `rand_core` 0.9 `OsRng` wrapped via `UnwrapErr` to satisfy hpke's infallible `CryptoRng + RngCore` bound. rustls's IANA-aligned Hpke* enums imported via the `internal` re-export path (documented external-adapter consumer surface, but off the stable interface). REALITY v2 Path C — pre-shared-key model (G5-1-v2 + S-5-reality-driver-v2 + S-5-reality-server + S-5-reality-rng-hook all complete; both E2E integration tests green): publisher distributes 32-byte `tag_secret` per `(client, short_id)` via signed bootstrap config. Client surface (4 pieces): (1) `reality::cert::AcceptAnyCertVerifier` (pub(crate)-scoped, REALITY-aware safety contract: PKI is bypassed because the cryptographic auth lives in session_id + ServerHello.Random HMAC chain, not in TLS PKI); (2) `reality::driver` with `build_reality_client_config` + `build_reality_client_connection` (arms per-thread `RealityRng` inject slot with the REALITY authenticator immediately before `ClientConnection::new`, so `SessionId::random` consumes the authenticator bytes and they enter rustls's transcript hash) + `drive_handshake` async byte-intercept loop manually driving `rustls::ClientConnection` via `write_tls`/`read_tls` + post-loop ClientFinished drain (TLS 1.3 requires flushing writes queued by the final `process_new_packets` after `is_handshaking()` goes false) + (optionally) `extract_server_hello_random` + `verify_server_response` on the first inbound record, refusing with typed `RealityDriverError::ServerAuth` if HMAC doesn't match (active-probe detection); (3) `reality::stream::RealityConnection<S>` — post-handshake `Connection` trait impl that shuttles length-prefixed (u32-BE) frames inside the TLS tunnel via rustls's plaintext writer + buffered plaintext reader (pre-loop `pump_decrypt` in `read_until` drains rustls-internal plaintext before blocking on `socket.read`, defending against TCP coalescing on loopback), with `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` cap on both `send` and `recv`, idempotent `close` driving `send_close_notify` + socket shutdown; (4) `RealityTransport::connect` wire-up — opens TCP, builds rustls `ClientConnection` pointed at `RealityConfig::sni`, drives the handshake, returns `Box<dyn Connection>` (no silent downgrade per security invariant #4). Server surface: `reality::handshake::{extract_client_hello_session_id, patch_server_hello_random}` wire-format mirrors; `reality::server_driver::{drive_server_handshake, build_reality_server_config, make_self_signed_cert}` server-side byte-intercept driver verifying the inbound ClientHello.session_id against an `(short_id, tag_secret)` allow-list and surfacing typed `AcceptedClient` on success (arms `RealityRng` with `compute_server_response` bytes before the first `process_new_packets` so `ServerHello.Random` receives the mutual-auth HMAC); `reality::server_stream::RealityServerConnection<S>` server-side `Connection` impl (same pre-loop `pump_decrypt` fix); `reality::listener::RealityListener` accept loop over TCP. `reality::rng::RealityRng` — custom `rustls::crypto::SecureRandom` impl wired as `CryptoProvider::secure_random`; per-thread one-shot `Cell<Option<[u8;32]>>` inject slot consumed by the first `fill(32)` call on the thread, all other fills delegate to ring's `SystemRandom`. Client builds session_id auth as `HMAC(HKDF(tag_secret, "lakko/reality/v2"), short_id || ts)` truncated to 16B. v2 dropped ECDH from v1 because rustls 0.23 doesn't expose TLS 1.3 ephemeral-X25519 hook — Path C uses stock rustls; cost is wire-incompatibility with upstream xtls/REALITY (Lakko closed ecosystem) + loss of authenticator-level forward-secrecy (cover TLS FS preserved, mitigated by tag_secret rotation through bootstrap config TTL). Strongly-typed `RealityConfig` (with `tag_secret_b64` instead of v1's `server_pubkey_b64`) / `RealityTagSecret` / `RealityShortId`, eager parse-time validation. 78 unit tests across `crates/lakko-transport/src/reality/` (25 handshake-wire + 14 authenticator + 8 config + 7 driver + 7 server_driver + 6 rng + 5 transport + 4 stream + 2 cert; the old "62 … (13 + 7 + 5 + 25 + 6 + 4 + 2 + 8 + misc)" was both stale and self-inconsistent — its own parts summed to 70) plus 3 E2E integration tests (all green: `reality_v2_listener_refuses_active_prober` (wrong-tag_secret REALITY prober) + `reality_v2_listener_refuses_a_vanilla_tls_prober` (G5-7-reality-probe — a plain non-REALITY TLS prober, the commonest censor probe vector, is refused; the refusal is a connection DROP — full probe-INDISTINGUISHABILITY from the cover site needs the deferred live-cover-proxy) + `reality_v2_loopback_roundtrip`). WebTransport real impl (G5-4): `WebTransportPt` over `web-transport-quinn` 0.11, cert-hash pinning (W3C `serverCertificateHashes` mode, SHA-256 of full DER cert) + system-roots fallback, u32-BE length-prefixed framing with `MAX_FRAME_BYTES` enforcement, 13 unit tests. `cert_hashes_b64` is the wire-config field name (renamed from `spki_pins_b64` for spec alignment — cert-hash ≠ SPKI).
