MASQUE foundation (G5-2): `MasqueConfig` parser (eager validation of proxy https + inner_target host:port + bearer), `build_connect_udp_request` emitting RFC 9298 §3 pseudo-headers + RFC 9297 §3.4 `capsule-protocol`, HTTP Datagram codec per RFC 9297 §3 + RFC 9298 §3 (QUIC varint Context ID, ctx=0 for CONNECT-UDP, 65 KiB hard cap). 32 unit tests. **`MasqueTransport::connect` is fully wired** (S-5-masque-handshake): opens a `quinn::Endpoint` (TLS 1.3 + `h3` ALPN + system roots), runs the h3 extended-CONNECT-UDP request, awaits the 200, and returns a `MasqueConnection` that send/recv-s UDP payloads over `quinn::Connection` datagrams (the h3-datagram higher-level surface is bypassed). `MasqueTransport::with_extra_roots(cfg, roots)` (G5-2 trust hook) folds caller-supplied DER trust anchors into the connect-time root store on top of the OS native certs — for pinning a **private-CA** proxy (the DoH-IP-pin / REALITY-custom-verifier posture) and to let a loopback e2e test trust a self-signed server. **Live roundtrip proven (G5-2)**: `masque_loopback_roundtrip` (`crates/lakko-transport/tests/masque_e2e.rs`) stands up a real in-process h3 CONNECT-UDP proxy (quinn server + `h3::server` extended-connect/datagram + verbatim QUIC-datagram echo) and drives production `connect` -> `send` -> `recv` through it on `with_extra_roots`-pinned trust. **That test caught + fixed a real bug**: `connect` dropped the h3 `SendRequest` at return, and h3's `SendRequest::drop` closes the whole HTTP/3 connection with `H3_NO_ERROR` once the last sender handle is gone — tearing the datagram tunnel down the instant it was established (the wiring/spike sprints validated compilation + the request path but never a datagram roundtrip). Fix: `MasqueConnection` now holds the `SendRequest` so its lifetime equals the tunnel's. **WS-over-tunnel (ADR-0026):** `byte_stream.rs` `ByteStreamConnection` adapts a framed `Connection` to one `AsyncRead + AsyncWrite` byte stream (pump task: split-duplex + mpsc + `select!` recv/send over the single `&mut conn`, the obfs-proxy relay idiom; writes chunked under `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`; 4 unit tests), and `ws_tunnel.rs` `tunneled_websocket` runs a real RFC 6455 client handshake + frame codec (`tokio-tungstenite`, `handshake`-only — no inner TLS, the tunnel encrypts) over it; the `services/obfs-proxy` loopback e2e proves a WebSocket (handshake + subprotocol + text/binary frames) round-trips through a REALITY tunnel + the byte-relay. See [`docs/security/transport.md`](docs/security/transport.md) T-1..T-15 **`BootstrapDialer` pins REALITY and Shadowsocks-2022 to their signed `remote` (the `endpoint` of `dial` chooses nothing), `recv` survives being dropped on Shadowsocks and WebTransport, `tunneled_request` writes in AEAD-sized chunks and decodes chunked responses (ADR-0085 section 14, 2026-10-30)** |
| `lakko-storage` | Encrypted local SQLite store — `EncryptedStore` / `SqliteStore` / `GroupStateStore` / `MessageStore` / `InMemoryStore` over `rusqlite` in WAL mode. **Application-layer ChaCha20-Poly1305, explicitly NOT SQLCipher** (the crate's own `cipher.rs` argues the choice; full-DB SQLCipher is a listed Phase-2 deferral), and there is **no blob store here** — content-addressed ciphertext blobs live in `services/storage` with the client half in `lakko-attachments` |
| `lakko-backup` | Encrypted-backup envelope — a `BackupPayload` (v8 since 2026-11-04, the only version read: account id, creation time, identity seed, contacts incl. the per-contact `account_id`, the known group ids, the ADR-0058 synced state, the own predecessors of ADR-0074 section 3.7, at most 8, each a 64-byte seed and its adoption time, and the account's own profile and visibility envelopes, opaque and at most `MAX_OWN_ENVELOPE_LEN` each, so a device restored with no other device alive gets its name, photo references and Who can find me back, and the received profiles: the newest ADR-0048 profile envelope this device holds from each other person, keyed on that person's 32-byte account signature key, sorted by key with none twice, each 1 to `lakko_contacts::PROFILE_ENVELOPE_MAX_LEN` bytes, at most `MAX_RECEIVED_PROFILES` (4096) entries and `MAX_RECEIVED_PROFILES_BYTES` (2 MiB) in all (both chosen, not measured), so after such a restore senders keep their names; `fit_received_profiles` turns a priority-ordered candidate list into one that encodes, and the client feeds each restored envelope back through its own profile receive gate (strictly newer stamp per key), so a stale backup never replaces a newer name; on UniFFI `buildBackupPayload(..., receivedProfiles: [BackupReceivedProfile]?)` + `ParsedBackupPayload.receivedProfiles`, on wasm a last `BackupReceivedProfile[]` argument of `buildBackupPayload` + the `receivedProfiles` getter; **no MLS state** - v2-v5 carried a whole-provider snapshot, removed because it is one device's leaf state, see ADR-0050 section 2.9a) wrapped in an AEAD envelope keyed by an Argon2id KEK derived from the BIP39 recovery phrase, uploaded as one opaque blob to `services/storage`. `head_frame.rs` carries the [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md) `0xB1` container framing (`frame_backup_blob` / `parse_backup_blob` → `BackupBlob::{Framed, Legacy}`) so head + envelope swap atomically in one `PUT`. `chain.rs` (the v2 sequence chain) is **dormant** — wired to no FFI and no client, which is exactly the correction ADR-0037 made to the earlier claim that it closed the rollback case. 71 tests (two proptests) + 4 fuzz targets (`payload_decode` new with v8) |
| `lakko-sync` | Hybrid logical clock + edit chain + drafts. **Single-device only, and no CRDT** — the crate ships the single-device variants deliberately, and its `edit.rs` carries a "Why no character-level CRDT" section: an edit is a flat `Vec<EditOp>` LWW chain ordered by HLC. Cross-device sync of edits / read receipts / drafts over DeviceGroup application messages is listed as Phase-2 out-of-scope. Depended on by `lakko-ffi-wasm` only, so it does not reach iOS or Android at all **Since ADR-0058 (2026-09-28) it also holds `synced/`: the one fold every client uses for the DeviceGroup-synced state** - chat folders as per-register HLC stamps plus the four existing maps (flags, read cursors, drafts, conversation order); strictly-greater stamp wins, equal stamps break on the canonical bytes, a folder deletion is final; `merge_from` (backup restore), `snapshot` (growth re-send), `to_bytes` / `from_bytes` (a Lakko-own length-prefixed encoding, not protobuf) and `should_send_snapshot`. Exposed on UniFFI and wasm (`SyncedState`); proptests pin commutativity / idempotence over random permutations **Saved messages ([ADR-0065](docs/adr/0065-attachment-lifetime-and-access.md) section 4, built 2026-10-02, no client uses it yet):** one more register, keyed (conversation ref, message id), `Present { saved_at_ms, snapshot (author, excerpt, attachment kind), blob_ids }` or `Removed` (a tombstone), folded like the others; it rides `FolderChanges` (proto field `saved = 6`) and therefore the `StateSnapshot`, so no new DeviceGroup kind; persisted state version 2 (version 1 refused); reads `saved()` / `is_saved` / `saved_blob_ids`; client limits `SAVED_MAX_CLIENT` 2000 and `SAVED_MAX_PER_CHANGE_SET` 32 (a live change set is one journal row), neither a decode bound; on both FFI surfaces (`composeSavedMessage` / `composeSavedRemoval` on UniFFI, `FolderChangeSet.addSaved` / `addSavedRemoved` on wasm, `saved` / `isSaved` / `savedBlobIds`) **Invite-link records ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md) section 4, 2026-10-16):** one more register keyed on the 16-byte link id (`LinkRecordEntry`: secret, kind, ledger id, expiry, use limit, uses, revoked, minted time, label, up to 16 answered requester keys), folded with a revoked entry above any live one so a revocation is final; `link_answer_verdict` -> `Accept | Answered | Dead`; carried as its own envelope `lakko/sync/link-record/v1\0` (not in the growth snapshot; sent by the catch-up), persisted state version 3 (version 2 refused); on both FFI surfaces. **Reworked 2026-10-17:** the entry also carries the ledger row's revocation seed (0 or 32 bytes, SECRET, left out of `Debug`) so any device that revokes a link deletes its row; the envelope's version byte is `0x02` (`0x01` refused) and the persisted state version 4 (3 and older refused); `LinkRecord.revocationSeed` on UniFFI, the seed after the ledger id in the wasm constructor. **2026-10-26:** the entry carries `shared` (copied on a device of the account), final like `revoked` (the fold orders `(revoked, shared, stamp, bytes)`), `LinkRecordEntry::editable()` (not revoked, never copied, never answered) is the one rule for editing a link's terms, `SyncedState::mark_link_shared`; envelope version `0x03`, persisted state version 5 (6 since the merge of `profile-avatar` on 2026-10-27, whose saved-message snapshot carries the message time); on UniFFI `LinkRecord.shared` / `.editable` + `markLinkShared`, on wasm the two getters + `markLinkShared` **Conversation ends ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 9, 2026-10-31; host-tested, no client wired):** one more register, keyed on the conversation ref, `Ended` or `Joined` with the MLS epoch of that event, folded by `(epoch, stamp, canonical bytes)` so a device applying an old removal late cannot end a group its account has rejoined (a channel writes epoch 0, so stamps decide there, and a complete tie keeps the conversation); it rides `FolderChanges` (proto `conversation_ends = 7`) and so the snapshot and the backup; persisted state version 7 (6 refused); never dropped, since no age can be shown safe against a device offline past the retention; `compose_conversation_ended` / `compose_conversation_joined` return the register to send or `None` when there is nothing to tell (a first join writes nothing; `CONVERSATION_ENDS_MAX_CLIENT` 4096 stops a new end's register, not the fold and not the wipe); reads `conversation_end`, `is_conversation_ended`, `ended_conversations`; on both FFI surfaces as `composeConversationEnded` / `composeConversationJoined` / `conversationEnd` / `isConversationEnded` / `endedConversations` / `conversationEndsMaxClient`, carried by `FolderChangeSet.conversationEnds` on UniFFI and `addConversationEnd` on wasm **Device acknowledgements (`docs/ux/identity-recovery.md` 17.5, 2026-10-29):** one more register keyed on a 32-byte device signature key (`synced/device_ack.rs`, `DeviceAckEntry`, tag 16; the persisted layout of every other register unchanged): the person saw the "a device was added to your account" warning for that device on some device of the account, so no device warns about it any more; write-once at the writer (`acknowledge_devices` returns exactly the envelopes it wrote), carried as `lakko/sync/device-ack/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ count(1..=16) ‖ count × (hlc ‖ key ‖ acknowledged_at_ms)` (sorted, canonical, fail-closed; driven by the `synced_state_decode` fuzz target), never in the growth snapshot; on UniFFI `acknowledgeDevices` / `isDeviceAcknowledged` / `acknowledgedDevices` / `deviceAckEnvelopes` / `applyDeviceAckEnvelope` + free `isDeviceAckEnvelope`, on wasm the same with flat-concatenated keys **`/g/` links in the link register ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 7, phase B, 2026-11-06):** kinds `Group` 3, `Channel` 4, `Publisher` 5 (`is_group_link`), each entry carrying `target_id` (32-byte group id or 16-byte channel id), `capability` (the sealed `/g/` blob, at most `group_invite::MAX_SEALED_LEN`) and, for a private channel subscriber link only, `invite_seed` (32 bytes, SECRET, out of `Debug`) and `admission_version`; contact and call records carry none (`BoundsError::LinkRecordTarget`); a `/g/` record's id must be `lakko_contacts::group_invite::group_link_record_id(ledger_id)` and its verdict is always `Dead`; revocation stays final; link-record envelope version `0x04`, persisted `STATE_VERSION` 11 (12 since the same day's comment reactions, below), older refused. On UniFFI the `LinkRecord` record gains `targetId` / `inviteSeed` / `admissionVersion` / `capability` (the byte fields have no default: pass empty for `/i/` and `/c/`) and `LinkRecordKind.group/channel/publisher` (appended), plus free `groupLinkRecordId(ledgerId)`; on wasm four optional trailing constructor arguments, the four getters, `LinkRecordKind.Group/Channel/Publisher` and `groupLinkRecordId`; the `synced_state_decode` fuzz oracle asserts a `/g/` link never answers a request **Own reactions on comments (ADR-0087 section 13a, phase B, 2026-11-06):** the own-reaction register (`synced/channel_reaction.rs`) is keyed `(channel, post cursor, comment cursor or none, emoji)`; envelope version `0x02` adds `target(1)` (1 post, 2 comment) and `comment_cursor(8)` after the post cursor (a post target must carry 0; `0x01` refused; `BoundsError::ChannelReactionTarget`); three reactions a comment, counted apart from the post (`BoundsError::TooManyCommentReactions`); pruned with the post; persisted `STATE_VERSION` 12. `set_comment_reaction` / `has_comment_reaction` / `comment_reactions_on`; `channel_reactions_on` now lists the post's own reactions only. On UniFFI `SyncedState.setCommentReaction(hlc, channelId, postCursor, commentCursor, emoji, reacted, postAtMs)` / `hasCommentReaction(channelId, postCursor, commentCursor, emoji)` / `commentReactionsOn(channelId, postCursor, commentCursor)`, the same names on wasm; comment reactions ride the existing `channelReactionEnvelopes` / `applyChannelReactionEnvelope` **Channel subscriptions (maintainer decision 2026-11-13, ADR-0050 section 2.9 amendment; host-tested, no client uses it yet):** one register per channel id (`synced/channel_subscription.rs`, tag 20): subscribed { role subscriber / owner / co-publisher, the account's one subscriber key per channel as its Ed25519 seed, read key 0 or 32 bytes, a co-publisher's PublisherGroup id, fallback name and handle } or a left tombstone (a later re-subscribe wins; on an exact tie the subscription), carried as its own envelope `lakko/sync/channel-subscription/v1\0` re-sent on growth, persisted `STATE_VERSION` 13, so in the backup; no epoch seeds, signing seeds or PublisherGroup state; on UniFFI and wasm as `setChannelSubscription` / `leaveChannelSubscription` / `channelSubscription` / `isChannelSubscribed` / `allChannelSubscriptions` / `channelSubscriptionEnvelopeFor` / `channelSubscriptionEnvelopes` / `applyChannelSubscriptionEnvelope` + `isChannelSubscriptionEnvelope` / `channelSubscriptionEnvelopeMaxLen` / `channelSubscriptionsMaxClient` **Admission seeds and the recorded owner key ([ADR-0090](docs/adr/0090-publisher-group-device-leaves.md), 2026-11-14, used by all three clients):** a register `synced/channel_admission_seed` (tag 21) keyed `(channel id, admission version, seed id)`, the seed id `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/sync/channel-admission-seed/id/v1", seed)`, value the 32-byte seed, write-once per seed, so two owner devices that mint one version at once both keep their seed and a ticket of that version is tried under every seed held for it; carried by its own DeviceGroup envelope `lakko/sync/channel-admission-seed/v1\0` (1 to 16 entries of 58 bytes), re-sent on DeviceGroup growth, persisted and in the backup, never in the growth snapshot; and the channel-subscription register gains `owner_account_key` (32 bytes, required for a co-publisher, absent otherwise; envelope version `0x02`, `0x01` refused), from which a co-publisher's device accepts a PublisherGroup Welcome only from a device of that key. Persisted synced state version 15 (older refused). On UniFFI `setChannelAdmissionSeed` / `channelAdmissionSeedsFor` / `channelAdmissionSeeds` / `channelAdmissionSeedEnvelopeFor` / `channelAdmissionSeedEnvelopes` / `applyChannelAdmissionSeedEnvelope` + free `isChannelAdmissionSeedEnvelope`, on wasm the same with flat seeds and `channelAdmissionVersions`; the `synced_state_decode` fuzz oracle covers the register |
| `lakko-calls` | SFrame, jitter buffer, codec negotiation, ICE/TURN. Call **safety number** (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-fingerprint) — `safety_number::call_safety_number(seed)` = domain-separated BLAKE3 (`blake3_derive_key_from_material("lakko/call/safety-number/v1", seed)`) of the shared per-call MLS-exporter seed, rendered as 4 groups of 4 decimal digits (golden-pinned, canonical so all platforms render byte-identical). Displayed in the in-call E2EE pill for an out-of-band MITM check (matching codes = same E2EE root key, invariant #4); FFI-exposed as `callSafetyNumber` (uniffi + wasm), wired into the SFU-call E2EE pill on web + iOS + Android. **Mid-call rekey ([ADR-0029](docs/adr/0029-mid-call-key-rotation.md), G-18)** — `call_key_ring::CallKeyRing`: the seed-based overlap keyer (current + previous `CallKey`; `try_open_with_overlap` / `try_open_framed_with_overlap` try current then, during the window, previous; `rekey_from_seed_at` / `prune_expired` / `has_live_previous`; time-source-free, `[OVERLAP_MIN_MS, OVERLAP_MAX_MS]` clamp, 7 tests in `call_key_ring.rs`). `GroupCallSession` delegates its overlap fields to it (23 tests in `group_call.rs`, 8 of them overlap/rekey). Note the whole crate's "property tests" are plain `#[test]` functions — `lakko-calls` has no `proptest` dependency. FFI-exposed (uniffi + wasm) + surfaced in the `lakko-sframe-native` C ABI, and wired into all three clients' SFU call sessions to rotate the call key on a mid-call MLS epoch advance. **Per-(leaf, stream) key derivation via the header KID ([ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md), supersedes [ADR-0034](docs/adr/0034-sframe-per-media-stream-key.md)'s per-media-TYPE discriminator)** — the SFrame header KID is now a per-`(sender_leaf, stream)` identifier `KID = (sender_leaf << 8) | stream_index`; `SframeHeader::kid_for(leaf, stream)` composes it (fail-closed `StreamIndexOverflow` on `stream > 255`), `leaf_of`/`stream_of` split it. `CallKey::participant_key(kid: u64)` folds the FULL KID into **both** HKDF `info` strings (`participant_key_for(leaf, stream)` is the sender convenience), so every one of a participant's concurrent streams — audio (0), camera (1), screen-share (2), … — derives a **disjoint** `(key, nonce_salt)` space for **any** stream count (0034's per-media-TYPE tag re-collided two video streams from one leaf; the KID is the only per-stream signal both endpoints share through the SFU, which forwards the RFC 9605 header verbatim). The receiver reads the KID off each frame's header and derives `participant_key(KID)` with **no** local media/kind signal. `ParticipantKey` carries the `kid` (`sender_leaf()`/`stream_index()` accessors); `seal_framed` stamps it, `open_framed` checks it. `MediaType` removed. Property test `three_streams_of_one_leaf_get_distinct_keys_and_nonces` (audio + 2 video). The `SframeHeader` codec / `header_decode` fuzz / ADR-0013 AAD are unchanged (extended-KID form). Threaded through `CallKeyRing` + `GroupCallSession` **Ended for everyone (2026-10-02, `docs/ux/calls.md` section 4.1.2):** `EndReason::EndedForAll` (wire 5) on `SignalingBody::End`, for a group or link call only: the call's starter ended it for everyone. The body states an intent; a client leaves the call only when the MLS-authenticated sender is the call's starter (chat group call: the person key recorded from the `GroupCallStart` sender; link call: the owner key of the signed capability) and treats it as a plain end otherwise. On UniFFI as `FfiCallEndReason::EndedForAll` (appended), on wasm as `"endedForAll"`; an unknown value still reads as `HungUp` **The media frame layout ([ADR-0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md), 2026-10-07; every stack's media path uses it since the native port of 2026-10-11):** `media_frame.rs` owns `clear_prefix ‖ ciphertext_and_tag ‖ sframe_header ‖ trailer` (`MediaFrameParts::split`, the 3-byte trailer `clear_prefix_len ‖ sframe_header_len ‖ version`, the AAD `sframe_header ‖ clear_prefix ‖ trailer`) and the sender's rule (`MediaCodec { Other, Vp8, Opus }`, `clear_prefix_len`: 10 bytes of a VP8 key frame, 3 of any other VP8 frame, none otherwise, never above `MAX_CLEAR_PREFIX_LEN` 16; `vp8_is_key_frame` reads the frame tag); `ParticipantKey::seal_media_frame` / `seal_media_frame_for_codec` / `open_media_frame` and `CallKeyRing::try_open_media_frame_with_overlap` seal and open it, one counter value per frame as before, the receiver naming no codec and refusing anything inconsistent before taking a slice. The header-first `seal_framed` / `open_framed` (the ADR-0013 layout this row describes above) are still in the crate and refuse the new layout, as the new open refuses theirs; no client's media path calls them any more (the golden tests and the SFU's synthetic-client test do). A second golden vector pins the new layout; three proptests and an every-truncation test cover the trailer parser; fuzz `media_frame_open` is compile-checked, not run. On wasm (`sealMediaFrame`, `sealMediaFrameWithPrefix`, `openMediaFrame`, `openMediaFrameWithOverlap`, `parseMediaFrame`, `MediaCodec`, `mediaCodecFromMime`, `mediaClearPrefixLen`, `vp8IsKeyFrame`) and on the C ABI; not on UniFFI **The sealed call relay and peer announcements ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2, 2026-10-16):** `relay.rs` - `RelayKey::from_call_seed` (`BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/call/relay/v1", seed)`), `seal` / `open` (`version 0x01 || nonce(12) || ChaCha20-Poly1305`, AAD `"lakko/call/relay/v1\0" || version`, plaintext <= 1024 bytes), `classify_relay_plaintext` -> `PeerAnnouncement` / `MediaState` / `Unknown`, `compose_peer_announcement(identity, call_id, peer_id)` and `verify_peer_announcement(group, call_id, relay_from, bytes)` (call id, `relay_from` == announced peer id, signer is a leaf of the group, signature, an ADR-0050 account binding on that leaf; returns leaf, key and account key); errors carry stable camelCase codes. 8 tests + fuzz `relay_open`. On UniFFI (`CallRelayKey`, `classifyCallRelayPlaintext`, `composeCallPeerAnnouncement`, `verifyCallPeerAnnouncement`, `sfuRoomForCall`; refusals as the appended `FfiError::CallRelay`) and wasm (same names, `callRelayError` property); used by all three clients' SFU sessions **Screen share ([ADR-0083](docs/adr/0083-screen-share.md), 2026-10-25, C-87):** `screen_share.rs` - the signed share state (`lakko/call/screen-share/v1\0`, MLS leaf key over call id, peer id, `seq`, sharing / stopped, sound flag; `compose_screen_share` / `decode_screen_share` / `verify_screen_share`), the order-independent one-share fold `ScreenShareState` (greatest live `(seq, signer_key)`, `next_start_seq`, `own_superseded`, `participant_left`), stream indices 2 (picture) and 5 (sound), suffix `~screen`; `RelayPayload::ScreenShare`; `SignalingBody::ScreenShare` (proto field 17). Fuzz `screen_share_decode`. On UniFFI and wasm as `composeCallScreenShare`, `verifyCallScreenShare`, `CallScreenShareState` **A name for the call (2026-11-07, `docs/ux/calls.md` 4.1, ADR-0078 section 2 amendment; host-tested, web only):** `call_name.rs` - a fourth relay payload `lakko/call/name/v1\0` (call id, peer id, `seq` >= 1, name 0..=255 bytes under the ADR-0048 display-name rule, empty = withdrawn), signed by the sender's MLS leaf key; `verify_call_name(group, call_id, peer_id, peer_signer_key, bytes)` (the screen-share shape), `call_name_is_newer` (strictly greater per peer), `RelayPayload::CallName`; property tests in the module; fuzz `relay_open` drives `decode_call_name`. On UniFFI (`composeCallName`, `verifyCallName` -> `VerifiedCallName`, `callNameIsNewer`, `CallRelayPayload::CallName` appended) and wasm (same names, relay kind `"call-name"` with a `callName` getter) **One-to-one camera and microphone state (2026-11-07):** `SignalingBody::MediaState { call_id, camera_on, microphone_on }` (proto `CallMediaState`, oneof field 18, tag `0x92 0x01`), sent by the 1x1 `CallSession` once the call connects and on every change, never a wake; `CallSession` and `GroupCallSession` ignore it, the client sets the other tile's camera picture and mute mark from it; proptests in `signaling.rs`, the `signaling_payload_decode` fuzz target covers it. On UniFFI `FfiCallSignalingBody::MediaState` (appended), on wasm `composeCallMediaStateSignaling` and a parsed kind `"mediaState"` with `cameraOn` / `microphoneOn`; the NSE extension classifies it `Other` and its golden pins the tag **Chat in a group call (2026-11-07, `docs/ux/calls.md` 4.1.4, ADR-0078 section 2 amendment):** `call_chat.rs` - a fifth relay payload `lakko/call/chat/v1\0` (call id, peer id, `seq` >= 1, text 1..=800 bytes, signed by the sender's MLS leaf key; `check_call_chat_text` (not white space only, no control character but LF and TAB), `clamp_call_chat_text`, `verify_call_chat(group, call_id, peer_id, peer_signer_key, bytes)`, `RelayPayload::CallChat`); ephemeral, a receiver dedups on `(peer id, seq)`; fuzz `relay_open` drives `decode_call_chat`. On UniFFI (`composeCallChat`, `verifyCallChat` -> `VerifiedCallChat`, `checkCallChatText`, `clampCallChatText`, `callChatMaxTextBytes`, `CallRelayPayload::CallChat` appended) and wasm (same names, relay kind `"call-chat"` with a `message` getter) |
| `lakko-channels` | Two-tier broadcast. Publisher path: PublisherGroup exporter seed + per-message AEAD + Ed25519 signature on every post. Subscriber path: HPKE seed envelope (`seal_seed` / `open_seed`) — full surface exposed via UniFFI + wasm-bindgen (G4-3c). See [ADR-0010](docs/adr/0010-channels-two-tier-broadcast.md). **Channel metadata record ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md))** — `meta.rs`: `ChannelMeta { name, description, updated_at_ms }` composed as the plaintext of an ordinary post (`CHANNEL_META_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/channel-meta/v1\0"`, 22 bytes, frozen; name 1..=64 scalars / 255 bytes, description 0..=300 scalars / 1024 bytes with line breaks allowed; `char::is_control` and the bidi/invisible `Cf` table refused, ZWJ/ZWNJ permitted; whole-record rejection, compose refuses what decode refuses) plus `classify_post_plaintext(&[u8]) -> PostContent::{Text, Meta, Malformed}` — the first content discriminator the post plaintext ever had, domain prefix checked first so a text post can never collide (the trailing NUL) and a domain match that fails a bound is `Malformed`, dropped rather than rendered — and `channel_meta_is_newer` (strictly greater). 23 tests + fuzz `channel_meta_decode`; the `post.rs` wire header is unchanged and the record consumes a real counter **Reactions on comments (ADR-0087 section 13a, phase B, 2026-11-06):** `reaction::CommentReactionKey` (HKDF info `lakko/channel/comment-reaction-key/v1`, labels over `post ‖ comment ‖ emoji`, never equal to a post label; `labels_for_comment`) and `write_key::CommentReactionWriteKey` (seed `lakko/channel/comment-reaction-write-key/v1` over account, channel, post, comment and label; `SubscriberWrite::AddCommentReaction` 0x05 / `DeleteCommentReaction` 0x06 with subject `comment_cursor ‖ label`); specs `reaction.sc.md`, `write_key.sc.md`; on UniFFI as `ChannelCommentReactionKey.derive(epochSeed, channelId, epochId)` with `label(postCursor, commentCursor, emoji)` / `resolveCounts(postCursor, commentCursor, candidates, counts)` and `ChannelCommentReactionWriteKey(accountIdentity, channelId, postCursor, commentCursor, label)` with `publicKey` / `signAdd` / `signDelete`; on wasm the same classes, counts as `resolveCountEmoji` / `resolveCountValues` |
| `lakko-contacts` | Username discovery (OPRF), invite capabilities, pseudonym lifecycle. **Profile envelope + username rule ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md))** — `profile.rs`: `Profile { display_name, username, updated_at_ms }`, a bounded fail-closed envelope (`lakko/profile/v1\0` domain, version byte, 8-byte BE stamp, one-byte-length name and username, then a one-byte-length account id of 0 or 16 bytes — the ADR-0021 peer-tag input that lets a group-call tile name a member who is not a contact, added 2026-09-19 as version `0x02`, `0x01` refused; min 29 / max 332 bytes) rejected as a whole on any bound violation (UTF-8, trimmed, 1-64 scalars, <= 255 bytes, no line break, no `Cc`, no `Cf` via the explicit `FORMAT_CHAR_RANGES` table), `compose_profile` / `try_unwrap_profile` / `decode_profile` / `normalize_display_name` / `profile_is_newer` (strictly greater), dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::Profile` (the enum is no longer `Copy`), fuzz `profile_decode`. `username.rs`: `check_canonical_username` + `UsernameError` (reason codes that never echo the input) — the one definition of the `[a-z0-9_]{3,32}` rule, which `lakko-discovery-client` depends on, placed here because it needs no Unicode tables and this crate is what the NSE extension links. `request.rs`: the **sealed plaintext is `profile_len(u16 BE) ‖ profile ‖ intro`**; `compose` takes `Option<&Profile>`, `open_envelope` returns `(Option<Profile>, intro)` — an invalid profile inside intact framing yields `None` with the intro kept, broken framing is `InvalidEncoding`. **Profile request ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) §2, trigger 4)** — `profile_request.rs`: `PROFILE_REQUEST_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/profile-request/v1\0"` (25 bytes, frozen) ‖ version `0x01`, 26 bytes and no fields, for the reasons the beacon gives; `compose_profile_request` / `try_unwrap_profile_request` / `decode_profile_request`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::ProfileRequest` ahead of the intro fallback, plus the pure `should_request_profiles(joined_just_now, unnamed_members, last_requested_at_ms, now_ms)` with `PROFILE_REQUEST_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` = 24 h (always on a join; otherwise only with an unnamed member and no sooner than a day after the last request; a backwards clock reads as recently asked). The answer is the ordinary profile envelope through the growth publisher, so N members answering one request are throttled and jittered like a growth re-send. 8 tests + fuzz `profile_request_decode`; on UniFFI and wasm as `composeProfileRequest` / `decodeProfileRequest` / `profileRequestEnvelopeLen` / `profileRequestMinIntervalMs` / `shouldRequestProfiles`; the extension crate exports decode and the length only. **Group name envelope ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md))** — `group_name.rs`: `GroupName { name, updated_at_ms }`, `GROUP_NAME_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/group-name/v1\0"` (20 bytes, frozen), wire `DOMAIN ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ name_len(1) ‖ name`, min 30 / max 285 bytes; an empty name clears, a non-empty one passes the profile display-name rule verbatim (`GroupName::validate` reuses `check_display_name`); `compose_group_name` / `try_unwrap_group_name` / `decode_group_name` / `normalize_group_name` / `group_name_is_newer` (strict); dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::GroupName` (step 7, ahead of the intro fallback). The envelope establishes nothing about authority: the receiver resolves the MLS sender to the account key at receipt (C-68) and admits the name only from the owner or an admin of the live role table (a table-less group is open), under the same per-group lock as the push routing secret (C-62 sub-invariant 5); a growth re-send carries the original stamp so it can never clobber a later rename (pinned by `a_growth_resend_with_the_original_stamp_never_clobbers_a_rename`). 19 tests + fuzz `group_name_decode`; the sibling-isolation tests in `profile.rs` and `committer_beacon.rs` now list every domain. **Shared-pin envelope ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 3)** — `pin.rs`: `PinOp { message_id, action: Pin | Unpin, updated_at_ms }`, `PIN_ENVELOPE_DOMAIN_V1 = "lakko/pin/v1\0"` (13 bytes, frozen), wire `DOMAIN ‖ version ‖ updated_at_ms(8 BE) ‖ action(1) ‖ message_id(8 BE i64)`, a fixed `PIN_ENVELOPE_LEN` = 31; one envelope names ONE message, so a conversation's shared pins are a set the receiver folds envelope by envelope and "several pins, no product limit" costs the codec nothing; `compose_pin` (infallible) / `try_unwrap_pin` / `decode_pin` / `pin_is_newer` (strictly greater per message; a re-send carries the original stamp and is not an update, and an unpin is STORED rather than deleted so a stale pin can never resurrect it); dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::Pin` (step 7c). Authority is the receiver's: owner or admin of the live role table resolved at receipt (C-68), any member of a table-less group, either participant of a one-to-one, under the per-group lock (C-62 sub-invariant 5). 10 tests + fuzz `pin_decode`; an admin posts it into the OLD group when it recreates one (a `None` successor = closed without one), the receiver admits it only from the owner or an admin resolved at receipt (C-68) under the per-group lock (C-62 sub-invariant 5), and the successor id is the admin's claim — `Open the new group` only once this device has joined it through the ordinary invitation path. 17 tests + fuzz `group_closed_decode`; on both FFI surfaces as `composeGroupClosedEnvelope(closedAtMs, successorGroupId?)` / `decodeGroupClosedEnvelope` / `groupClosedIsNewer` / `groupClosedEnvelopeMinLen` / `groupClosedEnvelopeMaxLen` + the `GroupClosed` dispatch kind (uniffi `FfiGroupClosed`, wasm `GroupClosed` with a `groupClosedError` code); `compose_registrar_seed` / `try_unwrap_registrar_seed` / `decode_registrar_seed` / `RegistrarSeedEnvelope::open`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::RegistrarSeed` (step 7d). The receiver keeps it only when addressed to its own account key and the sender resolves (C-68) to the owner. Fuzz `registrar_seed_decode`; FFI `composeRegistrarSeedEnvelope` / `decodeRegistrarSeedEnvelope` / `openRegistrarSeedEnvelope(plaintext, accountIdentity)` + the `RegistrarSeed` dispatch kind (payload = the whole envelope); the extension crate decodes it and exports the length only. **Group join-ticket (ADR-0028, G-3)** — `group_invite.rs` (`GroupInviteCapability` + `compose`/`decode_and_verify`: a bounded fail-closed Ed25519-signed capability carrying a group's `group_id` for a joiner-initiated external join; The ledger stores the sealed blob only; `/g/` keeps its preview sealed inside the capability because the plaintext capability also rides the attribution to the DS knock journal in the clear. On both FFI surfaces every compose returns `InviteLinkMint { sealed, linkSecret, issuerPubkey }` and each capability class gains an `openLink(sealed, linkSecret)` constructor + a `wire` getter (UniFFI refusal `FfiError::InviteLinkDamaged`, wasm an `Error` with `inviteLinkError: "damaged"`). FFI: `composeMediaState(cameraOn, microphoneOn)` + `decodeCallMediaState` -> `FfiCallMediaState` on UniFFI (the bool `decodeMediaState` is removed), `CallRelayPayload.mediaState(cameraOn, microphoneOn)`, `FfiIncomingEnvelope.microphoneOn`; wasm `composeMediaState(cameraOn, microphoneOn)`, `decodeCallMediaState` -> `CallMediaState`, `microphoneOn` on `CallRelayPayload` and `IncomingEnvelope`. A one-to-one call carries the same state as the call-signalling `MediaState` body (see the `lakko-calls` row). Web sends it and draws the remote mute mark; iOS and Android not yet. Client-only, no SFU/proto/server change — the SFU never parses the payload (invariant #2); UI-only, no SFrame/MLS surface. FFI-exposed (`composeMediaState`/`decodeMediaState` + the dispatch `cameraOn` field) on uniffi + wasm; wired into all three clients' SFU call sessions (broadcast on toggle / connect / peer-join; receive → `setParticipantCamera`). **Opaque push routing id ([ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md), invariant #5) + mint-epoch recency ordering ([ADR-0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md))** — `push_routing.rs`: `derive_push_gid(secret)` = **keyed-BLAKE3** with the 32-byte per-group `push_routing_secret` as the key and `"lakko/push/group-routing-id/v1"` as the message (deterministic, one-way, domain-separated, **stable** — **no epoch input**, unchanged by the v2 bump (`derive_push_gid_ignores_the_mint_epoch`), which is what lets a subscriber survive ordinary commits without re-subscribing — and golden-pinned so every platform derives the same id), plus the in-group distribution envelope `compose_push_routing_secret` / `try_unwrap_push_routing_secret` / `decode_push_routing_secret`: since ADR-0044 a fixed **70-byte** **v2** `domain(29) ‖ version(1)=0x02 ‖ mint_epoch(8, BE u64) ‖ secret(32)` payload (domain `lakko/push-routing-secret/v1\0`, **byte-identical to v1** — those 29 bytes are the `incoming_envelope` dispatch prefix and are frozen forever, so the in-payload version byte is the knob that moves) riding an **E2EE MLS application message** on the group journal, with a bounded fail-closed codec (**version gate before** exact-length + domain-match → `WrongDomain` / `BadLength` / `UnknownVersion`) and cross-kind isolation tests against every sibling envelope domain. **Hard flag day — v1 (62-byte) is refused with `UnknownVersion`, no dual-accept**, since a v1 envelope carries no `mint_epoch` and so has no position in the order. `PushRoutingSecretEnvelope { mint_epoch, secret }` is the decoded pair, and **`push_routing_secret_is_newer(incoming_epoch, incoming_secret, stored_epoch, stored_secret)`** (+ the `PushRoutingSecretEnvelope::supersedes` wrapper) is **the canonical total order, defined here once**: the pair `(mint_epoch, secret_bytes)` — epoch first, then the 32 secret bytes lexicographically — **strictly greater wins, equal is NOT newer** (what keeps a benign retry from reading as an update), wrong-length secrets **fail-closed to `false`**, never panics. `mint_epoch` is **opaque to this crate** — never read as wall-clock, never bounded, never fed to `derive_push_gid`; the crate owns the **ORDER** and requires only that one group's mints be non-decreasing, leaving the **CLAMP** (and the `Group::epoch()` binding that makes it checkable) to the caller — as well as the second caller obligation the ADR-0044 review made explicit (**C-62 sub-invariant 5**): the comparison is only meaningful against the caller's **current** stored pair, so every client must run its whole read-decide-write under one serializing per-`(account, group)` lock. A total order compared against a stale snapshot decides nothing. 41 `#[test]` in-module, incl. `a_stale_rebroadcast_never_undoes_a_rotation`, `the_owner_can_lose_an_exact_epoch_tie`, `convergence_is_arrival_order_independent`, `is_newer_is_a_strict_total_order`, `is_newer_matches_lexicographic_wire_bytes`, `domain_separator_bytes_survived_the_v2_bump`. Dispatched by the `incoming_envelope` classifier as `IncomingEnvelope::PushRoutingSecret { mint_epoch, secret }`, so it is never rendered as a chat bubble. Because the secret is independent CSPRNG input, the derived `push_gid` is algebraically unrelated to the DS `group_id` — push routes under it instead, and `services/push-gw` stays byte-identical unchanged across both ADRs (it only ever treated the value as an opaque `BINARY(32)`). FFI-exposed (`derivePushGid` / `composePushRoutingSecret` (now takes the epoch) / `decodePushRoutingSecret` (returns the pair) / **`pushRoutingSecretIsNewer`**) on uniffi + wasm — the order is consumed over the FFI so no client re-implements the comparison, three clients each hand-rolling it being exactly the drift class that produced the ADR-0044 finding. **Committer liveness beacon ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) decision 2, variant 1 — the maintainer's chosen heartbeat)** — `committer_beacon.rs`: a **field-less 27-byte** envelope `domain(26) "lakko/committer-beacon/v1\0" ‖ version(1)=0x01` riding an **E2EE MLS application message** on the group journal, `compose_committer_beacon()` (infallible, argument-less, byte-identical on every call and for every sender) / `try_unwrap_committer_beacon` / `decode_committer_beacon`, bounded + fail-closed (domain match, then **exact** length, then version gate → `WrongDomain` / `BadLength` / `UnknownVersion`), dispatched as its own `IncomingEnvelope::CommitterBeacon` **unit** variant so it is never rendered as a chat bubble, cross-kind-isolated against every sibling domain, and fuzzed (`committer_beacon_decode`). **Carrying no fields is the decision, not an oversight** — three plausible fields were each excluded for a reason recorded in the module doc: an **identity** would add an unauthenticated claim beside the MLS-authenticated sender leaf (a pure spoofing surface); a **timestamp** would let a candidate hold the committer duty by asserting its own freshness, a liveness claim **no peer can check**, where ADR-0045 measures age against the DS row's server-assigned `created_at` that every member reads identically; and a **claimed rank** is one every correct receiver must ignore, so carrying it only invites an implementation to trust it. What remains is the signal itself, plus the version byte as the forward-compat knob (ADR-0044's v2 bump proved it load-bearing on the sibling envelope). Two pure, total, clock-free functions live beside it and are consumed over the FFI so no client re-derives them: **`acting_committer_verdict(own_rank, observed, freshness_window_ms)`** → `ActingCommitter::{Act, Defer{to_rank}, NotACandidate}` (a non-candidate never sweeps and is decided without reading a beacon; **rank 0 short-circuits** so the owner never waits its turn; beacons from non-candidates and beacons past the window are dropped; only a **strictly** lower rank defers, so a device's own echoed-back beacon is harmless; **hearing nothing means Act**, which is the single most likely thing to get wrong), fed by `BeaconObservation { rank: Option<u32>, age_ms }` whose **both fields are the observer's own re-derivation** — rank from the MLS sender leaf through `members()` and `Group::committerRank`, age from the DS row, so nothing in the payload contributes to either; and **`should_emit_beacon(own_rank, last_emitted_at_ms, now_ms)`** (only candidates emit — three emitters in a 50-member group, not fifty; the first beacon of a session is immediate; otherwise one per interval; a backward local clock jump suppresses rather than spams). Four constants: `COMMITTER_BEACON_INTERVAL_MS` **60 s** and `COMMITTER_BEACON_FRESHNESS_WINDOW_MS` = `2*interval + worst_case_poll` = **150 s**, both **CHOSEN, not measured**, and the module says so — the beacon's latency budget is **not interactive** (what waits on it is a membership change ADR-0045 deliberately gave no deadline), so the interval is sized for **journal litter** (a beacon is a journal row persisting to the 30-day prune — ADR-0024 already logs that shape as a grudgingly-accepted anti-pattern, and a beacon is *continuous* rather than event-driven) and for **privacy** (each row publishes that this candidate had the app foregrounded at that moment, so a shorter interval buys a finer-grained activity schedule for no gain anyone waits on); the window's three terms each earn their place (one interval because a healthy candidate's newest beacon is already that old, a second to tolerate exactly one lost beacon, plus `COMMITTER_BEACON_WORST_CASE_POLL_MS` **30 s** — Android's deliberate long poll, observed from the client code — because a beacon is only *observed* at the observer's next poll), pinned by `freshness_window_is_the_documented_construction`. **The module is emphatic that this is NOT a leader election**: each candidate folds over its own poll-timed view, so two can both reach `Act` — a heartbeat removes the *wait*, not the *race*, and ADR-0045's stand-down journal re-read plus the staged-sweep rollback remain **required**. **Why not `lakko-presence`**, recorded because the crate's existence makes reaching for it the obvious move: its blinding buys nothing inside an already-AEAD-sealed MLS message; its `derive_token` attribution is an O(members) scan yielding a **pseudonym** where the fold needs a signature key, and a token is **not authenticating** (every member holds the seed, so any member can mint any other's) where the MLS sender leaf is; its clock model binds the **sender's** wall-clock epoch as a *matching precondition*, contradicting ADR-0045's DS-`created_at` rule and silently reading a clock-skewed candidate as permanently absent; and it is unwired (wasm-only, no UniFFI, no caller, and its `presence_seed` exporter does not exist). What would make it right is recorded too: a beacon travelling **outside** MLS. FFI-exposed on uniffi + wasm (`composeCommitterBeacon` / `decodeCommitterBeacon` / `actingCommitterVerdict` / `shouldEmitBeacon` + all four constants + `committerBeaconEnvelopeLen`). 33 `#[test]` in-module. **The mechanism is LIVE on all three clients** (ADR-0045 S4b `02f0037` web, S5+S6 `1f230ab` iOS + Android) — this row previously read *the mechanism is INERT*, which was true until those sprints and is not now. `composeCommitterBeacon` has a production call site on each (`apps/web/src/routes/Chat.tsx`, `apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatRepository.swift`, `apps/android/.../chat/ChatRepository.kt`), each driven by a **dedicated ticker rather than the poll** — a beacon's whole meaning is a cadence, and web's poll is event-driven — and each gated on **foreground**, the one rule the core cannot check and the one that rests on C-59: a beacon means *able to sweep*, the ADR-0041 background decrypt structurally cannot commit, so a background beacon would be a lie that makes every other candidate defer to a device that is never going to sweep. The fold's inputs are built the only way that is meaningful: an observation stores the sender's **32-byte signature key** resolved from the MLS-authenticated leaf at receipt, and is **ranked at verdict time** through `Group::committerRank` — never at receipt, because a rank is a *position* in the role table's canonical order and any promotion, demotion or removal renumbers everyone after it; storing a leaf would be worse still, since OpenMLS hands a vacated leaf to the next member added. Age comes from the DS row and an unparseable timestamp is **dropped rather than stored as fresh**, which would otherwise make the observer defer forever to something that can never age out. Pinned as **C-64**. **The freshness window is met on all three platforms:** it is `2 x interval + worst-case poll`, and since the web chat route gained an automatic receive loop (a 25 s DS long poll while the chat is open and the tab visible, or during a call — `apps/web/src/ds/long-poll-loop.ts`) the web observer meets it like both natives. This read "two platforms of three" while web polled only on mount, user actions and the Refresh button **Sealed push ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 3)** — `push_routing.rs` adds `derive_push_wake_signing_seed` / `derive_push_wake_public_key` / `derive_push_wake_unlock_key` (keyed-BLAKE3 under the routing secret, golden-pinned), and `push_wake.rs`: `seal_push_wake` / `open_push_wake` (a 234-byte box to push-gw's X25519 key over `push_gid ‖ kind ‖ nonce ‖ timestamp ‖ unlock key ‖ wake_pk ‖ sig`), `seal_push_subscription` / `open_push_subscription` (the device's token list under the unlock key, padded to 256-byte steps, AAD binds `push_gid` and `sub_id`), `PushProvider`; fuzz `push_wake_open`; on UniFFI and wasm as `derivePushWakePublicKey`, `derivePushWakeUnlockKey`, `pushWakeHeaderValue`, `sealPushSubscription`. **Invite links that make contacts ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md), 2026-10-16):** `invite.rs` `InvitePreview { display_name, username }` signed inside the capability (`SCHEMA_VERSION` 3, `MAX_WIRE_LEN` 663, `MAX_SEALED_LEN` 693), `compose(..., preview)`; `request.rs` `SCHEMA_VERSION` 5 with `link_answer_len(u8) || link_id(16) || link_secret(12)` (0 or 28) between the reply grant and the intro, `LinkAnswer` (redacted, constant-time `matches`), `OpenedRequest.link_answer`; `link_seal::link_secrets_match`; the two fuzz targets extended  **Device card ([ADR-0080](docs/adr/0080-device-cards.md))** — `device_card.rs`: `DeviceCard`, `DevicePlatform` (ios / ipados / macos / android / web / desktop, frozen bytes 1-6), `compose_device_card` / `try_unwrap_device_card` / `decode_device_card` / `device_card_is_newer` / `DeviceCard::normalized`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::DeviceCard` (step 7i); the extension crate answers `isDeviceCardEnvelope` and the lengths only. 15 tests + fuzz `device_card_decode`. **Link-request claim ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md), reworked 2026-10-17)** — `link_claim.rs`: one of the inviter's devices claims a request sent through one of the account's invite links on the account's DeviceGroup journal, so only one device answers it (claim-then-build, the ADR-0073 R.6 pattern). `LinkClaim { link_id, requester_account_key }` and its `request_key` (48 bytes), envelope `lakko/link-claim/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ link_id(16) ‖ requester_account_key(32)` (69 bytes, version before length), `compose_link_claim` (infallible) / `try_unwrap_link_claim` / `decode_link_claim`, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::LinkClaim` (step 7j), and the pure decision over claims the client feeds (`ObservedLinkClaim { message_id, author_key }`, the author a DeviceGroup leaf key resolved at receipt or the device's own): `link_claim_winner(claims, roster_keys)` (the earliest claim from a current roster device), `link_claim_outcome(own_key, claims, roster_keys)` -> `Won` / `Lost` / `NoWinner`, `link_claim_answered_elsewhere`; proptests `exactly_one_device_wins_across_any_interleaving`, `an_unrecorded_earlier_own_claim_still_wins`, `a_removed_devices_claim_hands_the_request_to_the_next_current_claim` + fuzz `link_claim_decode`; on UniFFI `composeLinkClaimEnvelope` / `decodeLinkClaimEnvelope` / `isLinkClaimEnvelope` / `linkClaimRequestKey` / `linkClaimOutcome` (-> the enum `FfiLinkClaimOutcome`) / `linkClaimAnsweredElsewhere` / `linkClaimEnvelopeLen` + the appended `LinkClaim` dispatch kind, the same on wasm (`linkClaimOutcome(ownKey, claimIds, claimAuthors, rosterKeys)` -> `LinkClaimDecision`); the extension crate answers `isLinkClaimEnvelope` and the length only. **Link-founded note (ADR-0079, 2026-10-17)** - `link_founded.rs`: `LinkFounded { link_id, requester_account_key, group_id(32), welcome }`, envelope `lakko/link-founded/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ link_id(16) ‖ requester_account_key(32) ‖ group_id(32) ‖ welcome_len(u16 BE) ‖ welcome` (Welcome 1 to `LINK_FOUNDED_MAX_WELCOME_LEN` = 32 768; `a_maximal_one_to_one_welcome_fits_the_note` in `lakko-ffi-uniffi` measures the bound. **Journal readability (ADR-0079, 2026-10-17)** - `journal_epoch.rs`: `JournalEpochs { base, commits }` (at most `JOURNAL_EPOCHS_MAX_COMMITS` = 64 effective commits by row id, older ones folded into the base), `epoch_at(row_id)`, `row_counts(row_id, sealed_epoch)` (a claim or note counts only when sealed at the journal's epoch at its row) and `observe(row_id, sealed, epoch_after, group_epoch_after)` (a commit takes effect when sealed at the journal's epoch at its row and the device's group moved past it); proptests `a_straddled_claim_never_wins_and_one_device_builds` and `a_straddled_note_never_counts`; on UniFFI `FfiJournalEpochs` + `journalEpochsStartingAt` / `journalRowCounts` / `journalEpochsObserve`, on wasm the `JournalEpochs` class, and `DeviceGroup.epoch()` on both (read through `as_group()`, no `lakko-mls` change) **Link refusal (ADR-0079 amendment, 2026-10-19)** - `link_refusal.rs`: `compose_link_refusal(inviter_signing_key, link_id, requester_account_key)` (infallible), `decode_link_refusal` (domain, version before length, exactly 135 bytes), `verify_link_refusal(wire, inviter_key, expected_link_id, own_account_key)` (`OtherLink` / `OtherRequester` / `BadKey` / `BadSignature`, codes via `code()`), carried as `MailboxKind::LinkRefusal` (5); proptests + fuzz `link_refusal_decode`; on UniFFI and wasm `composeLinkRefusal` / `verifyLinkRefusal` / `linkRefusalLen` **Contact request inside a group ([ADR-0086](docs/adr/0086-circle-directories.md) section 6, built ahead of the rest of that ADR 2026-11-05; core and FFI, host-tested, no client uses it yet)** - `group_contact_request.rs`: for a person no held reach grant reaches, the ordinary contact-request wire travels inside a group both are in, sealed whole to the target's X25519 key (the crate's sealed box under labels of its own), so the envelope `lakko/group-contact-request/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ sealed_len(4 BE) ‖ sealed` names nobody (maintainer, 2026-11-05: other members must not learn whom it is for; request 1 to `MAILBOX_MAX_BODY_LEN`, envelope min 85 bytes). `seal_group_contact_request(recipient_x25519_pub, request)`, `compose_group_contact_request` refusing what `decode_group_contact_request` refuses, dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::GroupContactRequest` (step 7o, ahead of the intro fallback), and `check_group_contact_request(envelope, own_x25519_priv, sender_account_key)`, the receiver's rule every member runs: `NotForMe` where the box does not open (drop, log nothing that names anyone), else the inner request must verify and its `from_pubkey` must be the account the MLS sender resolves to at receipt, C-68 (`requestInvalid` / `senderMismatch`); the client then drops it unless its setting lets this group's members find it (`allowsScope`) or the sender is a contact (UX `who-can-find-me.md` 6.4). Other members learn that one box was posted, when, and its length. On UniFFI and wasm as `composeGroupContactRequestEnvelope(recipientX25519Pub, request)` / `checkGroupContactRequest(plaintext, recipientX25519Priv, senderAccountKey)` (returns the request wire, or none / `undefined` when not for this member) / `isGroupContactRequestEnvelope` / `groupContactRequestEnvelopeMinLen` / `MaxLen` + the appended `GroupContactRequest` dispatch kind (payload = the whole envelope; wasm kind 27); the extension crate answers `isGroupContactRequestEnvelope` and the lengths only; fuzz `group_contact_request_decode` **Phase B of [ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) (2026-11-06, host-tested):** `channel_read_key.rs`, the owner's channel read key sealed to one co-publisher's account X25519 key, `lakko/channel-read-key/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ channel_id(16) ‖ member_account_key(32) ‖ sealed(80)` = 155 bytes, header as AAD, no version (the read key never changes), dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::ChannelReadKey` (step 7n2), fuzz `channel_read_key_decode`; on UniFFI `composeChannelReadKeyEnvelope(channelId, memberAccountKey, memberX25519PublicKey, readKey)` / `decodeChannelReadKeyEnvelope` / `openChannelReadKeyEnvelope(plaintext, accountIdentity)` / `channelReadKeyEnvelopeLen` (errors `channelReadKey.<code>`) and the appended dispatch kind `ChannelReadKey`, on wasm the same names and dispatch kind 28; the extension crate recognises it (`isChannelReadKeyEnvelope`, 155). **Earlier seeds to a new publisher (ADR-0087 section 7 amendment, maintainer 2026-11-08; core and FFI, host-tested, no client wired):** `channel_epoch_seeds.rs`, `lakko/channel-epoch-seeds/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ channel_id(16) ‖ member_account_key(32) ‖ count(1) ‖ sealed(48 + 36 × count)`, the list `count × (epoch_id(4 BE) ‖ seed(32))` sealed once to the member's account X25519 key with the clear header as AAD; count 1 to `CHANNEL_EPOCH_SEEDS_MAX` = 64 (chosen) in the clear so the length is exact before opening, the opened list re-checked (count, strictly ascending epochs), 163 to 2,431 bytes; `compose_channel_epoch_seeds` (any order, refuses none, more than 64 or an epoch twice), `try_unwrap_` / `decode_channel_epoch_seeds`, `ChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope::open` -> `Vec<ChannelEpochSeed>` (seed redacted in `Debug`, zeroed on drop), dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::ChannelEpochSeeds` (step 7n3), proptests + fuzz `channel_epoch_seeds_decode`. The owner's device sends it to a publisher it approved, beside the read key, and records per (channel, publisher, epoch) what it sent; a receiver keeps it only on that channel's PublisherGroup, addressed to its account, from the owner resolved at receipt (C-68), and adds only epochs it lacks. On UniFFI `composeChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope(channelId, memberAccountKey, memberX25519PublicKey, seeds: [FfiChannelEpochSeed{epoch, seed}])` / `decodeChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope` -> `FfiChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope{channelId, memberAccountKey, count}` / `openChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope(plaintext, accountIdentity)` -> `[FfiChannelEpochSeed]` / `channelEpochSeedsMax` / `channelEpochSeedsEnvelopeMinLen` / `MaxLen` (errors `channelEpochSeeds.<code>`) and the appended dispatch kind `ChannelEpochSeeds`; on wasm the same names with parallel `epochs: number[]` + flat `seeds` (32 bytes each) in and a `ChannelEpochSeeds` class (`epochs`, flat `seeds`) out, dispatch kind 29; the extension crate recognises it (`isChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope`, 163 to 2,431). **A publisher asks the owner to rotate (same amendment; only the owner's device rotates a channel since 2026-11-08):** `channel_rotate_request.rs`, a fixed 49-byte `lakko/channel-rotate-request/v1\0 ‖ 0x01 ‖ channel_id(16)` a publisher sends on the PublisherGroup after removing a subscriber; the owner's device rotates at its next publisher pass when the MLS sender resolves at receipt to a current publisher, ignores it from anyone else, and folds requests before the rotation into one; `compose_channel_rotate_request` (infallible) / `try_unwrap_` / `decode_channel_rotate_request` -> the channel id, `IncomingEnvelope::ChannelRotateRequest([u8; 16])` (step 7n4), proptests + fuzz `channel_rotate_request_decode`; on UniFFI `composeChannelRotateRequestEnvelope(channelId)` / `decodeChannelRotateRequestEnvelope(plaintext)` -> channel id / `channelRotateRequestEnvelopeLen` (49), errors `channelRotateRequest.<code>`, appended dispatch kind `ChannelRotateRequest` (payload = the channel id); on wasm the same names, dispatch kind 30; the extension crate recognises it (`isChannelRotateRequestEnvelope`, 49). The co-publisher preview (kind 2) carries an inviter name (`compose_co_publisher_invite_capability(..., inviter_name)`, a trailing optional argument on both FFI surfaces) and `ChannelInviteCard::open` accepts kind 2 as well as kind 3; a channel preview refuses a member count. `group_invite::group_link_record_id(ledger_id)` = first 16 bytes of `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/sync/group-link-id/v1", ledger_id)`, the link-register id of a `/g/` link **Retired `contact` keys ([ADR-0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) section 1, maintainer decision 2026-11-06, host-tested):** the `contact` key stays one shared key per account; Remove contact rotates it (version + 1, the new key registered, the profile with the new grant republished in every remaining contact's one-to-one). `reach.rs`: `RETIRED_CONTACT_VERSIONS_KEPT` = 8 (chosen, not measured), `contact_key_versions(current)` (the versions to keep registered: current, then up to eight retired), `classify_contact_key(account_seed, current_version, proving_key)` -> `ContactKeyStanding::{Current, Retired{version}, NotContactKey}`, and `contact_key_row_verdict(standing, sender_is_contact)` -> `ContactKeyRowVerdict::{Process, ProcessAndRegrant, Drop}`, the one rule: a mailbox row under a retired key from a current contact is processed and the current grant republished to them, from anyone else dropped. On UniFFI `retiredContactVersionsKept` / `contactKeyVersions` / `classifyContactKey` / `contactKeyRowVerdict` / `mailboxRowContactVerdict(accountSeed, currentContactVersion, provingKey?, senderIsContact)` with `FfiContactKeyStanding` / `FfiContactKeyRowVerdict`; on wasm the same names (`ContactKeyStanding` class with `kind` / `version`, `ContactKeyRowVerdict` 0 / 1 / 2); the extension crate carries `mailboxRowContactVerdict` only (`contact_key.rs`) **PublisherGroup device envelopes ([ADR-0090](docs/adr/0090-publisher-group-device-leaves.md), 2026-11-14):** `publisher_device_request.rs`, a co-publisher's publishing device asks an owner device to admit or retire one of its devices on the PublisherGroup (`lakko/publisher-device-request/v1\0`, 84 to 7208 bytes: channel id and either a KeyPackage, the requesting account's 97-byte mailbox binding and 0 to 16 committer delegations, or a 32-byte device key), and `channel_admission_mark.rs`, a publishing device's admission mark merged by maximum (`lakko/channel-admission-mark/v1\0`, 57 bytes); dispatched as `IncomingEnvelope::PublisherDeviceRequest` / `ChannelAdmissionMark` (steps 7n5 / 7n6), cross-kind isolation tests, fuzz `publisher_device_request_decode` and `channel_admission_mark_decode`. An owner device acts on a request only from a current publisher account whose KeyPackage binding (admit) or named leaf (retire) is that account. On UniFFI `composePublisherDeviceAdmit` / `composePublisherDeviceRetire` / `decodePublisherDeviceRequestEnvelope` / `publisherDeviceRequestDelegations` / `composeChannelAdmissionMarkEnvelope` / `decodeChannelAdmissionMarkEnvelope` and the appended dispatch kinds `publisherDeviceRequest` / `channelAdmissionMark`, on wasm the same names (dispatch kinds 31 / 32); the extension surface recognises both (`isPublisherDeviceRequestEnvelope`, `isChannelAdmissionMarkEnvelope`) **Own binding verdict (`own_binding.rs`, [ADR-0074](docs/adr/0074-account-takeover-resistance.md) "Joining after a rotation", 2026-11-15, G-56):** `binding_verdict(bound, current, predecessors, OwnLink::{Absent, Key, Unknown}, holds_conversations)` -> `BindingVerdict::{Keep, KeepUnderCurrentKey, Wait, Mint(key)}`, the one rule for which account key a device of the account binds its conversation identity under: target = the conversation key the own link names, else the oldest held predecessor, else the current key; a binding under a held predecessor, or under the current key on a device holding conversations, is kept; a target whose seed is not held, or an unreadable link when a mint is due, is `Wait` (the device binds nothing, publishes no KeyPackage and joins nothing until the earlier keys arrive). The 17 shared vectors are its test table (`the_shared_truth_table`), carried in every client suite through the FFI. On UniFFI as `ownBindingVerdict` -> `FfiBindingVerdict` with `FfiOwnLink`, on wasm as `ownBindingVerdict` -> `BindingVerdict` (`kind`, `mintKey`, `isKept`) with `OwnLinkKind` |
| `lakko-auth-client` | Pure-Rust **OPAQUE-PAKE client** for `services/auth` — the registration + login halves of the flow `services/auth/src/opaque.rs` implements server-side, plus the wire types and the cipher-suite pin. This is where the `opaque-ke` dependency (and, transitively, `voprf`) enters the client graph |
| `lakko-auth-token` | HMAC-SHA256 **session credentials** issue + verify — the contract between `services/auth` (issues) and every bearer verifier (`auth`, `discovery`, `storage`, `gif-proxy`). **Since [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (2026-10-03, flag day, `v1` refused) two five-part credentials:** the **access token** `v2.<account_id_b64url>.<key_generation>.<expires_at_ms>.<tag>` (tag under the shared secret; the bearer everywhere; default TTL 900 s) and the **refresh credential** `r1.<account_id_b64url>.<key_generation>.<expires_at_ms>.<tag>` (tag under `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/session-refresh/v1", secret)`, so an access verifier refuses it by version AND could never mistake its tag; accepted by `POST /auth/session/refresh` only; default 30 days, sliding; no random id, no DB row: revoked by the generation, never individually). `TokenSecret::{issue, issue_refresh, verify, verify_refresh}`; **a third kind since 2026-10-04, generalised 2026-10-06 into the fresh-auth grant** `k1.<account_id_b64url>.<key_generation>.<expires_at_ms>.<tag>` (tag under `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/fresh-auth-grant/v1", secret)`; minted by auth's password `POST /login/finish`, passkey `POST /webauthn/auth/finish` and `POST /signup/finish`; proves the person authenticated within `LAKKO_FRESH_GRANT_TTL_SECS`, default 600 s; reusable within its lifetime, dead after a key reset because it names the generation; `issue_fresh_grant` / `verify_fresh_grant`); `verify` returns `VerifiedToken { account_id, key_generation }`. The access token is stateless at every verifier but auth, which refuses a generation below the account's on every bearer request. `blake3` is a direct dependency for the refresh key. Unit tests for every refusal + the `token_verify` fuzz target, which drives both kinds |
| `lakko-ds-proof` | **The request proof a claimed DS journal requires ([ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md))**, shared by `services/ds` (verify) and both FFI surfaces (sign, S2) the way `lakko-auth-token` is shared by auth and discovery. `canonical_bytes` = `"lakko/ds/proof/v1" ‖ 0 ‖ method ‖ 0 ‖ path ‖ 0 ‖ query ‖ 0 ‖ BLAKE3(body) ‖ epoch(8 BE) ‖ unix_ms(8 BE) ‖ nonce(16)` (NUL separators so no field can run into the next; the body enters as a hash so the proof binds the published bytes; golden-pinned `881c504b…`), `sign` / `verify` (window first - `PROOF_WINDOW_MS` = 120 s - then Ed25519; errors `Malformed` / `UnknownVersion` / `OutsideWindow` / `BadSignature`), the header `x-lakko-ds-proof: v1.<epoch>.<ms>.<nonce_b64url>.<sig_b64url>` (`Proof::encode` / `parse`, `MAX_HEADER_LEN` 256), `access_signing_key(exporter_seed)` (the per-epoch group key from `Group::export_secret("lakko/ds/access/v1", group_id, 32)`), `MAILBOX_EPOCH` = 0, and `mailbox_pseudonym` / `mailbox_owner_matches` over the ADR-0005 `IdentityPseudonym` derivation (used, not copied). **The client half is `DsProofSigner`** - `for_group(epoch, exporter_seed)` / `for_mailbox(identity_seed)`, `sign_at(req, now_ms, nonce)` (clock and nonce are the caller's, so one type serves the host, the browser and the NSE), `verifying_key`, and the two request bodies a claim needs, `group_claim_body_json` / `mailbox_claim_body_json`, defined here so no client spells a field name. Depends on `lakko-crypto` + `lakko-contacts` only. 10 tests + fuzz `proof_parse`. **On all three FFI surfaces (S2)**: `Group.dsAccessSigner(identity)` / `DeviceGroup.dsAccessSigner` / `PublisherGroup.dsAccessSigner` (the exporter derivation at the current epoch - re-derive after every commit), `dsMailboxSigner(identity)`, `dsProofHeaderName()`, `dsMailboxEpoch()`, and `DsProofSigner.{epoch, verifyingKey, sign(method, path, query, body), groupClaimBodyJson, mailboxClaimBodyJson, mailboxPseudonym}` on UniFFI + wasm (`ds_proof.rs` in each; wasm reads `Date.now()` on wasm32 and the system clock on the host); the extension surface carries `Group.dsAccessSigner` + `dsMailboxSigner` + `sign` only - no claim body, since registering a key is the foreground app's write (C-59). `groupClaimBodyJson` is **superseded by ADR-0057** (the DS no longer accepts that body). **Journal ownership ([ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)) - `statement.rs`:** `journal_root_seed(account_identity_seed, journal_id)` = BLAKE3-derive-key(`lakko/ds/journal-root/v1`, seed ‖ id) and `journal_registrar_seed(root_seed, member_account_key)` = BLAKE3-derive-key(`lakko/ds/journal-registrar/v1`, root ‖ member) (golden-pinned), `StatementSigner`, and one signed binary statement per kind (`claim` / `reset` / `access` register-epoch with revocations / `registrars` / `root` transfer / `push-binding`): `lakko/ds/statement/v1\0 ‖ kind ‖ journal_id ‖ signer ‖ unix_ms ‖ nonce ‖ fields ‖ sig`, bounded (`MAX_STATEMENT_LEN`, lists <= 256 distinct keys), exact, fail-closed, sent as `{"statement_b64"}` and verified by window then signature; `push_binding_key(pepper, push_gid)` is the DS's keyed hash. Fuzz `statement_decode`. FFI: `dsJournalRootSeed` / `dsJournalRegistrarSeed` / `dsJournalRootSigner(accountIdentity, journalId)` / `dsStatementSigner(seed)` -> `DsStatementSigner.{publicKey, claim, reset, registerEpoch, replaceEpoch, registrars, registrarsSet, rootTransfer, pushBinding}` returning `{path, bodyJson}` (`ds_statement.rs` on UniFFI + wasm). **Since the second security pass:** `RegisterEpoch` carries a signed mode byte (`RegisterMode::{Append, Replace}`; `registerEpoch` = Append, `replaceEpoch` = Replace), a seventh kind `RegistrarsSet` (route `registrars-set`, up to `MAX_REGISTRARS` = 4096 entries, the signer may not list itself), and `composeRootHandoverEnvelope` / rank `root` (3) on the registrar-seed envelope carries the old owner's root seed to a new owner **The `push-binding` statement is retired by ADR-0078 phase 3:** its kind byte 6 is refused and `push_binding_key` and `pushBinding` are gone from both FFI surfaces. **ADR-0081 (2026-10-18) - `commit.rs`:** `commit_body_json(statement, row)` (`{"statement_b64", "row_b64"}`, an Append `RegisterEpoch` only, a non-empty row), `commit_row_hash` (`BLAKE3(row)`, what the DS keeps with the registration), `is_commit_statement`, `COMMIT_PATH_SEGMENT`, `StatementSigner::sign_commit_at`; on both FFI surfaces as `DsStatementSigner.commit(journalId, epoch, epochKey, revoke, row)` returning the `/commit` path and the exact body; `registerEpoch` is removed from both surfaces (the DS refuses a plain Append on `/access`). |
| `lakko-device-link` | **The camera-less add-device confirmation ([ADR-0049](docs/adr/0049-identity-recovery-word-vault.md) section 2, amended 2026-09-28).** The signed-in (old) device chooses six uniformly random digits (rejection sampling) and shows them; the person types them on the new device; SPAKE2 (RustCrypto `spake2` 0.4, Ed25519 group, old device = side B and moves first) over that code, with both identities and the transcript binding the auth request id and the login handle, yields a payload key and two HMAC confirmation keys (HKDF-SHA-256). The new device seals the ADR-0056 pairing payload (ChaCha20-Poly1305, transcript as AAD) and sends `confirm_a` over the transcript and the sealed bytes; `Responder::accept(self, ..)` checks it in constant time and opens the payload, else `WrongCode` - consumed, so one guess per request; `ConfirmationCheck::verify` checks the old device's `confirm_b`. Replaces the SHA-256 comparison code a relay could grind (the security pass finding). Deps: `lakko-crypto` + `spake2` (default features off, no new backend: curve25519-dalek 4 / hkdf 0.12 / sha2 0.10 already in the graph; not independently audited and does not zeroize its own scalar or password copy - recorded for G-39). 12 tests (incl. a 200k-sample digit-uniformity check and three proptests) + fuzz `device_link_accept`; on UniFFI (`DeviceLinkResponder` / `DeviceLinkInitiator` / `deviceLinkNormalizeCode` / `deviceLinkFormatCode`, `FfiError::DeviceLinkWrongCode` + `DeviceLink` appended) and wasm (same names, `"<code>: <reason>"` errors) |
| `lakko-discovery-client` | Pure-Rust **VOPRF client** for `services/discovery` — blinds a username (`blinder.rs`) so the server evaluates without learning it, plus `normalize.rs`, the NFKC + lowercase + trim canonicalisation both sides must agree on byte-for-byte. **Username character set ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) §4):** `validate_username` normalises then requires `[a-z0-9_]`, 3-32 characters (the rule itself is `lakko_contacts::username::check_canonical_username`, re-exported with `UsernameError`), and `OprfBlinder::blind` runs it before blinding (`DiscoveryClientError::InvalidUsername`), so neither register nor lookup can carry a non-conforming name — reversing the module's earlier stance of not rejecting confusables. `blind_channel` deliberately keeps its own policy (normalise, refuse empty). 25 tests |

a wrong or unknown word is `Ok(false)`, only malformed input is a `WordCheckError` with a stable kebab code), written once so the three clients cannot disagree; nothing in it touches a server. **`vault.rs`** is the passkey vault only: `seal_entropy_under_secret` / `open_entropy_under_secret` seal the 16-byte entropy under `HKDF-SHA-256(prf_output, "lakko/passkey-vault/seal/v1")` (a 44-byte blob); the word vault (positions, Argon2id stretch, verifier, sealed entropy) was deleted on 2026-10-02. 28 `#[test]` across the crate (counted, not run for this row) + the `mnemonic_parse` fuzz target **`recovery_payload.rs` ([ADR-0074](docs/adr/0074-account-takeover-resistance.md) "Joining after a rotation", 2026-11-15, G-56):** the another-device recovery payload **v3**, the codec the three clients used to hand-write as v2 (now deleted there): `compose_recovery_payload` / `decode_recovery_payload` over `grant(32) ‖ phrase_entropy(16) ‖ count(1, 0..=8) ‖ count × (seed(64) ‖ adopted_at_ms(8 BE))` (own predecessors oldest first; exact length `49 + 72 × count`; a duplicate seed, or the seed the entropy itself derives, refused), `wrap_recovery_row` / `unwrap_recovery_row` (`"lakko/recovery-payload/v3" ‖ sealed`; another version, v2 included, refused as `unsupported-row`), `RECOVERY_EXPORTER_LABEL` `lakko/device-recovery/v3`; errors with frozen kebab codes; the golden 193-byte plaintext pinned here and in both FFI crates; fuzz `recovery_payload_decode`. On UniFFI and wasm as `composeRecoveryPayload` / `decodeRecoveryPayload` / `wrapRecoveryRow` / `unwrapRecoveryRow` / `recoveryExporterLabel` / `recoveryExporterKeyLen` / `recoveryPredecessorsMax` (`FfiError::RecoveryPayload` appended last); the seal stays the client's (`exportSecret` + `sealWithKey`) |
