every client used to loop it for any call not yet connected, so a person alone in a group call heard a phone ringing), `call_grid(count, width, height)` (equal tiles filling the call area with no scroll: the column count that gives the largest tile within a 3:4..16:9 shape and the class's minimum tile width, pages beyond the cap of 6 / 9 / 16; integer arithmetic so the three clients round alike) and `call_stage(people, pinned)` (pinned, else speaking, else most recent speaker, else first with a picture, else first other person; own tile only when alone); on both FFI surfaces as `ringbackPlays` / `callGrid` / `callStage`. **And `layer_display`** (2026-10-04, ADR-0067 step 5): which of a remote person's two video tracks a tile shows, the main one or its light companion (`LayerDisplay`: frame and tick events in milliseconds in, `ShownLayer` out, counters of switches and displayed stalls; thresholds chosen, not measured: a stall is counted after 150 ms, the tile leaves the full track only after 300 ms without a frame (`FULL_STOPPED_MS`, raised after a live call where a publisher's frames arrived in clumps), a light track is playing within 250 ms, the way back takes 1000 ms steady, a first frame is waited for 1500 ms), as a `LayerDisplay` object on UniFFI and wasm. **And two rules of [ADR-0070](docs/adr/0070-call-media-through-a-loss-burst.md) step 5 in `call_group`:** `tile_video_quality(people, tile_width_px, pinned_or_stage)` (which picture a tile asks the SFU for: one other person, or a pinned / stage tile at least 960 px wide, main; under 240 px, light; else middle) and `middle_sender_wanted(people)` (the middle camera sender is active from three people), on both FFI surfaces as `tileVideoQuality` / `middleSenderWanted` with the three constants; read by the web client and, since 2026-10-11, by iOS and Android. Five so far, and the last two were each minted the day a person found the clients disagreeing: `capture::call_holds_capture` (whether a live call is holding the microphone, so the composer's recorder must stand aside — the argument is "is a call live AT ALL", not "in this chat", because a call collapsed into the bar of `docs/ux/calls.md` section 4.5 is live in every chat) and `call_bar` (whether that bar is on screen and which ONE thing its status line says — a sum type rather than three booleans, because the spec's "never two at once" is an invariant a view expressed in booleans is one careless branch from breaking). Then: `conversation::order_conversations` (the order of the ONE merged conversation list — pinned first, archived last, newest between, keyed on a six-field `ConversationRow`, returning the input **INDICES** rather than rows so a client reorders what it already holds instead of marshalling every avatar and preview across an FFI boundary twice; total, so two conversations nothing has happened in yet never reshuffle on an unrelated refresh) and `lookup::looks_like_a_name` (whether a typed string is worth a network lookup — **the predicate IS `lakko_contacts::username::check_canonical_username`**, not a copy of it, so it cannot drift behind the rule the discovery blinder enforces; asking first is the difference between a quiet "type the whole name" and making a person pay a proof of work to be told no), and `info` (what the name at the top of a conversation opens and what is on it — `info_target` plus `person_info_sections` / `group_info_sections` / `channel_info_sections` over three capability records the CLIENT computes and this crate never re-derives, with section 2.1's `Never` list carried as `InfoSection::group_only` on each block so a group block added later inherits the rule instead of having to be remembered into it). **The channel info since 2026-11-05 ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 15, UX `group-and-channel-privacy.md` 12.1, 14, 15; host-tested, no client moved yet):** `channel_info_sections` takes `ChannelInfoCapabilities { role: ChannelRole (Owner / Publisher / Subscriber), has_handle, has_other_publishers, members_of_groups_and_channels_on }` and returns a group's order: `Handle` (with an `@name`), `ChannelDescription`, `RenameGroup` (owner), `InvitePeople` + `InviteLink` (owner, publishers), `Subscribers` (everyone), `Admins` = Publishers (owner, publishers), `Reactions` + `GroupType` (owner), `MembersCanFindMe` (new block, appended last, `members-can-find-me`; while the setting includes groups and channels), then `Unsubscribe` (a subscriber) or `DeleteGroup` (the owner, no publisher left); the Manage block is gone and `ChannelHandle` / `SubscriberCount` / `ManageChannel` / `ChannelMembers` are produced by no rule (kept for the uniffi ordinals). Public or private changes no block and is not an input. `conversation_info_sections` answers a channel through this rule (admin = publisher, alone = no publisher left, no switch); its group arm and `person_info_sections` / `group_info_sections` are unchanged. On UniFFI as the `ChannelRole` enum + the record, on wasm as `ChannelRole` (0 / 1 / 2) + `new ChannelInfoCapabilities(role, hasHandle, hasOtherPublishers, membersOfGroupsAndChannelsOn)`. **The third one is the worked example of why this crate exists**, and the cost is on the record: the info rule was written three times — Android as enum cases, iOS as a struct of booleans, web as an array of kebab strings — and the three disagreed on three separate points, each a sentence of one spec rather than a platform difference. `Remove contact` existed on web and Android while iOS carried a comment asserting no client had one; `Unsubscribe` existed on web and on neither native, whose comments asserted the operation did not exist while both already held the two functions it is made of; and `Delete this chat` reached an ended one-to-one only on Android, so on the other two a one-to-one that ended could never be closed at all. All three were found by a person opening two phones side by side, not by any test. Here for the same reason `should_request_profiles`, `acting_committer_verdict` and `push_routing_secret_is_newer` are in Rust: three hand-written copies of one rule drift, and the drift is only found when a person notices the clients disagreeing. **Deliberately its own crate rather than a module of `lakko-contacts`** — sorting a conversation list is not a fact about contacts, and the crates an external reviewer reads ([`docs/security/external-review-scope.md`](docs/security/external-review-scope.md)) should not grow presentation logic; nothing here is security-relevant and nothing here should become so, so a rule that needs a key, a secret or a peer's assertion is in the wrong crate. Its only dependency is `lakko-contacts`, for that one username rule. 27 tests; on **both** FFI surfaces (`orderConversations` / `bareName` / `looksLikeAName` / `infoTarget` / `personInfoSections` / `groupInfoSections` / `channelInfoSections` + the `ConversationKind` / `ConversationRow` pair and the three capability records — uniffi records and constructible wasm classes following `BeaconObservation`), so no client re-implements any of the rules. One asymmetry worth stating, because it is what the tests on each side can and cannot do: the sections cross to uniffi as a typed `InfoSection` enum and to wasm as their **kebab-case names**, which is the vocabulary web's own section type has always been written in — so web is handed the names rather than translating them. iOS unit tests link `LakkoCore` and so can assert the rule THROUGH the boundary, and so does **web**: its vitest loads the wasm through the node glue, which several suites already do — the earlier claim here that it has no wasm was stale and was repeated into three agent briefs before a web agent corrected it (2026-09-24). Android's host JVM suite genuinely has no native library, so there alone the rule is asserted once in Rust and the client tests cover the wiring. The distinction matters: a client test driving a spy can only assert WHAT THE RULE WAS ASKED, never that the client asked it about the right thing — and handing a rule the wrong input is the failure a spy is blind to by construction **`av_align` (2026-10-12, [ADR-0070](docs/adr/0070-call-media-through-a-loss-burst.md) note of that date):** keeping one remote person's sound and picture together in a group call. One `AvAlign` per remote person; `read(now_ms, readings)` takes, for the audio receiver and each of the main / middle / light video tracks, whether packets flow, the measured jitter-buffer delay, the network-only need where reported, whether a tile shows the track and whether the engine synchronises it, and returns the playout-delay target to set per receiver with the skew and a reason per track. Integer milliseconds, saturating, no clock of its own; told which setters the platform has (`AvCapability`) and says what it then cannot do; every constant chosen, not measured. 31 tests in the module by a count of test attributes (one a proptest over arbitrary readings, plus a deterministic receiver model); nothing seen on a call. On both FFI surfaces as the `AvAlign` object, `avAlignCapability`, `avCapabilityName` / `avAudioReasonName` / `avVideoReasonName`, `avAlignReadingMs` / `avAlignDeadBandMs` / `avAlignSoundWaitMaxMs`; consumed by web `av-align.ts`, iOS `AvAlignment.swift`, Android `AvAlign.kt`, none of which restates the rule **`message_links` (2026-11-01, `docs/ux/message-links.md`):** `split_message_links(text)` returns the text as pieces with or without a link (pieces, not offsets: the clients count characters differently), joining back to the input: `http` or `https` at a word start, ending at white space or an invisible character, sentence punctuation and an unopened closing bracket left out, no user before the host, at most 2048 bytes; it decides what is drawn as a link and nothing about safety. On UniFFI and wasm as `splitMessageLinks`. **`initials` (2026-11-04, `.claude/skills/ux-ui/design-system.md` Avatar):** `avatar_initials(name)` gives the letters of a photo-less avatar (first letter of each of the first two words, else the first two letters of a one-word name; upper case, grapheme clusters, white space ignored, clusters with no letter, digit or emoji skipped, empty when nothing counts), on UniFFI as `avatarInitials(name: String)` and on wasm as `avatarInitials(name)`. **`guest_name` (2026-10-17):** the name prefilled for a guest joining a call from a link, "<adjective> <animal>" from the 48 + 50 words the earlier Lakko call client used (`guest_name(random)`, the randomness is the caller's); every pair passes the ADR-0048 display-name rule (test). On UniFFI as `guestName(random: u64)`, on wasm as `guestName(random: u32)`; web `JoinCall.tsx`, iOS `JoinCallLandingScreen.swift`, Android `JoinCallScreen.kt` prefill with it (Android falls back to the catalog word where the native library is absent, the host tests) **`subscribers` (2026-11-13, `docs/ux/group-and-channel-privacy.md` 15.1 / 7.3; host-tested, not walked):** `subscriber_list(cards, listed_keys, count, can_remove)` builds a channel's subscriber list from the member cards a viewer opened (subscriber key, account, named, self) and, for the owner and publishers of a private channel, every registered key: a card with no name is not a named row, one row per account with all its keys, a named person is never listed again among the others, `Remove` on every row but the viewer's own when `can_remove`, and the "{n} others." count (count minus the keys shown by name; never below the rows listed); found when an owner saw a nameless card as "Member" and both subscribers again as anonymous rows. On UniFFI as `channelSubscriberList` (`ChannelSubscriberCard` / `ChannelSubscriberPerson` / `ChannelSubscriberList`), on wasm as `channelSubscriberList(cardSubscriberKeys, cardPersonKeys, cardFlags, listedKeys, count, canRemove)` **ADR-0083 (2026-10-25):** `StageCandidate.is_screen` (UniFFI default false, wasm stage flag 8): `call_stage` stages a share after a pin and before the speaker, and never this device's own share **`comments` (2026-11-13):** `collapsed_comments(visible, rows_read, first_page_read, more_rows, server_count)` says what follows the first two comments under a collapsed channel post (nothing, `Show {n} more comments`, `Show more comments` or `Comment`), never counting a comment the reader hides (a banned author's, one that does not open) and taking only unread rows from the service's count; on UniFFI as `collapsedComments` -> `CollapsedComments`, on wasm as `collapsedComments` -> `CollapsedComments` (`kind`, `count`), called by all three clients **`media_mosaic` (2026-11-19, `docs/ux/media-messages.md` section 11):** `media_mosaic(bubble_width, gap, items: &[MediaSize]) -> MediaMosaic` lays out the photos and videos of one message in its bubble (1 item: the section 10 box; 2 side by side; 3 one large and two stacked; 4 to 10 in rows of 2 and 3; more than 10 clamped to the first 10), integer arithmetic, tiles in input order with `x` from the start edge, every row filling the width; constants chosen, not measured (`MOSAIC_GAP` 2, row heights 80..200, single 360, pair 260, triple 300); on UniFFI as `mediaMosaic(bubbleWidth, gap, items: [MediaSize]) -> MediaMosaic` and on wasm as `mediaMosaic(bubbleWidth, gap, widths, heights)` (tiles a flat `Uint32Array` of x, y, width, height), plus `mediaMosaicGap` / `mediaMosaicMaxItems`; Tool: `tools/l10n` (`lakko-l10n` bin: `check`, `compile --out`, `keygen`, `sign`; `just l10n-check`). Wired into all three clients (S2-S5: every screen from the catalog, live switch; S6: iOS and Android fetch, verify, store and apply the signed bundle from `/.well-known/lakko-translations`, dormant until `LakkoTranslationKey` / `LAKKO_TRANSLATION_KEY` is pinned; `just gen-translation-key` / `just sign-translations`) |
| `lakko-feed` | **The on-device feed ([ADR-0063](docs/adr/0063-on-device-feed.md)).** A pure engine the three clients share: `FeedRule` (name under the ADR-0048 display-name rule, groups / channels, local include / exclude conversation ids, include muted, six weights 0..=100, half-life, 1..=30-day window, media only, hide own, for me, min reactions, hide / only words, suggestion rate) with a bounded canonical codec (`lakko/feed-rule/v1\0`, <= 4096 bytes, trailing bytes / unknown flags refused, errors are reason codes); `FeedRule::export` empties the conversation ids and sets `sources_stripped`; the Latest / Popular / For me presets; `rank(rule, conversations, candidates, now_ms)` -> `FeedEntry { index, replies }`, filtering then folding replies under the top of their chain (8 steps, cycles stand alone) then a total order (score, time, conversation, message id), scored in integer fixed point (`2^(-age/half_life)` by table, engagement `n / (n + 1 + isqrt(audience))`, 0 for channels) so wasm, iOS and Android cannot disagree; `mentions_username` (`@` + canonical username on boundaries); `contiguous_seen`, the one definition of how far a conversation is read. Depends on `lakko-contacts` (the display-name and username rules) and `thiserror`. Holds no messages, keys or state. 31 tests incl. order-independence and fuzz-property proptests; fuzz `feed_rule_decode`. On both FFI surfaces as `feed.rs` (`rankFeed`, `encodeFeedRule` / `decodeFeedRule` / `validateFeedRule` / `exportFeedRule`, `feedPresetRule`, `mentionsUsername`, `contiguousSeen`, `normalizeFeedWord`; the kind is `FeedConversationKind`, since `ConversationKind` belongs to `lakko-ui-rules`; refusals are `FfiError::FeedRule` / an `Error` with `feedRuleError`, both `"<code>: <reason>"`); not on the extension surface |
| `lakko-vault` | **The conversation vault, the pure half ([ADR-0064](docs/adr/0064-conversation-backup-and-device-re-entry.md) sections 1-2; the blob delete key of [ADR-0065](docs/adr/0065-attachment-lifetime-and-access.md) section 6). Built 2026-10-02; no service route and no client uses it yet.** `keys`: `VaultRoot::from_account_seed` (the account identity's 32-byte Ed25519 seed, the input `journal_root_seed` takes) and from it `vault_id` / `vault_key` / `vault_write_seed` per `VaultRef` (`0x01 ‖ kind ‖ id_len ‖ id` for a conversation, `0x02` index, `0x03` devices) and `blob_delete_seed(blob_id)`, all BLAKE3 derive-key with frozen contexts; the root never leaves the type. `seal`: `seal_object` / `open_object`, ChaCha20-Poly1305, wire `format ‖ nonce ‖ ciphertext`, AAD `lakko/vault/object/v1\0 ‖ vault_id ‖ kind ‖ chunk_no ‖ version` so the server cannot serve one object as another, plaintext framed and zero-padded to a size bucket (powers of two from 1 KiB to 64 KiB, then 64 KiB steps; one plaintext has one padded form), `MAX_OBJECT_BYTES` 4 MiB; buckets and cap chosen, not measured. `proof`: the request signature for the storage routes, `x-lakko-storage-proof: v1.<ms>.<sig>` over `lakko/storage/proof/v1 ‖ method ‖ path ‖ BLAKE3(body) ‖ unix_ms`, 120 s window, no nonce (a replayed write fails its version). `record`: bounded, exact, fail-closed codecs for `ConversationHead` (ref, kind, title, people, membership, pins, chunk table, newest messages, up to two re-entry GroupInfos, the registrar seed), `HistoryChunk`, `DevicesHead`, `VaultIndex`, and the one `VaultMessage` row (author account key, text, attachments with thumbnail, reply, forward, reactions, edited / deleted, call, `last_op_id`); structural caps only, the ADR-0047 media bounds stay with `lakko-attachments` at the FFI. `merge_messages`: union by message id, higher `last_op_id` wins, bytewise tiebreak; commutative, associative and idempotent by proptest. Depends on `lakko-crypto`, `thiserror`, `base64`. 78 tests; fuzz `vault_head_decode`, `vault_chunk_decode`, `vault_index_decode`, `vault_object_open`, `storage_proof_parse` (compile-checked, not run). Channel records are not in it yet |
| `lakko-circle` | **Circle directories, the pure half ([ADR-0086](docs/adr/0086-circle-directories.md), built 2026-10-30, host-tested, no client uses it).** A one-way key tree over a person's contacts, an epoch chain, a ribbon filter sealed under the epoch key (about 3.7 bytes per term), numbered rows sealed under the epoch key, each holding a card sealed under its search terms (`card.rs`: 65 slots of 32 bytes, `SEALED_CARD_LEN` 2,893, `ROW_LEN` 2,992; a row refuses anything that is not a sealed card), and `DirectoryWriter`, which turns membership and row changes into signed writes for `services/ds`. Specs `tree.sc.md`, `filter.sc.md`, `card.sc.md`; seven fuzz targets; on both FFI surfaces as `circle.rs` (`FfiError::Circle`, stable codes) |
| `lakko-presence` | Blinded per-conversation presence tokens (`PresenceToken` / `derive_token` / `current_epoch` / `PresenceTracker`). **Unwired**: exposed on wasm only, no UniFFI, no client caller, and the `presence_seed` MLS exporter it is specified against does not exist — see the `lakko-contacts` committer-beacon row for a worked argument on why reaching for this crate is the obvious move and the wrong one |
| `lakko-push` | Push wake-up payload **framing** — the compact-JSON v1 wire format (`{v,k,gp,ep}`, kinds `g` / `m` / `w`) carrying only a 32-byte pseudonym + a journal cursor, so the provider learns nothing and the client fetches + MLS-decrypts on wake. **Not encrypted**: the crate's whole dependency set is `thiserror` / `serde` / `serde_json` / `base64`, and its own docs list server-side payload encryption under "What's NOT here" — the privacy argument is that there is nothing in the payload worth encrypting, which is a different claim. Web Push RFC 8291 framing lives in `services/push-gw`, not here |
| `lakko-transparency` | CONIKS-style key transparency log (Merkle tree, signed roots, inclusion proofs). **Two-way conversation link (ADR-0074 section 3.7)** — `conversation_link.rs`: 201 bytes `version ‖ conversation_key ‖ current_key ‖ issued_at(8 BE) ‖ sig_by_conversation_key ‖ sig_by_current_key`, both over `lakko/transparency/conversation-link/v1\0 ‖ ...`, no expiry; `ConversationLink::sign` / `open`, `verify_conversation_link(bytes, trusted_current_key)` -> the conversation key; on both FFI surfaces as `signConversationLink` / `verifyConversationLink`; fuzz `conversation_link_open`. Identity supersession proof (G-4, invariant #6, sprints 1–3) — `supersession.rs`: a compact Ed25519 blob signed by the OLD identity key over the NEW key (`SupersessionProof::sign` + strict `verify_supersession` — sig → trusted-old-key binding → ≤30-day window → freshness) so a rotation bridges trust without a blind TOFU re-bounce. Sprint 2: `verify_supersession_chain(pinned, evidence, now)` ties it to the KT log — operator-root sig + no-rollback + Merkle inclusion + `username_hash` match + newer version + proof authorises exactly the entry's key (leaf commits the `(username_hash, version, pubkey)` tuple, not the sig bytes, so point-malleability can't fork an entry). Sprint 3: FFI surface for the proof primitive — `sign_supersession_proof(old_seed, new_seed, issued, expires)` + `verify_supersession(proof, trusted_old_pubkey, now)` on UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) + wasm (`signSupersessionProof`/`verifySupersession`); sign derives both pubkeys from each identity's `ed25519_seed()` so the embedded old key always matches the signer. The chain verifier is **no longer Rust-internal** — this row used to say it "stays Rust-internal … lands with the service", and it landed: `verify_supersession_chain` is exposed on UniFFI and wasm, reaches the generated Swift/Kotlin bindings, and is exercised end-to-end by `services/discovery/tests/recovery_kt_e2e.rs`. **Client-authorised leaf (G-30 #2, ADR-0019)** — `leaf_auth.rs`: `sign_leaf_genesis` / `verify_leaf_genesis` (a 64-byte Ed25519 genesis self-sig over `"lakko/transparency/leaf-genesis/v1" ‖ username_hash ‖ pubkey`, self-certifying the served key's first log entry; domain-separated from the supersession proof), FFI-exposed. **Merkle consistency + witness co-sign (G-30 #1, ADR-0020)** — `merkle.rs`: `consistency_proof(old_size, leaves)` + `verify_consistency(old_size, old_root, new_size, new_root, proof)` (RFC 6962 §2.1.2, reconstructs BOTH roots, bounded + panic-free); `witness.rs`: `sign_witness_cosig` / `verify_witness_cosig` (a 105-byte blob `version ‖ tree_root(32) ‖ log_size(8 BE) ‖ sig(64)`, domain `lakko/transparency/witness/v1`, FFI verify-only — signing stays in `services/witness`) + **`verify_witness_quorum` (K-of-N, ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1)** — the dedup-by-DISTINCT-key K-of-N generalisation (counts distinct pinned keys that produced a valid cosig over the served `(tree_root, log_size)`, never cosigs, so a replayed / duplicated cosig or a duplicate pinned key can never inflate the quorum; fail-closed on `threshold == 0` (`WitnessQuorumBadThreshold`) / below K (`WitnessQuorumNotMet`); N=1/K=1 ≡ `verify_witness_cosig`), FFI verify-only. The transparency service + 3-client wiring landed (see the `services/` table + ADR-0019/0020/0039). **Shared account-key derivation (ADR-0036)** — `account.rs`: `account_username_hash(account_id) = BLAKE3("lakko/transparency/account/v1" ‖ account_id)` (`ACCOUNT_KEY_DOMAIN`), the ONE definition — promoted from discovery — shared by discovery (log append), auth (genesis-attestation mint), and the clients (verify); a `golden_pins_the_account_username_hash` drift test makes a domain change fail CI instead of silently fail-closing the ADR-0036 genesis-attest gate **Own conversation key (2026-11-15, G-56):** `own_conversation_key(link, current_key)` in `conversation_link.rs`, the account's OWN link verified against THIS device's current key (never the fetched one): `None` for an absent, empty, unverifiable or self-naming link, else the conversation key; what the binding verdict of `lakko-contacts::own_binding` and the clients' DeviceGroup lineage guard read. On both FFI surfaces as `ownConversationKey` |
| `lakko-call-jwt` | Call-admission JWT (Phase 3b4) — JWS Compact + EdDSA-Ed25519 + JWKS publication, shared between `services/auth` (mint) and FFI crates (parse / verify). **Since [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2 (2026-10-16) the claims are exactly `iss`, `aud`, `room` (64 lower-case hex), `peer_id` (32 lower-case hex, random per token), `role`, `iat`, `exp`, as a closed set (`deny_unknown_fields`: a token with `sub` or `call_id` does not decode); `verify_jwt` checks both hex shapes. `examples/mint_test_jwt` takes `--peer-id` and derives the room from `--call-id`** |
| `lakko-attest` | Action attestation (G-13, [ADR-0015](docs/adr/0015-device-removal-authorization.md), sprint 1) — a compact fixed-layout Ed25519-signed blob `version‖account_id(16)‖action_hash(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)` (121 B) proving an account completed a passkey ceremony for a specific action. `ActionAttestation::sign` (auth side) + `verify_bound` (strict: signature → account/action binding → freshness) for the peer-verified device-removal path. Built on `lakko_crypto::signing` Ed25519 — no JWS/JWKS-fetch dep in the audited core; the trusted verifying key is supplied by the caller. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, bounded parser, 7 unit/property tests. **Genesis attestation (G-30 finding 2, [ADR-0036](docs/adr/0036-auth-attested-genesis.md))** — a second fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `version‖account_id(16)‖username_hash(32)‖pubkey(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)` (153 B, domain `lakko/genesis-attest/v1`, distinct from device-attest so the two cannot cross-verify even under a shared signing key) proving the OPAQUE-authenticated account presented THIS pubkey as its genesis KT-log key (`crates/lakko-attest/src/genesis.rs`). Two verify entry points — `verify_genesis_binding` (client: all-four-field binding, **no** freshness — a permanent artifact) + `verify_genesis_fresh` (transparency gate: `username_hash`+`pubkey` binding + freshness, no `account_id`). PREVENTS KT-log first-entry squatting that ADR-0019's self-sig only detects. 8 genesis unit tests incl. action↔genesis domain-crossing; fuzz `genesis_attestation_open`. **Backup head (G-11, [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md))** — a THIRD fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `format_version‖account_id(16)‖backup_version(8 BE u64)‖envelope_hash(32)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64)‖sig(64)` (129 B, domain `lakko/backup-head/v1`, distinct from device-attest + genesis so no two of the three cross-verify even under the shared signing key — a three-way domain-crossing test) committing an encrypted backup envelope as the account's **latest** at an auth-assigned monotone `backup_version`, closing the fresh-install backup-rollback residual (`crates/lakko-attest/src/backup_head.rs`). Auth-mint (`services/auth`) + client-verify only — no KT-log gate, binds `account_id` **directly**; timestamps in **milliseconds** (unlike the genesis template's seconds). Two verify entry points — `verify_backup_head` (FULL: sig + `account_id` + `envelope_hash` + freshness, returns the head for the caller's version-floor check) + `verify_backup_head_binding` (binding only). The head rides inside the ONE storage blob as a `0xB1` container-tag prefix (`crates/lakko-backup/src/head_frame.rs` `frame_backup_blob` / `parse_backup_blob` → `BackupBlob::{Framed, Legacy}`; `services/storage` untouched, invariant #2), so head + envelope swap atomically in a single `PUT`. 12 backup-head unit tests incl. the three-way action↔genesis↔backup domain-crossing; fuzz `backup_head_open` + `backup_blob_parse`. **Release manifest (GA update notice, [ADR-0038](docs/adr/0038-update-available-notice.md))** — a FOURTH fixed-layout Ed25519 blob `format_version‖latest_release(8 BE u64)‖min_supported_release(8 BE u64)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64)‖sig(64)` (89 B, domain `lakko/release-manifest/v1`, distinct from device-attest + genesis + backup-head — a four-way domain-crossing test), the odd one out: **GLOBAL** (binds no `account_id` — the same manifest is served to every client) and signed by a dedicated **OFFLINE publisher** release key (NOT the shared auth attest key), only ever **client-verified** — the signed "update available" notice (`crates/lakko-attest/src/release_manifest.rs`). `verify_release_manifest` (sig + caller-controlled freshness; `i64::MAX` disables the staleness gate) + the pure `release_status` / `release_status_for` → `UpdateStatus { UpToDate, UpdateAvailable, UpdateRequired }` (`min_supported` takes precedence). 12 release-manifest unit tests incl. the four-way domain-crossing; fuzz `release_manifest_open`. **Key-reset attestation ([ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md), built 2026-10-03)** — the FIFTH auth-signed fixed-layout kind (`crates/lakko-attest/src/key_reset.rs`): `version(1) ‖ account_ref(32) ‖ device_group_journal_id(32) ‖ key_generation(8 BE u64) ‖ new_signature_pubkey(32) ‖ signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE i64) ‖ sig(64)` = **177 bytes**, domain `lakko/key-reset-attest/v1`; `account_ref = BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/key-reset/account-ref/v1", account_id)` so the DS, which verifies it, never learns an account id and cannot link it to the KT-log `username_hash`; `device_group_journal_id` repeats `lakko_mls::DeviceGroup::group_id_for`'s derivation (golden-pinned here, equality-pinned in `lakko-ffi-uniffi`). `KeyResetAttestation::{for_account, sign, open}`, `verify_key_reset_binding` (signature + the three bindings, NO freshness: discovery and the DS compare the generation with their stored value instead) and `verify_key_reset_fresh` (plus a caller-supplied age bound, the client's 30-day rule). Minted by `services/auth` inside the reset transaction; the five-way domain-crossing test also covers the username certificate. Fuzz `key_reset_attestation_open`. On both FFI surfaces as `verifyKeyResetAttestation` / `verifyKeyResetAttestationFresh` / `keyResetAccountRef` / `deviceGroupJournalId` (verify only) **Since [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 6 (2026-10-16) the key-reset attestation is version 2, 145 bytes, without `device_group_journal_id`** (`version ‖ account_ref ‖ key_generation ‖ new_signature_pubkey ‖ signed_at ‖ sig`; version 1 refused), `verify_key_reset_binding` / `_fresh` take no journal, and the root endorsement (`sign_ds_root_endorsement`) is removed; the 177-byte layout above is history. **Donation statements (`docs/ux/donations.md` section 3, built 2026-11-16, host-tested only)** — two more kinds in `crates/lakko-attest/src/donation.rs`, signed OFFLINE by a dedicated donations key (`tools/donations`) and only ever verified elsewhere: a method statement (`lakko/donation-method/v1`: `version(1)=2 ‖ signed_at(8 BE) ‖ id_len(1) ‖ id ‖ kind(1: 1 sbp, 2 revolut, 3 bmc, 4 crypto) ‖ icon(1: 0 none, 1 btc, 2 eth, 3 ton) ‖ link_len(2 BE) ‖ link ‖ coin_len(1) ‖ coin ‖ network_len(1) ‖ network ‖ address_len(2 BE) ‖ address ‖ qr_flag(1) ‖ [qr_sha256(32)] ‖ has_phone(1) ‖ sig(64)`, at most 1300 bytes (version 2 since 2026-11-18, version 1 refused; the phone statement stays version 1); id `[a-z0-9_-]{1,32}`, link `https://` and address <= 512 bytes with no white space, coin / network <= 64; a crypto method carries coin + network + address and nothing else and any icon, any other kind icon none, no crypto field and at least one of link / QR / phone) and a phone statement (`lakko/donation-phone/v1`: `version ‖ id_len ‖ id ‖ phone_len ‖ phone ‖ sig`, phone 1..=32 bytes of `+0-9 ()-`, at most 131 bytes); length-prefixed rather than fixed, canonical (an absent field is a zero length, a present one never empty), bounds checked before any copy, field rules checked on sign and on open. `sign_donation_method` / `sign_donation_phone` (tool), `verify_donation_method` / `verify_donation_phone(bytes, key, expected_id)` (refuses another method's phone, `IdMismatch`), `DonationMethod::decode_unverified` / `DonationPhone::decode_unverified` (discovery without a key), `DonationError::code()` (`bad-length` / `bad-version` / `bad-kind` / `bad-field` / `kind-mismatch` / `bad-signature` / `id-mismatch`). 12 tests incl. three proptests (round trip, every truncation and every one-bit flip refused, wrong key refused, arbitrary bytes never panic) and a test over all ten domain separators of the crate (distinct, none a prefix of another) plus a splice test against every other kind; fuzz `donation_open`. On wasm only (`crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/donation.rs`): `verifyDonationMethod(statement, key) -> DonationMethod` (getters `id`, `kind` as `"sbp" | "revolut" | "bmc" | "crypto"`, `icon` as `"none" | "btc" | "eth" | "ton"`, `link?`, `coin?`, `network?`, `address?`, `qrSha256?` (32 bytes), `hasPhone`, `signedAtMillis`) and `verifyDonationPhone(statement, key, id) -> string`, each throwing an `Error` with a `donationError` code (the codes above plus `bad-key`); no UniFFI (iOS and Android open the web page). Residuals: G-57 |
| `lakko-pow` | Client-side Equix proof-of-work **solver** — the half the clients run against the challenges `lakko-pow-server` issues. Separate from the server crate because the client only solves and the server only verifies. **Uses the `equix` JIT on every native target except iOS** (an app may not map executable memory there) and wasm32 (none exists), and **checks every solution of a run** against the effort gate rather than only the first: measured 2026-09-27 at 8 bits, 16-21 s -> 0.6 s on a Mac, about 2 min -> about 1 s on the Android emulator, 6.8 s in the iOS simulator (interpreter). Solver memory is reused across attempts. 7 tests |
| `lakko-pow-server` | Server-side Equix proof-of-work — challenge issue + verify + consume + prune, used by `services/auth` only since ADR-0054 S3 (discovery, the DS and push-gw spend anonymous tokens instead; it was shared across `services/{auth,discovery,push-gw}` for the G-29 dedup; previously duplicated near-verbatim in each service's `src/pow.rs`, a drift risk where a security fix to one copy could miss the others). `issue_challenge(pool, effort)` + `verify_proof(pool, proof)` (atomic `SELECT FOR UPDATE → DELETE` single-use consumption — a stolen proof can't be replayed) + `spawn_pruner(pool)` + the pure crypto core (`verify_crypto`: Equix `verify_bytes` + SHA-256 leading-zero-bits effort gate) + `configured_effort_bits(var_name)` (each service names its own env knob: `LAKKO_POW_EFFORT_BITS` / `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_POW_EFFORT_BITS` / `LAKKO_PUSH_GW_POW_EFFORT_BITS`). `From<PowError> for (http::StatusCode, String)` so each service's thin axum `issue_challenge` handler `?`-converts. Distinct from `lakko-pow` (the wasm-portable CLIENT *solver*); the server side keeps the `equix` `compiler` JIT. Each service's `src/pow.rs` is now a thin re-export + handler shim. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, 5 unit tests |
| `lakko-anon-token` | **Anonymous rate-limit tokens, the pure primitive ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) decision 2, S2).** A privately verifiable Privacy-Pass-shaped token on `voprf` 0.5 in **VOPRF mode** (Ristretto255-SHA512, the version and suite `lakko-discovery-client` / `services/discovery` already pin), so the batched DLEQ proof lets a client check the issuer evaluated under the key published for the `(context, epoch)` (anti-tagging). `TokenContext` is a closed enum with frozen wire bytes, one per rationed action group rather than per service (`discovery-lookup` 1, `discovery-register` 2, `invite-consume` 3, `ds-knock` 4, `push-register` 5) so the issuer can ration each on its own quota; `epoch = floor(unix_secs / window_secs)`, default window one day (`DEFAULT_WINDOW_SECS`), and a verifier accepts the current and the previous epoch only. **No key table:** `IssuerKey::derive(master, context, epoch)` = BLAKE3 derive-key over `master ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch` then RFC 9497 `DeriveKeyPair` with `info = "lakko/anon-token/v1" ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch` (golden-pinned public key); `issue` evaluates a batch of at most `MAX_BATCH` (32) blinded elements with one DLEQ proof, refusing an empty / oversized batch or any invalid or identity point before evaluating. Client: `TokenRequest::new` (random 32-byte nonce per token, input `"lakko/anon-token/v1 input" ‖ version ‖ context ‖ epoch ‖ nonce`, blind) and `finalize` (proof checked against the caller-supplied public key, all or nothing). Token wire, fixed 106 bytes: `version(1) ‖ context(1) ‖ epoch(8 BE) ‖ nonce(32) ‖ output(64)`, bounded fail-closed `Token::decode`. `verify_token(master, bytes, expected_context, now, window)` checks layout, context, epoch window and the VOPRF output (constant-time compare) and returns a `SpendId` (BLAKE3 over version, context, epoch, nonce) for the caller's spent-set. **S3 added `wire.rs`**: `TOKEN_HEADER = "x-lakko-anon-token"`, `Token::to_header_value` / `decode_header_value` (base64url without padding, 142 characters, length-checked before decoding, canonical), `encode_b64url` / `decode_b64url_fixed`, `TokenRequest::blinded_elements_b64url` / `finalize_b64url`, and `AnonTokenError::code()` (frozen kebab codes both FFI surfaces carry). `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, wasm32-portable, no new third-party crate (`base64` 0.22 was already in the tree). 31 tests (16 unit + 15 integration incl. 2 proptests) + fuzz `token_verify`, which also drives the header decoder. **`call-join` (wire byte 12, ADR-0078 section 2, 2026-10-16): one per call-admission token at `POST /call/jwt`** |
| `lakko-anon-token-server` | **Server bookkeeping for anonymous tokens ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) decision 2)** — the `lakko-pow` / `lakko-pow-server` split again. `AnonTokenServerConfig::from_env` (`LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_MASTER_SECRET` 64 hex, unset = dormant, malformed = error; `LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_WINDOW_SECS` default 86400, zero or malformed = error, since a window that differs between services fails every token); the issuer's per-account quota `reserve_issuance` (one transaction, row lock, all-or-nothing, a limit of 0 issues nothing) over `anon_token_issuance`; a verifier's spent-set `spend` (one INSERT on the `(context, spend_id)` primary key, a duplicate is `AlreadySpent`) over `anon_token_spent`; hourly pruners dropping rows older than the previous epoch. Both counters **fail closed** on a store error (unlike the ADR-0033 / ADR-0037 limits). Ships the two schemas as `ISSUANCE_MIGRATION_SQL` / `SPENT_SET_MIGRATION_SQL`; a service copies the file byte for byte into its own `migrations/` and pins the copy with a test (auth's `0019_anon_token_issuance.sql` does, and discovery `0019`, the DS `0003` and push-gw `0004` for the spent-set). **S3 added the spend gate** (`gate.rs`): `TokenVerifier::new(pool, Option<config>)` → `spend_header(headers, context)` (disabled → `503 anon-tokens-disabled` first, then `401 anon-token-required` / `-malformed` / `-wrong-context` / `-expired` / `-invalid` / `-spent`, `500 internal` on a store error, body `{"error": code}`), the `require_token` axum middleware over `TokenVerifier::gate(context)`, `spawn_pruner`, `log_startup`; and a `testing` feature (`testing::mint_header`) for other crates' tests. 14 tests, 7 of them gated on `LAKKO_ANON_TOKEN_TEST_DATABASE_URL` |
| `lakko-sfu-proto` | SFU signaling wire frames (SFU multi-party arc, subsprint 1, complete) — `ServerFrame` / `ClientFrame` / `RelayTarget` / `PeerSnapshot` / `ErrorCode`, serde tagged-enum (`kind`, kebab-case), `MAX_INBOUND_FRAME_BYTES` cap + encode/decode + base64url payload helpers. Extracted from `services/sfu/src/protocol.rs` so the **server** (`services/sfu`, whose `protocol.rs` now re-exports this crate — its `client_frame_decode` fuzz target transitively fuzzes this parser) and the **clients** (via `lakko-ffi-uniffi::sfu_signaling` — full typed `SfuServerFrame` enum + `parse_sfu_server_frame` + `compose_sfu_*` + payload helpers — and `lakko-ffi-wasm::sfu_signaling` — `validateSfuServerFrame` + `composeSfu*` + payload helpers; the web reads fields via a TS type over native `JSON.parse` since wasm-bindgen doesn't return enum-with-data ergonomically) parse / emit one wire definition with no drift. `payload_b64` is opaque to the SFU (opacity invariant). `kind_str()` on both `ServerFrame` + `ClientFrame` returns the stable kebab `kind` tag (single source of truth for low-cardinality metric labels / structured logs; drift-guard tests assert it matches the serde tag — used by the SFU's `direction`+`kind`-labelled `lakko_sfu_signaling_frames_total`). **Frames added by [ADR-0070](docs/adr/0070-call-media-through-a-loss-burst.md) (2026-10-05 to 10-11):** `ClientFrame::SdpOffer` gained `light_mids` and `middle_mids` (which of the offer's video lines carry the light and the middle camera sender; bounded by `MAX_LIGHT_MIDS` / `MAX_LIGHT_MID_BYTES`, checked by `light_mids_problem` / `middle_mids_problem`); `ClientFrame::VideoWants` (per publisher `VideoQuality::{Main, Middle, Light}`, the whole set each time, at most `MAX_VIDEO_WANTS` 64); `ServerFrame::VideoGiven` (what the SFU is in fact sending a subscriber, under downlink control) and `ServerFrame::VideoNeeded { main, middle }` (to a publisher about its own two heavy senders; both booleans required). On UniFFI as `SfuServerFrame::VideoGiven` / `VideoNeeded`, `composeSfuSdpOfferWithMiddle`, `composeSfuVideoWants`; on wasm as `parseSfuVideoGiven` / `parseSfuVideoNeeded` and the same two composers. **[ADR-0068](docs/adr/0068-server-side-bitrate-control.md) amendment (2026-10-11):** `ServerFrame::PublishBudget { main_kbps, middle_kbps }` (kind `publish-budget`), the bitrate ceiling for the receiving publisher's main and middle camera senders; both fields required, each 1..=`MAX_PUBLISH_BUDGET_KBPS` (20000), refused on decode outside the bounds (`publish_budget_problem`). On UniFFI as `SfuServerFrame::PublishBudget`, on wasm as `parseSfuPublishBudget` (the two ceilings as a flat pair). By a count of test attributes, not run for this note: 36 in the crate, 11 in the UniFFI module, 10 in the wasm module **Since [ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2 (2026-10-16):** `peer_tag` / `PEER_TAG_*` are deleted; `room_id(call_id)` = `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/sfu/room/v1", call_id)` with `room_hex` / `is_room_hex` (golden-pinned); `Welcome` is `{peer_id, room, roster, server_ts}`, `PeerJoined` and `PeerSnapshot` carry the peer id alone **ADR-0083 (2026-10-25):** `SdpOffer.screen_mids` (bounded like `light_mids`) and `PublishBudget.screen_kbps: Option<u32>`; on UniFFI `composeSfuSdpOfferWithScreen` and `SfuServerFrame.publishBudget.screenKbps`, on wasm `composeSfuSdpOfferWithScreen` and a third ceiling from `parseSfuPublishBudget` |
| `lakko-tuf` | TUF v1.1 client-side metadata verifier (GA-5 scaffold + rotation + target-bytes verifier + the **delegated-targets walk** + URL conventions module; this header used to say "defense-in-depth delegation rejection", which the delegation walk replaced — `verify_chain` no longer refuses a `delegations` block, and the crate's own module doc still carries the stale refusal text). **No production consumer**: nothing outside its own fuzz crate depends on it, consistent with the network-fetch and install-glue follow-ups named at the end of this row. Root / Snapshot / Targets / Timestamp Ed25519-signed envelopes, chain-of-trust walk via `verify_chain`, version-rollback detection, expiry check, threshold-of-keys per role. `verify_root_chain` walks the root version chain — each new root must be signed by the previous root's `root` role, anchoring at a binary-pinned trust-anchor key id set. `verify_target_bytes` closes the last metadata-side gate — given a `TargetMeta` from `verify_chain` + the actual fetched binary, confirms length + SHA-256 digest match; unknown hash algos skipped (forward-compat); refuses if no known algo got verified. **Delegated-targets walk (GA-5) landed** — `Targets.delegations` is now the typed `Delegations { keys, roles: Vec<DelegatedRole> }` (was opaque `serde_json::Value`); `verify_chain` no longer refuses a delegations block (it returns the top-level direct targets map), and `resolve_target(name, top_targets, provider, now)` walks the TUF §5.6 delegation tree to resolve a single target: pre-order DFS, each delegated role's metadata verified against the keys + threshold its DELEGATING role published (reusing the audited `verify_signatures`), `paths` fnmatch-glob (ReDoS-safe iterative matcher, `*`/`?`) XOR `path_hash_prefixes` (SHA-256 hex prefix) matching, `terminating` short-circuits a branch, bounded against a hostile graph (`MAX_DELEGATION_DEPTH=32` + visited-role cycle guard + `MAX_DELEGATED_ROLES_VISITED=256` budget). The crate stays OFFLINE — the caller supplies a `DelegatedTargetsProvider` (any `FnMut(&str)->Option<Vec<u8>>`) to fetch delegated metadata by role name; the crate never fetches. `urls` module pins the TUF v1.1 §5 non-consistent-snapshot URL convention (`{base}/{version}.root.json` / `{base}/timestamp.json` / `{base}/snapshot.json` / `{base}/targets.json` / `{base}/targets/{name}`) so platform-specific install glue does not re-derive it from the spec; `target_artifact_url` carries the path-traversal defence (refuses empty name, NUL, `/`, `\\`, `..`). `mirrors` module (GA-5-mirrors) adds pure-Rust mirror selection + failover: `Mirror { url_base, confined_target_dirs }` (a mirror may be CONFINED to glob target-path patterns — a low-trust mirror cannot serve a target outside its remit), `MirrorList::for_metadata()` (all mirrors) / `for_target(name)` (only confinement-allowed, refuses `NoMirrorForTarget`), and `try_in_order(candidates, fetch)` failover (first-success wins, aggregates `AllFailed` per-mirror errors); the crate stays offline (the caller's `fetch` owns the network) + selection is a TRANSPORT choice independent of trust (bytes verified only via `verify_chain`/`verify_target_bytes`). Network fetch + platform-side install glue (atomic-swap binary replacement) are out-of-scope follow-ups |
| `lakko-ffi-uniffi` | UniFFI surface for Swift / Kotlin bindings. **The bulk of this surface is the MLS one and is documented in the `lakko-mls` row, not here** — group + proposal + staged-sweep + ownership-transfer + typed-refusal + beacon; note in particular the `flat_error` constraint that ties this crate's hands: a variant lowers as its **ordinal**, so a new `FfiError` variant must be **appended**, never inserted, or older bindings decode unrelated errors as the wrong variant. SFrame call-key surface (G3-sframe step 1, re-keyed by [ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md)): `CallKey.from_seed(seed) → Arc<CallKey>`, `CallKey.participant_key(sender_leaf, stream_index) → Arc<ParticipantKey>` (the row used to show a one-argument `participant_key(sender_leaf)`; the per-`(leaf, stream)` KID added the second), `ParticipantKey.seal(aad, plaintext) → SealedFrame { counter, ciphertext }`, `ParticipantKey.open(aad, ct, counter) → Vec<u8>`, plus `seal_framed` / `open_framed` and the `CallKeyRing` overlap pair whose open takes the whole `kid`. `ParticipantKey` wraps the underlying `lakko_calls::sframe::ParticipantKey` in a `Mutex` so WebRTC's codec-thread call into seal is safe without external serialisation. New `FfiError::Call(String)` variant collapses CallKey seed-length / SFrame counter-exhaustion / AEAD-fail. 15 unit tests in `sframe.rs` pin the round-trip + distinct-participant + wrong-counter + receiver-side semantics. The MLS-exporter seed plumbing (`Group.export_secret("lakko/call/v1", call_id, 32)`) is the caller's job — same posture G4-mls-seed established for channels. Per-platform wiring into libwebrtc's `FrameEncryptorInterface` / `FrameDecryptorInterface` landed via the G3-sframe-native arc (N-1..N-6, closes G-28) — see the `lakko-sframe-native` row below + the `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/` C++ bridge + the iOS Obj-C++ shim at `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` + the Android `lakko-sframe` Gradle module. **Anti-censorship transport FFI (ADR-0026, no wasm — browser sandbox)** in `transport.rs`: `tunneled_http_request(config, anchor, now, endpoint, method, path, host, headers, body) → FfiTunneledResponse` (verify the signed config against a pinned trust anchor → `BootstrapDialer::from_signed_config` → one HTTP/1.1 request over the winning obfs tunnel, fail-closed) + `fetch_bootstrap_config(doh_domain, path, trust_anchor, now) → Vec<u8>` (DoH-resolve + IP-pinned HTTPS GET + parse + `BootstrapConfig::verify` → the verified raw config bytes; an unverified config never leaves the call). Both `FfiError::Transport`, no silent fallback (#4). The native clients route through these when armed (iOS `AntiCensorshipTransport.perform`, Android `AntiCensorshipEngine`). **Release-manifest verify surface (ADR-0038)** in `release.rs`: `verifyReleaseManifest(manifest, verifying_key, now_millis, max_staleness_millis) → VerifiedReleaseManifest { latestRelease, minSupportedRelease, signedAtMillis }` + the pure `releaseUpdateStatus(current, latest, min_supported) → UpdateStatus` — **verify-only** (signing is the offline publisher tool's job); every verify failure folds into `FfiError::Release` (no stale-vs-invalid split — the client fails toward UpToDate). **Anonymous tokens ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S3)** in `anon_token.rs`: `AnonTokenRequest(context, epoch, count)` (blind; `blindedElementsB64url()`, `issueBodyJson()` for `POST /auth/tokens`) → `finalize(evaluationsB64url, proofB64url, publicKeyB64url)` → `[AnonToken { context, epoch, headerValue }]`, all or nothing on the DLEQ check, the key taken from `GET /auth/tokens/key/{context}/{epoch}` and never from the issuance response; plus `anonTokenHeaderName()` / `anonTokenContexts()` / `anonTokenDefaultWindowSecs()` / `anonTokenMaxBatch()` / `anonTokenEpochAt()` / `anonTokenIsLive()`. Two appended variants: `FfiError::AnonToken` (message `"<code>: <reason>"`, the frozen `AnonTokenError::code()`) and `AnonTokenAlreadyFinalized`. **ADR-0075 / ADR-0076 (2026-10-12/13):** `emailVaultSeal` / `emailVaultOpen` / `normalizeRecoveryEmail` (`email_vault.rs`), and on `SyncedState` the recovery-email and own-username registers (`setRecoveryEmail` / `recoveryEmailEnvelope` / `applyRecoveryEmailEnvelope`, `setOwnUsername` / `ownUsernameEnvelope` / `applyOwnUsernameEnvelope`, free `isRecoveryEmailEnvelope` / `isOwnUsernameEnvelope`); the same names on wasm. **Recovery (ADR-0072, 2026-10-02):** `phraseCheckRandomPositions()` / `phraseCheckWords(mnemonic, positions, words)` (`phrase_check.rs`) and `passkeyVaultSeal(mnemonic, prfOutput)` / `passkeyVaultOpen(prfOutput, sealed)` (`passkey_vault.rs`, new on this surface); `RecoveryMnemonic` is 12 words with 16-byte entropy; three appended variants `FfiError::PhraseCheck`, `PasskeyVaultNotOpened`, `PasskeyVault`; every Shamir function is removed. The native libraries must be rebuilt from this tree |Two appended variants: `FfiError::AnonToken` (message `"<code>: <reason>"`, the frozen `AnonTokenError::code()`) and `AnonTokenAlreadyFinalized` **Group-call protocol (ADR-0070):** `sfu_signaling.rs` carries `SfuServerFrame::VideoGiven` / `VideoNeeded`, `composeSfuSdpOffer(sdp, lightMids)`, `composeSfuSdpOfferWithMiddle`, `composeSfuVideoWants`; `ui_rules.rs` carries `tileVideoQuality`, `middleSenderWanted` and the `LayerDisplay` object. **The ADR-0071 media frame is deliberately not on this surface:** native per-frame media goes through the `lakko-sframe-native` C ABI, so `sframe.rs` here still exposes only the header-first `seal_framed` / `open_framed` pair, which no client's media path calls **2026-10-11 and 10-12:** `sfu_signaling.rs` adds `SfuServerFrame::PublishBudget { mainKbps, middleKbps }` (ADR-0068 amendment); `ui_rules.rs` adds the `AvAlign` object with its `AvReadings` / `AvPlan` records, `AvCapability`, the reason enums and their name functions (ADR-0070, the `lakko-ui-rules` row) **Donations (2026-11-16, `docs/ux/donations.md` section 4, G-57):** `donation.rs` mirrors the wasm surface for the native donation screens: `verifyDonationMethod(statement, key) -> DonationMethod` (record: `id`, `kind: DonationKind` {Sbp, Revolut, Bmc, Crypto}, `icon: DonationIcon` {None, Btc, Eth, Ton}, `link?`, `coin?`, `network?`, `address?`, `qrSha256?`, `hasPhone`, `signedAtMillis`) and `verifyDonationPhone(statement, key, id) -> String`; refusals are the appended `FfiError::Donation` with the core's code first in the message (`bad-key`, `bad-signature`, `id-mismatch`, ...) |

Wired through to the browser's `RTCRtpScriptTransform` API by `G3-web-sframe step 2` (separate sub-sprint). **Release-manifest verify surface (ADR-0038)** in `release.rs`: `verifyReleaseManifest` → `VerifiedReleaseManifest` (`latestRelease` / `minSupportedRelease` / `signedAtMillis` getters) + `releaseUpdateStatus` + the `UpdateStatus` number-enum; malformed input throws `"<code>: <reason>"`, a wrong word returns `false`) and `passkeyVaultSeal` / `passkeyVaultOpen` (`passkey_vault.rs`); `word_vault.rs` (`wordVaultEnroll`, `WordVaultAnswer`) and the Shamir module are deleted |a refusal throws an `Error` whose message is `"<code>: <reason>"` (`"already-finalized: ..."` on a second finalize) **Media frame ([ADR-0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md)) and the group-call protocol (ADR-0070):** `sframe.rs` adds `ParticipantKey.sealMediaFrame(frame, codec)` / `sealMediaFrameWithPrefix` / `openMediaFrame`, `CallKeyRing.openMediaFrameWithOverlap(wire)`, `parseMediaFrame` (KID, counter and clear-prefix length without opening), `MediaCodec`, `mediaCodecFromMime`, `mediaClearPrefixLen`, `vp8IsKeyFrame`; | `lakko-sframe-native` | Flat C ABI around `lakko_calls::sframe` (G3-sframe-native N-1, closes G-28 alongside N-2..N-6). Bottom of the iOS Obj-C++ + Android JNI native-shim stack — every per-frame seal / open / RFC 9605 wrap_frame / unwrap_frame call from the platform shims goes through these 19 `extern "C"` functions (13 base + the 6 `call_key_ring_*` the ADR-0029 rekey added). `crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]`: staticlib for iOS link (lipo'd from `aarch64-apple-ios` + `aarch64-apple-ios-sim` + `x86_64-apple-ios`); the Android `libsframe_jni.so` statically links the cargo-ndk staticlib output for each ABI. Opaque handle types `CallKey` / `ParticipantKey` + status enum `lakko_sframe_status_t` (`Ok / NullPointer / BadSeedLength / KeyDerivation / CounterExhausted / AeadFailed / WireParse / KidOverflow / CtrOverflow`). Memory ownership: every `*mut u8` / `*mut CallKey` / `*mut ParticipantKey` returned transfers ownership; matched-pair `_free` symbols reclaim. cbindgen-generated header mirrored to stable `target/lakko-include/lakko_sframe_native.h` (a build artifact — it only exists after `just cross-build-sframe-native-*`; Xcode + Gradle NDK build systems do NOT glob HEADER_SEARCH_PATHS, so the stable mirror is what they `-I`). 10 unit tests + 6 integration tests (`tests/sfu_opacity_e2e.rs` — SFU opacity, AAD-tampering rejection, wrong-key isolation, multi-party fan-in, cross-call isolation, counter monotonicity) + 3 cross-stack golden tests (`tests/golden_cross_stack.rs` — the `extern "C"` `seal_framed` / `open_framed` pinned to the canonical `GOLDEN_WIRE_HEX` the core / web-wasm / iOS-Android-UniFFI golden tests all share, so the native media-shim C ABI is proven byte-identical to every other stack; the automatable headless slice of G-26). Cross-compile via `just cross-build-sframe-native-ios` + `just cross-build-sframe-native-android`. C++ bridge layer at `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/` (N-3) consumes this ABI; iOS Obj-C++ shim at `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` (N-4) + Android Gradle module `apps/android/lakko-sframe/` (N-5) wrap the bridge for their respective platform's `webrtc::FrameEncryptorInterface` / `FrameDecryptorInterface` surface. **Mid-call rekey ([ADR-0029](docs/adr/0029-mid-call-key-rotation.md), G-18)** — a `CallKeyRing` opaque handle + `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_{new,free,rekey,prune,participant_key,open_framed_with_overlap}` (`Mutex`-guarded so the codec-thread open races the signaling-thread rekey safely), renamed to `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_t` via cbindgen. The C++ bridge's `LakkoFrameDecryptor` now holds the ring (overlap open + `Rekey`/`Prune`) and `LakkoFrameEncryptor` swaps its seal key on `Rekey` (superseded 2026-11-10: both are built on one shared `LakkoCallKeys` per call, which alone has `Rekey` / `Prune`; ADR-0029 amendment); `test_bridge.cc` gains `test_mid_call_rekey_overlap` (10th assertion). **Per-(leaf, stream) key derivation via the header KID ([ADR-0035](docs/adr/0035-sframe-per-stream-kid-key.md), supersedes [ADR-0034](docs/adr/0034-sframe-per-media-stream-key.md)'s per-media-TYPE discriminator)** — the participant-key entry point takes a `stream_index: u8` and composes `SframeHeader::kid_for(sender_leaf, stream_index)` (`KID = (sender_leaf << 8) | stream_index`) internally; the open-framed entry reads the KID off each frame's header (`open_framed_with_overlap(header.kid, …)`) with **no** media argument. `LakkoFrameEncryptor::Create` takes the `stream_index` (stamped into the seal-side KID); `LakkoFrameDecryptor` **drops** the `cricket::MediaType` argument entirely and reads the KID from the header. The `BadMediaType` status variant + the `LAKKO_SFRAME_MEDIA_TYPE_*` constants are removed. Closes the two-video-streams (camera + screen-share) reuse 0034 missed. Cross-stack golden regenerated to the ADR-0035 canonical vector (`910300…`) **The ADR-0071 media frame ([ADR-0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md), entry points 2026-10-07, used by the bridge since 2026-10-11) — the counts and the per-frame path described earlier in this row are from before it:** the C ABI now has 22 `extern "C"` functions; the three added are `lakko_sframe_media_clear_prefix_len(codec, frame)`, `lakko_sframe_seal_media_frame` and `lakko_sframe_call_key_ring_open_media_frame_with_overlap`, with `LAKKO_SFRAME_MEDIA_CODEC_OTHER` 0 / `_VP8` 1 / `_OPUS` 2 and `LAKKO_SFRAME_MAX_CLEAR_PREFIX_LEN` 16 as literals tied to the core by compile-time assertions so cbindgen emits them into the header. `apps/shared/sframe-bridge` calls only these on the media path: `LakkoFrameEncryptor::Encrypt` asks for the clear-prefix length and seals the media frame, `GetMaxCiphertextByteSize` allows the frame plus 36, `LakkoFrameDecryptor::Decrypt` opens through the ring with no codec, and `LakkoFrameEncryptor::SetCodec` (an atomic, safe against a concurrent `Encrypt`) is how the platform shim names the sender's negotiated codec; until told, a video frame keeps nothing clear. `lakko_sframe_seal_framed` / `_open_framed` and the ring's header-first open are still exported and the bridge does not call them. `tests/media_frame_c_abi.rs` (3 tests) pins the new layout's golden vector through the C ABI; `test_bridge.cc` now holds 15 tests, all on the new layout (the golden frame opens, the codec decides the clear prefix, every single-byte flip is refused, the header-first layout is refused, `SetCodec` racing `Encrypt`, the rekey overlap, an empty frame is not a decrypt failure) **A second thing in the shared bridge, not part of this crate's C ABI (2026-10-12, [ADR-0070](docs/adr/0070-call-media-through-a-loss-burst.md)):** `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/receiver-delay.h` sets a remote receiver's minimum playout delay by calling libwebrtc's `RtpReceiverInterface::SetJitterBufferMinimumDelay` by vtable slot, with the slot encoding and the argument's shape written out per platform instead of through a class declaration (the pinned M125 headers are not what decides it: the three shipped binaries do not share one vtable encoding). It is offered only when the loaded WebRTC image's identity (Mach-O `LC_UUID`, ELF build id) is one the slots were read from, and only after a bracket probe of the receiver; otherwise nothing is set. So the native shim's dependence on libwebrtc's C++ ABI is no longer only the frame encryptor / decryptor interfaces. iOS reaches it through `LakkoReceiverDelay` in `SFrameAttach.{h,mm}`, Android through `me.lakko.sframe.ReceiverPlayoutDelay` and `org.webrtc.LakkoRtpReceiverAccess` in the `lakko-sframe` module. Host test `apps/shared/sframe-bridge/test-receiver-delay.sh` (`test_receiver_delay.cc`, a fake receiver); never run on a device. In the same commit `tools/build-sframe-jni.sh` passes `-fexperimental-relative-c++-abi-vtables` for `arm64-v8a` only (the prebuilt engine's x86_64 build calls through absolute slots, read from the binary) and both `libsframe_jni.so` files were rebuilt; not run on an emulator |
| `lakko-ffi-extension` | Slim decrypt-only UniFFI surface for the iOS NotificationServiceExtension (NSE) — [ADR-0041](docs/adr/0041-ios-silent-push-nse-decrypt.md) Phase 2. `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; depends ONLY on `lakko-mls` + `lakko-attachments` + `lakko-contacts` + `lakko-calls` (+ transitive `lakko-crypto` / `lakko-proto` / openmls) — `cargo tree` confirms tokio, `lakko-transport`, `lakko-channels`, `lakko-sfu-proto`, `lakko-call-jwt`, and tungstenite are **all absent** (193 crates vs the full `lakko-ffi-uniffi` surface's 356). `lakko-contacts` was added for the background service-envelope filter and is deliberately cheap: measured on `aarch64-apple-ios` with `--edges normal` it adds **exactly 5** crates (172 → 177 — itself, `tracing` ×3, `pin-project-lite`), because its only dependencies are `thiserror` and `lakko-crypto`, both already present. `lakko-calls` was added for the call-signalling classifier and measured the same way: it adds **exactly 1** crate (177 → 178, itself), because its whole dependency set — `lakko-mls`, `lakko-crypto`, `lakko-proto`, `prost`, `lakko-contacts`, `thiserror`, `zeroize` — was already in the tree; SFrame, the jitter buffer and the codec tables are unreferenced and dead-stripped, since the crate touches only `lakko_calls::signaling::parse_signaling_payload`. Exposes exactly the read-only-throwaway NSE decrypt subset and nothing more: `Identity.from_seed` (ADR-0017) + `restore_storage`, `load_group`, a read-only `Group.group_kind()` ([ADR-0062](docs/adr/0062-conversation-kind.md); no iOS caller yet, since the NSE does not title by sender and call rings take their title from the app's `CallPeerNameMirror`), `Group.process_incoming_verifying_roles` (with the sender leaf — the audited ADR-0022 role-verifying path), `parse_body_envelope` → `ParsedBody` (ADR-0023/0024; since 2026-10-12 it also carries the ADR-0073 re-send marker as `resend_origin_group_b64` / `resend_origin_message_id`, so the NSE stays quiet about a message the device already holds; since 2026-11-13 also `channel_invite: ChannelInviteNotice { opened, channel_name, publisher }`, a channel or publisher invitation card opened offline through `lakko-contacts`, so the notification names the invitation instead of showing its link), and `decode_push_routing_secret` → `FfiPushRoutingSecret` (ADR-0042/0044 — **decode only**, so the NSE can recognise a service envelope and suppress it instead of rendering it as lossy-UTF-8 garbage; `compose` / `derive_push_gid` / `push_routing_secret_is_newer` are deliberately NOT brought over, since the NSE only asks the classification question), `decode_group_name_envelope` + `group_name_envelope_min_len` / `_max_len` for the [ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md) group-name envelope (the third envelope kind the NSE must recognise and suppress; decode only, no compose, the golden pins the 20-byte prefix, 30 / 285 lengths and first byte `0x6c`), `decode_pin_envelope` + `pin_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) shared-pin envelope (a fifth service envelope the NSE suppresses; decode only, the golden pins 31 bytes and first byte `0x6c`), `decode_profile_request` + `profile_request_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) profile request (the fourth service envelope the NSE suppresses; a newcomer sends one right after joining, so the newest row on a push wake is one whenever someone has just joined; decode only, its golden pins 26 bytes and first byte `0x6c`), and — since `94da492` — `decode_committer_beacon` + `committer_beacon_envelope_len` for the [ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) committer beacon, the **same classification question reached by a second envelope kind** and **more** reachable than the first rather than less: a routing secret is broadcast on a membership change, while a beacon is emitted by every owner/admin every 60 s, so in an active group the newest journal row — the one the NSE previews — is frequently a beacon, and unfiltered it reaches the notification body as the literal text `lakko/committer-beacon/v1` plus two control bytes (`parse_body_envelope`'s deliberate lossy-UTF-8 legacy fallback, load-bearing for pre-1.19 wire compat, so the fix belongs here and not in `lakko-attachments`, which does not depend on `lakko-contacts`). The envelope **length** is exported rather than hand-copied as `27` into Swift precisely because a stale literal fails in the **dangerous** direction — a too-narrow peek silently stops suppressing beacons and restores the defect — and `golden_pins_the_swift_prefilter_shape` pins the exported value **against literals** (27 and `0x6c`) so a wire revision fails Rust CI rather than a notification; `compose_committer_beacon` / `acting_committer_verdict` / `should_emit_beacon` and the timing constants are DELIBERATELY absent, since this process structurally cannot sweep (C-59) so a beacon from it would be a lie, and it holds no observation history to fold. **`parse_call_signal` → `CallSignal { Offer { call_id, video, group } / End { call_id } / Other { call_id } }`** is the call-wake classifier `docs/ux/calls.md` §7 names as the single transport change the whole of §2 depends on: until it existed the NSE could not tell a call offer from any other non-chat payload, so an iPhone never rang. It is CLASSIFY only over `lakko_calls::signaling::parse_signaling_payload` — no compose, so the extension can never answer, decline or end a call (each is an MLS application message, i.e. a ratchet advance C-59 forbids) — and it carries no SDP, no ICE candidate and no timestamps, since a ring needs none of them. `group` separates the 1×1 offer from the group-call announce because their accept paths differ and nothing else on this surface recovers the distinction; folding the announce into `Other` would have meant a group call never rings, silently. Two goldens pin it: `golden_pins_the_wire_shape_swift_branches_on` (whole-wire hex for a video offer, a voice offer and an end, plus the one `media_flags` byte that is the whole of "say what kind of call it is") and `golden_pins_every_body_tag_the_ios_peek_tabulates` (all seven `body` oneof tags — `0x52` offer / `0x5a` answer / `0x62` candidate / `0x6a` end / `0x72` group-start / `0x7a` group-join / `0x82 0x01` group-leave — because the iOS `nseLooksLikeCallSignaling` byte peek hand-copies that table, the ADR-0044 drift class again, failing dangerously: a renumbered tag stops the peek matching and the frame falls through to the lossy chat fallback). **`Group.account_key_at(leaf)`** resolves a leaf to the ADR-0050 account key that names the *person* (not a leaf key, which names one of their phones), so a wake can be titled with a contact's name instead of "Lakko"; one leaf in, one key out, never the roster — and at receipt the same value already arrives on `ProcessedMessage.Application.sender_account`, resolved at the one moment the roster IS the epoch the message was sealed at (C-68), which is what a caller should prefer. **No compose / send / channel / transport / persist surface** — the FFI carries no `snapshot_storage` method at all, the structural bottom layer of the read-only-throwaway no-write guarantee (C-59). The Swift API shape mirrors `lakko-ffi-uniffi` so the Phase-3 NSE handler calls the same names against the slim framework. Cross-compiled to a separate `LakkoCoreExtension.xcframework` (its own FFI module, so the app + NSE carry both frameworks with no symbol clash) via `just build-ios-extension`; the dead-stripped linked decrypt footprint is ~1.6 MB vs the 333 MB `LakkoCore.xcframework`, so it fits the ~30 MB NSE budget with an order of magnitude of headroom. 9 unit tests incl. the full `fromSeed → restoreStorage → loadGroup → processIncomingVerifyingRoles → parseBodyEnvelope` round-trip recovering plaintext, plus a golden that pins the 70-byte envelope shape the Swift-side cheap pre-filter hardcodes — so a wire-format drift fails in Rust CI instead of silently un-suppressing the filter — and 8 more in `committer_beacon.rs`, incl. `an_unfiltered_beacon_would_otherwise_render_as_chat_text` (which asserts the defect the arm exists to close, so the arm cannot be deleted silently) and `advertised_length_matches_the_composed_wire`. 41 tests in total as of the call-classifier landing (3 lib + 11 `call_signaling` + 8 `committer_beacon` + 6 each in `group_name` / `profile` / `push_routing` + 1 `attachments`), the call module's own including `an_unfiltered_offer_would_otherwise_render_as_chat_text` and a cross-kind isolation test proving the call arm and the two service-envelope arms never accept each other's bytes **ADR-0087 phase B (2026-11-06):** `isChannelReadKeyEnvelope` / `channelReadKeyEnvelopeLen` (155) and `isPublisherStepDownEnvelope` / `publisherStepDownEnvelopeLen` (46), recognise only, golden-pinned beside the other `conversation_envelopes` recognisers; since 2026-11-08 also `isChannelEpochSeedsEnvelope` / `channelEpochSeedsEnvelopeMinLen` (163) / `channelEpochSeedsEnvelopeMaxLen` (2431) and `isChannelRotateRequestEnvelope` / `channelRotateRequestEnvelopeLen` (49) **Retired `contact` keys (2026-11-06):** `mailboxRowContactVerdict(accountSeed, currentContactVersion, provingKey?, senderIsContact)` -> `FfiContactKeyRowVerdict` (`contact_key.rs`), the app's rule for a mailbox row, verdict only (the regrant is the app's send) |

### `services/` — Backend (Rust)

| Service | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `auth` | OPAQUE-PAKE + WebAuthn passkey, no PII stored. **Genesis-attestation mint (ADR-0036, G-30 finding 2)** — `POST /auth/genesis/attest` (`genesis_attest.rs`): a bearer-authed (`AuthenticatedAccount` extractor), stateless endpoint that signs a 153-byte `GenesisAttestation` binding the OPAQUE-authenticated `account_id` + the derived `username_hash` (`lakko_transparency::account_username_hash`, never from the request body) to the client-supplied genesis pubkey, reusing the device-attest signing key + JWKS (domain-separated so the two attestation kinds cannot cross-verify), TTL 3600 s. Holding a valid session token IS the proof of OPAQUE-authentication that only the account holder can produce — the anti-squatting basis. **Backup-head mint (ADR-0037, G-11)** — `POST /auth/backup/head` (`backup_head.rs`): a bearer-authed (`AuthenticatedAccount`), **STATEFUL** endpoint that read-bumps a per-account monotone `backup_version` counter (`backup_head_versions` table, migration `0015`, one transaction via the MariaDB `LAST_INSERT_ID` idiom — the first auth state outside authentication proper, storing ONLY `(account_id, version)`: no key material, no envelope bytes, no hash) then signs a 129-byte `BackupHead` over `(account_id, backup_version, envelope_hash, signed_at)` with the shared attest signing key (domain-separated `lakko/backup-head/v1`), returning `{ backup_head_b64, backup_version }`. The version is auth-assigned, never client-supplied → a rollback regression is structurally impossible; a restoring client verifies the head to detect a storage-tier rollback. The sole deviation from the ADR-0036 stateless genesis mint (and the reason a migration is required). **A second table has since joined it** on the same endpoint: `backup_head_mint_limits` (migration `0016`) backs the ADR-0037 F5b per-account mint rate-limit — `LAKKO_BACKUP_HEAD_MINT_RATE_LIMIT` + `_WINDOW_SECS`, default limit 0 = disabled, fail-open — so "0015 is the reason a migration is required" is no longer the whole story. **Anti-abuse without proof of work ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S4, 2026-09-28)** — `/signup/start` carries the ONLY proof of work left in the system (`/pow/challenge` issues at `LAKKO_SIGNUP_POW_EFFORT_BITS`, default 8, to be set by measurement), and `/signup/finish` carries none: it must consume a `signup_states` row the start wrote after its proof verified (migration `0020`, TTL 600 s, deleted in the account-creating transaction; none → `410`, recovery-upload mismatch → `400`), so one proof creates at most one account. Every other former proof route (login / recovery login / password-reset start, TOTP recover, device authorize start / redeem, device request, TOTP enroll start; the vault recover routes of that list were removed on 2026-10-02) lost its `pow` field and is limited in `src/limits.rs` instead: DB-backed fixed-window counters keyed on a BLAKE3 of what the request names (login handle, target account), charged before any lookup so unknown and known names trip alike, fail closed (`503 rate-limiter-unavailable`) on a store error; plus in-memory per-address token buckets (addresses never stored). A limit answers `429 {"error", "retry_after_secs"}` + `Retry-After`, never `401`; codes `too-many-attempts` / `device-request-limited` / `signup-rate-limited`; knobs `LAKKO_AUTH_LIMIT_*` / `LAKKO_AUTH_IP_LIMIT_*` (`N/SECS`, defaults chosen, not measured). The per-row TOTP lockout was removed: it answered `429` only for an existing row, an existence oracle (the word vault and the recovery code it was removed alongside no longer exist at all) **Sign in by name (ADR-0054 S5, `login_handle.rs`, migration `0021`):** since S3 a discovery lookup needs an anonymous token and a token needs a session, so the pre-session routes (login / recovery login / password-reset start, TOTP recover) take `handle` (base64url, 32 bytes; `loginHandle` on both FFI surfaces = `BLAKE3-derive-key("lakko/auth/login-handle/v1", canonical username)`, defined in `lakko_contacts::username`) instead of `account_id`; `login_handles` stores only `keyed-BLAKE3(LAKKO_AUTH_HANDLE_PEPPER, handle)` -> account id (one handle per account, FK cascade on account deletion), bound with bearer `PUT` / removed with `DELETE /auth/login-handle` (`409 handle-taken`); an unbound handle resolves to a stand-in account id derived from its key, so the same lookups run and OPAQUE takes its fake-credentials path with a credential identifier stable per name; the account id reaches the client only in the finish response. Pepper unset -> those routes and `/auth/login-handle` answer `503 login-by-name-disabled`. The passkey allow-list start still takes `account_id`. **Since [ADR-0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md) S1 (2026-09-28) the device routes do not:** `POST /device/request` takes `{handle, pairing_payload_b64}` (resolved like the S5 routes, the per-target counter keyed on the handle, `503` without the pepper) and an unknown handle's request is stored against its stand-in, which no device polls, so `/device/request/status` answers alike for both; migration `0022` drops the `device_requests` foreign key for that (account deletion clears the row explicitly); `POST /device/authorize/redeem` takes `{grant_token_b64}` alone, finds the grant by `SHA-256` through a new unique index on `device_grants.grant_hash` (locking read + delete in one transaction), and its response's `account_id_b64` is where the fresh device learns the id **Device link (ADR-0049 section 2, amended 2026-09-28)** — `device_request.rs` relays the `lakko-device-link` SPAKE2 exchange opaquely and enforces one exchange per request (migration `0024`, `device_requests` rebuilt: `pake_b` once, `pake_a` + `sealed_payload` + `confirm_a` once, `outcome` + `confirm_b` once): anonymous `POST /device/request {handle}` / `/device/request/status` / `/device/request/payload`, bearer `GET /device/requests`, `POST /device/requests/{id}/pake`, `POST /device/requests/{id}/result`, `DELETE /device/requests/{id}`; a stand-in (unknown-handle) request answers every anonymous route like a real request nobody answered; the SHA-256 comparison code is gone. **Wrong-code budget (second security pass, migration `0025`):** wrong-code outcomes are counted per account; 3 in 24 h pause the account's device requests for 1 h, 6 for a day (`LAKKO_AUTH_LIMIT_DEVICE_LINK_WRONG_CODE_{SHORT,LONG}` + `_SUSPEND_{SHORT,LONG}_SECS`, chosen); a paused account's new requests are filed under the stand-in, answer like any other, and the pake route refuses. **Recovery after [ADR-0072](docs/adr/0072-recovery-model-simplification.md) (2026-10-02), migrations `0026`-`0029`:** `0026` drops `recovery_contacts` + `otp_challenges` (factor (d)); `0027` is the trace of a server-side three-word verifier built and removed the same day (its `passkey_vaults` flag-day delete and column comment stand); `0028` re-keys `login_handles` on `account_id` with a unique index on `handle_key`, fixing a MariaDB 1213 deadlock between concurrent binds (gap and next-key locks on the old unique `account_id` index, whose UUIDv7 entries for new accounts are neighbours); `0029` drops `word_vaults` + `vault_opens` and the scope-5 limit rows. **No `/vault/enroll`, `/vault/recover/*`, `GET`/`DELETE /vault` or `/recovery-contact/*` route exists; `word_vault.rs` is deleted; the server stores nothing about three words.** `passkey_vault.rs` (bearer; `LAKKO_VAULT_ENC_KEY` wraps blobs at rest, unset -> `503` on these routes only): `PUT /vault/passkey {credential_id_b64, sealed_entropy_b64}` (exactly 44 bytes, the credential must be one of the account's passkeys), `POST /vault/passkey/get` (blob or `null`), `DELETE /vault/passkey`, `GET /vault/passkeys`; `DELETE /webauthn/credentials/{id}` also deletes that passkey's blob; a device with no cached account signs in through the discoverable `POST /webauthn/auth/start {}`. **Recovery-credential replace (ADR-0017 fresh identity) — folded into the key reset by [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) (2026-10-03); the standalone `/recovery/credential/{start,finish}` routes are DELETED.** **Key reset (`src/key_reset.rs`, bearer):** `POST /account/keys/reset/start {recovery_request}` -> `{recovery_response}` (stateless, the OPAQUE registration start under `KIND_RECOVERY`) and `POST /account/keys/reset/finish {recovery_upload, new_signature_pubkey_b64}` -> `{key_reset_attest_b64 (URL-safe base64, 177 bytes), key_generation, session_token, session_expires_at_ms, refresh_token, refresh_expires_at_ms}`, ONE transaction in this order: `UPDATE accounts SET identity_version = LAST_INSERT_ID(identity_version + 1)` (the key generation; no migration, the column keeps its name), delete the in-flight recovery-login + password-reset states and upsert `KIND_RECOVERY`, delete `passkey_vaults` then `webauthn_credentials` then `webauthn_states` + `webauthn_action_grants` then `device_grants` + `device_requests`, mint the `lakko-attest` key-reset attestation with the shared attest key, issue the caller a fresh pair; the password envelope and the TOTP secret are kept; no passkey action grant. **Fresh-auth grant (2026-10-04 for the reset, generalised 2026-10-06; `session.rs` `FreshGrant` / `require_fresh_grant`):** the password `POST /login/finish`, the passkey `POST /webauthn/auth/finish` and `POST /signup/finish` also answer `fresh_grant` + `fresh_grant_expires_at_ms` (`LAKKO_FRESH_GRANT_TTL_SECS`, default 600; no other landing route mints one: not TOTP, the recovery login, a device redeem, a password reset, a refresh or the reset itself).
