Five actions refuse a session that carries none with `403 {"error":"fresh-auth-required"}`, checked before body validation and before any limiter or wrong-code budget, so a stolen session or an unlocked device can neither perform them nor burn the owner's budgets: the key reset (`fresh_grant` in both bodies; `finish` re-checks equality under the row lock that bumps the generation, which spends every grant of the old generation), `DELETE /account` (header `x-lakko-fresh-grant` for an account with no passkey; for an account WITH a passkey the ADR-0014 action grant is itself the proof and the header is not needed), adding a device (`POST /device/authorize/start`, `POST /device/requests/{id}/pake` and `/result`, `fresh_grant` in the body), and adding a passkey (`POST /webauthn/register/{start,finish}`, so a session cannot mint itself a credential that mints grants). TOTP deliberately mints none (the authenticator usually lives on the lost phone, and a code alone must not reset or delete). Changing the password runs on the recovery seed through the anonymous `/password/reset/*` and is gated on the CLIENTS (a live grant or the local three-word check). Both calls charge `Scope::RecoveryReplace` (12, 10 per hour) and `finish` also `Scope::KeyReset` (13, default 3 per day, `LAKKO_AUTH_LIMIT_KEY_RESET`); the ADR's `503 key-reset-disabled` is not built (the attest key is always loaded). **Sessions (`src/session.rs`, ADR-0073 section 3):** every session-landing route (`/signup/finish`, `/login/finish`, `/recovery/login/finish`, `/password/reset/finish`, `/webauthn/auth/finish`, `/totp/recover`, `/device/authorize/redeem`) answers the flattened `SessionPair` (`session_token` = the `v2` access token, `LAKKO_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECS` default 900; `refresh_token` = the `r1` credential, `LAKKO_REFRESH_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS` default 30, sliding; both `*_expires_at_ms`; `key_generation`) issued at the account's current generation; `LAKKO_SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_DAYS` is gone. Anonymous `POST /auth/session/refresh {refresh_token}` (per-address `Category::Attempt`) -> the pair, `401 {"error":"key-generation-superseded"}` when the credential's generation is below the row's, `401 {"error":"refresh-invalid"}` for a bad / expired / unknown credential or no account (one body, enumeration-safe; a generation ABOVE the row is refused the same way). **`webauthn::require_session`** now reads `accounts.identity_version` on every bearer request and refuses a lower generation with the same JSON `401 key-generation-superseded` (every other failure stays the plain bodiless `401`; above the row = plain `401`). Limit scope numbers 5 (the removed verifier) and 6-8 (factor (d)) are not reused |a paused account's new requests are filed under the stand-in, answer like any other, and the pake route refuses **Unclaimed-account sweep (ADR-0032 guest lifecycle, 2026-10-02, `src/unclaimed.rs`):** every 600 s, accounts older than `LAKKO_AUTH_UNCLAIMED_ACCOUNT_TTL_SECS` (default 86400, `0` = off) with no `login_handles` row and no `webauthn_credentials` row are deleted in batches (the foreign keys cascade); off when the login-handle pepper is unset, since then no account can have a handle. 3 pure tests + 1 gated on `LAKKO_AUTH_TEST_DATABASE_URL` **ADR-0078 phase 6 (2026-10-16):** the key-reset attestation the reset mints is 145 bytes (version 2, no journal id). **Call admission without the account ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2, 2026-10-16; supersedes the ADR-0033 throttle):** `POST /call/jwt` has no session: body `{room, role}` (refused before any token is spent when malformed), one `call-join` anonymous token (`x-lakko-anon-token`, the shared gate over the spent-set migration `0034`), then `room_cap.rs` (in memory, 100 admissions per room per 60 s, `LAKKO_AUTH_ROOM_ADMISSION_LIMIT` / `_WINDOW_SECS`, `429 room-admission-limit`), then a token with a random `peer_id`; the answer `{jwt, expires_at_unix_secs, turn}` carries a TURN credential whose username tag is 16 random bytes (`turn.rs` `mint_random`). `POST /call/turn-credential` is gone; migration `0035` drops `call_admission_events` and `throttled_calls`. The `call-join` issuance quota is 100 a day, 8 for an account younger than `LAKKO_AUTH_YOUNG_ACCOUNT_AGE_SECS` (86400); all chosen, not measured |
| `ds` | MLS Delivery Service (FIFO journal per group + per-device mailbox). **Wake forwarding ([ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S1, `src/wake.rs`):** a group publish may carry `x-lakko-wake: <push_gid 43-char base64url>;message|call`, parsed strictly before the append (malformed -> 400, nothing stored; on a mailbox -> 400); on a claimed journal whose proof verified, the DS forwards `(push_gid, message_id, kind)` to push-gw's internal listener fire-and-forget (5 s timeout, 256 in flight), capped per journal and per `push_gid` (`LAKKO_DS_WAKE_BURST` 30, `LAKKO_DS_WAKE_PER_MINUTE` 60, chosen not measured), logging only kind and outcome; an unclaimed journal validates the header and drops the wake. `LAKKO_DS_PUSH_GW_URL` + `LAKKO_DS_PUSH_GW_SECRET` both unset -> dormant, one set -> refuses to start. The header is not covered by the ADR-0053 proof signature (S5 item). **[ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md) S1 landed** in `src/access.rs` + migration `0002_journal_access.sql` (`ds_group_access` per `(group_id, epoch)`, `ds_mailbox_access`, `ds_group_knocks`, `ds_proof_nonces`, and a `pow_challenges` table dropped by `0004` in ADR-0054 S3): a **claimed** group's `GET`/`POST messages` and `GET knocks` and a claimed mailbox's `GET messages` require an `x-lakko-ds-proof` header verified by `lakko-ds-proof` under one of the two newest registered epoch keys (mailbox: the identity key at epoch 0), the nonce consumed through the `ds_proof_nonces` primary key so a replay is refused, every failure one uniform `401 proof required`; an unclaimed journal keeps the Phase 1.3 open posture, as do a mailbox `POST` (the cold inbox) and a group's `POST knock` (16 KiB payload, response carries the latest epoch; since ADR-0054 S3 it spends one `ds-knock` anonymous token in `x-lakko-anon-token`, checked only after the body validates so a malformed knock costs none, through `lakko-anon-token-server`'s gate over the spent-set migration `0003`; the `/pow/challenge` route, `LAKKO_DS_POW_EFFORT_BITS` and the `lakko-pow-server` dependency are gone). Registration is **ADR-0057's since 2026-09-28** (`src/ownership.rs`, migration `0006`: `ds_group_owner`, `ds_group_access` with `registered_by` / `registered_rank`, `ds_group_registrars`, `ds_push_bindings`): `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/{claim,reset,access,registrars,registrars-set,root,push-binding}` each take one signed `lakko-ds-proof` statement (Append only at `latest + 1`, a member at most one past the newest admin/root epoch; Replace only over strictly lower ranks, no root override; `registrars-set` root-only; a `root` naming the current root is a 204 probe); a stranger gets the uniform `401` before its nonce is spent, rank shortfalls are `403`, conflicts `409`; an epoch key opens the journal but registers nothing. A wake whose `push_gid` is not the publishing journal's binding (keyed-BLAKE3 under `LAKKO_DS_BINDING_PEPPER`, unique) is dropped; pepper unset -> binding `503 push-binding-disabled` and every wake dropped. `PUT /ds/mailbox/{hex}/access` requires the key the pseudonym derives from (`400` otherwise), idempotent. The pruner also sweeps knocks (7 d) and nonces (2 x window). Body handlers take collected `Bytes` so the proof covers the published bytes. `LAKKO_DS_TEST_DATABASE_URL`-gated route tests in `access.rs` and `ownership.rs` over the shared `test_support.rs` (skip silently without it). **Attested root reset ([ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) S4, built 2026-10-03; `src/key_reset.rs`, migration `0009` adds `ds_group_owner.key_generation` default 0):** `PUT /ds/group/{hex}/reset` takes an optional `key_reset_attest_b64` (standard base64; `400` on any other statement route); when present it is verified under the pinned `LAKKO_DS_ATTEST_KEY` (unset -> `503 key-reset-disabled` on this arm only; the DS depends on `lakko-attest` and never fetches the JWKS), must name the path's journal (else the uniform `401`) and carry a generation strictly above the stored one (else `409`), and then the Reset is heard from ANY signer: the signer becomes the root, the epoch chain, the registrar set and the push binding are dropped, the statement's epoch key and registrars written, the generation recorded; the nonce is spent only after those checks. A journal nobody claimed is claimed by an attested reset. The DS compares `account_ref` to nothing (it never learns the account id, invariant #5) **ADR-0078 phase 6 (2026-10-16):** the attested reset (`src/key_reset.rs`, `LAKKO_DS_ATTEST_KEY`, the `lakko-attest` dependency) is removed; `reset` takes the statement alone (`deny_unknown_fields`), migration `0012` drops `ds_group_owner.key_generation`. **ADR-0078 phase 3 (2026-10-16):** the wake header is the sealed blob (312 base64url characters, version byte checked), forwarded unread to push-gw as `{wake, envelope_pointer}` and capped per journal only; the push binding (route, table, pepper, the binding check on a wake) is removed by migration `0011`. **ADR-0081 (2026-10-18, `src/commit.rs`, migration `0013`):** `POST /ds/group/{hex}/commit` takes an Append `RegisterEpoch` statement for N+1 and the commit row, proven by an epoch-N key (another proof epoch is `400`), and in one transaction verifies both, applies the ADR-0057 rank rules at `latest + 1`, appends the row and records `commit_row_hash` / `commit_message_id` with the registration (`201`); the identical re-send answers the original row (`200`) under any retained key, before the write tier and without spending a nonce; another key or row at a registered epoch, or an epoch other than `latest + 1`, is `409`, decided before the write tier. **Open question 1, decided 2026-10-18 by the maintainer:** `PUT .../access` refuses a plain Append for every journal with `400 {"error":"append-needs-commit"}` (checked before the signature), so a new epoch exists only with its row (C-80); claim, Replace (which clears the replaced epoch's row binding), `registrars`, `registrars-set` and `root` are unchanged. DB-gated tests in `commit.rs` and `ownership.rs`; the shared test helper registers epochs through the commit route. **Admitting key per mailbox row (ADR-0060 section 2, amended 2026-11-06; migration `0016`):** a stored mailbox row records the reach key that admitted it (`ds_mailbox_messages.reach_pk`: the key a direct proof named, or a delegation's issuer; NULL for a proofless write) and the owner's signed read returns it as `reach_pk_b64` (absent when NULL, and on every other queue), so the owner's device can tell a row written under a retired `contact` key. The DS knows no classes: the retired-key bound is the set of keys the owner keeps registered, and a key it removed is the uniform `401`. A copy of the database now shows which registered key admitted each row (the shared `contact` key does not name a contact) |
| `storage` | **Since ADR-0065 (2026-10-26): every blob request is answered from a blob index (`BlobIndex`, `SqlIndex` on MariaDB when `LAKKO_STORAGE_DATABASE_URL` is set, else an append-only `FileIndex` beside the blobs; no account in a row), `PUT` needs an upload pass and a delete public key, `GET` / `HEAD` / `POST /blob/keep` a read pass (`POST /blob/pass/{upload,read}`, one anonymous token each, HMAC pass `p1.`, 1 h, budgets in memory), `DELETE /blob/{hex}` a storage proof under the row's delete key; a daily sweep removes blobs not accessed for 365 days; 404 `blob-not-found` means gone.** Ciphertext blob proxy — **backed by the local filesystem rooted at `LAKKO_BLOB_DIR`, not SeaweedFS**; SeaweedFS / S3 is a Phase-4+ migration the source names as future work, and this row asserted it as present for a long time. Two surfaces: the content-addressed blob store (`blob.rs` / `stream.rs`, the ADR-0023 attachment target) and the **backup blob** (`backup.rs` — `PUT` / `GET` / `DELETE /backup`, session-token gated, `MAX_BACKUP_BYTES`-capped), which is the storage half of [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md) and stays byte-opaque: the auth-signed head rides *inside* the blob, so no migration was needed here. **A third surface since 2026-11-14 (ADR-0064 section 1, host-tested only, not deployed):** the vault routes `PUT`/`DELETE /vault/{id}` and `PUT`/`GET /vault/{id}/head` and `/vault/{id}/chunk/{n}` (`src/vault.rs`, migration `0004`): a claim spends one `vault-claim` token, every other request is signed with the vault's write key (`lakko_vault::proof`), a body is `version(u64 BE) ‖ sealed` accepted only at current + 1, a bad proof is one 401 whether the vault exists or not, per-vault limits chosen (64 MiB, 256 chunks, 240 writes an hour) |
| `gif-proxy` | GIF search + media proxy in front of GIPHY — `lakko-svc-gif-proxy` ([ADR-0047](docs/adr/0047-media-messages-and-gif-proxy.md) §4). Stateless (no DB, no disk), `127.0.0.1:8091`, nginx `/api/gifs/` (web) + `api.lakko.me/gifs/` (native) with the usual prefix strip. Session-token auth identical to `storage`. `GET /gifs/trending?offset=` + `GET /gifs/search?q=&offset=` (fixed page of 24, `rating=pg-13`, q trimmed 1..=100 chars, offset <= 4999) return `{items: [{id, preview_url, full_url, width, height}], next_offset}` where both URLs are proxy paths `/gifs/media/{id}/{preview\|full}`, never GIPHY's; `GET /gifs/media/{id}/{variant}` takes an alphanumeric id only, resolves it through GIPHY's API (or a short-lived **per-account** rendition map filled by that account's own searches, so a preview does not cost an API call and a cache hit reveals nothing about other accounts), checks the rendition host (`https`, `giphy.com` / `*.giphy.com`, on every redirect hop too), and streams it with a size cap (preview 2 MiB, full 8 MiB) and a content-type allowlist (`image/gif`, `image/webp`, `video/mp4`). No client-supplied URL is ever fetched. No API key → every `/gifs` route `503 gif-provider-not-configured`; upstream failure → `502 gif-upstream-failed` (10 s timeout); per-account token buckets (search+trending, media) → `429 rate-limited` + `Retry-After`. Logs method, route template, status and latency only — never a query, id, account or GIPHY URL (the API key rides in it); nginx access/error logging is off on those locations for the same reason |
| `channels` | Phase 4 channel-broadcast backend. Publisher / subscriber / rotation paths complete. `ChannelStore` trait with two impls: `InMemoryChannelStore` (test default, dev fallback) + `SqlChannelStore` (MariaDB, production; migrations under `services/channels/migrations/000{1..4}_*.sql`). Toggled at boot via `LAKKO_CHANNELS_DATABASE_URL`. Background prune loop covers both posts (30-day TTL) + stale subscribers (90-day TTL via `last_active_at`, FK cascade drops envelope), hourly cadence; toggles: `LAKKO_CHANNELS_RETENTION_SECS` / `LAKKO_CHANNELS_SUBSCRIBER_RETENTION_SECS` / `LAKKO_CHANNELS_PRUNE_INTERVAL_SECS`. **Batched rotation ([ADR-0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md)):** a two-phase staged prepare (`POST /rotate/stage`, chunked ≤5000, `channel_staged_envelopes` migration `0003`, never served) + atomic `POST /rotate/commit`, alongside the legacy single-shot `POST /rotate` — C-42 preserved as "the commit is atomic". **Multi-epoch envelope retention ([ADR-0031](docs/adr/0031-multi-epoch-envelope-retention.md)):** `channel_envelopes` keyed `(channel, subscriber, epoch)` (migration `0004`); each write path files at a concrete epoch + prunes to the highest N (`LAKKO_CHANNELS_ENVELOPE_RETENTION_EPOCHS`, default 4, floor 1); `fetch_envelope` returns the highest retained epoch (unchanged wire), the new `fetch_envelopes` (`GET .../subscribers/:pk/envelopes`) the whole retained `[(epoch, envelope)]` set — an offline subscriber catches up by opening each into a client-side `epoch → seed` map (`deliver_envelope` refuses a future epoch, so the read path never serves a pre-committed rotation). Server stays opaque — never decrypts `ChannelPost` bytes or HPKE seed envelopes; `lakko-channels` crate is `dev-dep` only. See [`docs/security/calls.md`](docs/security/calls.md) C-40..C-42 **Phase B of ADR-0087 (2026-11-06, migration `0012`; DB-gated tests run against local MariaDB):** the counts read `GET /v1/channels/{id}/reactions?posts=` carries each post's `comments`, a counter maintained in the add / delete transaction (`channel_comment_counts`); reactions on comments, `POST .../posts/{post}/comments/{comment}/reactions` (write-proof op 5, one `channel-react` token, 201 / 200, `404 unknown-comment`), `DELETE .../comments/{comment}/reactions/{writer_key_hex}/{label_hex}` (op 6, 204), `GET .../posts/{post}/comment-reactions?comments=` (at most 50, `bad-comments` / `too-many-comments`), rows in `channel_comment_reactions` (cascade with the comment); `DELETE /v1/channels/{id}` (owner proof over an empty body, 204): rows deleted, a tombstone kept (`channel_deleted`), every route that then names the channel answers `410 {"code":"channel-deleted"}` (an extension marker on the 404 plus a router layer), a repeat delete 204 without a proof, a create of that id 410 |
| `edge` | **A Phase-0 stub, not a service.** `main.rs` is 28 lines whose whole body logs "edge service starting (Phase 0 stub)"; there is no router, no cascade and no signaling, and it has not been touched since the Phase-0 foundations commit. The intended scope — broadcast cascade + audio-room signaling — is the *plan*, and the row read as if it had shipped |
| `discovery` | Username lookup via OPRF. Also fronts the identity-bundle store (the AccountIdentity pubkey + the ADR-0016 supersession proof + the ADR-0019 `leaf_auth`); on a key change it best-effort appends the new binding to `transparency` (reconciler sweep), threading `leaf_auth` so the log entry is client-authorised. **Genesis-attest sidecar (ADR-0036)** — migration `0016` adds an opaque `genesis_attest` column; discovery stores + forwards it to the transparency append (`genesis_attest_b64`) verbatim (never parsed, invariant #2), `COALESCE`-preserved across non-rotation re-uploads (a rotation carries none — its supersession proof already authorises it). **No proof of work since [ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S3** (`/pow/challenge`, `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_POW_EFFORT_BITS`, `src/pow.rs` and `lakko-pow-server` removed; migration `0020` drops `pow_challenges` after `0019`, the byte-identical spent-set): `POST /discovery/oprf` spends one `discovery-lookup` anonymous token (`x-lakko-anon-token`, the shared gate in `lakko-anon-token-server`), `POST /discovery/channel/register` one `discovery-register`, the three ledger `.../consume` routes one `invite-consume`; `lookup` and `channel/lookup` carry no token (their enumeration step is the OPRF evaluation) and keep the per-IP limiter; the session-gated writes are counted per account by a separate write bucket (`rate_limit::write_middleware`, sized by the per-account lookup knobs). A contact-invite consume of an absent id now answers the documented `404` (it was a `500`: MariaDB's `INSERT IGNORE` downgrades the FK violation). **Key reset ([ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) S3, built 2026-10-03; `src/key_reset.rs`, migration `0026` adds `identity_bundles.key_generation` (default 1) + an opaque `key_reset_attest VARBINARY(256)`):** the **change rule** on `POST /discovery/identity` — a key CHANGE is accepted only with a supersession proof (presence-only, as before) or a `key_reset_attest` (standard base64) that discovery VERIFIES under the pinned `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_ATTEST_KEY` (signature, the session account's `account_ref`, the uploaded key) at a generation strictly above the row's; an attested change stores the attestation + generation, bumps the cool-down marker, appends `is_reset` to the KT log and **deletes every KeyPackage row of the account** in the same transaction; the ADR-0017 **heal** is retired, so a change carrying nothing is `409 identity-change-needs-attestation`; other codes `400 key-reset-attestation-invalid`, `409 key-generation-not-newer` (replay), `429 identity-changed-too-recently`, `503 key-reset-disabled` (no key pinned: no key can change without a proof). The **generation floor** on the two identity writes (`POST /discovery/identity`, `POST /discovery/keypackage`): a token whose generation is below the row's is `401 key-generation-superseded`; a higher one is accepted and not recorded. `GET /discovery/identity/{id}` returns `key_generation`. **`DELETE /discovery/keypackage/device/{id}` is NOT under the floor** (an old-generation token can delete a row within the access TTL; G-46). A pre-existing defect fixed by the new tests: concurrent first publishes of different accounts deadlocked on the `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` gap lock of an absent row (`500`); the bounded upsert retry now covers MariaDB 1213 / 1205. Route tests DB-gated on `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_TEST_DATABASE_URL` |A contact-invite consume of an absent id now answers the documented `404` (it was a `500`: MariaDB's `INSERT IGNORE` downgrades the FK violation) **Unclaimed-account sweep (ADR-0032 guest lifecycle, 2026-10-02, `account.rs`):** every 600 s, identity bundles older than `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_UNCLAIMED_ACCOUNT_TTL_SECS` (default 86400, `0` = off) whose account has no `usernames` row are deleted with the account's KeyPackages, name-search rows, public record and owned invite rows (`purge_owned_rows`, shared with `DELETE /discovery/account`); the delete re-checks the username in the same statement, so a name claimed between the scan and the delete keeps the account **Locate (ADR-0080, `src/locate.rs`):** anonymous `GET /discovery/locate` answers `{city, country}`, part of it, or `{}` for the request's own address as `rate_limit::source_ip` trusts it (X-Real-IP, else the rightmost X-Forwarded-For entry, else the socket), behind the per-IP bucket of the other anonymous routes, `Cache-Control: no-store`, no session, no token, no table, no log line; the DB-IP City Lite file (MaxMind format, `maxminddb` 0.32, ISC) is read once at startup from `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_CITY_DB`, unset or unreadable = `{}` with one startup warning. Route tests over a pool never reached plus a TRACE-level capture that finds neither the address nor the answer **Batch OPRF for publishing (2026-11-04, ADR-0087 section 4 and ADR-0060 amendments):** `POST /discovery/oprf/batch` (`oprf.rs` `evaluate_batch`) evaluates 1..=66 blinded elements (`MAX_OPRF_BATCH` = `lakko_contacts::MAX_HANDLE_TERMS + 1`, room for a rename's old `@name`) in request order for one `discovery-register` token and no session, under the per-IP bucket; a malformed batch is a 400 before the token is spent, a dormant service answers 503 first; lookups stay one `discovery-lookup` token per `/discovery/oprf` evaluation **Donations (`docs/ux/donations.md`, 2026-11-15, `src/donate.rs`):** anonymous `GET /discovery/donate` (the ways to donate allowed for the request's country, from the same city database as `/discovery/locate`; never the phone), `GET /discovery/donate/{id}/qr` (PNG) and `POST /discovery/donate/{id}/phone` with `{"action":"reveal"}`, all behind the per-IP bucket, no store, no log line naming the address, the country or the method; the methods (kinds `sbp` / `revolut` / `bmc` / `crypto`, an optional `countries` rule `RU,BY` or `!RU,!BY`, an unknown country failing an allow-list and passing a refuse-list) are read once from the JSON file `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_DONATIONS_FILE`; unset or refused = an empty list and one startup warning **Donations (`docs/ux/donations.md` section 3, `src/donate.rs`; signed since 2026-11-16, host-tested only):** reads the signed file once at startup from the systemd credential `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/lakko-donations` (unit `LoadCredential=lakko-donations:/etc/lakko/credstore/lakko-donations.json`, root 0600; plain at rest on systemd 249) or, without one, `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_DONATIONS_FILE`; at most 1 MiB, 16 methods, QR PNG <= 512 KiB. Each entry `{id, countries?, statement_b64, phone_b64?, qr_png_b64?}`: the statement must parse and pass the field rules and name the entry's id, an embedded QR must hash to the signed `qr_sha256`, a phone statement must exist exactly when `has_phone` and name the same id; with `LAKKO_DISCOVERY_DONATIONS_KEY` (base64url) every signature is verified too. Any problem refuses the whole file (empty list, one warning naming only the method's position). `GET /discovery/donate` -> `{"methods":[{"id","statement_b64"}]}` (country rule unsigned, server-side), `GET /discovery/donate/{id}/qr` -> the PNG, `POST /discovery/donate/{id}/phone` `{"action":"reveal"}` -> `{"statement_b64"}`; standard base64. Residuals: G-57 |
| `transparency` | CONIKS-style KT log — `lakko-svc-transparency`. Persists the Merkle tree + operator-signed roots; serves `GET /transparency/{root,latest/:key,proof/:idx}` + `operator-key`. Append (`POST`, append-secret-gated) verifies the client `leaf_auth` and refuses an unauthorised new binding under `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_LEAF_AUTH` (G-30 #2, ADR-0019). Witness integration (ADR-0020): **co-signs-on-read** — signs the head root fresh per `/root`, reuses a cached witness cosig keyed on `(tree_root, log_size)`, re-obtains it only on growth, and serves **no** cosig (never a stale one) when the witness is unreachable. **K-of-N witness quorum (ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1)** — `witness_client.rs` `list_from_env` fans out to a LIST of witnesses (`LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URLS` + `_COSIGN_SECRETS`, comma-separated 1:1 positional, empty-entry fail-closed; backward-compat singular `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_URL` / `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_COSIGN_SECRET` fallback — the shorthand `_SECRET` this row used to print names no real variable — plus `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_WITNESS_TIMEOUT_SECS`; nothing set → dormant); `root_with_cosig` best-effort collects a BUNDLE served as `witness_cosigs_b64: Vec<String>` (a down / refusing witness is absent, never blocking, never a stale per-witness cosig); migration `0005` widens the single-row `witnessed_root` cosig → a concatenated bundle AND adds a per-witness `witness_high_water` table (each witness handed a consistency proof from ITS OWN last-co-signed position, advanced only on its own success — anti-stranding auto-rejoin of a witness that was down during a partial-success growth). The **client** enforces the K-of-N threshold (`verify_witness_quorum`); the server is best-effort collection only. Operator Ed25519 key `load_or_init` (+ a dev/e2e `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_OPERATOR_SEED_HEX` fixed-seed override, env-gated, loud-warn, prod-inert). **Auth-attested genesis gate (ADR-0036, G-30 finding 2)** — migration `0004` adds an opaque `genesis_attest` sidecar (never in `canonical_bytes`, so it touches no root/proof); the genesis/reset arm of the append gate (`store.rs`) additionally runs `verify_genesis_fresh` (fail-closed) behind default-OFF `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_REQUIRE_GENESIS_ATTEST` + pinned `LAKKO_TRANSPARENCY_GENESIS_ATTEST_KEY` (`operator_key.rs`, loud startup diagnostics per flag×key combo), so a genesis leaf without a valid account-authenticated attestation is refused — turning first-entry squatting from detected to PREVENTED; the ADR-0019 self-sig gate stays as belt-and-suspenders |
| `witness` | Independent KT-log witness co-signer — `lakko-svc-witness` (ADR-0020, G-30 #1 split-view defence). A dedicated Ed25519 key (separate trust domain from the operator — a deploy property). `POST /witness/cosign` (cosign-secret-gated, constant-time): verifies the operator root sig + no-rollback + a Merkle consistency proof from its last-co-signed high-water, then co-signs + advances; **refuses a fork** (a same-size divergent root → 409 `NotConsistent`). `GET /witness/key` publishes the verifying key. Persists only `(root, log_size)` high-water — never accounts (invariant #5). Server stays opaque (invariant #2). **K-of-N (ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1): this service is UNCHANGED** — a witness quorum is N INDEPENDENT instances of this same binary (each its own Ed25519 key + DB + high-water); the K-of-N logic lives entirely in the transparency fan-out + the client-side `verify_witness_quorum` gate, so no witness-service code change was needed |
| `push-gw` | Fan-out to **four** providers, not three — `providers/{apns,fcm,unifiedpush,webpush}.rs`, with `ProviderKind::WebPush` a first-class variant (Web Push RFC 8291 framing lives here, deliberately not in `lakko-push`). Public routes: per-device `/push/register` / `/push/unregister` and group `/push/group/subscribe` / `/push/group/unsubscribe` over `push_group_subscriptions` (migration `0002`), each spending one `push-register` anonymous token (`x-lakko-anon-token`) since ADR-0054 S3 through `lakko-anon-token-server`'s gate (spent-set migration `0004`, `pow_challenges` dropped by `0005`; `/pow/challenge`, `LAKKO_PUSH_GW_POW_EFFORT_BITS` and `lakko-pow-server` removed). **The wake is not public since [ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) S1:** `/push/notify` is deleted (it had no caller) and `/push/notify-group` is served only on an internal listener (`LAKKO_PUSH_GW_INTERNAL_BIND`, default `127.0.0.1:8092`, `src/internal.rs`) behind `x-lakko-internal-secret` compared in constant time (`LAKKO_PUSH_GW_INTERNAL_SECRET`; unset -> the listener does not start), body `{push_gid (64 hex), envelope_pointer, kind}`, no PoW; the DS is its only caller. **The group id it keys on is an opaque `BINARY(32)` it never interprets** — which is exactly why [ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md) / [0043](docs/adr/0043-push-routing-secret-rotation-on-removal.md) / [0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md) could change that value's *meaning* from the DS journal key to a derived `push_gid` while leaving this service byte-identical unchanged; a `grep` here finds no `routing_id`, and that is the design, not a gap **ADR-0078 phase 3 (2026-10-16):** the public routes are `GET /push/wake-key`, `POST /push/subscribe {sub_id, push_gid, wake_pk, sealed}` and `POST /push/unsubscribe {sub_id}`; `push_tokens` and `push_group_subscriptions` are dropped (migration `0006`), so no device pseudonym and no token in the clear is stored. The internal notify takes the sealed wake; push-gw opens it with its first-boot X25519 key (table `push_wake_key`), verifies the wake-key signature, refuses a timestamp more than 10 min off and a seen nonce (in memory), caps per `push_gid` (`LAKKO_PUSH_GW_WAKE_BURST` / `_PER_MINUTE`), opens each matching row with the unlock key and sends; the payload names the subscription as `s`. |
| `transport-rendezvous` | **A Phase-0 stub, not a service.** `main.rs` is 29 lines logging "transport-rendezvous service starting (Phase 0 stub)" — no signing, no config serving, no routes, untouched since Phase-0 foundations. The signed-bootstrap-config machinery that exists is all **client-side** (`BootstrapConfig::verify` + `DohFetcher` in `lakko-transport`, `fetch_bootstrap_config` on the UniFFI surface); there is no server counterpart yet, and the ADR-0026 Phase-5 deploy is where one would land |
| `obfs-proxy` | Obfs-terminating proxy — `lakko-svc-obfs-proxy` (lib+bin, ADR-0026 Phases 1-3). Runs the obfs server listeners — **REALITY + Shadowsocks-2022** (TCP) + **WebTransport** (QUIC, via the new `lakko_transport::WebTransportListener`) — terminates each tunnel into a framed `Connection`, and **byte-relays** it to a fixed internal HTTP target (`relay.rs`: full-duplex frame↔TCP via `select!`+mpsc). A dumb, stateless, **confined** relay — never parses the inner HTTP (MLS-E2EE), only reaches the one configured internal address (no open relay), holds no account state (invariant #5). `config.rs` env-parses the target + REALITY (bind/cover-SNI/allow-list) + SS-2022 (bind/PSK) + WebTransport (bind/cover-SNI); `main.rs` runs the accept loops + an axum `health`/`ready`/`version` control plane. The obfs ports are raw, not HTTP. `tests/loopback_e2e.rs` proves all three (REALITY + SS-2022 + WebTransport) end-to-end (client → listener → relay → fake internal HTTP origin → response). **MASQUE: `lakko_transport::MasqueListener` is built (quinn server + h3 extended-CONNECT-UDP accept + 200 + a datagram server `Connection`; handshake verified) but is NOT wired into the proxy** — a real finding: MASQUE `CONNECT-UDP` carries **unreliable, unordered QUIC datagrams**, so relaying a *reliable* internal HTTP service over it does not work (the response datagram is dropped on close → `BadResponse("no header terminator")`; QUIC datagrams are never retransmitted nor flushed ahead of `CONNECTION_CLOSE`). The MASQUE loopback e2e is `#[ignore]`'d documenting this; the three reliable-stream transports cover the use case (MASQUE is for tunnelling UDP, not TCP/HTTP — using it would need a reliability layer the proxy should not add). Native client routing (Phase 4) **landed** end-to-end on iOS + Android (FFI `fetch_bootstrap_config` + arming + the seam on every native REST client — see the ADR-0026 row). **WS-over-tunnel has landed too** — this row used to list it as following: `lakko_transport::tunneled_websocket` over a `ByteStreamConnection`, the UniFFI `open_tunneled_websocket`, both native `SfuSignalingClient`s consuming it, and this service's own non-ignored `reality_tunnel_carries_a_websocket_round_trip` proving the path. Only the Phase-5 deploy + live censored-net test remain. 12 tests (5 loopback e2e of which the MASQUE one is `#[ignore]`d, 4 config, 3 relay) |
| `sfu` | Minimal Rust SFU on `str0m` (stack chosen in `S-3-B-spike`). **Signaling half shipped** — Lakko-native typed wire protocol (welcome / peer-joined / peer-left / signal-relay / ping/pong / error), `lakko-call-jwt` admission, per-peer mpsc fanout, cross-call isolation. The frame definitions themselves moved out to `crates/lakko-sfu-proto` (`src/protocol.rs` is now a re-export shim) so server and both FFI clients parse one definition. 29 non-media unit tests + 9 in `tests/integration_signaling.rs`. **Media half** (`S-3-B-routing-media`): steps 1–5 landed — `str0m =0.19.0` pinned with `rust-crypto` feature (pure-Rust crypto provider, no openssl/aws-lc-rs); `services/sfu/src/media.rs` ships `MediaPeer` (wraps `str0m::Rtc`) + `MediaRouter` (per-call peer map) + `MediaCommand` + `MediaTaskHandle` (async/sync boundary, `oneshot` reply channels, `try_send` back-pressure with `Backpressure` / `TaskGone` / `Media(MediaError)` typed errors) + `spawn_media_task(bind_addr)` (real `tokio::net::UdpSocket` bind + tokio `select!` over inbound UDP, command channel, earliest-peer-timeout sleep; drains `Rtc::poll_output` → `UdpSocket::send_to` on `Output::Transmit`; prunes peers whose `Rtc::is_alive` flipped after DTLS/ICE failure). `Config::media_bind: Option<SocketAddr>` parses `LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_BIND` (unset / empty → signaling-only; malformed → loud `Invalid`); `AppState::new_with_media` + `build_app_with_media` wire the handle through `Router::with_media`, `main.rs` spawns the task when configured. SDP / ICE wire frames (`ClientFrame::SdpOffer` + `IceCandidate`, `ServerFrame::SdpAnswer`, `ErrorCode::BadSdp` / `BadIceCandidate`) lazy-promote a media peer on first offer via `peer_or_insert`. RTP fanout (step 5): `drain_outputs` returns `DrainSummary { earliest_deadline, pending: Vec<PendingFanout> }`, each `Output::Event(Event::MediaData(_))` is captured tagged with originating `MediaPeerId`; `fanout_media_data(router, &pending)` walks non-origin peers and calls `MediaPeer::forward_media_data` (typed `MediaForward { Sent / NoWriter / NoCodecMatch / WriteFailed / WrongLayer }` outcome wrapping `Rtc::writer(mid).match_params(params).write(...)` — SFrame ciphertext stays opaque, the SFU forwards bytes without inspection; the fifth variant arrived with simulcast, below). `src/media.rs` now carries 44 unit tests (incl. async round-trip + real UDP-bind smoke + 7 fanout tests) — this row asserted "17" here and "30" a few sentences later, two counts for the same thing and both stale. **Track-routing half** (`S-3-B-tracks` steps 1–3b, complete): `TrackIn` / `TrackOutEntry` / `TrackOutState` bookkeeping + `Event::MediaAdded` capture; `propagate_new_tracks_in` opens a `ToOpen` on every non-origin peer; `negotiate_if_needed` + `drive_renegotiations(router, peers)` dispatch an SFU-initiated `ServerFrame::SdpOffer` back to each peer's outbound mpsc and `accept_renegotiation_answer` promotes the `TrackOutEntry` to `Open` so `forward_media_data` resolves the destination mid. Proven end-to-end in `services/sfu/tests/synthetic_client_e2e.rs`: a single-client test drives the production `spawn_media_task` through a full ICE+DTLS handshake against a real `str0m::Rtc`, and `two_clients_cross_fanout_forwards_media_verbatim` (step 3b) proves a publisher A's 80-byte payload surfaces **verbatim** as `Event::MediaData` at a subscriber B through the full renegotiation wire — the integration-side close of C-39. **Fanout fairness** (Jun 2026): `fanout_media_data` returns a `FanoutOutcome { sent / no_writer / no_codec_match / write_failed / wrong_layer }` tally (one increment per `frame × destination peer`) + emits `lakko_sfu_media_frames_forwarded_total` + `lakko_sfu_media_frames_dropped_total{reason}` Prometheus counters, so a starved subscriber (a `no_writer` spike) is observable rather than silently dropped; `fanout_visits_every_non_origin_peer_for_three_plus` (N ∈ {3,4,5}) + `fanout_tally_is_per_frame_times_non_origin_peers` pin that one origin frame is offered to every non-origin peer exactly once (origin never). **Simulcast-layer selection LANDED** — this row long carried it as the deferred follow-up "wire-touching, needs simulcast-capable test clients", and both halves are now built: `MediaPeer::select_layer` + a per-`TrackOutEntry` `chosen_rid` with a first-layer latch, per-`(Mid, Rid)` `LayerBitrateMeter`s, a PLI keyframe request on a switch, and the bandwidth policy `drive_layer_selection` driven from the live media loop, counted by a third Prometheus counter `lakko_sfu_simulcast_layer_switches_total{direction}`; the simulcast-capable client exists as `simulcast_publisher_negotiates_and_forwards_a_layer_over_the_live_sfu`. **Signaling liveness** (2026-09-19): the socket pings every peer on the WebSocket protocol layer (`LAKKO_SFU_SIGNALING_PING_SECS`, now 5) and drops one silent for `LAKKO_SFU_SIGNALING_IDLE_SECS` (now 15), announcing the leave — before this a vanished peer stayed registered until TCP or nginx gave up. **Call lifetime** (2026-09-20, `src/call_lifetime.rs`): the SFU decides when a call is over — a room that has stood empty for `CALL_EMPTY_GRACE` (5 s) is ended, a later socket for that call id gets `ErrorCode::CallEnded` in place of the Welcome and is closed, and `GET /v1/calls/{call_id}?token=<call jwt>` answers `unknown` / `live` / `closing` / `ended` for the call the token names (nginx routes it beside `/v1/connect`); the clients close the chat's own record on either signal, so a call whose last participant's app died stops being offered within seconds instead of the record's freshness window. **Packet forwarding since 2026-10-02 ([ADR-0066](docs/adr/0066-sfu-forwards-rtp-packets.md)):** everything in this row that describes frames (`Event::MediaData`, `forward_media_data`, `writer.write`, re-packetization) is history. Every peer's `Rtc` runs in RTP mode; `MediaPeer::relay_packet` -> `fanout_packets_into` -> `forward_packet` writes each `Event::RtpPacket` to every other peer of the call with `StreamTx::write_rtp`, payload untouched, under the publisher's sequence number and timestamp plus a per-stream offset (`src/stream_map.rs`), so loss reaches a subscriber as a gap. In frame mode str0m held everything behind a missing packet until N complete frames had queued (15 audio, 30 video): measured on a live call as a 300 ms stop per lost audio packet and 2.3-2.8 s of no video per loss burst. Tests at ADR-0066: 167 unit, 13 signaling, 16 in `tests/synthetic_client_e2e.rs` (two new video tests fail against the frame-mode SFU). **Since then (ADR-0067, 0068, 0070; 2026-10-04 to 10-11) the media half grew well past what this row lists; `services/sfu/README.md` is the reference.** Modules: `uplink_estimate.rs` (the SFU's own estimate of a publisher's path, sent as REMB under `LAKKO_SFU_BITRATE_CONTROL=server`; a cut for over-use needs a standing queue; generalised as `PathEstimator` for the downlink), `fec_receive.rs` / `fec_send.rs` / `video_fec.rs` (`LAKKO_SFU_VIDEO_FEC`, off by default), `playout_delay.rs` (a minimum playout delay per (subscriber, publisher), on unless `LAKKO_SFU_PLAYOUT_DELAY=0`), `downlink_probe.rs` + `downlink_allocation.rs` (each subscriber's downlink estimated from transport-wide feedback, probed before a larger picture is given, the larger tracks given up first, reported in `ServerFrame::VideoGiven`; off unless `LAKKO_SFU_DOWNLINK_CONTROL=1`), `burst_keyframe.rs` (a key frame asked of the publisher when a burst of loss on one of its video streams ends unrepaired, and the burst's packets no longer asked for; on unless `LAKKO_SFU_BURST_KEYFRAME=0`), `video_needed.rs` (`ServerFrame::VideoNeeded`: a publisher's main and middle senders are needed only while some subscriber names them in `video-wants`, on at once and off after 10 s; on unless `LAKKO_SFU_VIDEO_NEEDED=0`), `uplink_fallback.rs` (simulcast publishers only, off unless `LAKKO_SFU_UPLINK_FALLBACK=1`). The camera arrives as three ordinary senders named in the offer (`light_mids`, `middle_mids`) and leaves as three tracks under the MSID stream ids `<peer>`, `<peer>~mid`, `<peer>~light`; the main or the middle one is forwarded only when the subscriber's `video-wants` asks for it, the light one always (each of its packets twice, on unless `LAKKO_SFU_LIGHT_DUPLICATE=0`); the rid-based simulcast path is kept for a publisher that sends rids, and no current client does. Every key-frame request for a publisher track goes through one bounded gate. The vendored `str0m` fork carries fifteen changes (`UPSTREAM.md`; pinned by `tests/fork_delta_guard.rs`). **Opacity is unchanged (C-39):** the SFU reads RTP headers and header extensions, takes key frames from the generic frame descriptor, and of a payload interprets only the one-byte RED header where video RED is negotiated (with video FEC on it also computes ULPFEC over payload bytes, as opaque bytes); it does not read the VP8 bytes ADR-0071 leaves in the clear. **2026-10-11 and 10-12:** `publish_budget.rs` (ADR-0068 amendment: `ServerFrame::PublishBudget`, a ceiling for a publisher's main and middle senders from the SFU's own estimate; only under `LAKKO_SFU_BITRATE_CONTROL=server`, on unless `LAKKO_SFU_PUBLISH_BUDGET=0`; counter `lakko_sfu_publish_budget_sent_total`); `burst_keyframe.rs` now classifies each packet still missing at a burst's end as known media, known not media or unknown, from arrived FEC masks and from the end-of-frame flag and frame id of the generic frame descriptor (`frame_descriptor::packet_position`), and asks for the key frame on one packet of known media whatever the FEC share; and the session extension map (`session_extension_map` in `media.rs`) holds no RFC 6464 audio level, so the SFU neither accepts it from a publisher nor offers it to a subscriber, whatever the configuration (three unit tests and a synthetic-client test). By a count of test attributes in the sources (not run for this note): about 500 unit tests, 13 signaling, 60 in `tests/synthetic_client_e2e.rs` of which 9 are `#[ignore]`d. Live calls so far were browsers only (Chrome, Firefox, Safari, Edge on the test stack); no native client has been on a call against this build. See [`services/sfu/README.md`](services/sfu/README.md) + [`services/sfu/UPSTREAM.md`](services/sfu/UPSTREAM.md) + [ADR-0011](docs/adr/0011-rust-sfu.md) + [`docs/security/calls.md`](docs/security/calls.md) C-39 **No account at the SFU ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2, 2026-10-16):** `Peer` is `{id, room, outbound}`, the peer id is the token's random `peer_id` and a second live socket with it is refused; rooms are keyed on the token's `room`; no `peer_tag`, no `account_pseudonym`, no account prefix in logs. `GET /v1/rooms/{room}` (no token; `400` unless 64 lower-case hex) replaces `GET /v1/calls/{call_id}`, routed by nginx in `infra/nginx/sites-available/sfu.conf` and the test stack. The media loop's logs still name the random peer id and the room (ADR-0078 section 11, phase 8) **Screen share (ADR-0083, 2026-10-25):** `TrackRole::Screen` for the lines an offer names in `screen_mids` (one video and one audio line per offer, at most 8 over a peer's life), forwarded to every subscriber under `<peer>~screen`, never paused by `video-wants` nor switched by `video-needed`, given the downlink before any camera, and a `screen_kbps` reserve in the publish budget (300 to 1500 kbit/s, chosen); host-tested |

### Other

- `schemas/` — protobuf schemas; **single source of truth** for the wire format
- `apps/` — clients (iOS / iPadOS / macOS / watchOS / Android / desktop / web), arrive in Phase 1. **iOS WebRTC pod (G3-1)** shipped: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/WebRTCPeerConnection.swift` wraps `RTCPeerConnectionFactory` + `RTCPeerConnection` from the WebRTC SPM package (`webrtc-sdk/Specs`, exact `125.6422.07` since 2026-11-09, ADR-0088; `stasel/WebRTC` 125.0.0 before); `CallSession.swift` drives real SDP offer/answer + ICE through it. **Android WebRTC pod (G3-2)** shipped: `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/calls/WebRtcPeerConnection.kt` wraps `PeerConnectionFactory` + `PeerConnection` from `io.github.webrtc-sdk:android` (pinned `125.6422.07`); `CallSession.kt` drives real SDP offer/answer + ICE through it via `suspendCancellableCoroutine` around libwebrtc's `SdpObserver`; `RECORD_AUDIO` + `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS` permissions wired into AndroidManifest.xml. Both platforms: audio-only on first pass; video capture + TURN bootstrap deferred (G3-{1,2}-video / G3-{1,2}-turn) and now landed. Video capture surface: iOS `LocalCameraCapture.swift` wraps `RTCCameraVideoCapturer`; Android `CameraVideoCapture.kt` wraps `Camera2Enumerator` + `CameraVideoCapturer`; both target 720p / 30 fps front-camera by default. Session-side wire-up (G3-{1,2}-video-ui, Jun 2026): `CallSession` on both platforms exposes an observable local video track (`@Published RTCVideoTrack?` on iOS / `StateFlow<VideoTrack?>` on Android), constructs the capture pipeline pre-SDP-build on `dial(video=true)` + `acceptCall(withVideo=)`, tears down camera in the shared `teardownMediaPipeline()` helper on both end paths. Rendering `session.localVideoTrack` through `LocalVideoPreviewView` inside the in-call UI landed as G3-{1,2}-video-ui-screen (Jun 2026): `CallUiState` on both platforms exposes a `localVideoTrack` published / StateFlow surface + a `bind(to:)` / `bindTo(...)` helper that subscribes the matching `CallSession` channel through Combine (iOS) / a coroutine collector (Android). `CallScreen.{swift,kt}` render `LocalVideoPreviewView` inside `ParticipantTile`'s video surface when `participant.isLocal && camOn && localVideoTrack != nil`, with the mirror flag fed from `settings.mirrorLocal`; the prejoin preview gets the same dispatch. Remote-video reception + render landed as G3-{1,2}-remote-video (Jun 2026): the WebRTC pod `Delegate` gains `onRemoteVideoTrack`, fired from libwebrtc's Unified-Plan track-added callback (`didAdd rtpReceiver:streams:` on iOS / `onAddTrack` on Android, video tracks only — audio plays out automatically); `CallSession` exposes a `remoteVideoTrack` observable, `bind(to:)` / `bindTo(...)` bridge it alongside the local track, and `ParticipantTile` renders it **un-mirrored** for the sole remote participant (`soleRemoteParticipantId`, the 1×1 case — the single handle is never painted onto the wrong tile in a group call). G3-{1,2}-remote-video-nway (the per-participant track map keyed on the SFU MSID stream-id = source peer id) **has since landed on both natives** — `CallUiState.remoteVideoTracks: [String: RTCVideoTrack]` / `StateFlow<Map<String, VideoTrack>>` with `setRemoteVideoTrack(_:forParticipant:)`, keyed `"peer-<streamId>"` in each `SfuCallUi` and rendered per tile in each `CallScreen`; so the sole-remote handle described here is the 1×1 path, not the group one. Both `CallScreen.{swift,kt}` still seed `seedMockState()` for the mock design path; `bind(to:)` is the integration seam for the real `CallSession`. Session-bind adapter (G3-{1,2}-session-bind, Jun 2026): `CallUiState.driveFrom(session)` maps `CallSession.state` → `CallPhase` + synthesises the 1×1 roster (local always; sole remote at `connected`/track-arrival, `camOn` from track presence) + bridges both video tracks via a full-rebuild-on-every-emission collector; `CallScreen` accepts an optional `session` param and drives from it (mock fallback when nil), end-call routes to `session.hangUp()`. Call presentation (G3-{1,2}-call-present, Jun 2026): `ChatScreen` now presents the live `CallScreen(session:)` over the chat — iOS via a nested `CallHostView` observing `activeCall` + a `.fullScreenCover`; Android via a full-screen Compose `Dialog` — once the call is live (outgoing/connecting/connected/ending/done), with the `ringing` incoming-call prompt kept as a lightweight inline banner. `ChatViewModel` already owned `activeCall` + `startCall` (toolbar phone button) + accept/hangUp/clearFinished + the incoming-offer path, so the whole video/audio/SFrame adapter is now **runtime-active** on outgoing dial + accept-incoming; the in-call End button routes to `session.hangUp()`. Call controls (G3-{1,2}-call-controls, Jun 2026): the in-call mic / camera buttons drive the real WebRTC tracks — `CallSession` exposes observable `micEnabled` / `cameraEnabled` + `setMicrophoneEnabled` / `setCameraEnabled` (toggling `RTCAudio/VideoTrack.isEnabled` via the pod), `driveFrom` maps them onto the local participant tile, and the buttons route to the session. Call-key wiring (G3-{1,2}-call-key, Jun 2026) — **unblocks the call feature**: previously `setCallKey` was never called in the chat-call path, so `attachSFrame` always failed and every call refused per security invariant #4 (the SFrame keying plumbing was simply absent). Now `ChatRepository.deriveCallKey(groupIdHex, callId)` derives the per-call seed `group.export_secret(identity, "lakko/call/v1", callId, 32)` (audit-pack C-1 — deterministic per MLS epoch, so offerer + answerer derive identical bytes) + `own_leaf_index()`, and `ChatViewModel` arms `session.setCallKey(seed, localLeaf)` before dial/accept on both the outgoing and incoming paths (a failed derivation leaves the seed unset → the call refuses, fail-closed). E2EE pill (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-active, Jun 2026): `CallSession.e2eeActive` flips true when `setCallKey` arms the seed; `driveFrom` maps it onto `E2eeState(active:)` so the in-call pill shows the green lock only when SFrame keying is real (it stayed at the negotiating default before). Connection quality (G3-{1,2}-conn-quality iter1, Jun 2026): the in-call header shows a real quality signal sampled from WebRTC `getStats`. The pod's `sampleConnectionStats()` (iOS `async`, Android `suspend` over `getStats`) walks the stats report for the active ICE `candidate-pair` (`state == succeeded`, preferring `nominated`), reads `currentRoundTripTime` (s→ms) and flags `relayed` when either candidate's `candidateType == "relay"`; `CallSession` exposes `rttMs` (`@Published Int?` / `StateFlow<Int?>`) + `connectionRelayed` (`Bool`) driven by a 2 s background poll loop armed on the first connected transition and torn down with the media pipeline. `driveFrom`/`rebuildFromSession` map `rttMs` → the local participant's `QualityTier` (UX display bands, not measured SLOs: nil→good, <100→excellent, <200→good, <350→fair, <600→poor, else→bad) + republish a `ConnectionDetail(rttMs, downBps, upBps, connectionType=relay/direct/unknown)`. iter2 (Jun 2026): the popover's down/up lines show a live bitrate — `ConnectionStatsSnapshot` carries the candidate-pair's cumulative `bytesReceived`/`bytesSent`, `CallSession` keeps the previous (bytes, monotonic-time) sample and derives `downBps`/`upBps` in **bits per second** = `deltaBytes * 8 / dt` (dt from `ProcessInfo.systemUptime` / `System.nanoTime()` so poll jitter doesn't skew it; guarded `dt > 0.1 s`, deltas clamped `>= 0`), reset to nil in `teardownMediaPipeline`. Web parity (G3-3-conn-quality, Jun 2026): `apps/web/src/calls/call-session.ts` `CallSession` grows a `getStats()` poll loop (armed on the first `connected` transition, cleared in `dispose()`) that emits a typed `WebConnectionStats { rttMs, relayed, downBps, upBps }` via an optional `onConnectionStats` callback — same candidate-pair selection (succeeded + nominated) + same monotonic-clock byte-delta bitrate math; `Chat.tsx::buildCallSession` wires it into a `callConnStats` signal + the shared `callActions.setConnectionDetail` / `setQuality`. iter3 (per-remote-participant quality for group calls — needs the SFU multi-party wire) is deferred. Web session-bind (G3-3-web-session-bind, Jun 2026): the full `apps/web/src/routes/Call.tsx` in-call UI is now driven by the live `CallSession` (the web analog of iOS/Android session-bind + call-present). `Call` gains optional `embedded` / `onExit` props; in embedded mode it skips the mount-time `callActions.reset()` (the parent owns lifecycle), routes end-call + the ended-overlay dismissal through `onExit` (→ `CallSession.hangUp`) instead of navigating home, and hides the text E2EE pill (the 1×1 web path is P2P DTLS-SRTP, not SFrame — a "secured" text chip would mislead, and a text chip mid-call is poor UX). `Chat.tsx` maps the session's `WebCallState` → the shared store (`setPhase` + a synthesised 1×1 roster + `setE2ee` inactive) and presents `<Call embedded onExit>` as a full-screen overlay for live states (`outgoing`/`connecting`/`connected`/`ending`), keeping the lightweight inline banner only for `ringing` (Accept/Decline). The remote `<audio>` sink was lifted to a stable slot so it doesn't unmount when the banner gives way to the overlay. `tsc --noEmit` clean. Web call controls (G3-3-web-call-controls, Jun 2026): the in-call mic button now drives the real session — `CallSession.setMicrophoneEnabled(on)` flips the captured audio track's `enabled` flag (local-only gate, no renegotiation), and a `Chat.tsx` effect watching the store's local-participant `micOn` mirrors the `callActions.setMic` toggle onto the live session. Web 1×1 video shipped (later): the outbound caller's 📹 video button (`dial({audio,video})`) AND the incoming answerer's "Accept with video" (accept audio-only at capture, then camOn=true drives the shipped `addLocalVideo` mid-call camera-on via perfect-negotiation), local + remote video tiles — so the camera button drives a real local video track (the answerer captures audio-only until it opts in, fixing the old acquire-then-mute). `remoteWantsVideo` (from the offer's `mediaVideo`) gates the second accept button. Deferred web follow-ups: per-remote-participant quality (iter3) — still deferred; the roster's "Peer" placeholder is **not**, since the ADR-0021 resolution landed (`displayName`, else the chat label, else `"Peer"` as a last resort). Prejoin Join (G3-{1,2}-prejoin-join, Jun 2026): tapping the chat call button now PREPARES the session (builds it + arms the SFrame key) without dialing — the session sits `idle` (mapped to the prejoin phase), the in-call screen presents the prejoin mic/camera preview, and the Join button calls `CallSession.dial` carrying the chosen mic/camera; a Cancel button tears the prepped-but-undialed session down (iOS: `session.hangUp` + `onExit`; Android: `ChatViewModel.cancelActiveCall`). The presentation gate (`isLive` / `callIsLive`) now includes `idle` so the prejoin shows for an outgoing call before it dials. Incoming calls keep the ringing banner → `acceptCall` (no greenroom). Deferred follow-ups: live 2-device verification (local builds only here; seed-match asserted by construction so far), hand-raise/screen-share controls (no real backing — stay mock), the e2ee comparable-fingerprint string (G3-{1,2}-e2ee-fingerprint — **DONE** Jun 2026: `lakko_calls::safety_number::call_safety_number` = domain-separated BLAKE3 of the shared per-call seed → 4×4-digit code, golden-pinned + canonical, FFI `callSafetyNumber` (uniffi + wasm), rendered in the in-call E2EE pill on **all three** clients, the native 1×1 path included; `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES. **Web reached that state late and the reason is the lesson.** The number was derived and written into the store (`sfu-call-ui.ts`, `multi-joiner-call.ts` → `callActions.setE2ee({ shortFingerprint })`) but the only component rendering it, `Call.tsx`'s `E2eePill`, sat behind `<Show when={props.embedded !== true}>` while `Chat.tsx` always mounts `<Call embedded={true}>` — so it showed on **no live web call**, group calls included, surviving only on the standalone `/call` design route. The suppressing comment gave a **correct** reason for the wrong scope: the 1×1 web path really is P2P DTLS-SRTP with no SFrame and no number, but the same component also renders the SFU group call, which has both. **A layout flag was answering a cryptographic question**, and it drifted the moment a second call kind shared the component. Fixed by asking the question directly — `apps/web/src/calls/safety-pill.ts` `showsSafetyNumber(e2ee)` shows the pill exactly when there **is** a number, which the 1×1 path already denies it by publishing `{ active: false, shortFingerprint: null }`; so the misleading-chip hazard is closed by the data rather than by a second gate someone must remember, and a future path that arms SFrame gets the pill automatically. It is a **module, not a condition in the JSX**, because `vitest` runs with no jsdom and no Solid transform — nothing in `routes/Call.tsx` is reachable from a test, which is how a wrong condition survived there unnoticed) **SFrame keying done** via the G3-sframe-native arc (N-1..N-6, closes G-28): see [`docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md`](docs/ROADMAP-PHASE3-CLOSEOUT.md) G3-1 / G3-2 rows + the new shared `apps/shared/{webrtc-cpp-headers,sframe-bridge}/` + `apps/android/lakko-sframe/` Gradle module + `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SFrameAttach.{h,mm}` Obj-C++ shim. Boot-time selector probe (`SFrameAvailability` on both platforms) enforces security invariant #4 — refuse-to-start if libwebrtc's setFrameEncryptor / setFrameDecryptor are not present. **Web SFrame worker (SFU-3-sframe-c, Jun 2026)** — the browser mirror of the native shim, built on the W3C Encoded Transform API: `apps/web/src/calls/sframe-transform.ts` is the main-thread attach controller (`attachSenderSFrame` / `attachReceiverSFrame` set an `RTCRtpScriptTransform` over one shared module worker; `isSFrameTransformSupported` gates it and the attach helpers THROW when `RTCRtpScriptTransform` is absent — invariant #4, no plaintext-media fallback) and `sframe-transform-worker.ts` is the off-thread engine (loads the wasm core via `loadWasm`, then per frame: encrypt → `ParticipantKey.sealFramed`; decrypt → peek the header KID via `unwrapSframeFrame`, lazily derive that sender's key into a per-leaf `Map`, then `openFramed` — any frame that fails to parse or authenticate is dropped, never forwarded as plaintext). AAD = the SFrame wire header per [ADR-0013](docs/adr/0013-sframe-cross-platform-aad.md) (no host RTP `additional_data`), so a frame sealed in this worker opens byte-for-byte under the native `sframe-bridge` shim and vice versa. **That description is the Jun 2026 state; three things in it changed in Oct 2026.** (1) The frame layout is [ADR-0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md)'s: the worker seals with `ParticipantKey.sealMediaFrame(frame, codec)` and opens with `parseMediaFrame` + `CallKeyRing.openMediaFrameWithOverlap`; a bounded codec header stays in the clear at the start of a VP8 frame, the SFrame header sits at the end, and the AAD is the header, the clear prefix and the trailer (still no host RTP `additional_data`). The codec of a frame comes from `sframe-codec.ts` (frame metadata, else the sender's payload-type table, else nothing clear). (2) The engine moved to `sframe-worker-core.ts`, and there are two ways a browser hands it frames, chosen in `sframe-transport.ts`: `RTCRtpScriptTransform`, or `createEncodedStreams()` where that is absent; `isSFrameTransformSupported` is false, and the attach helpers throw, only when a browser has neither, and `link-join-gate.ts` refuses such a browser before a guest account is created. (3) The native bridge seals and opens the same ADR-0071 layout since 2026-10-11, so the cross-stack claim holds again by construction; no web-to-native media has been exchanged on a call since either change. `vite.config.ts` repeats `vite-plugin-wasm` under `worker.plugins` so the worker bundle resolves the wasm import; `tsc --noEmit` clean + `vite build` emits the worker chunk with the wasm glue inside it. Not wired into the 1×1 `CallSession` — that path is P2P DTLS-SRTP with no SFU and stays so — but **the SFU-multiparty wire-up this deferred to has landed**: `sfu-call-session.ts` and `multi-joiner-call.ts` both call `attachSenderSFrame` / `attachReceiverSFrame`, so the worker is live for every web group call. Deferred gate (cannot run headless): live cross-platform RTP verification of a web↔native frame over the SFU forwarding path. **Web multi-party session (SFU-3-web-b, Jun 2026)** — `apps/web/src/calls/sfu-call-session.ts` `SfuCallSession` composes the whole web SFU call: it opens the `SfuSignalingClient` (`lakko-sfu-v1` WS, JWT-admitted), builds one `RTCPeerConnection` to the SFU, `getUserMedia` + `addTrack` + `attachSenderSFrame` on every local track (publish offer is client-initiated, sent via `sendSdpOffer`; SFU replies `SdpAnswer`), answers SFU-initiated `SdpOffer`s through a serialized queue (subscribe renegotiation → `sendSdpAnswer`), and on `ontrack` runs `attachReceiverSFrame` + surfaces the remote `MediaStreamTrack` keyed by MSID stream id. Refuses at `connect()` (before any capture) if `RTCRtpScriptTransform` is absent (invariant #4). `tsc --noEmit` clean + `vite build` pulls the full signaling+SFrame+worker graph. **Chat-UI wire-up (SFU-3-web-{c,…}, Jun 2026)** — `apps/web/src/calls/sfu-call-ui.ts` `createSfuCallUi` binds the session callbacks onto the shared `callActions` store (phase + roster + E2EE) and exposes a reactive `remoteStreams` signal (MSID stream id → `MediaStream`). `apps/web/src/routes/Chat.tsx` routes the call button by chat size: a group chat (`group.memberCount > 2`) calls `startGroupCall` — derive the per-call SFrame seed via `group.exportSecret(identity, "lakko/call/v1", callId, 32)` + leaf via `group.ownLeafIndex` (audit-pack C-1, deterministic per MLS epoch), mint the call-admission JWT (`CallJwtClient`, bound to the live `auth.identitySecrets().sessionToken`), build `createSfuCallUi`, connect; 1×1 chats stay on the P2P `CallSession`. The existing store-driven `Call.tsx` overlay renders the SFU roster tiles automatically (no per-call-type branching in the view); remote audio plays through one hidden `<audio>` per `remoteStreams` entry (keyed by stream identity). SFU base URL from `VITE_LAKKO_SFU_URL` (`sfu-config.ts`); unset → the group-call button surfaces a clear error (1×1 unaffected). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` emits the worker chunk from the real entry graph (Chat.tsx → adapter → session → SFrame → worker). Per-tile remote VIDEO rendering (SFU-3-web-e, Jun 2026): the SFU already pins the downstream MSID stream-id to the source peer id (`add_media(.., Some(source_peer.to_string()), ..)` in `services/sfu/src/media.rs`; `MediaPeerId == PeerId`, the same string the signaling roster carries), so `ev.streams[0].id` maps straight to the roster tile. The adapter flips a remote participant's `camOn` on video-track arrival (new `callActions.setParticipantCamera`), `Call.tsx` `ParticipantTile` gains a `remoteStreamFor` prop and renders a real `<video muted playsinline>` (muted — audio plays through the hidden `<audio>` sinks, so no double audio) when a stream resolves, and `Chat.tsx` passes the `peer-<id>` → stream lookup; the mock + 1×1 paths pass no `remoteStreamFor` and keep the avatar/placeholder. **Client-initiated mid-call renegotiation on the web SFU path has since landed** (this row deferred it as "needs perfect-negotiation glare handling"): `sfu-call-session.ts` carries `publishVideo()`, a `negotiationReady` latch, an `onnegotiationneeded` handler and the canonical perfect-negotiation collision check — the file's own header comment still reads as if it were deferred, and CLAUDE.md inherited that. Still deferred: the live cross-platform RTP verification (no deployed SFU here). **Native SFU signaling clients (SFU-3-native-signaling, Jun 2026)** — the Swift + Kotlin mirrors of the web `sfu-signaling-client.ts`: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuSignalingClient.swift` (over `URLSessionWebSocketTask`, subprotocol via `webSocketTask(with:protocols:)`) and `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/calls/SfuSignalingClient.kt` (over Ktor's WebSockets plugin on the OkHttp engine — `libs.ktor.client.websockets` promoted from androidTest to a production dep). Both connect to `{baseUrl}/v1/connect?token=<jwt>`, parse inbound frames through the UniFFI typed `parseSfuServerFrame` → `SfuServerFrame` enum (Swift cases / Kotlin sealed-class) dispatched to callbacks, and send via the `composeSfu*` helpers — transport + framing only. Both platforms compile green (`xcodebuild` + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCEEDED — the iOS green-up also surfaced that the `LakkoCore.xcframework` + `liblakko_sframe_native.a` must be recross-compiled via `just build-ios` + `just cross-build-sframe-native-ios` after any SFU / `seal_framed` change, since the Swift binding + the `SFrameAttach.mm` bridge reference symbols that only exist in a freshly-built static lib). **iOS native `SfuCallSession` (SFU-3-ios-session, Jun 2026)** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuCallSession.swift`, the native analog of the web `SfuCallSession`: `@MainActor` class composing `SfuSignalingClient` + `WebRTCPeerConnection`. `connect()` refuses if the SFrame shim is unavailable (invariant #4), opens signaling, builds the PC, `addLocalAudioTrack` + `armSFrameEncryptors(seed, localLeaf)` (fail-closed), `createOffer` → `sendSdpOffer`; `onSdpAnswer` → `applyRemoteAnswer`; SFU-initiated `onSdpOffer` → serialized-queue `createAnswer` → `sendSdpAnswer`; local ICE trickles up via `sendIceCandidate` (the SFU's own candidates ride in the SDP — no server→client ICE frame). The `WebRTCPeerConnection.Delegate` gained an `onRemoteReceiver(RTCRtpReceiver, streamId)` hook (fired for EVERY receiver, audio + video) so the session arms `armSFrameDecryptor(for:seed:)` on each incoming track (invariant #4). Audio publish on the first pass (video capture into the SFU session + the `CallUiState`/Chat wire-up are follow-ups). The **Android `SfuCallSession`** (SFU-3-android-session, Jun 2026) is the Kotlin twin — `apps/android/.../calls/SfuCallSession.kt`, same publish/subscribe/renegotiation/ICE/SFrame shape over `WebRtcPeerConnection` + `SfuSignalingClient`, taking a `Context` + a main-confined `CoroutineScope` (signaling callbacks run on the scope; the pod delegate fires on libwebrtc threads and hops back); `WebRtcPeerConnection.Delegate` gained the matching `onRemoteReceiver(RtpReceiver, streamId)` hook. Both compile green (`xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL). Remaining for both natives: video capture into the SFU session + the live SFU round-trip. **iOS chat-UI adapter (SFU-3-ios-callui, Jun 2026)** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuCallUi.swift`, the iOS analog of the web `createSfuCallUi`: a `@MainActor` class binding `SfuCallSession`'s callbacks onto a `CallUiState` (state→`CallPhase` + E2EE pill active on connect; welcome/peer-joined/peer-left→roster; remote video track→`setRemoteVideoTrack`, per-participant since the n-way map landed — this read "sole-remote" when the adapter still held one handle). Closures capture the `CallUiState` + name resolver directly (not `self`), avoiding the init-order/retain-cycle trap. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. The Android twin `apps/android/.../calls/SfuCallUi.kt` (SFU-3-android-callui, Jun 2026) is the same binding over `CallUiState` (StateFlow), taking a `Context` + `CoroutineScope` for the underlying `SfuCallSession`; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Both native `SfuCallUi` adapters now match the web `createSfuCallUi`. **iOS group-call trigger core (SFU-3-ios-trigger, Jun 2026)** — the trigger logic landed: `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/SfuConfig.swift` reads the SFU URL from the `LakkoSfuUrl` Info.plist key (analog of web `VITE_LAKKO_SFU_URL`); `ChatRepository.memberCount(groupIdHex:)` wraps `group.memberCount()` for the group-vs-1×1 branch; `ChatViewModel.startGroupCall()` derives the per-call SFrame seed/leaf via the existing `repository.deriveCallKey` (MLS exporter), mints the JWT (`CallJwtClient` bound to a `sessionTokenProvider` closure — `LakkoApp` passes `{ authRepository.identitySecrets?.sessionToken }`), builds `SfuCallUi(config:, ui: sfuCallUiState)`, and connects (fail-closed: a missing SFU URL / token / seed NSLogs + refuses). `activeSfuCall` + `sfuCallUiState` are published. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. **iOS chat-UI presentation (SFU-3-ios-present, Jun 2026)** — the iOS SFU group call is now wired end-to-end: `CallScreen` gained an `init(externalState: CallUiState, onExit:)` (injects the `SfuCallUi`-driven state via `StateObject(wrappedValue:)` + an `externallyDriven` flag that skips the prejoin branch — the SFU call is already connecting when presented), `ChatViewModel.isGroupChat` (via `ChatRepository.memberCount`) branches the `ChatScreen` call button (group → `startGroupCall`, 1×1 → `startCall`), and `ChatScreen` presents `CallScreen(externalState: sfuCallUiState)` in a `fullScreenCover` gated on `activeSfuCall` (dismiss → `hangUpGroupCall`). `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED. **Android group-call trigger + presentation (SFU-3-android-trigger+present, Jun 2026)** — the Kotlin twin of the iOS trigger + present: `app/build.gradle.kts` adds a `LAKKO_SFU_URL` `buildConfigField` (sourced from the gradle property `lakko.sfu.url`, empty when unset); `apps/android/.../calls/SfuConfig.kt` reads `BuildConfig.LAKKO_SFU_URL` (trims trailing slashes, `null` when blank — analog of the iOS `SfuConfig`); `ChatRepository.memberCount(groupIdHex):Int?` wraps `group.memberCount()` for the group-vs-1×1 branch; `ChatViewModel` gains a `sessionTokenProvider: () -> String?` ctor param (wired from `MainActivity` to `authRepository.identitySecrets.value?.sessionToken`), `activeSfuCall: StateFlow<SfuCallUi?>`, a reused `sfuCallUiState: CallUiState`, `isGroupChat`, `startGroupCall()` (derive seed/leaf via `repo.deriveCallKey` MLS exporter → mint JWT via `CallJwtClient` → build `SfuCallUi(applicationContext, config, viewModelScope, ui = sfuCallUiState)` → `connect()`; fail-closed: missing SFU URL / token / seed logs + refuses per invariant #4; pre-sets the UI phase to Connecting so the externally-driven `CallScreen` never flashes the prejoin), and `hangUpGroupCall()`. `ChatScreen` branches the call button (`isGroupChat` → `startGroupCall`, else 1×1 `startCall`), disables it while either call kind is up, and presents the existing `CallScreen(onExit = hangUpGroupCall, state = sfuCallUiState)` (no `session` — the `SfuCallUi` adapter owns the state, so no Compose surgery needed unlike iOS's `externalState` injection) in a full-screen `Dialog` gated on `activeSfuCall`. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. The native SFU group-call client is now wired end-to-end on web + iOS + Android. **Native SFU video capture (SFU-3-native-video, Jun 2026)** — the native `SfuCallSession` (iOS `SfuCallSession.swift` + Android `SfuCallSession.kt`) now publishes the local camera when `config.video` is set: it builds the capture pipeline (`LocalCameraCapture` / `CameraVideoCapture`, front camera 720p/30 — the same pods the 1×1 path uses), adds the video track BEFORE arming SFrame + building the offer (so the offer advertises a video m-line and `armSFrameEncryptors` — which already walks `[audio, video]` senders — seals the video sender alongside audio; the SFU never sees a plaintext frame, invariant #2/#4), surfaces the local track through a new `onLocalVideoTrack` callback that the `SfuCallUi` adapter maps onto `CallUiState.setLocalVideoTrack` (self-preview tile), and tears the camera down in `hangUp` (iOS `stop()`; Android `stop()` + `dispose()`). Soft-fails to audio-only on no-camera / start failure (silent video sender keeps the m-line; receiver sees a black frame). Both adapters also gained a `setCameraEnabled` passthrough (local-only `isEnabled` gate — meaningful only on a `config.video` call, since the track is added at connect). `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. The receive path already renders remote video (sole-remote tile). **Native video-call entry point (SFU-3-native-video-entry, Jun 2026)** — the chat header now offers a second call button (iOS `video.fill` toolbar item; Android `🎥 Video` text button + a new `onStartVideoCall` on the `Header` composable) that starts the call AS a video call: `ChatViewModel.startGroupCall(video:)` threads the flag into `SfuCallSession.Config.video` (group chats publish SFrame-sealed camera through the SFU), and 1×1 chats route to `startCall(video: true)` (the prejoin still lets the user toggle the camera before joining). The audio button stays `video=false`. Both buttons disable while a call is up. `xcodebuild` BUILD SUCCEEDED + `:app:compileDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Remaining for the SFU arc: the live SFU round-trip (no deployed SFU yet) and the cross-platform RTP interop verification (G-26 deferred gate) — web SFU-group client-initiated mid-call renegotiation is no longer on this list, see the perfect-negotiation note above. **iOS silent-push NSE decrypt ([ADR-0041](docs/adr/0041-ios-silent-push-nse-decrypt.md), Phases 0-3 landed end-to-end)** — the `apps/ios/LakkoNotificationService` NSE renders the real decrypted message preview on a push wake via a read-only throwaway MLS decrypt (mirroring the web `148753b` + Android `ae9a087` siblings): `NsePushDecrypt.swift` + `NseDecryptDeps.swift` reconstruct a fresh throwaway `Identity.fromSeed` → `restoreStorage` → `loadGroup` from the App-Group-shared inputs (Phase 1), decrypt over the slim `LakkoCoreExtension.xcframework` (Phase 2, `lakko-ffi-extension`), read-only DS-fetch after the app cursor, `processIncomingVerifyingRoles`/`parseBodyEnvelope` for the newest preview, then DISCARD — never persisting, structurally no-write across three layers (C-59); fail-closed to the generic "New message" banner, and an anti-censorship-armed user short-circuits to generic rather than making a direct un-tunneled DS connection. `lakko-security` APPROVE-WITH-NOTES. **Scope note — this bullet stops at the Jun 2026 call arc and is not the whole of `apps/`.** Everything the client sprints of ADR-0029 (mid-call rekey), ADR-0032 (multi-joiner call links — `multi-joiner-call.ts` / `MultiJoinerCall.swift` / `MultiJoinerElection`), ADR-0035 (per-`(leaf, stream)` KID), ADR-0042/0043/0044 (push routing) and ADR-0045 (proposal-based membership, the beacon, ownership transfer, self-eviction, typed refusals, automatic re-request) built on the three clients is documented in **those ADR rows**, not repeated here — a reader who takes this bullet as an inventory of `apps/` will miss most of the current client code. **The same goes for the group-call media of ADR-0066 to ADR-0071 (Oct 2026), which replaced much of what this bullet says about SFU video:** on all three clients the camera is three ordinary senders (main, light, middle; SFrame stream indices 1, 3, 4) instead of one sender or simulcast, a tile asks for a picture with `video-wants`, reads `video-given`, and a publisher switches its main and middle encodings by `video-needed` (web `apps/web/src/calls/{camera-sender,video-wants,video-given,sender-activity,layer-display}.ts`; iOS `Calls/{CameraSender,VideoWants,VideoGiven,SenderActivity,LayeredRemoteVideoView}.swift`; Android `calls/{CameraSenders,VideoWants,VideoGiven,SenderActivity,RemoteLayeredVideoView}.kt`), with the stage view and its pin button per `docs/ux/group-call-grid.md` 6.2. The web side has been on live calls in Chrome, Firefox, Safari and Edge; the iOS and Android side (2026-10-11) has unit tests and has not been run on a device, an emulator or a call
- `infra/` — single-VPS Hetzner deployment via systemd + system-nginx + system-MariaDB; see `infra/README.md` (migration from lakko_me and daily ops). **SFU deploy (ADR-0011 Rust SFU, co-located on the main VPS)**: `infra/systemd/lakko-sfu.service` runs `lakko-svc-sfu` (env-var configured from `lakko.env`, in `lakko.target`); `infra/nginx/sites-available/sfu.conf` fronts `sfu.lakko.me` (`/v1/connect` WS + `/health` `/ready` `/version` + mTLS `/metrics` → `127.0.0.1:9641`); the UDP media plane (`LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_BIND`, default `0.0.0.0:43210`) is a direct public port (not proxied), and **`LAKKO_SFU_MEDIA_ADVERTISE_IP` must name the public IP**: an unspecified bind cannot be an ICE host candidate, and until 2026-09 the SFU silently sent every SDP answer with no candidate at all, so no deployed media could connect — it now refuses to start instead. `just deploy` builds + installs the binary (deploy.sh `SERVICES` now includes `sfu` + `channels`); `just deploy-sfu` is the one-time setup (cert + vhost + UDP firewall); `just dev-sfu` / `restart-sfu` / `logs-sfu`. The stale LiveKit-fork infra (ADR-0008 — `livekit.yaml`, go build, separate CCX22) was retired. See `infra/README-calls.md`
- `docs/adr/` — Architecture Decision Records
- `docs/ROADMAP.md` — tactical decomposition of every phase
- `docs/security/` — audit-readiness pack: threat model, crypto overview, MLS state machine invariants (I-1..I-8), call-side cryptography (**C-1..C-89**, C-81 unused; C-86 the ADR-0081 Welcome admission, C-70 the [ADR-0062](docs/adr/0062-conversation-kind.md) conversation kind, C-71 the [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) attested-or-proven key change at the directory — this bullet said C-1..C-55 for a long time and then named C-56/C-57 individually, leaving C-58..C-69 invisible, C-59 among them, which the `apps/` bullet cites by name for the NSE — media-key pipeline + call-admission JWT + link-based invite + owner fingerprint + server-handler drift sentinels + URL discrimination + composer/acceptor pipelines + Welcome-ferry envelope + KP-forward envelope + incoming-envelope dispatcher + 1×1 and multi-party E2E chain + owner-handover protocol + SFrame keying math + RFC 9605 wire-header round-trip + SFU signaling-layer zero-payload-inspection / cross-call isolation / backpressure semantics + channels-backend publisher-path / subscriber-handshake opacity + atomic rotation + FFI subscriber-side surface opacity + MLS-derived channel seed FFI surface + SFrame native-shim stack invariants C-45..C-50: lakko-sframe-native C ABI single-path + bad-seed-refused-at-five-layers + boot-time selector-probe enforcement of invariant #4 + attach-between-addTrack-and-SDP-build sequencing + per-sender ParticipantKey cache thread-safety + refcount machinery balanced AddRef↔Release across libwebrtc handoff + TURN credential coturn-REST-shape + per-call expiry C-51 + PublisherGroup persistence + rotation invariant C-52 + SFrame native-shim host-side validation C-53: fifteen standalone C++17 tests in
