`apps/shared/sframe-bridge/test_bridge.cc`, all on the ADR-0071 frame layout since 2026-10-11 (it was ten on the header-first layout): round-trip within the reported maximum size + host `additional_data` not authenticated while the SFrame header is + multi-sender cache + bad-seed + ciphertext-body tamper + counter monotonicity with out-of-order + per-call key isolation + payload-size extremes + the ADR-0071 golden frame opens (the `LakkoFrameDecryptor` the iOS/Android shims wrap opens the vector the core, the wasm FFI, the C ABI test and the web suite pin; since ADR-0069 an encryptor starts at a random counter, so its seal is proved by opening what it sealed) + the codec decides the clear prefix + every single-byte flip of a sealed frame is refused + the header-first layout is refused + `SetCodec` racing `Encrypt` + the ADR-0029 rekey overlap + an empty frame is not a decrypt failure; the live cross-stack exchange is still G-26, wired via `just test-sframe-bridge` and `.github/workflows/sframe-bridge.yml` + channel rotation policy C-54: pure `RotationPolicy::should_rotate(self, now_ms, state)` in `crates/lakko-channels/src/rotation_policy.rs` (a method on the policy, not a three-argument free function) (24 h / 1 000 posts default, both independently disable-able, saturating arithmetic on the time delta tolerates host-clock jumps backward) + web / iOS / Android orchestration driving the fanout — since [ADR-0030](docs/adr/0030-batched-rotation-fanout.md) that is the two-phase `POST /rotate/stage` (bounded chunks) then one atomic `POST /rotate/commit`, not the single-shot `POST /v1/channels/:id/rotate` this bullet named, which still exists server-side but is no longer what the cron calls — `apps/web/src/channels/rotation-cron.ts` + `apps/ios/Lakko/Channels/RotationCron.swift` (Swift `actor`) + `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/channels/RotationCron.kt` (Kotlin coroutine driver), all reading the same UniFFI / wasm-bindgen policy decision so the cadence cannot drift between platforms) + SFU multi-party client SFrame coverage + cross-platform AAD C-55 (ADR-0013, layout amended by ADR-0071): all three SFU clients — the web worker (`RTCRtpScriptTransform` or encoded streams), native iOS/Android `SfuCallSession` — seal media end-to-end through the one `lakko_calls` core, since ADR-0071 `media_frame` (`seal_media_frame` / `open_media_frame`) in place of `seal_framed` / `open_framed`; AEAD AAD = the RFC 9605 §4.3 header (KID+CTR) plus the bounded clear codec prefix and the 3-byte trailer, all taken from the frame's own bytes, never host RTP `additional_data`, which is what lets a frame cross stacks; native clients arm encryptors on every local sender (audio + video) before the offer + decryptors on every remote receiver, fail-closed (invariant #4); only the live cross-platform RTP verification over a deployed SFU remains (G-26), KT-log client-authorised leaf C-56 (genesis self-sig + rotation chain, ADR-0019) + witness co-signing C-57 (Merkle consistency + fail-closed root gate, ADR-0020) closing G-30 #2 + #1, transport layer (**T-1..T-15** — the `lakko-transport` row already says T-1..T-15 and these two disagreed; T-13 REALITY authenticator, T-14 WebTransport cert-hash pinning, T-15 MASQUE/CONNECT-UDP — pluggable wire identifiers + scaffold loud-fail + selector race + 4-stage rendezvous verify + canonical signing bytes + kebab-case kind tag + binary-pinned trust anchor + DoH-bootstrap chain integrity + chunked AEAD stream primitive + SS-2022 outer header layer + SS-2022 TCP wiring + ECH config list parser), known gaps (**G-1..G-46** — G-45 / G-46 minted 2026-10-03 by ADR-0073 (the phase-2 old-identity leaves; the stolen-session reset); G-28 closed via the G3-sframe-native arc N-1..N-6; the old "G-1..G-27" hid G-31 (cross-stream SFrame nonce reuse), G-33/G-34 (the nonce-reuse sweep), G-35, G-36, G-37/G-38 and **G-39, the open external-crypto-review RELEASE GATE of [ADR-0046](docs/adr/0046-external-crypto-review.md)** — including the cargo-audit baseline for transitive deps + RFC 9605 wire-header interop gap **now partially closed**). Entry: [`docs/security/README.md`](docs/security/README.md); consolidated invariant + gap cross-reference: [`docs/security/invariant-index.md`](docs/security/invariant-index.md). **Scoping brief for the G-39 external review: [`docs/security/external-review-scope.md`](docs/security/external-review-scope.md)** — boundaries, measured sizes, reading order, what is already verified, the ranked questions, and what a reviewer must not assume (chiefly: nothing is deployed, `transparency` / `witness` / `obfs-proxy` have no systemd unit at all, and **ten of eleven CI workflows are `workflow_dispatch`-only including `ci.yml` itself**, so a green history is not evidence that a merged commit was checked)
- `tools/` — developer tooling: `audit-index-check/` (the invariant-index gate), `local-fuzz-run/`, `spec-drift-check.sh` (the `.sc.md` gate), `build-sframe-jni.sh`, `audio/`, `release-manifest/`. The old lead-in read "codegen, fuzz harnesses, benchmarks" — codegen lives in the justfile (`regen-proto` / `regen-bindings`), the fuzz harnesses in each crate's own `fuzz/` directory, and there are no benchmarks here. `tools/bootstrap-config/` (`lakko-bootstrap-config`, `just gen-bootstrap-key`, `just sign-bootstrap-config`) is the offline tool that signs the ADR-0026 bootstrap config. `tools/release-manifest/` is the offline publisher CLI (`lakko-release-manifest` bin, workspace-member crate `lakko-release-manifest-tool`, [ADR-0038](docs/adr/0038-update-available-notice.md)): `keygen` mints the dedicated Ed25519 release-signing keypair (a `0600` seed + the base64url pubkey for the client pins) and `mint` signs the raw 89-byte `ReleaseManifest` blob served as the "update available" notice (justfile `gen-release-key` / `mint-release-manifest`). `tools/donations/` (`lakko-donations` bin, crate `lakko-donations-tool`, `just gen-donations-key` / `just sign-donations <file>`) is the offline tool for the signed donation methods (`docs/ux/donations.md` section 3): `keygen --out <seed>` writes a 0600 seed and prints the base64url public key; `sign --key <seed> --in <methods.json> --out <signed.json>` reads the plain methods file (`id`, `kind`, `link`, `qr_file` relative to the file, `phone`, `coin`, `network`, `address`, `icon` btc / eth / ton on a crypto method, `countries`), signs one statement per method and phone, embeds each QR (PNG <= 512 KiB, file <= 1 MiB), verifies the result against the key's public half and writes it 0600
- `ci/` — GitHub Actions + self-hosted SLSA L3 builders. `ci/sfu-e2e/` (G3-4) brings up a Dockerised `lakko-svc-sfu` against a fixture-JWKS nginx sidecar; the matching `#[ignore]`-gated tokio tests live at [`services/sfu/tests/e2e_dockerized.rs`](services/sfu/tests/e2e_dockerized.rs) and run from [`.github/workflows/sfu-e2e.yml`](.github/workflows/sfu-e2e.yml) on **`workflow_dispatch` ONLY** — the `sfu-e2e` PR label and the nightly cron this bullet advertised were disabled in May 2026 for quota conservation and the workflow says so in place, so nothing runs this suite unless someone dispatches it by hand. The mobile half (G3-4-mobile, Variant A — manual-trigger only) ships scaffolding: iOS XCTest target `LakkoTests` (`apps/ios/LakkoTests/SfuHealthTest.swift` against `$LAKKO_SFU_E2E_URL/health`) + Android instrumented test `apps/android/app/src/androidTest/kotlin/me/lakko/app/sfu/SfuHealthInstrumentedTest.kt` running under `AndroidJUnitRunner`. Single-`macos-14` workflow at [`.github/workflows/g3-4-mobile.yml`](.github/workflows/g3-4-mobile.yml) (`workflow_dispatch` only) brings up the same compose stack, boots iPhone 15 sim + Pixel 6 API-34 emulator via `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner`, runs both test suites against `/health` AND (G3-4-mobile-call iter1, May 2026) against the full WebSocket-signaling round-trip — `mint_test_jwt` pre-step mints a JWT signed by the fixture seed, both `SfuSignalingTest.swift` + `SfuSignalingInstrumentedTest.kt` open `/v1/connect?token=<jwt>` with the `lakko-sfu-v1` subprotocol and assert the typed `Welcome` frame. iOS env-var injection rides scheme `environmentVariables` block (`$(LAKKO_SFU_E2E_URL)` / `$(LAKKO_SFU_E2E_JWT)` build-setting interpolation), Android via `instrumentation-arguments`. Dual-instance live-RTP between two synthetic peers (real audio frames flowing through SFU media task) is the deferred G3-4-mobile-call-iter2 sub-sprint. `ci/censorship-lab/` (G5-7) ships a `sni-observer/` Rust binary that parses inbound TLS ClientHellos and writes observed SNIs + JA3/JA4 fingerprints to a JSONL log; G5-7-fingerprint sub-sprint (May 2026) extended the parser with JA3 (Salesforce 2017 — MD5 over `version,ciphers,extensions,groups,points` raw string, GREASE-filtered per RFC 8701) + JA4 (FoxIO 2023 compact form — `t<ver><sni><nc><ne><alpn>_<hash_a>_<hash_b>`, two 12-char hex SHA-256 truncations of sorted ciphers and sorted extensions+sigalgs). Matching `#[ignore]` tests at [`crates/lakko-transport/tests/sni_observer_e2e.rs`](crates/lakko-transport/tests/sni_observer_e2e.rs) (SNI round-trip + JA3+JA4 shape assertions + G5-7-transport-matrix iter1+iter2+iter3 covers all 5 production Lakko transports: `observer_records_shadowsocks_as_non_tls` (TCP, SS-2022 indistinguishable from random — T-10 + T-11), `observer_records_reality_cover_sni` (TCP, cover SNI verbatim + real inner NEVER on the wire), `observer_records_masque_outer_proxy_sni` (UDP, QUIC Initial decrypted via RFC 9001 §5.2 — initial salt + HKDF + AES-128-GCM AEAD + header protection removal — inner_target NEVER on the wire), `observer_records_webtransport_outer_sni` (UDP, same shape, URL path NEVER on the wire). The observer's TCP listener + UDP/QUIC listener emit JSONL with a `transport: "tcp"|"udp"` discriminator); CI workflow [`.github/workflows/censorship-lab.yml`](.github/workflows/censorship-lab.yml) on **`workflow_dispatch` ONLY** — same May 2026 quota-conservation disablement as `sfu-e2e`, the `censorship-lab` label and cron arms are commented out. **GFW string-match censorship (G5-7-gfw-string)** landed — `sni-observer/src/detector.rs` turns the observer into a string-matching censor: `LAKKO_SNI_BLOCKLIST` (CSV: `domain` exact / `*.domain` suffix / `~keyword` substring, case-insensitive) classifies each observed SNI, adding `blocked` + `blocked_by` to every JSONL line; a HIDDEN SNI (ECH / REALITY cover / non-TLS → `observed_sni:null`) is always Allowed (the evasion). 8 detector unit tests + the live e2e `observer_blocks_a_listed_sni_and_allows_others` (blocked SNI → `blocked:true` + matched rule; unlisted → `blocked:false`) + the Shadowsocks evasion assertion (`blocked:false` on the null-SNI flow), all green firsthand against the dockerised observer. **REALITY active-probe refusal (G5-7-reality-probe)** landed — `reality_v2_listener_refuses_a_vanilla_tls_prober` (a plain non-REALITY TLS prober, the commonest censor probe, is refused by a connection drop; full probe-INDISTINGUISHABILITY from the cover site needs the deferred live-cover-proxy). **IR-style throttle MODEL (G5-7-throttle)** landed — `sni-observer/src/throttle.rs` token-bucket (deterministic, caller-supplied clock) quantifying how a bandwidth cap degrades a flow + a boot diagnostic (`LAKKO_SNI_THROTTLE_BYTES_PER_SEC`); 7 unit tests. The observer is drop-only, so the throttle is MODELLED, not applied — wiring it onto a live forwarded flow needs a proxy layer (the only remaining censor-capability residual). The lab's capability matrix is otherwise complete

## Security invariants — must not be violated

1. **No plaintext PII on the server.** Phone / email are **never** stored — not even encrypted. Only OPAQUE-blinded records and a hash for recovery.
2. **The server only sees ciphertext + public artefacts** (KeyPackages, transparency-log entries). No message keys, chain keys, root keys, media keys. **Exception ([ADR-0075](docs/adr/0075-recovery-email.md), accepted 2026-10-12):** for an account whose owner has not saved a recovery phrase, the server holds the phrase entropy sealed under a key derived from the username and the recovery email address; the operator, who sees that address when sending mail, can open it. Saving a phrase ends the exception for that account.
3. **The MLS state machine is sacrosanct.** Any change to `lakko-mls` requires an ADR + property tests + (after Phase 2) an external crypto audit.
4. **No fallback to non-E2EE.** Strict E2EE only — if the handshake does not complete, the connection does not open. No silent downgrade.
5. **The social graph is not materialised on the server.** Add-contact = encrypted envelope to a pseudonym; presence — blinded per-conversation tokens; discovery — OPRF blinded lookup.
6. **Identity rotation** — only via a KT log supersession proof. Old keys do not come back.
7. **Hardware-backed device keys** are mandatory on every platform (Secure Enclave / StrongBox / TPM 2.0 / non-extractable WebCrypto). **Exception ([ADR-0085](docs/adr/0085-desktop-client.md), decided 2026-10-16):** on a desktop computer without hardware key storage (every Linux in v1, Windows without a usable TPM) the device key is protected by the operating system's software key store; a machine with no key store at all is refused.

## Code-style rules

- **Language: English only** in code, comments, doc strings, commit messages, and documentation (`docs/`, `README.md`, ADRs, migration headers). Russian is allowed only in interactive chat between the maintainer and the AI agent. AI agents must produce English when committing or editing files.
<!-- REUSE-IgnoreStart -->
- **Every file** carries the `SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-or-later` header. Rust: `// SPDX-…`; Markdown: `<!-- SPDX-… -->`. No exceptions. CI blocks otherwise.
<!-- REUSE-IgnoreEnd -->
- **`#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`** on every crate, except `lakko-ffi-uniffi`, `lakko-ffi-wasm`, `lakko-noise-wasm` and `lakko-segment-wasm` (which carry justified unsafe — document it with a `// SAFETY:` comment). `lakko-noise-wasm` is `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` with `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` only on its `#[no_mangle]` exports (maintainer, 2026-11-07, ADR-0088); `lakko-segment-wasm` the same, plus the `getrandom` custom-backend symbol (ADR-0089).
- **No `unwrap()` / `expect()`** in production code — only in tests and benchmarks. Use `?` and `thiserror` for errors.
- **No `panic!()`** in library code (allowed in `bin` for unrecoverable startup errors).
- **No `println!` / `eprintln!`** — log via the `tracing` crate.
- **`clippy::all`, `clippy::pedantic`, `clippy::nursery`** — all warnings on. `-D warnings` in CI.
- **`cargo fmt`** is mandatory — pre-commit hook + CI gate.
- **Documentation** on public items — `missing_docs` warning on.
- **Do not add dependencies** without need. Every dep is a potential supply-chain vulnerability. `cargo deny` blocks unknown licences.

## Commands (via the justfile)

```
just check                     # cargo check --workspace --all-targets
just test                      # cargo test --workspace
just lint                      # cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings
just fmt                       # cargo fmt --all
just fmt-check                 # cargo fmt --all --check
just audit                     # cargo deny check
just reuse                     # reuse lint
just regen-proto               # regenerate types from schemas/
just regen-bindings            # Swift + Kotlin bindings from lakko-ffi-uniffi
just fuzz-check                # smoke-build all fuzz targets on stable
just fuzz <fuzz_dir> <target>  # run a specific target on nightly + cargo-fuzz
just fuzz-quick                # run EVERY target for 60s each (local mirror of nightly CI workflow)
just fuzz-list                 # list all available fuzz targets
just deploy                    # build + install + systemctl restart on the VPS (ssh lakkome@lakko.me)
just restart                   # systemctl restart lakko.target
just restart-svc <svc>         # systemctl restart lakko-svc-<svc>
just logs <svc>                # journalctl -u lakko-svc-<svc> -f
just status                    # systemctl status lakko.target
just deploy-coturn             # bootstrap / refresh coturn VPS (idempotent; LAKKO_TURN_HOST, default turn.lakko.me)
just reload-coturn             # SIGUSR2 reload (TLS cert + config refresh, no relay session drop)
just restart-coturn            # full restart (drops live sessions; use only for listening-port changes)
just logs-coturn               # journalctl -u coturn -f on the coturn VPS
just turn-smoke [host]         # local smoke probe (DNS / STUN / TURN-over-TLS / mTLS gate)
just ci                        # everything CI does, locally
```

## Fuzz harnesses

`crates/lakko-mls/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-proto/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-contacts/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-channels/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-calls/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-transport/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-tuf/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-call-jwt/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-backup/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-transparency/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-attest/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-sync/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-push/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-attachments/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-auth-token/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-mnemonic/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-ds-proof/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-anon-token/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-device-link/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-l10n/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-feed/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-vault/fuzz/`, `crates/lakko-circle/fuzz/`, and `services/sfu/fuzz/` are separate crates outside the main workspace (they need nightly + libfuzzer-sys). Targets live in `fuzz/fuzz_targets/*.rs`.

Current targets:
- **`lakko-mls/mls_message`** — `MlsMessageIn` wire-format parser (TLS codec)
- **`lakko-mls/key_package`** — `KeyPackage` wire-format parser
- **`lakko-mls/history_placeholder`** — `HistoryMessagePlaceholder::decode` bounded parser for the cross-platform history-bundle `payload_bytes` placeholder (G-6); attacker-touchable after AEAD-open. Also asserts the encode/decode round-trip is stable on any input that parses
- **`lakko-mls/devicegroup_payload`** — `DeviceGroup::parse_devicegroup_payload` (the receiver-side DeviceGroup journal parser, I-8): prost-decode + body-variant extraction on bytes that arrive as an `Incoming::Application` payload from the ZERO-TRUST, opaque Delivery Service, so a malicious / corrupt journal entry reaches this path before any group state is consulted. A body-less or unrecognised-variant payload must surface as a graceful `Err`, never a panic
- **`lakko-mls/pairing_qr`** — `pairing::decode` (the device-pairing payload parser, v2 since [ADR-0056](docs/adr/0056-pairing-names-account-by-handle.md)): prost-decode + structural binding checks (version == 2 / 32-byte login handle + constant-time match / rendezvous-pseudonym length). The MOST attacker-controllable input in the system — a victim physically scans a QR a malicious actor crafted, or the auth relay serves a posted payload — so the parser must stay panic-free before any device is enrolled. Binds against the fixed `fuzz_api::PAIRING_QR_EXPECTED_HANDLE` and **asserts** `fuzz_api::pairing_qr_acceptance_is_bound`: anything the decoder accepts carries version 2 and exactly that handle
- **`lakko-mls/history_bundle_open`** — `history_bundle::open_bundle` (the receiver-side history-bundle open path, I-6/I-7): the OUTER `SealedBundle` prost-decode + the pinned-`algorithm` discriminator gate (I-7) + the nonce-length check + the AEAD-open (I-6: a forged ciphertext is an auth failure, not a parse failure) + the inner `HistoryBundle` decode, on attacker-controlled `sealed_bytes` (unauthenticated at decode time). Fixed all-zero secret so a valid AEAD tag cannot be forged — the harness exercises the decode + gates + the auth-failure path
- **`lakko-mls/role_table_decode`** — `RoleTable::decode` (the ADR-0022 `0xFF02` GroupContext role-table body parser): the bounded, fail-closed codec (version gate + `admin_count` cap + exact-length check) on attacker-controlled bytes that travel inside a Welcome / `GroupContextExtensions` commit a malicious group founder / member can craft, reaching the parser before any role lookup. Any panic / overflow / runaway allocation is a vulnerability
- **`lakko-mls/welcome_rendezvous_decode`** — `WelcomeRendezvous::decode` (ADR-0027 Phase 3, G-3): the bounded, fail-closed envelope parser (`version ‖ sig_key_len ‖ sig_key ‖ welcome`) on attacker-touchable bytes — a prospective external joiner decodes group-journal entries that a malicious poster / the zero-trust DS could have crafted, before any inner Welcome is opened. Version gate + sig-key/welcome length bounds + slice arithmetic must stay panic-free
- **`lakko-mls/group_kind_decode`** — `fuzz_api::decode_group_kind` ([ADR-0062](docs/adr/0062-conversation-kind.md), C-70): the `0xFF03` conversation-kind body codec. It travels inside a Welcome or a `GroupContextExtensions` commit any member (or a malicious founder) can craft, and `join_from_welcome` reads it before the group is built; exact length, then version, then kind, every other input an error. Asserts no panic and that anything decoded re-encodes to the identical two bytes
- **`lakko-mls/pending_external_joins_decode`** — `group::decode_pending_external_joins` (ADR-0027, the persisted retained-request row `Group::load` reads back from the MLS storage snapshot): the bounded, fail-closed decoder (version gate + count cap + per-KeyPackage length bound + re-validation against the ciphersuite + repeated-joiner and trailing-bytes refusal). Local bookkeeping wrapped under the device key, but the snapshot also travels inside the phrase-sealed backup a hostile storage tier serves, and it is the first Lakko-owned parser `Group::load` runs — so it must stay panic-free on arbitrary input
- **`lakko-mls/self_removal_proof_decode`** — `SelfRemovalProof::decode` + `covers` ([ADR-0081](docs/adr/0081-devicegroup-commits-cannot-strand.md) amendment, C-85): the DeviceGroup self-removal proof arrives in the removal-authorisation slot of a journal envelope any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, and in a rejoin knock any stranger can post, and the gate decodes every slot entry before any state is consulted. Fixed 137-byte layout, version gate; asserts no panic and that whatever decodes re-encodes to the identical bytes, then drives `covers` (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-mls/wrapped_entry_open`** — `entry_wrap::unwrap_entry_commit` and the clear epoch reader (G-55): a wrapped owner-device channel entry as a member reads it from a PublisherGroup journal; the magic / version / epoch header, the AEAD refusal under a fixed key, `epoch_of_message` / `epoch_after_message` on the same bytes, and a wrap-open round trip (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-mls/rejoin_knock_open`** — `pairing::open_rejoin_knock` (G-55): a sealed DeviceGroup rejoin knock as a sibling reads it from the knock queue (anyone with a token writes there); the header checks, the AEAD refusal under a fixed lineage seed, the padding parser fed directly, and a seal-open round trip (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-proto/wire_envelope`** — `wire::v1::Envelope` prost decoder
- **`lakko-proto/mls_app_payload`** — `mls_app::v1::ApplicationPayload` prost decoder
- **`lakko-proto/media_call_key`** — `media::v1::CallKeyEpoch` + `CodecCapability` decoders
- **`lakko-contacts/contact_request_decode_and_verify`** — outermost stranger-inbox parser (signature + HPKE-sealed envelope)
- **`lakko-contacts/contact_ack_decode_and_verify`** — ContactAck parser (Bob → Alice reply after accept)
- **`lakko-contacts/invite_capability_decode_and_verify`** — contact-invite (Phase 1.18) capability parser (the plaintext a sealed `/i/` link opens to since 2026-09-28; the server no longer parses it)
- **`lakko-contacts/call_invite_capability_decode_and_verify`** — call-invite (Phase 3c3) capability parser (the plaintext a sealed `/c/` link opens to since 2026-09-28; the server no longer parses it)
- **`lakko-contacts/group_invite_decode`** — `group_invite::decode_and_verify` on the PLAINTEXT capability (ADR-0028, G-3): since the 2026-09-28 sealed-link amendment a joiner reaches it on the bytes a sealed `/g/` link opens to, and an admin on the capability a joiner's join attribution carries from the DS knock journal; either way a stranger chooses the bytes, so the bounded, fail-closed parser (version gate + length-prefixed group_id bound + Ed25519 verify) must stay panic-free; it carries a length-prefixed sealed preview (bounded by `MAX_SEALED_PREVIEW_LEN` before any allocation), and on a capability that verifies the target drives `open_preview` under a fixed key, so the AEAD refusal and the preview plaintext decoder are exercised on attacker-chosen bytes too
- **`lakko-contacts/invite_link_open`** — `link_seal::open` + the kind's `decode_and_verify` via `fuzz_api::open_sealed_link` (the 2026-09-28 sealed-link amendment of ADR-0028 / ADR-0009): the sealed blob a ledger serves under an id (the only link form since 2026-10-19) is chosen byte for byte by a malicious ledger and reaches the kind/version gate, the length split, HKDF and the AEAD before anything else. First byte picks the kind, the rest is opened under a FIXED secret; asserts no panic and that anything accepted re-seals and re-opens to the same plaintext
- **`lakko-contacts/group_invite_preview_decode`** — the group-invite preview plaintext decoder (ADR-0028 v2), fed directly through `lakko_contacts::fuzz_api::group_invite_preview_plaintext`: the version gate, the two one-byte length prefixes, UTF-8 validation, the display-name character table, the member count and the trailing-bytes refusal. The plaintext is authored by a link's issuer and opened by anyone holding the link secret; asserts no panic and that anything the decoder accepts also validates and re-encodes to the identical bytes. Added by the 2026-09-28 security pass because `group_invite_decode` cannot reach this decoder
- **`lakko-contacts/group_join_attribution_decode`** — `group_join_attribution::decode_attribution` (ADR-0028 OQ2, G-3): the join-attribution envelope a joiner posts to the group journal alongside its external-join proposal. An admin's poll loop decodes journal entries a malicious poster / the zero-trust DS could have crafted — the bounded, fail-closed parser (version gate + fixed 32-byte joiner-key + capability bounds + **joiner Ed25519 self-signature verify** + embedded `GroupInviteCapability` Ed25519 verify) must stay panic-free before any field is surfaced to the admin
- **`lakko-contacts/call_welcome_unwrap`** — owner-side welcome-envelope domain-separator parser
- **`lakko-contacts/call_kp_forward_unwrap`** — owner-side KP-forward envelope parser (subsequent joiners)
- **`lakko-contacts/incoming_envelope_dispatch`** — composite owner-side polling-loop dispatcher
- **`lakko-contacts/media_state_decode`** — `media_state::decode_media_state` (call camera on/off signal): the fixed 28-byte `domain(26) ‖ version(1) ‖ flag(1)` envelope that rides the opaque SFU `signal-relay` payload between call peers. A malicious / compromised peer (or the zero-trust SFU relaying arbitrary bytes) can craft the payload, so the bounded, fail-closed decoder (exact-length + domain match + version gate + flag validation) must stay panic-free before the flag flips a roster `camOn` gate; since 2026-11-07 (flag day) only version `0x02` is accepted, its state byte a flag set (bit 0 camera, bit 1 microphone, other bits refused), and the target asserts that only version `0x02` decodes and whatever decodes re-composes to its bytes
- **`lakko-contacts/push_routing_secret_decode`** — `push_routing::decode_push_routing_secret` ([ADR-0042](docs/adr/0042-push-opaque-group-routing-id.md), invariant #5) **+ the `push_routing_secret_is_newer` total order ([ADR-0044](docs/adr/0044-push-routing-secret-recency.md))**: the in-group push-routing-secret distribution envelope. The 32-byte `push_routing_secret` rides an MLS application message on the group journal, so the bytes reach this bounded, fail-closed decoder after arriving from the ZERO-TRUST, opaque Delivery Service — a malicious member or a corrupt journal entry could have crafted them, and the receive-loop dispatch reaches this parser **before any state is consulted** (the same threat class as `media_state_decode` / `welcome_rendezvous_decode`). Any arbitrary input is valid: a plaintext that is not a push-routing-secret envelope must surface as a graceful `Err` (wrong domain / bad length / unknown version), never a panic. Covers the 29-byte domain-prefix match, the **exact**-length gate on the **v2** `version(1) ‖ mint_epoch(8, BE) ‖ secret(32)` payload, and the version discriminator — which the v2 bump moved **ahead** of the length gate, so the parser reads `payload[0]` before it knows the payload length and an empty payload must be caught by the split rather than by an index, both reachable from raw bytes here. The target also drives the **ordering decision** a decoded envelope feeds, since a decoded envelope is never used for anything else: the attacker-supplied `(mint_epoch, secret)` is compared against a fixed stored pair in **both directions** (the order is total and infallible by contract — no input may panic it), and `supersedes` against the value itself is asserted `false` on every corpus entry that parses — the F-A **"a retry is not an update"** property, pinned on attacker-chosen bytes
- **`lakko-contacts/profile_decode`** — `profile::decode_profile` ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md)) (v4 since [ADR-0084](docs/adr/0084-profile-photo.md): the avatar length byte and the square / size / duration bounds of its references are asserted on anything decoded): the variable-length profile envelope, reached from a group journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft and from a contact request's sealed plaintext any stranger can craft, before any state is consulted. Covers the domain prefix, the version gate, the two one-byte length prefixes, UTF-8 validation, the `Cf` table and the canonical-username rule; asserts no panic, that anything decoded lies within the min/max length bounds, and **round-trip stability** — whatever decodes re-composes to the identical bytes (the encoding is canonical) and decodes to the identical profile — plus that a profile is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/committer_beacon_decode`** — `committer_beacon::decode_committer_beacon` ([ADR-0045](docs/adr/0045-remote-commit-epoch-fork.md) decision 2, variant 1) **+ the `acting_committer_verdict` fold**: the committer liveness beacon rides an MLS application message on the group journal, so the bytes reach this bounded, fail-closed decoder after arriving from the ZERO-TRUST, opaque Delivery Service — a malicious member or a corrupt journal entry could have crafted them, and the receive-loop dispatch reaches this parser **before any state is consulted** (the same threat class as `media_state_decode` / `push_routing_secret_decode`). The parser surface is deliberately tiny (the payload is one version byte, because the beacon carries no fields at all); what is **not** small is the consequence of getting either gate wrong — the exact-length gate is what stops a v2 field being read by a v1 decoder, the domain gate is what stops a beacon rendering as a chat bubble, and both are reachable from raw bytes here including the degenerate bare-separator case (domain matched, zero-byte payload) that must be an `Err` rather than an `Ok(None)` fall-through. The target also drives the **fold**, since a decoded beacon is never used for anything else: the same fuzzer bytes become an adversarial observation set (ranks, ages and the freshness window all attacker-chosen, ages spread across the whole `u64` range) and two invariants are **asserted** rather than merely exercised — a `None` own-rank is always `NotACandidate` (a plain member can never be talked into sweeping) and a `Defer` never names a rank at or above this device's own (the strict-inequality rule that keeps a device's own echoed-back beacon from deadlocking it)
- **`lakko-contacts/profile_request_decode`** — `profile_request::decode_profile_request` ([ADR-0048](docs/adr/0048-profile-names.md) §2 trigger 4): the newcomer's "introduce yourselves" envelope rides an MLS application message on the group journal, reached from a row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft before any state is consulted. The parser is domain, exact length, version byte; asserts a foreign domain is never a request and that the only bytes that decode as one are the composed wire
- **`lakko-contacts/pin_decode`** — `pin::decode_pin` ([ADR-0052](docs/adr/0052-reply-forward-pin.md) decision 3): the shared-pin envelope (pin / unpin ONE message for everyone), reached from a conversation-journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, before any state or role is consulted. Fixed length, so the parser is domain, version (checked before length), exact length, action byte; asserts no panic, the fixed length, round-trip stability and that a stamp is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/group_closed_decode`** — `group_closed::decode_group_closed` ([ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md) phase 2, `docs/ux/identity-recovery.md` §16.5): the group-closed envelope (an admin closed the group and names the recreated one), reached from a group-journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, before any state or role is consulted. The parser is domain, version (checked before the remaining length), the 8-byte stamp, a one-byte successor length that admits exactly 0 or 32, and a trailing-bytes refusal; asserts no panic, that a decoded envelope is exactly one of the two advertised lengths with the successor present iff the longer, round-trip stability (compose is infallible and canonical), and that a stamp is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/registrar_seed_decode`** — `registrar_seed::decode_registrar_seed` ([ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)): a journal root's registrar-seed envelope rides a group-journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, reached before any state is consulted. Fixed length; asserts the dispatcher classifies the same bytes the same way and that opening a hostile seal under a fixed key never panics
- **`lakko-contacts/group_name_decode`** — `group_name::decode_group_name` ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md)): the group-name envelope, reached from a group journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, before any state or role is consulted. Covers the domain prefix, the version gate, the one-byte length prefix, UTF-8 validation and the display-name character table; asserts no panic, that anything decoded lies within the min/max length bounds, round-trip stability and that a name is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/introduction_open`** — `IntroductionRecord::decode` + `introduction_tags` + `open_introduction` under a fixed OPRF output, and `IntroductionPayload::decode` fed directly ([ADR-0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) §4): a contacts-of-contacts record (up to 65 search-term entries, each a tag and a wrapped content key, plus one sealed payload) any contact can craft, parsed by a searcher before any key is derived; asserts no panic and round-trip stability of the record and payload encodings
- **`lakko-contacts/introductions_bundle_decode`** — `decode_introductions` + `decode_introduction_offer` (ADR-0060 §4): the padded bundle a contact relays and the offer an owner sends; the size-bucket count check runs before allocation; asserts no panic, a bucket count, and round-trip stability
- **`lakko-contacts/visibility_decode`** — `visibility::decode_visibility` (ADR-0060 §7): the "who can find me" setting envelope carried over the owner's DeviceGroup; asserts no panic, the canonical-form rule on anything decoded, round-trip stability and that a stamp is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/reach_state_decode`** — `visibility::decode_reach_state` (ADR-0060 §7): the reach-key version counters; asserts no panic, the entry cap and round-trip stability
- **`lakko-contacts/reach_grant_decode`** — `reach::ReachGrant::decode` (ADR-0060 §1): the fixed 37-byte grant carried in a profile v3 envelope and an introduction record; asserts no panic, round-trip stability, and that deriving the public key of any decoded seed is total
- **`lakko-contacts/mailbox_row_open`** — `mailbox::open_mailbox_row` under a fixed mailbox key ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) phase 1): every row in a person's mailbox is written by a stranger or the zero-trust DS, so the version byte, the bucket length check, the AEAD refusal and the padded-plaintext parser (kind, body length, zero padding) see attacker-chosen bytes first; the target also drives the plaintext parser directly, and asserts no panic and that anything opened re-seals to a row that opens to the same kind and body
- **`lakko-contacts/mailbox_binding_decode`** — `MailboxBinding::decode` + `verify` (ADR-0078 phase 1): the fixed 97-byte identity-signed binding carried in every reach grant, introduction and public record; asserts no panic and round-trip stability
- **`lakko-contacts/device_card_decode`** — `device_card::decode_device_card` ([ADR-0080](docs/adr/0080-device-cards.md)): a device card arrives as a DeviceGroup `ServiceEnvelope` from another device of the account or a corrupt DS row, reaching the bounded decoder (closed platform byte, five one-byte-length strings, UTF-8 and character tables) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, the length bounds, canonical round trip, `normalized` being the identity on a valid card, and that a card is never newer than itself
- **`lakko-contacts/link_claim_decode`** — `link_claim::decode_link_claim` + the dispatcher ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md), reworked 2026-10-17): a link-request claim arrives as a DeviceGroup `ServiceEnvelope` from another device of the account or a corrupt DS row and reaches the fixed-length decoder (domain, version before length) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, the one length, canonical round trip, the dispatcher agreeing, and, over arbitrary claim sets with a removed device, that `link_claim_outcome` names at most one winner, the author of the earliest roster claim, never a device outside the roster
- **`lakko-contacts/link_refusal_decode`** — `link_refusal::decode_link_refusal` + `verify_link_refusal` ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md) amendment, 2026-10-19): a link refusal arrives in a sealed mailbox row a stranger or the zero-trust DS can write, and reaches the fixed-length decoder (domain, version before length) before any pending contact is consulted; asserts no panic, that anything decoded re-composes to the identical bytes, and that verification under a fixed inviter key never accepts input the fuzzer could not have signed
- **`lakko-contacts/group_contact_request_decode`** — `group_contact_request::decode_group_contact_request` + the dispatcher + `check_group_contact_request` ([ADR-0086](docs/adr/0086-circle-directories.md) section 6, 2026-11-05): a contact request carried inside a group rides an application message any member (or the zero-trust DS replaying a corrupt row) can craft and reaches the bounded decoder (domain, version before length, a u32 sealed-box length bounded before the copy) before any state is consulted, and every member then tries to open the box; asserts no panic, the advertised length bounds, canonical round trip, the dispatcher agreeing, and that the receiver's rule under a fixed key refuses a box the fuzzer could not have sealed to it (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-contacts/channel_read_key_decode`** — `channel_read_key::decode_channel_read_key` + the dispatcher ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 7, phase B): the owner's channel read key sealed to one co-publisher rides a PublisherGroup application message any publisher (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, reaching the fixed-length decoder (domain, version before length) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, the one length, canonical round trip, the dispatcher agreeing, and that opening a hostile seal under a fixed key never panics (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-contacts/channel_epoch_seeds_decode`** — `channel_epoch_seeds::decode_channel_epoch_seeds` + the dispatcher ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 7, amended 2026-11-08): the earlier-seeds envelope an owner sends a new publisher rides a PublisherGroup application message any publisher (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, reaching the decoder (domain, version before any length, a clear count 1 to 64, the exact length it names) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, canonical round trip within the 163 to 2,431-byte bounds, the dispatcher agreeing, that opening under a fixed key never panics, and that the opened-list parser fed the same bytes accepts only strictly ascending epochs
- **`lakko-contacts/channel_rotate_request_decode`** — `channel_rotate_request::decode_channel_rotate_request` + the dispatcher ([ADR-0087](docs/adr/0087-group-and-channel-handles-and-privacy.md) section 15, amended 2026-11-08): a publisher's request that the owner rotate rides a PublisherGroup application message any publisher (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, reaching the fixed-length decoder (domain, version before length) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, canonical round trip at the one length, and the dispatcher naming the same channel
- **`lakko-contacts/link_founded_decode`** — `link_founded::decode_link_founded` + the dispatcher ([ADR-0079](docs/adr/0079-invite-link-makes-contacts.md), order of 2026-10-17): a link-founded note (request key, group id, founding Welcome) arrives as a DeviceGroup `ServiceEnvelope` from another device of the account or a corrupt DS row and reaches the bounded decoder (domain, version before any length, fixed part, a length-prefixed Welcome bounded at 32 768 bytes before it is copied) before any state is consulted; asserts no panic, the advertised length bounds, canonical round trip, and the dispatcher agreeing
- **`lakko-contacts/committer_delegation_decode`** — `committer_delegation::decode_committer_delegation` (ADR-0060 §6): a proposer's committer-delegation envelope (`added_account_key(32) ‖ count(1..=16) ‖ count × 185-byte delegation blob`) rides a group-journal row any member (or the zero-trust DS) can craft, reached before any state is consulted; structural only (the blobs are checked in `lakko_ds_proof::committer`). Asserts no panic, the length bounds, round-trip stability, and that the dispatcher classifies the same bytes the same way
- **`lakko-channels/channel_meta_decode`** — `meta::classify_post_plaintext` ([ADR-0051](docs/adr/0051-group-and-channel-names.md)): the reader-side classifier every decrypted channel post now passes through before the text path. The plaintext is authored by a publisher but the classifier runs on every subscriber, so the domain prefix check, the version gate, the two length prefixes and the character tables must never panic; asserts `Text` returns the input verbatim and a `Meta` result is within bounds, validates and re-composes byte-identically
- **`lakko-channels/post_decode`** — wire-format parser for `ChannelPost` (Phase 4a)
- **`lakko-channels/post_verify_and_decrypt`** — full receiver pipeline (decode → Ed25519 verify → AEAD open)
- **`lakko-channels/subscribe_open_seed`** — subscriber-side HPKE seed-envelope opener (Phase 4b)
- **`lakko-channels/epoch_id_from_be_bytes`** — 4-byte big-endian epoch-id parser
- **`lakko-channels/member_card_open`** — `member_card_epoch` + `open_member_card` ([ADR-0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) section 3): the channel member card (`version(1) ‖ epoch_id(4 BE) ‖ nonce(12) ‖ ciphertext`, bounded to `MEMBER_CARD_MIN_LEN..=MEMBER_CARD_MAX_LEN`) served from the channels server's member list, which the server or any uploading subscriber can fill with arbitrary bytes before a reader consults any key. Reads the clear epoch, then opens under a FIXED seed and a fixed `(channel, subscriber)` binding (the fuzzer cannot forge a tag, so it exercises the length bounds, version gate, slicing and the AEAD refusal); asserts an opened card fits `MAX_MEMBER_CARD_PROFILE_LEN`
- **`lakko-calls/header_decode`** — `SframeHeader::decode` (RFC 9605 §4.3, ADR-0013): the SFrame frame-header codec. The header is the on-wire prefix of every sealed media frame AND the AEAD AAD; a receiver decodes it — via the `lakko-sframe-native` C ABI on iOS/Android or the wasm worker on web — on bytes handed over by an untrusted SFU / peer BEFORE the AEAD authenticates the frame, so the config-byte packing (`X ‖ KKK ‖ Y ‖ CCC`) + the variable-length extended big-endian KID/CTR fields are fully attacker-controlled. Asserts decode never panics + a success reports a `consumed` within the buffer and `MAX_HEADER_LEN` + canonical-form round-trip stability + the encoder-side `encode → decode` round-trip on a fuzzer-chosen `(kid, ctr)`
- **`lakko-calls/signaling_payload_decode`** — `parse_signaling_payload` (call-signaling wire parser): decodes the plaintext `CallSignalingPayload` that falls out of an `Incoming::Application` frame — E2EE-decrypted but crafted by any group member, so the prost decode + oneof-presence gate + the fixed 16-byte `call_id` length check + array copy + `MediaFlags::from_bits` + `EndReason::from_proto` (C-13 unknown-fold) are attacker-touchable before any field is trusted. Asserts no-panic + round-trip stability of every accepted body (transitively the 16-byte `call_id` invariant)
- **`lakko-calls/media_frame_open`** — `MediaFrameParts::split` + `CallKeyRing::try_open_media_frame_with_overlap` ([ADR-0071](docs/adr/0071-codec-header-in-the-clear.md)): a sealed media frame is `clear_prefix ‖ ciphertext_and_tag ‖ sframe_header ‖ trailer`, and a receiver reads the 3-byte trailer on bytes from an untrusted SFU or peer BEFORE the AEAD authenticates anything. Asserts the split never panics and accounts for every byte within the layout's bounds, that the open under a fixed seed never panics and never accepts (with and without a retained previous key), and that the sender's prefix rule stays within the frame and the bound and a sealed input opens back to itself (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-calls/relay_open`** — `RelayKey::open` under a fixed call seed, `classify_relay_plaintext`, `decode_call_name` (raw and behind its domain; bounds, seq >= 1 and exact length asserted, 2026-11-07), `decode_call_chat` (raw and behind its domain; bounds, the text rule, seq >= 1 and exact length asserted, 2026-11-07), and `decode_peer_announcement` fed directly ([ADR-0078](docs/adr/0078-servers-cannot-link-accounts.md) section 2): every `signal-relay` payload arrives from a peer the SFU admitted by an anonymous token, so the length bounds, the version byte, the AEAD refusal and the announcement layout (domain, version, call id, peer-id length and charset, signer key, signature) see attacker-chosen bytes first. Asserts no panic, that the open under a fixed key never accepts unsealed input, that a payload the key sealed opens back to it, and that a decoded announcement's peer id is within bounds and its length matches the layout (compile-checked, not run)
- **`lakko-transport/shadowsocks_decode_request`** — SS-2022 client-to-server outer-header parser (Phase 5, T-10 surface; fixed-zero PSK + fresh ReplayWindow)
- **`lakko-transport/shadowsocks_decode_response`** — SS-2022 server-to-client outer-header parser (fixed-zero PSK + fixed expected request-salt; exercises the `WrongResponseSalt` branch)
- **`lakko-transport/rendezvous_decode_and_verify`** — full attacker-controlled rendezvous pipeline: `serde_json::from_slice::<BootstrapConfig>` → on Ok, `BootstrapConfig::verify` with fixed trust anchor + fixed `now` (T-4 / T-5 / T-7 / T-8 surfaces)
- **`lakko-transport/ech_decode`** — ECH config list parser (T-12 surface; DNS HTTPS RR bytes are attacker-controlled if the DNS path is poisoned)
- **`lakko-backup/envelope_v2_header_of`** — v2 backup envelope plaintext chain-header parse (G-11 surface; cheapest attacker-touchable path, no recovery phrase needed)
- **`lakko-backup/envelope_v2_open`** — full v2 AEAD-open path under a fixed phrase (covers schema-version gate + salt/nonce slicing + chain-header decode + ct_len arithmetic + Argon2id KEK + AEAD-open)
- **`lakko-backup/backup_blob_parse`** — `parse_backup_blob(blob)` (G-11, [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md)): the container-tag framing discriminator on the restore path. The whole stored blob is attacker-controlled (a hostile storage tier returns arbitrary bytes on `GET /backup`), and this peek runs BEFORE any head verification or AEAD-open — covers the `0xB1` framed-vs-legacy byte-0 discriminator, the framed-but-truncated hard-fail (`0xB1` + too short → `Truncated`, never a panic on a hostile length), and the bounded head-slice + envelope-tail split
- **`lakko-svc-sfu/video_fec`** — `video_fec` ([ADR-0067](docs/adr/0067-loss-resilient-call-media.md) step 4): the SFU's ULPFEC receiver (`fec_receive.rs`) and sender (`fec_send.rs`) driven with arbitrary sequences of a publisher's media and FEC packets, attacker-chosen by an admitted peer; asserts the window bounds and the shape of rebuilt packets (built, not run)
- **`lakko-svc-sfu/client_frame_decode`** — `serde_json::from_slice::<ClientFrame>` on attacker-controlled WS text frame bytes (C-39 surface; admitted-but-malicious peer reaches this parser post-JWT, pre-routing)
- **`lakko-tuf/signed_root_from_json`** — outermost TUF metadata parser for the `Root` role envelope (GA-5)
- **`lakko-tuf/signed_snapshot_from_json`** — outermost TUF metadata parser for the `Snapshot` role envelope
- **`lakko-tuf/signed_targets_from_json`** — outermost TUF metadata parser for the `Targets` role envelope (widest deserialiser surface; carries the targets `BTreeMap`)
- **`lakko-tuf/signed_timestamp_from_json`** — outermost TUF metadata parser for the `Timestamp` role envelope
- **`lakko-tuf/delegation_walk`** — the delegated-targets walk (GA-5): feeds the fuzz input as a delegated role's metadata through `resolve_target` (via `fuzz_api::resolve_delegated_from_bytes`), exercising the delegated-metadata `serde_json` parse + signature / threshold / `_type` / expiry verification + the walk's depth / cycle / budget bounds on fully attacker-controlled bytes (a compromised mirror serving arbitrary delegated targets)
- **`lakko-call-jwt/jwt_parse_unverified`** — JWS Compact parser (3 dot-segments → base64url-decode → JSON-decode header + claims). UTF-8 gate at the harness edge keeps coverage focused on the base64 / JSON parser branches
- **`lakko-call-jwt/jwt_verify_with_jwks`** — full JWS pipeline: signature path + kid lookup + algorithm-confusion check + base64 of signature + `Signature::from_bytes` length check + `exp` clock comparison. Fixed all-zero-seed single-key JWKS + fixed `now_secs` so the harness is reproducible and the fuzzer can learn the deterministic `kid`
- **`lakko-call-jwt/jwks_parse`** — `parse_jwks(doc_bytes)` JSON decoder; the auth service publishes the JWKS at `/.well-known/jwks.json` and a compromised publish endpoint can serve arbitrary bytes
- **`lakko-transparency/supersession_open`** — `SupersessionProof::open` bounded wire parser (G-4): the fixed 145-byte layout (version ‖ old_pubkey ‖ new_pubkey ‖ issued_at ‖ expires_at ‖ sig) + Ed25519-verify against the embedded old key. Attacker-touchable — a peer hands these bytes in after a rotation, and the verify path is now FFI-exposed
- **`lakko-transparency/supersession_verify`** — full `verify_supersession(bytes, trusted_old_pubkey, now)` pipeline: parse + embedded-key sig check, then the trusted-old-key match + `<= 30-day` window gate + freshness/expiry. Fixed all-zero-seed trusted old key + fixed `now` so the harness is reproducible
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_log_inclusion`** — the FFI inclusion gate: operator-root signature + Merkle audit-path reconstruction binding the `(username_hash, pubkey, version)` leaf, on attacker-controlled root + proof bytes
- **`lakko-transparency/conversation_link_open`** — `verify_conversation_link` and `ConversationLink::open` (ADR-0074 section 3.7): a directory serves the 201-byte two-way link beside the identity bundle, so a malicious directory chooses every byte a client parses on each lookup; fixed layout, version gate, two Ed25519 verifications under a fixed current key; asserts nothing accepted links a key to itself (built, not run)
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_leaf_genesis`** — `verify_leaf_genesis(sig, username_hash, pubkey)` (G-30 #2, ADR-0019): the 64-byte genesis self-sig domain-separated parse + Ed25519-verify against the served key
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_supersession_chain`** — the full rotation chain gate: root sig + no-rollback + Merkle inclusion + the old-key supersession proof + strictly-newer version + freshness, on attacker-controlled inputs
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_consistency`** — `verify_consistency(old_size, old_root, new_size, new_root, proof)` (G-30 #1, ADR-0020): the RFC 6962 §2.1.2 consistency-proof verifier, reconstructing both roots from attacker-controlled proof bytes (a fork must fail)
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_witness_cosig`** — `verify_witness_cosig(bytes, witness_key, expected_tree_root, expected_log_size)` (ADR-0020): the 105-byte cosig blob bounded parse + binding-to-served-root + Ed25519-verify, the FFI surface a malicious serving tier can feed arbitrary bytes
- **`lakko-transparency/verify_witness_quorum`** — `verify_witness_quorum(cosigs, pinned_keys, threshold, expected_tree_root, expected_log_size)` (ADR-0039, G-30 finding-1): the K-of-N quorum verifier. The cosig list AND the pinned key set are attacker-influenceable bytes a malicious serving tier can craft (duplicate / replay a cosig, flood the list, splice a wrong-root cosig), so the dedup-by-distinct-key counting loop, the `threshold == 0` / `threshold > N` fail-closed guards, and the per-(key × cosig) `verify_witness_cosig` bounds must all stay panic-free before any quorum verdict
- **`lakko-attest/attestation_open`** — `ActionAttestation::open(bytes, verifying_key)` (G-13, [ADR-0015](docs/adr/0015-device-removal-authorization.md)): the device-removal attestation parser — a fixed-layout 121-byte blob (`version‖account_id(16)‖action_hash(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)`) that arrives ALONGSIDE a peer's DeviceGroup `Remove` commit, so a malformed envelope is fully attacker-controlled and reaches this bounded parser before any state is consulted. Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/genesis_attestation_open`** — `GenesisAttestation::open(bytes, verifying_key)` (G-30 finding 2, [ADR-0036](docs/adr/0036-auth-attested-genesis.md)): the auth-attested genesis parser — a fixed-layout 153-byte blob (`version‖account_id(16)‖username_hash(32)‖pubkey(32)‖expires_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)`) served as an identity-bundle sidecar field a malicious discovery / DS can craft, so a malformed envelope is fully attacker-controlled and reaches this bounded parser before any state is consulted (client first-observation verify + the transparency append gate). Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/backup_head_open`** — `BackupHead::open(bytes, verifying_key)` (G-11, [ADR-0037](docs/adr/0037-backup-head-rollback-commitment.md)): the auth-signed backup-head parser — a fixed-layout 129-byte blob (`format_version‖account_id(16)‖backup_version(8 BE)‖envelope_hash(32)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE)‖sig(64)`) that arrives as the `0xB1`-framed prefix of a storage backup blob a malicious storage tier crafts, so a malformed envelope is fully attacker-controlled and reaches this bounded parser before any state is consulted (the client restore-path verify). Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/release_manifest_open`** — `ReleaseManifest::open(bytes, verifying_key)` (GA update notice, [ADR-0038](docs/adr/0038-update-available-notice.md)): the signed "update available" parser — a fixed-layout 89-byte blob (`format_version‖latest_release(8 BE)‖min_supported_release(8 BE)‖signed_at_unix_millis(8 BE)‖sig(64)`) fetched from a static host a malicious network / serving tier could tamper, so a malformed envelope is fully attacker-controlled and reaches this bounded parser before any state is consulted (the client's pre-notice verify — before any banner is rendered). Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/username_cert_open`** — `UsernameCertificate::open(bytes, verifying_key)` ([ADR-0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) section 5): the discovery-signed username certificate — a fixed-layout 153-byte blob (`version‖oprf_hash(64)‖account_id(16)‖issued_at_unix_millis(8 BE)‖sig(64)`, domain `lakko/username-cert/v1`, the same length as a genesis attestation so only the domain separates them) carried opaquely inside a profile envelope any group member, channel subscriber or stranger can craft, reaching this bounded parser before any state is consulted. Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/key_reset_attestation_open`** — `KeyResetAttestation::open(bytes, verifying_key)` ([ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md)): the key-reset attestation parser — a fixed-layout 145-byte blob since ADR-0078 phase 6 (`version(2)‖account_ref(32)‖key_generation(8 BE)‖new_signature_pubkey(32)‖signed_at(8 BE)‖sig(64)`) that reaches discovery's identity upload and a client's own persisted publish-owed record, so a malformed envelope is fully attacker-controlled and reaches this bounded parser before any state is consulted. Opened against a FIXED verifying key (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid Ed25519 sig, so it exercises the length / version / field-slicing / signature-parse / verify-fail path)
- **`lakko-attest/donation_open`** — `DonationMethod` / `DonationPhone` decode and open (`fuzz_api::open_donation`, `docs/ux/donations.md` section 3): a donation statement is served by discovery, which a hostile operator controls, so every byte reaches the length-prefixed parsers before anything is shown; both parsers run on the same input under a FIXED key (the fuzzer cannot forge a signature), exercising the length prefixes, bounds checked before any copy, UTF-8, the character and per-kind rules and the verify failure; asserts that whatever decodes re-signs to a statement of the same length that decodes to the same content
- **`lakko-sync/sync_replay`** — the multi-device sync receive path (`fuzz_api::replay_and_check`). `lakko-sync` carries no on-the-wire byte parser of its own (the DeviceGroup substrate owns deserialization, Phase 2); the attacker-relevant surface is the LWW / HLC **logic** fed by a peer device's ops after AEAD-open. The harness decodes the fuzz bytes into a receive sequence of adversarial `Hlc` stamps + ops and asserts the ordering-integrity invariants a malicious/compromised peer must not break: HLC monotonicity (`tick` / `merge_remote` never wind the local clock backwards), `EditChain::merge` == append (documented commutativity), and resolve idempotence — a property oracle, not a parse-crash target (the arithmetic is already saturating). Order-independence under HLC ties is deliberately NOT asserted (`current` tie-breaks by vector position by design)
- **`lakko-push/push_decode_json`** — `PushPayload::decode_json(bytes)` (`fuzz_api::decode_push_payload`): the on-device push-payload parser. A push notification's `data` payload is relayed verbatim by the OS push pipeline (APNs / FCM / UnifiedPush) and decoded on wake-up before any further processing, so the input is fully attacker-controlled. Drives the full pipeline — `serde_json::from_slice` → the schema-version gate → the kind dispatch (`g` / `m` / `w` + field-presence rules) → the base64 pseudonym decode + 32-byte length check
- **`lakko-attachments/message_body_parse`** — `parse(bytes)` + `parse_body(bytes)` (`fuzz_api::parse_message_body`): the receiver-side MLS Application message-body decoder. A malicious or compromised group member can craft arbitrary envelope bytes, which reach this decode path on receipt before any rendering. Drives the full receive chain — `serde_json::from_slice::<Envelope>` → the schema-version gate → the bare-UTF-8 `from_utf8_lossy` fallback → the per-ref base64 decoders (`decode_blob_id` / `decode_key` / `decode_nonce`, each a base64 decode + fixed length check) on every decoded reference — AND (ADR-0023/0024) the `parse_body` classifier: the `edit_of` / `delete_of` / `react_to` / `typing` / `read_up_to` op fields, the `MAX_EMOJI_BYTES` cap, and the >1-op-field / reaction-without-emoji fail-closed degrade-to-`Chat` paths. **ADR-0047:** the same bytes also drive the media-metadata surface — the lenient `kind` / `duration_ms` / `waveform` / `width` / `height` / `thumb` decode, the thumb's own decoders and `inline_media_type` — and the harness **asserts** `fuzz_api::media_metadata_within_bounds`: every media field that survives parse is within its bound, and re-composing the parsed refs then parsing again yields the same refs (sanitization is idempotent); **ADR-0052:** `fuzz_api::reply_within_bounds` asserts every reply snapshot that survives parse is within its bound and that `compose_reply -> parse_body` is idempotent on it, and `fuzz_api::forward_within_bounds` the same for a forward (name within bound, origin whole or absent, `compose_forward -> parse_body` idempotent)
- **`lakko-auth-token/token_verify`** — `TokenSecret::verify` + `TokenSecret::verify_refresh` + `TokenSecret::verify_fresh_grant` (the `k1` fresh-auth grant, since 2026-10-04) (`fuzz_api::verify_token`, [ADR-0073](docs/adr/0073-account-key-reset.md)): the `v2` access-token and `r1` refresh-credential parsers + verifiers. A client or peer presents an access token on every bearer request and a refresh credential on auth's refresh route, so the string is fully attacker-controlled and reaches the parser before admission. The harness verifies the same input as BOTH kinds against a FIXED 32-byte secret (the fuzzer cannot forge a valid HMAC, so it exercises the dot-split → version gate → base64url account_id decode + 16-byte length check → `u64` generation parse → `i64` expiry parse → base64url tag decode + 32-byte length check → HMAC compute + constant-time compare → expiry check) at a FIXED `now_ms`. UTF-8 gate at the harness edge keeps coverage on the token parser
- **`lakko-mnemonic/mnemonic_parse`** — the BIP39 recovery parsers (`Mnemonic::from_phrase` + `from_entropy`, via `fuzz_api::parse_mnemonic`). `from_phrase` parses a user-typed / device-relayed recovery phrase; `from_entropy` rebuilds the phrase from 16 bytes that arrive from outside the device: the plaintext of an opened passkey-vault blob (served by the server) and the another-device transfer v2 payload (ADR-0072). The harness runs both on the same input — the raw bytes through `from_entropy` (16-byte length gate → BIP39 checksum reconstruction) and, when valid UTF-8, the string through `from_phrase` (12-word count gate → wordlist lookup → checksum)
- **`lakko-mnemonic/recovery_payload_decode`** — `fuzz_api::decode_recovery_payload` ([ADR-0074](docs/adr/0074-account-takeover-resistance.md) "Joining after a rotation", G-56): the another-device recovery payload v3 and its row tag. The row arrives in a rendezvous mailbox anyone can write to and its prefix is read before any key is involved; the opened plaintext carries the grant, the phrase entropy and up to eight predecessor seeds a joining device stores before it binds. The count is bounded before any copy and the length must be exact; asserts that whatever decodes re-composes to the identical bytes
- **`lakko-ds-proof/proof_parse`** — `Proof::parse` + `verify` (`fuzz_api::parse_and_verify`, [ADR-0053](docs/adr/0053-ds-journal-authorisation.md)): the `x-lakko-ds-proof` header a claimed journal's request carries. Anyone can send the DS a request, so the header is fully attacker-controlled and reaches the parser before any key is consulted — the five-field split, the version gate, the two base64url decodes with fixed-length checks, the `u64` parses, and then the window + Ed25519 verify against a fixed key on whatever parses. Asserts no panic
- **`lakko-ds-proof/delegation_parse`** — `Delegation::decode` + `parse_header_value` + `verify_delegation` (`fuzz_api::delegation_parse`, [ADR-0060](docs/adr/0060-scoped-discovery.md) section 2): the fixed 185-byte reach-key delegation in the `x-lakko-ds-delegation` header of a write to a closed mailbox, attacker-controlled and parsed before any reach key is looked up — the length and version gates, the base64url header decode (length checked first), and then the mailbox / expiry / 24 h lifetime / Ed25519 checks against a fixed mailbox and clock. Asserts no panic and that whatever parses re-encodes to the identical bytes and header text
- **`lakko-ds-proof/statement_decode`** — `SignedStatement::decode` + `verify` (`fuzz_api::decode_statement`, [ADR-0057](docs/adr/0057-journal-ownership.md)): the body of every journal-ownership route (claim, reset, register epoch, registrars, root transfer, push binding) is attacker-controlled and decoded before any root or registrar is looked up. Bounded, exact, panic-free; whatever parses must re-encode byte for byte
- **`lakko-device-link/device_link_accept`** — `Responder::accept` (`fuzz_api::accept_arbitrary`, ADR-0049 section 2 as amended 2026-09-28): the signed-in device's verify of the new device's `pake_a`, `confirm_a` and sealed pairing payload, all three relay-controlled. Under a fixed responder (fixed request id, handle and RNG) the input is split `pake_a(33) || confirm_a(32) || sealed(rest)`; the length gates, the SPAKE2 side-byte and point decode, the key schedule, the constant-time MAC compare and the AEAD open must never panic, and the harness asserts nothing is ever accepted
- **`lakko-feed/feed_rule_decode`** — `decode_feed_rule` (`fuzz_api::decode_and_check`, [ADR-0063](docs/adr/0063-on-device-feed.md)): a shared feed is a file or a `/feed/import#` link someone else made, so every byte is attacker-chosen. Domain, version, flag bits, option bytes, bounded counts before allocation, UTF-8 and every bound; asserts no panic, that a decoded rule validates and re-encodes to the identical bytes, and that its export carries no conversation id. The same properties also run as proptests over random and damaged valid encodings in `crates/lakko-feed/tests/decode_hostile.rs`
- **`lakko-vault/vault_head_decode`**, **`vault_chunk_decode`**, **`vault_index_decode`** — the three record decoders of the conversation vault ([ADR-0064](docs/adr/0064-conversation-backup-and-device-re-entry.md)): a restored device decodes them from bytes a storage tier served, after the AEAD opened them under the account's own key, so a hostile input needs a device of the account or a rollback to an older genuine object; bounded counts before allocation, exact length, every bound. Each asserts no panic and that whatever decodes re-encodes to the identical bytes (the head target also drives `DevicesHead`; the chunk target also asserts a self-merge is sorted with unique ids)
- **`lakko-vault/vault_object_open`** — `open_object` under a fixed key: the format byte, the bucket check, the AEAD refusal, and the padding parser fed directly, since a fuzzer cannot forge the tag
- **`lakko-vault/storage_proof_parse`** — the `x-lakko-storage-proof` header the storage service will parse on every vault and blob-delete request, attacker-controlled and reached before any key is looked up; asserts no panic and that whatever parses re-encodes to the identical text
- **`lakko-circle/card_open`** — `open_card` under a fixed term, the padding parser behind the AEAD, and a seal-open round trip ([ADR-0086](docs/adr/0086-circle-directories.md) section 2): a sealed card sits in a row anyone in the circle can read and the owner or the Delivery Service can replace, and a reader does up to 65 AEAD trials on it. Length and format are checked first; asserts no panic, a parsed card shorter than its padding, one sealed length, and that a card this crate sealed opens to itself
- **`lakko-circle/{head_decode,grant_decode,path_walk,filter_open,row_open,state_decode}`** — the other parsers of a circle directory (ADR-0086): the head and the member grant (canonical encodings), a member's walk up its path over edges the Delivery Service chose, a sealed filter and a sealed row with the plaintext parsers behind their AEADs, and the owner's device-local state
- **`lakko-anon-token/token_verify`** — `Token::decode` + `verify_token` (`fuzz_api::decode_and_verify`, [ADR-0054](docs/adr/0054-anti-abuse-without-client-work.md) decision 2): the anonymous token a consuming service (discovery, the DS, push-gw) receives on an anonymous request. Anyone can send one, so the bytes are fully attacker-controlled and reach the parser before any state is consulted — the fixed-length gate, the version gate, the context byte, and then, for every context under a fixed master secret and clock, the epoch window, the key derivation, the VOPRF evaluation and the constant-time compare. Since S3 the same input, read as UTF-8, also drives `decode_header_value` (the `x-lakko-anon-token` text a service decodes first). Asserts no panic, that a token which decodes re-encodes to the identical bytes, and that a header value which decodes re-encodes to the identical text
- **`lakko-l10n/bundle_accept`** — `accept_bundle` ([ADR-0061](docs/adr/0061-interface-localization.md)): the signed translation bundle a native client downloads from a static host a malicious network or operator could tamper, under a fixed verifying key and policy. The fuzzer cannot forge the signature, so it exercises the size bound, the header layout, the length arithmetic and the signature refusal
- **`lakko-l10n/message_parse`** — the ADR-0061 message syntax: `Message::parse`, then `format` in every supported language and `check_translation` against itself, plus the same bytes loaded as a source catalog. Translation strings are signed but written by translators; the parser must never panic

The `fuzz_api` modules (marked `#[doc(hidden)]`; `lakko-mls`, `lakko-contacts` and the other crates whose targets above name one) are the public entry points for harnesses; they are not part of the supported public API.

Fuzzing corpora are not committed (`fuzz/corpus/`, `fuzz/artifacts/` are gitignored); they are spun up locally.

The `.github/workflows/fuzz-quick.yml` workflow actually RUNS every fuzz target for a bounded budget (60s default, configurable via `workflow_dispatch`) on nightly cron (03:00 UTC) — `just fuzz-check` only smoke-builds. Matrix runs one job per fuzz crate (one matrix entry per `crates/*/fuzz` + `services/sfu/fuzz`, kept in lock-step with the `just fuzz-check` list); crashes upload to the workflow run page as 14-day-retention artifacts. Concurrency limited to 1 so overlapping nightly runs don't pile up.

## Local dev

### First time

```bash
# 1. Workspace .env (loaded via dotenvy in each service main() in debug)
cp .env.example .env
# tweak per-service DATABASE_URLs / RUST_LOG if needed

# 2. MariaDB locally — a shared docker container `mariadb:latest` on 3306.
# Each service has its OWN database — sqlx tracks applied migrations in a
# hardcoded `_sqlx_migrations` table per DB, so two services on the same DB
# see each other's migrations as "missing" and refuse to start.
docker exec -i mariadb mariadb -u root -p<root_pw> <<'SQL'
  CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS lakko_auth
    CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
  CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS lakko_ds
    CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
  CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS lakko_discovery
    CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
  CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS lakko_push_gw
    CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
  CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS 'lakko'@'%' IDENTIFIED BY 'devpass';
  GRANT ALL ON lakko_auth.*      TO 'lakko'@'%';
  GRANT ALL ON lakko_ds.*        TO 'lakko'@'%';
  GRANT ALL ON lakko_discovery.* TO 'lakko'@'%';
  GRANT ALL ON lakko_push_gw.*   TO 'lakko'@'%';
  FLUSH PRIVILEGES;
SQL
# Find the root password with `docker inspect mariadb | grep ROOT_PASSWORD`.
```

### Running a service

| Method | Command |
|---|---|
| CLI / justfile | `just dev-auth` / `just dev-ds` / `just dev-storage` |
| `cargo run` directly | `cargo run -p lakko-svc-auth` |
| RustRover | local Run Config (Cargo command `run --package lakko-svc-auth`) |

All three paths are identical — env vars come from `.env` via `dotenvy::dotenv()` in `main()` (debug build) either way. RustRover Run Configurations live in `.run/` (gitignored) — every developer creates one locally via the GUI or copies the example below.

Each service reads its own `LAKKO_<NAME>_DATABASE_URL` first, falls back to the generic `DATABASE_URL` if unset. The fallback is for the case of running a single service in isolation; the canonical `.env` has all per-service URLs set.

Production uses a completely different mechanism (systemd `EnvironmentFile=` / `/etc/lakko/lakko.env`); `dotenvy` is gated by `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` and is not called in release.

#### Example RustRover Run Config

Run → Edit Configurations → `+` → Cargo. Fill in:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Name | `lakko-svc-auth (dev)` |
| Command | `run --package lakko-svc-auth --bin lakko-svc-auth` |
| Working directory | `$PROJECT_DIR$` |
| Environment variables | (optional, can stay empty — `.env` is picked up) |

If you want to duplicate env vars inline (RustRover sets them **before** dotenvy and overrides `.env`):

```
LAKKO_AUTH_DATABASE_URL=mysql://lakko:devpass@127.0.0.1:3306/lakko_auth
LAKKO_BIND=127.0.0.1:8081
RUST_LOG=info,sqlx=warn,lakko_svc_auth=debug,lakko_mls=debug
RUST_BACKTRACE=1
```

### Verify

```bash
curl -i http://127.0.0.1:8081/health   # → 200, "ok"
curl -i http://127.0.0.1:8081/ready    # → 200, "ready" (if the DB is up)
curl -i http://127.0.0.1:8081/version  # → 200, JSON
```

## Pre-commit checklist

- [ ] `just fmt` is clean
- [ ] `just lint` is clean (zero warnings)
- [ ] `just test` is clean
- [ ] `just reuse` is clean (SPDX headers in place)
- [ ] Changes in crypto / mls / proto include an ADR reference in the commit message
- [ ] Changes in `schemas/` are backwards-compatible (proto reserved fields, never delete numbers)
- [ ] If security-relevant — a property test or fuzz target has been added

## Specialized skills — routing (read BEFORE writing code)

This repo ships domain-specialist skills under `.claude/skills/`. They carry the per-area invariants, build commands, and review checklists so you do not rediscover them each turn. **Routing is mandatory, not optional** — before editing files in an area, invoke the owning skill via the `Skill` tool so its SKILL.md loads into context. This OVERRIDES the default "just start editing" behavior.

| When the task touches… | Invoke this skill FIRST |
|---|---|
| `crates/` or `services/` (general Rust) | `rust-core` |
| `services/sfu/` or `crates/lakko-calls/` (SFU / media / SFrame) | `sfu-media` |
| `apps/ios/` | `ios-client` |
| `apps/android/` | `android-client` |
| `apps/web/` | `web-client` |
| `infra/` or deploy / ops | `infra-deploy` |
| Writing tests, running a verification pass, triaging a failure | `qa` |
| Designing / reviewing a user-facing screen or flow, cross-platform UI consistency | `ux-ui` |
| An architectural decision (new crate / protocol / schema migration / security-model change), writing an ADR, or keeping docs (ADRs / module map / ROADMAP / invariant index) in sync | `architecture` |
| Authoring / updating a module's codespeak spec (`<module>.sc.md`), or a spec-drift failure | `spec` (mandatory when you change a module that HAS a `.sc.md` — update it in the same change; `just spec-check` gates it) |
| A task spanning >1 area, or unsure which area owns it | `lakko-dev` (coordinator — it classifies, routes, and sequences cross-cutting work) |
| Reviewing security-sensitive changes (crypto / MLS / transport / auth) | `lakko-security` (run ONLY on explicit maintainer request — never auto-gate) |
| An independent **external-auditor** review of a surface (not of a diff): an audit engagement, a G-39-style crypto review, an audit report, a retest of reported findings | `audit-lead` (entry point; it scopes, dispatches the `audit-*` workstreams, runs the verification gate, assembles the report). Run ONLY on explicit maintainer request |

Rules:
- **UX-first for any user-facing feature.** If a feature involves user interaction — a new screen, a changed flow, a new interaction, anything a user sees or touches — designing the UX via `ux-ui` is one of the FIRST steps, BEFORE the architecture/schema/core implementation. The UX spec (states, flow, controls, empty/loading/error cases, cross-platform shape) shapes the contract; discovering a needed state after the core is built is a rework loop. This applies whether the work goes through `lakko-dev` or you handle it directly. Skip only for a purely non-user-facing change (backend / crypto / infra with no UI surface).
- **Single-domain task** → invoke the one owning skill, then do the work.
- **Cross-cutting task** (e.g. a Rust change wired through FFI to all 3 clients) → invoke `lakko-dev` and let it sequence the work (UX design first for user-facing work → schema → Rust core → FFI → parallel client fan-out → verify) via sub-agents.
- The background skills (`rust-core`, `ios-client`, `android-client`, `web-client`, `sfu-media`, `infra-deploy`) are model-invocation only — YOU must call them; they do not self-trigger by path. Skipping the routing step is a process violation.
- `lakko-security` is an explicit-request gate. You MAY remind the maintainer it is available when crypto/MLS/transport/auth is touched, but do NOT run it unprompted.
- **The `audit-*` team is not `lakko-security` and never substitutes for it.** `lakko-security` reviews **one change** in-house, working *from* the invariant index. The audit team reviews a **surface at a frozen commit** as an outside firm would, treating this repository's documentation — the invariant index, the ADR table, CLAUDE.md itself — as **claims under test** rather than as evidence, and delivering findings with severities, a coverage statement and a retest instead of a verdict on a diff. Entry point `audit-lead`; workstreams `audit-recon` / `audit-crypto` / `audit-protocol` / `audit-impl` / `audit-clients` / `audit-backend` (the implementation workstream also owns the measured tooling pass), gated by `audit-verify`. They are **read-only: an auditor reports and never repairs** — implementing a fix is separate work the maintainer commissions after the report lands. Engagements are recorded under [`docs/audit/`](docs/audit/README.md). Also an explicit-request gate; never run unprompted.

## Workflow for the AI agent
